P·L EVANGELIUM SECUNDUM LUCAM 114

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 114 ·

EVANGELIUM SECUNDUM LUCAM

One book of the Glossa ordinaria — the whole gloss, book by book.

PL 114, coll. 243–356  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  New English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Anselm of Laon and his school

Anselmus Laudunensis et schola

twelfth century

The masters of the cathedral school of Laon, gathered around Anselm (c. 1050–1117), to whom the making of the Glossa ordinaria on the Bible is traditionally credited. The gloss is a school's work rather than one man's: it draws its comment from the Fathers — Rabanus, Isidore, Bede, Jerome, Gregory — and names them as it goes, and it grew by accretion over generations. Migne prints it under the name of Walafrid Strabo, an ascription now known to be mistaken.

PROLOGUS B. HIERONYMI.

( Vide inter ejus opera. )

PROLOGUE OF BLESSED JEROME.

( Vide inter ejus opera. )

PROOEMIUM LUCAE IN EVANGELIUM SUUM.

VERS. 1.-- Quoniam quidem, etc. (BEDA.) Lucas de omnibus quae fecit Jesus et docuit, etc., usque ad magis ordinarent narrationem quam historiae texerent veritatem.

243DVERS. 3.-- Omnia. (ID.) Non omnia quae assecutus, sed de omnibus quae ad fidem legentium confirmandam credit idonea. Ex consulto enim multa praeterit, quae alii dicunt, ut diversa in Evangelio gratia refulgeret, et propriis quibusdam libri singuli mysteriorum gestorumque miracula eminerent. Theophile. Theophilus, amans Deum, etc., usque ad fidem perpetuae divinitatis et temporaneae dispensationis illius debet ordinem nosse.

PROEM OF LUKE ON HIS OWN GOSPEL.

VERS. 1.-- Forasmuch indeed, etc. (BEDA.) Luke, concerning all the things which Jesus did and taught, etc., as far as they were setting a narrative in order rather than weaving the truth of history.

243DVERS. 3.-- All things. (ID.) Not all the things which he had attained, but out of all those which he judges apt for confirming the faith of his readers. For of set purpose he passes over many things which the others tell, so that a varied grace might shine out in the Gospel, and that in the several books the marvels of the mysteries and of the deeds might stand out, each by certain things of its own. Theophilus. Theophilus, one loving God, etc., as far as he ought to know the faith of the everlasting Godhead and the order of his dispensation in time.

CAPUT PRIMUM.

VERS. 5.-- Fuit in diebus Herodis regis Judaeae. (AMBR.) Vitulus sacerdotalis hostia. Per vitulum ergo hoc Evangelium figuratur, in quo a sacerdotibus inchoatur, et in vitulo consummatur, id est in Christo, qui pro mundi vita immolatur.

Sacerdos quidam. Implevit ordinem, dixit regem, et regionem in qua fuit, sacerdotem et gentem ejus et uxorem illius indicat. Zacharias. Zacharias, memor Domini: cui apparet Angelus a dextris altaris, quia ei qui memor Domini est demonstratur mysterium.

De vice Abia. (BEDA.) Abia, Pater Dominus, etc., 244Dusque ad sicut per septem Testamentum declaratur Vetus.

Elizabeth. Elizabeth, Dei mei saturitas, signat Mariam quae plena Deo fuit.

VERS. 6.-- Erant autem ambo. (BEDA, AMBR.) Plena laudatio quae genus in majoribus, mores in aequitate, officium in sacerdotio, factum in mandatis, judicium in justificationibus comprehendit. Justi ante Deum. (AMBR.) Non enim omnis qui justus est ante homines justus est ante Deum; aliter enim vident homines, aliter Deus: homines in facie, Deus in corde. Ideoque fieri potest, ut aliquis affectata bonitate populari justus videatur mihi, justus tamen non sit ante Deum, si justitia non ex mentis simplicitate formetur, sed adulatione simuletur.

245AVERS. 7.-- Et non erat illis. (BEDA.) Divinitus procuratum fuit, ut de provectis diuque fructu conjugii privatis Joannes nasceretur, ut inopinato ortu prolis et ipsos donum Dei gratius afficeret, et caeteros stupor miraculi pararet auditui futuri prophetiae. Unde postea subditur: Posuerunt omnes qui audierant in corde suo dicentes: Quis putas puer iste erit?

VERS. 8.-- Factum est autem. Cum ex praecepto Moysi uno sacerdote decedente, unus succedere jubeatur, tempore David statutum est ut vicissim ministrantes per octonos dies a sabbato usque ad sabbatum tempore vicis suae, singuli castimoniae studerent, nec interea domum tangerent.

VERS. 9.-- Sorte. (BEDA.) Non nova tunc sorte electus, quando incensum erat adolendum, etc., usque 245Bad praedicitur dum ille promittitur praecursurus.

VERS. 12.-- Et Zacharias turbatus est videns, et timor irruit super eum, etc. (AMBR.) Solemus turbari et a nostro affectu alienari quando perstringimur superioris potestatis occursu. Sed sicut humani defectus est terreri, ita angelicae benignitatis est paventem suo aspectu blandiendo solari; contra, daemones, si quos sua praesentia territos senserint, ampliore horrore concutiunt.

VERS. 13.-- Quoniam exaudita est deprecatio tua: et uxor tua Elizabeth pariet tibi filium, et vocabis nomen ejus. Exaudita est magis quam petisti. Rogasti pro liberatione plebis, et donatus est tibi praecursor.

(AMBR.) Multa divina beneficia hic sunt congesta. Primum, deprecationis fructus, deinde sterilis partus. 245CTum laetitia plurimorum, et magnitudo virtutis, Altissimi etiam prophetia promittitur. Quinetiam ne qua esset dubitatio, futuri nomen designatur. Tantis igitur supra votum fluentibus, non immerito diffidentia poena plectitur silentii.

VERS. 14.-- Et erit gaudium, etc. (BEDA.) Singularis meriti est indicium quoties hominibus a Deo vel imponitur nomen, vel mutatur, etc., usque ad quia hic salutem multis praedicat, ille vero omnibus dat. (AMBR.) Gaudium et exsultatio. Monentur parentes non minus gratias agere, etc., usque ad matres quia honorantur praemiis conjugii.

VERS. 15.-- Vinum et siceram, etc. (BEDA.) Magnae coram Deo virtutis est praedicare in deserto gaudia coelestia, etc., usque ad et jam erat spiritus gratiae.

245DVERS. 16.-- Et multos filiorum Israel convertet. (ID.) Cum Joannes Christo testimonium perhibens, in fide Christi baptizans, dicatur filios Israel ad Deum convertisse, patet Christum esse Dominum Deum Israel.

VERS. 17.-- Praecedet ante. (ID.) Sicut Elias praeco judicis, etc., usque ad ille cum eo manifestatur in gloria. In spiritu et virtute. (AMBR.) Bene junguntur spiritu et virtute. Non enim sine virtute spiritus, nec sine spiritu virtus est. Elias virtutem habuit abstinentiae, et patientiae, et convertendi animos populorum a perfidia ad fidem, spiritum autem prophetandi habuit.

Convertat, etc., (BEDA.) Corda patrum in filios convertit, 246Aetc., usque ad fere eadem verba quae angelus de Joanne, Malach, praedixit de Elia.

VERS. 18.-- Unde, etc. (ID.) Si homo esset qui promittebat, impune liceret quaerere signum, etc., usque ad et infidelitatis esset poena quam meruit.

VERS. 19.-- Ante Deum. (ID.) Cum ad nos veniunt angeli, sic exterius implent ministerium, ut tamen ante Deum interius per contemplationem assistant. Quia etsi angelus est spiritus circonscriptus, summus spiritus qui Deus est, incirconscriptus est, intra quem currit angelus quocunque moveatur vel mittatur.

VERS. 22.-- Et ipse erat innuens illis, et permansit mutus, etc. Sine voce corporales actus indicare moliens, nec exprimens voluntatem. Cui similis populus Judaeorum, 246Bactuum suorum impotens reddere rationem.

VERS. 23.-- Ut impleti sunt, etc. Quia vicis suae tempore, pontifices templi tantum officiis mancipati, non solum a complexu uxorum, sed etiam a domorum ingressu abstinebant. Nostris autem sacerdotibus quibus non carnalis successio, sed spiritualis perfectio quaeritur, qui quotidie praesto debent esse altari, perpetua castitas indicitur.

VERS. 24.-- Occultabat. (AMBR.) Occultat quia partus sui erubescit aetatem, etc., usque ad neque enim ea quae senilem non erubesceret coitum, erubesceret partum.

VERS. 25.-- Quia sic fecit. (BEDA.) Gesta sunt haec, inquit Joannes Chrysostomus, etc., usque ad quia per ipsum afflictio poenitentiae erat praedicanda.

VERS. 26.-- In mense autem sexto. (HIER.) Sicut sexto 246Cmense missus est Gabriel angelus a Deo in civitatem Galilaeae, ita sexto millenario missus est Christus,

Gabriel. (BEDA.) Ideo angeli ex nomine aliquando signantur, ut ex ipso nomine quid ministraturi veniant, demonstretur. Gabriel fortitudo Dei, quia illum nuntiat qui ad debellandum diabolum veniebat.

VERS. 27.-- Ad virginem. Mulier a diabolo seducta mortem intulit: contra mulier ab angelo edocta salutem edidit.

VERS. 28.-- Dominus tecum. Quam novo castitatis amore ad coelestia sustulit, et post mediante humana natura, omni plenitudine divinitatis consecravit. Benedicta tu in mulieribus. Quae, sine exemplo muliebris conditionis, et virgo et mater est, et Deum genuit.

VERS. 29.-- Turbata est, etc. Quia nesciebat si 246Dpraeteritum an futurum esset, quod angelus dixit.

VERS. 30.-- Et ait angelus ei: Ne timeas, Maria. Turbatam insolita salutatione quasi familiarius notam vocat ex nomine. Ne timeat jubet, et quare gratia plenam vocaverit, plenius explicat.

VERS. 31.-- Jesum. Jesus salvator, sive salutaris dicitur. Ipse enim salvum faciet, non populum Israel, sed suum populum, sive ex praeputio, sive ex circumcisione, in unum ovile sub uno pastore congregatum.

VERS. 32.-- Sedem David patris ejus. (BEDA.) Id est Israeliticam plebem, etc., usque ad in his qui de oleastro incisi in olivam bonam sunt inserti.

VERS. 35.-- Sanctum vocabitur Filius Dei. Jesus nascitur sanctus, qui conditionem naturae corruptibilis 247Avinceret, et commistione copulae carnalis conceptus non est. Nos conditione corruptibilis naturae constricti, per gratiam possumus sanctificari. Et congruit, ut quae contra morem virgo concepit, supra morem humanae consuetudinis Dei Filium generet.

VERS. 36.-- Et ecce, etc. (BEDA.) Ne virgo desperet de partu, etc., usque ad praecursorem quoque de anu sterili nasciturum cognosceret.

Cognata tua. Quia de tribu Levi, id est de tribu Dei.

(BEDA.) Legimus, quod Aaron qui de tribu Levi erat, etc., usque ad quae secundum Deum spiritualis cognationis consortio non carebant.

VERS. 38.-- Ecce ancilla Domini. Non de singularitate meriti se extollit, sed suae conditionis et divinae cognationis per omnia memor, se ancillam illius 247Besse fatetur, cujus mater eligitur, et cum magna devotione promissionem angeli optat impleri.

VERS. 39.-- Exsurgens autem Maria in diebus illis, abiit in montana cum festinatione in civitatem Juda. (AMBR.) Non incredula de oraculo, etc., usque ad dum subditur: Et factum est ut audivit.

VERS. 40.-- Et intravit in domum Zachariae. Hinc discant sanctae mulieres, quam sedulitatem praegnantibus debeant exhibere cognatis.

Et salutavit Elizabeth. (BEDA.) Prior salutat, quia decet ut quanto castior, tanto humilior sit.

VERS. 41.-- Exsultavit, etc. Quia lingua non poterat, animo exsultante salutat, et suae praecursionis officium inchoat. Ecce apparet quod angelus dixerat: Spiritu sancto replebitur adhuc ex utero matris suae.

247CVERS. 42.-- Benedicta tu. (BEDA, AMBR.) Conveniunt verba Evangelii cum verbis psalmi in quo promittitur David: De fructu ventris tui ponam super sedem tuam (Psal. CXXXI). Et eadem voce Maria benedicitur ab Elizabeth qua a Gabriele, ut et angelis et hominibus veneranda, et nostris feminis praeferenda monstretur.

VERS. 43.-- Mater Domini. Sentio miraculum, mysterium cognosco, mater Domini verbo feta, Deo plena.

VERS. 44.-- Ut facta. Ipso momento quo vox intrat ad aures corporis, virtus spiritualis cor intrat audientis, et non solum matrem, sed etiam sobolem amore advenientis accendit.

Exsultavit. (BEDA.) Revelante Spiritu quo impleta erat, etc., usque ad credidit virgo, ubi sacerdos negavit.

247DVERS. 46.-- Magnificat anima mea Dominum. (ID.) Primo dona sibi spiritualiter concessa profitetur, etc., usque ad in ejus praeceptis observandis impendo.

VERS. 47.-- Et exsultavit. (ID.) (ID.) Quia terrena non curo, prosperitas non emollit me, adversitas non frangit, sed sola illius memoria delectat, a quo salus speratur aeterna, illius divinitate laetor cujus temporali conceptione mea caro fetatur.

VERS. 48.-- Quia respexit. (ID.) Ecce quam vilia de se sentit, etc., usque ad et de parvis et infirmis magnos facit et fortes.

Quia fecit mihi magna. Quia in me carnem sumpsit, et me a peccatis mundavit, et dona Spiritus sancti mihi contulit.

248AEt misericordia. (BEDA.) A specialibus donis ad generalia Dei judicia se convertit, etc., usque ad subdit quid contemptores mereantur.

VERS. 53.-- Esurientes implevit bonis. (ID.) Qui aeterna toto studio desiderant; saturabuntur, cum Christus apparuerit in gloria. Sed qui terrenis gaudent, in ultimo inanes totius beatitudinis dimittuntur. In praesenti etiam videmus humiles divina gratia impleri, superbos lumine veritatis privari.

VERS. 55.-- Sicut locutus est ad patres nostros. Memoriam patrum quibus haec salus revelata est faciens, Abraham nominatim exprimit, cui primo ipsa incarnatio manifestata est. Et semini ejus in saecula. Non tam carne genitis, quam fidei vestigia secutis, quibus adventus Salvatoris in saecula 248Best promissus, quia ipsa promissio haereditatis nullo fine claudetur. Nam et usque in finem saeculi credentes non deerunt, et beatitudinis erit gloria perennis.

VERS. 56.-- Mansit autem Maria. (BEDA.) Tandiu mansit, donec videret praecursoris nativitatem, propter quam maxime venerat. Nec causa sola familiaritatis tandiu mansit, sed ut tanto tempore et Elizabeth et Joannis cresceret profectus. Nam si in primo adventu Mariae, Spiritu sancto sunt repleti, multum est illis superadditum tanta mora mansionis.

VERS. 59.-- Die octavo. Per circumcisionem resurrectio Domini figuratur, quia et octava die, id est post sabbatum, facta est. Et sicut illa a 248Creatu mortis absolvere solebat, sic ista immortalis vitae nativitatem, et in auctore nostro exhibuit, et in nobis monstravit sperandam.

Et vocabant eum. (BEDA.) Joannis celebrata nativitas, est Novi Testamenti inchoata gratia, etc., usque ad gratiam Evangelii si recte intelligatur exprimit.

VERS. 62.-- Innuebant autem patri ejus, etc. Qui innuunt patri de nomine pueri, significant illos qui testimonio legis intendunt astruere gratiam fidei.

VERS. 67.-- Et prophetavit dicens. (BEDA.) Magna largitas divinae pietatis. Ecce loquela quae sola ablata est diffidenti, cum spiritu prophetiae restituta est credenti.

VERS. 68.-- Benedictus. Qui dum bonitatem Dei 248Drogavit, pro liberatione praecursor donatus est.

Visitavit. Quod proxime faciendum cognoverat, prophetico more quasi jam factum narrat.

VERS. 69.-- Cornu salutis nobis. (BEDA.) Per cornu quod carnem excedit, cum omnia ossa carne tegantur, regnum Christi significatur, quo mundus et gaudia carnis superantur. In cujus figuram David et Salomon cornu olei, sunt in regni gloriam consecrati, ut prius Saul lenticula.

VERS. 71.-- Salutem ex inimicis nostris, et de manu, etc. Erexit nobis salutem ex inimicis, et est explanatio superioris versiculi, in quo breviter idem praemiserat: Et erexit cornu salutis nobis.

VERS. 72.-- Ad faciendam misericordiam cum 249Apatribus nostris, et memorari testamenti, etc. Locutus est, et erexit nobis salutem ad faciendam misericordiam, ut misericordiam quam patribus promisit impleat in nobis, et juramentum quod fecit patribus de liberatione nostra, in nobis per Christum compleatur.

VERS. 76.-- Et tu, puer, propheta Altissimi vocaberis: praeibis enim ante faciem Domini parare, etc. (BEDA.) Non mirum si alloquitur infantem octo dierum, etc., usque ad quae agnoscebat se in filios suscepisse.

VERS. 79, 80.-- Ad dirigendos pedes nostros in viam pacis. Puer autem crescebat, etc. Ut per omnia gressus nostrorum operum illuminatoris nostri gratiae concordent, donec ad mansionem perpetuae pacis intremus.

CHAPTER ONE.

VERS. 5.-- There was in the days of Herod, the king of Judaea. (AMBR.) The calf is the priestly victim. By the calf, therefore, this Gospel is figured, in which the beginning is made from the priests and the end is made in the calf, that is, in Christ, who is offered up for the life of the world.

A certain priest. He has filled out the order: he has named the king, and the region in which he was; he points out the priest and his nation and his wife. Zacharias. Zacharias, mindful of the Lord: to whom an Angel appears at the right of the altar, because to him who is mindful of the Lord the mystery is shown.

Of the course of Abia. (BEDA.) Abia, the Lord is Father, etc., 244Das far as just as by the seven the Old Testament is declared.

Elizabeth. Elizabeth, the fullness of my God, signifies Mary, who was full of God.

VERS. 6.-- And they were both. (BEDA, AMBR.) A full praise, which comprehends birth in his forebears, character in fairness, office in the priesthood, deed in the commandments, judgment in the justifications. Just before God. (AMBR.) For not everyone who is just before men is just before God; for men see in one way, God in another: men in the face, God in the heart. And therefore it can come about that someone, by an affected popular goodness, should seem just to me, and yet not be just before God, if his justice is not formed out of simplicity of mind but is feigned by flattery.

245AVERS. 7.-- And there was not unto them. (BEDA.) It was divinely provided that John should be born of parents advanced in years and long deprived of the fruit of wedlock, so that by the unlooked-for birth of the child the gift of God might touch them the more gratefully, and the astonishment of the miracle might prepare the rest for the hearing of the prophecy to come. Whence afterwards it is added: All they that had heard laid it up in their heart, saying: What manner of child, thinkest thou, shall this be?

VERS. 8.-- And it came to pass. Whereas by the precept of Moses, when one priest died, one is bidden to succeed him, in David's time it was appointed that, ministering by turns for eight days at a time from sabbath to sabbath in the time of their course, they should each give themselves to chastity, and should not meanwhile touch their house.

VERS. 9.-- By lot. (BEDA.) He was not then chosen by a new lot, when the incense was to be burned, etc., as far 245Bas he is foretold while that other is promised as one who will go before.

VERS. 12.-- And Zacharias was troubled at the sight, and fear fell upon him, etc. (AMBR.) We are wont to be troubled and estranged from our own feeling when we are shaken by the encounter of a higher power. But as it belongs to human weakness to be terrified, so it belongs to angelic kindness to console with soothing one who is afraid at its appearing; on the contrary, the demons, if they have perceived any terrified by their presence, shake them with a larger horror.

VERS. 13.-- Because thy prayer is heard: and thy wife Elizabeth shall bear thee a son, and thou shalt call his name. It was heard for more than thou didst ask. Thou didst pray for the deliverance of the people, and a forerunner is given thee.

(AMBR.) Many divine benefits are heaped together here. First, the fruit of the prayer; then the childbearing of a barren woman. 245CThen the gladness of very many, and greatness of virtue, and even the prophecy of the Most High is promised. Moreover, that there might be no doubt, the name of the child to come is appointed. Since therefore so many things flow beyond what he had prayed for, not undeservedly is his diffidence punished with the penalty of silence.

VERS. 14.-- And there shall be joy, etc. (BEDA.) It is a token of singular merit as often as a name is either imposed upon men by God or changed, etc., as far as because this one preaches salvation to many, but that one gives it to all. (AMBR.) Joy and exultation. The parents are admonished to give thanks no less, etc., as far as mothers, because they are honoured with the rewards of wedlock.

VERS. 15.-- Wine and strong drink, etc. (BEDA.) It is of great virtue before God to preach heavenly joys in the desert, etc., as far as and already there was the spirit of grace.

245DVERS. 16.-- And he shall convert many of the children of Israel. (ID.) Since John, bearing witness to Christ and baptizing in the faith of Christ, is said to have converted the children of Israel to God, it is plain that Christ is the Lord God of Israel.

VERS. 17.-- He shall go before. (ID.) As Elias is the herald of the judge, etc., as far as he is made manifest in glory with him. In the spirit and power. (AMBR.) Spirit and power are rightly joined. For there is no spirit without power, nor power without spirit. Elias had the power of abstinence, and of patience, and of turning the minds of peoples from faithlessness to faith; and the spirit of prophesying he had.

That he may turn, etc., (BEDA.) He turns the hearts of the fathers unto the children, 246Aetc., as far as almost the same words which the angel foretold of John, Malachi foretold of Elias.

VERS. 18.-- Whereby, etc. (ID.) If it were a man who was promising, it would be allowable to seek a sign unpunished, etc., as far as and it would be the penalty of unbelief which he deserved.

VERS. 19.-- Before God. (ID.) When angels come to us, they so fulfil their ministry outwardly that nevertheless they stand within, before God, by contemplation. For although an angel is a circumscribed spirit, the highest Spirit, who is God, is uncircumscribed, and within him the angel runs wherever he is moved or sent.

VERS. 22.-- And he was making signs to them, and remained dumb, etc. Labouring to indicate bodily acts without a voice, and not expressing his will. Like to him is the people of the Jews, 246Bunable to render an account of their own acts.

VERS. 23.-- When they were fulfilled, etc. Because in the time of their course the priests of the temple, given over to its offices alone, abstained not only from the embrace of their wives but even from entering their houses. But to our priests, from whom not a carnal succession but a spiritual perfection is sought, who ought to be daily at hand at the altar, perpetual chastity is enjoined.

VERS. 24.-- She hid herself. (AMBR.) She hides herself because she blushes at the age of her childbearing, etc., as far as for neither would she who would not blush at intercourse in old age blush at the childbearing.

VERS. 25.-- Because thus hath he done. (BEDA.) These things were done, says John Chrysostom, etc., as far as because through him the affliction of penance was to be preached.

VERS. 26.-- And in the sixth month. (HIER.) As in the sixth 246Cmonth the angel Gabriel was sent by God into a city of Galilee, so in the sixth millennium Christ was sent,

Gabriel. (BEDA.) For this reason angels are sometimes marked out by a name, so that from the name itself it may be shown what they come to minister. Gabriel is the strength of God, because he announces him who was coming to war down the devil.

VERS. 27.-- To a virgin. A woman seduced by the devil brought in death: on the contrary, a woman taught by an angel brought forth salvation.

VERS. 28.-- The Lord is with thee. Whom by a new love of chastity he raised up to heavenly things, and afterwards, human nature mediating, consecrated with all the fullness of the Godhead. Blessed art thou among women. Who, without example in the condition of women, is both virgin and mother, and brought forth God.

VERS. 29.-- She was troubled, etc. Because she did not know whether 246Dwhat the angel said was past or to come.

VERS. 30.-- And the angel said to her: Fear not, Mary. Her, troubled by the unwonted salutation, he calls by name as one more familiarly known. He bids her not to fear, and more fully explains why he had called her full of grace.

VERS. 31.-- Jesus. Jesus is called saviour, or salutary. For he himself shall save, not the people of Israel, but his own people, whether from the uncircumcision or from the circumcision, gathered into one fold under one shepherd.

VERS. 32.-- The throne of David his father. (BEDA.) That is, the Israelite people, etc., as far as in those who, cut from the wild olive, have been grafted into the good olive.

VERS. 35.-- The Holy One shall be called the Son of God. Jesus is born holy, who was to overcome the condition of corruptible nature, 247Aand he was not conceived by the mingling of carnal union. We, bound fast by the condition of corruptible nature, can be sanctified through grace. And it is fitting that she who conceived as a virgin against custom should bring forth the Son of God above the custom of human usage.

VERS. 36.-- And behold, etc. (BEDA.) Lest the virgin should despair of the childbearing, etc., as far as she might know that the forerunner too was to be born of a barren old woman.

Thy cousin. Because she was of the tribe of Levi, that is, of the tribe of God.

(BEDA.) We read that Aaron, who was of the tribe of Levi, etc., as far as which according to God did not lack the fellowship of a spiritual kinship.

VERS. 38.-- Behold the handmaid of the Lord. She does not exalt herself for the singularity of her merit, but, mindful in all things of her own condition and of the divine kinship, she confesses herself to be the handmaid 247Bof him whose mother she is chosen to be, and with great devotion desires the angel's promise to be fulfilled.

VERS. 39.-- And Mary rising up in those days, went into the hill country with haste into a city of Juda. (AMBR.) Not incredulous of the oracle, etc., as far as when it is added: And it came to pass, that when she heard.

VERS. 40.-- And she entered into the house of Zachary. From this let holy women learn what attentiveness they ought to show to kinswomen with child.

And saluted Elizabeth. (BEDA.) She salutes first, because it is fitting that the chaster she is, the humbler she should be.

VERS. 41.-- He exulted, etc. Because he could not with the tongue, he salutes with an exulting spirit, and begins the office of his forerunning. Behold, there appears what the angel had said: He shall be filled with the Holy Ghost even from his mother's womb.

247CVERS. 42.-- Blessed art thou. (BEDA, AMBR.) The words of the Gospel agree with the words of the psalm in which it is promised to David: Of the fruit of thy womb I will set upon thy throne (Psal. CXXXI). And Mary is blessed by Elizabeth with the same voice as by Gabriel, so that she may be shown venerable both to angels and to men, and to be preferred before our women.

VERS. 43.-- The mother of the Lord. I perceive the miracle, I acknowledge the mystery: the mother of the Lord, teeming with the Word, full of God.

VERS. 44.-- As was made. In the very moment in which the voice enters the ears of the body, a spiritual power enters the heart of the hearer, and kindles not only the mother but also the offspring with love of her who comes.

He exulted. (BEDA.) By the revealing of the Spirit with whom she was filled, etc., as far as the virgin believed where the priest denied.

247DVERS. 46.-- My soul doth magnify the Lord. (ID.) First she professes the gifts spiritually granted to her, etc., as far as I spend in the keeping of his commandments.

VERS. 47.-- And hath exulted. (ID.) (ID.) Because I do not care for earthly things, prosperity does not soften me, adversity does not break me, but the memory of him alone delights me, from whom eternal salvation is hoped for; I rejoice in the Godhead of him by whose conception in time my flesh is made fruitful.

VERS. 48.-- Because he hath regarded. (ID.) Behold how lowly are the things she feels concerning herself, etc., as far as and out of the small and the weak he makes them great and strong.

Because he hath done great things to me. Because in me he took flesh, and cleansed me from sins, and conferred upon me the gifts of the Holy Ghost.

248AAnd mercy. (BEDA.) From the special gifts she turns herself to the general judgments of God, etc., as far as he adds what the despisers deserve.

VERS. 53.-- He hath filled the hungry with good things. (ID.) They who desire eternal things with all zeal; shall be filled, when Christ shall have appeared in glory. But they who rejoice in earthly things are sent away at the last empty of all blessedness. In the present life also we see the humble filled with divine grace, and the proud deprived of the light of truth.

VERS. 55.-- As he spoke to our fathers. Making mention of the fathers to whom this salvation was revealed, she names Abraham expressly, to whom the incarnation itself was first made manifest. And to his seed for ever. Not so much to those born of the flesh as to those who have followed the footsteps of faith, to whom the coming of the Saviour was promised for ever, 248Bbecause the promise of the inheritance itself shall be closed by no end. For both unto the end of the world believers shall not be lacking, and the glory of blessedness shall be everlasting.

VERS. 56.-- And Mary abode. (BEDA.) She abode so long, until she should see the nativity of the forerunner, for the sake of which above all she had come. Nor did she abide so long for the sake of familiarity alone, but that in so long a time the advancement both of Elizabeth and of John might grow. For if at Mary's first coming they were filled with the Holy Ghost, much was added to them over and above by so long a delay of her stay.

VERS. 59.-- On the eighth day. By circumcision the resurrection of the Lord is figured, because it too was made on the eighth day, that is, after the sabbath. And as the one was wont to absolve from 248Cthe guilt of death, so the other both exhibited in our author the birth of immortal life, and showed that it is to be hoped for in us.

And they called him. (BEDA.) The celebrated nativity of John is the grace of the New Testament begun, etc., as far as it expresses the grace of the Gospel, if it be rightly understood.

VERS. 62.-- And they made signs to his father, etc. They who make signs to the father about the child's name signify those who by the testimony of the law aim to build up the grace of faith.

VERS. 67.-- And he prophesied, saying. (BEDA.) Great is the bounty of the divine mercy. Behold, the speech which alone was taken away from him while he doubted is restored, together with the spirit of prophecy, to him now believing.

VERS. 68.-- Blessed. Who, while he besought the goodness of God, 248Dwas given a forerunner in place of deliverance.

He hath visited. What he had known was shortly to be done, he relates after the manner of the prophets as though it were already done.

VERS. 69.-- A horn of salvation to us. (BEDA.) By the horn, which rises above the flesh, since all the bones are covered with flesh, the kingdom of Christ is signified, by which the world and the joys of the flesh are overcome. In figure of which David and Solomon were consecrated to the glory of the kingdom with a horn of oil, as Saul before them with a vial.

VERS. 71.-- Salvation from our enemies, and from the hand, etc. He hath raised up for us salvation from our enemies; and it is an explanation of the earlier verse, in which he had briefly set the same thing before: And he hath raised up a horn of salvation to us.

VERS. 72.-- To perform mercy to 249Aour fathers, and to remember his testament, etc. He hath spoken, and hath raised up salvation for us, to perform mercy: that the mercy which he promised to the fathers he may fulfil in us, and that the oath which he made to the fathers concerning our deliverance may be accomplished in us through Christ.

VERS. 76.-- And thou, child, shalt be called the prophet of the Highest: for thou shalt go before the face of the Lord to prepare, etc. (BEDA.) It is no wonder if he addresses an infant of eight days, etc., as far as which he acknowledged that he had received among his sons.

VERS. 79, 80.-- To direct our feet into the way of peace. And the child grew, etc. That in all things the steps of our works may accord with the grace of him who enlightens us, until we enter into the dwelling of perpetual peace.

CAPUT II.

  • 249B

VERS. 1.-- Exiit edictum a Caesare. (AUG., BED.) Augustus duodecim annis circa nativitatem Christi in pace regnavit, etc., usque ad hic primum Judaea facta est stipendiaria Romanis.

VERS. 2.-- Prima. (BEDA.) Jam diversis temporibus, pleraeque partes terrarum leguntur descriptae, sed haec est prima earum quae totum orbem concluserunt, quae professio est mentium, quia nullus excluditur. Vel certe tunc primum coepit, quando Cyrinus a Caesare in Syriam missus est, censor patrimoniorum futurus.

VERS. 4.-- Nazareth. (ID.) Quotidie Dominus in Nazareth concipitur, etc., usque ad quod instantia loquentis magistri nihil valet nisi augmentum superni 249Cjuvaminis ut audiatur acceperit.

VERS. 6.-- Factum est autem. Alibi concipi, alibi nasci Dominus voluit, ut insidiantis Herodis furorem facilius evaderet. Humilitas Christi commendatur, quia non solum incarnari, sed etiam illo tempore nasci voluit, quo mox natus censui Caesaris propter nostram liberationem subderetur.

VERS. 7.-- Pannis eum involvit. Vilibus induitur pannis, ut stolam immortalitatis reciperemus. Manus et pedes stringuntur; ut manus nostrae ad bene operandum, pedes in viam pacis dirigantur; parvulus factus, ut nos perfecti simus.

VERS. 8.-- Et pastores. (BEDA.) Nato summo pastore, pastores ab insidiis noctis, etc., usque ad super quos divina gratia largius coruscat.

249DVERS. 9.-- Et ecce angelus Domini. (ID.) Et concipiendum, et conceptum, et natum Dominum, coeli cives testantur, ut et mortales sufficienter imbuant, et suum auctori servitium impendant.

VERS. 12.-- Infantem pannis involutum. (ID.) Crebris infantia Salvatoris et angelorum testimoniis, et evangelistarum est inculcata, ut mentibus nostris altius infigatur quid pro nobis sit factus: In quo humilitas commendatur: Quia cum esset dives, pauper pro nobis factus est, ut inopia sua nos ditaret (II Cor).

VERS. 14.-- Et in terra pax. Quando peccando eramus a Deo extranei, extraneos nos angeli deputabant; sed quia cognovimus regem nostrum, recognoverunt 250Anos angeli cives suos. Et timet angelus adorari ab humana natura quam in suo rege considerat.

Hominibus bonae voluntatis. (BEDA.) Qui suscipiunt natum Christum, non his qui audita ejus nativitate sunt turbati et eum persecuti. Non est pax impiis, dicit Dominus (Isa. XLVIII).

VERS. 16.-- Et venerunt festinantes. (BEDA.) Non cum desidia Christi quaerenda est praesentia, etc., usque ad quasi in praesepio invenient.

VERS. 17.-- Videntes autem. Visio vel cognitio Dei est, et haec est sola vita hominum ut cognoscant verum Patrem, et quem misit Jesum Christum esse unum Deum.

VERS. 18.-- Mirati sunt. Mirantur et de mysterio incarnationis et de tanta pastorum attestatione, qui 250Bfingere audita nescirent, sed simplici facundia vera praedicarent. Sed Dominus non rethores, sed piscatores ad evangelizandum destinavit. Sic et in Vetere Testamento suae dispensationis nuntios pastores ordinavit. Ideo non est parvipendenda pastorum attestatio.

VERS. 20.-- Et reversi, etc. (BEDA.) Sic spirituales pastores modo dormientibus aliis contemplando coelestia subvehuntur, etc., usque ad dum dicunt: Gloria in altissimis Deo, et in terra pax hominibus bonae voluntatis.

VERS. 22.-- Tulerunt illum. Parvuli qui de toto regno Judaeorum ad templum portabantur, si erant de tribu Levi, in ministerium domus perpetuo detinebantur. Si de alia tribu, pretio dato a parentibus primogeniti redimebantur ad propria referendi.

250CVERS. 23.-- Sicut scriptum. (BEDA.) Scriptum est in lege: Mulier quae suscepto, etc., usque ad cum bonorum operum fructibus supernae beatitudinis gaudia subit.

VERS. 24.-- Par turturum. (ID.) Haec oblatio pauperum erat, qui non sufficerent offerre agnum, ut per omnia humilitas Domini pateat.

VERS. 25.-- Et ecce homo. Non solum angeli, sed omnis aetas et sexus testimonium nato reddunt puero. Et sicut ab omnium saeculorum fidelibus praesagiebatur, ita veniens omnium sanctorum laude praedicatur.

VERS. 27.-- Puerum. Cum pueritia post septem annos infantiae incipiat, Jesus frequenter puer dicitur, 250Dnon tam aetate quam pro servitio, unde Propheta: Ecce puer meus, quia Filius hominis non venit ministrari, sed ministrare (Isa. XLII).

VERS. 29.-- Nunc dimittis servum tuum. (AMBR.) Qui vult dimitti, veniat in Hierusalem, etc., usque ad tunc dimittetur ut non videat mortem, quia viderat vitam.

VERS. 34.-- Ecce positus est hic. In ruinam illorum qui steterant, et resurrectionem illorum qui ceciderant; vel in ruinam vitiorum, et in resurrectionem virtutum. Non in se tantum, sed in suis quoque praedicatoribus positus est in ruinam et resurrectionem, unde Apostolus: Christi bonus odor sumus Deo, in his qui salvi fiunt, et in his qui pereunt. Bono enim odore, alii salvantur, alii pereunt (II Cor. II).

251AVERS. 35.-- Gladius. (BEDA.) Dolor Dominicae passionis, etc., usque ad zizania germinare conspicit.

Ut revelentur. Ante erat incertum qui Judaeorum Christi gratiam reciperent, qui conspuerent: sed audita nativitate revelantur cogitationes, et Herodes et alii turbantur, quidam ad Christi magisterium accedunt.

VERS. 36.-- Et erat Anna, etc. Quia per virum et mulierem vita totius mundi perdita est, in testimonio vitae per Christum redeuntis Anna conjungitur Simeoni. Convenit mysteriis Ecclesiae, quod Anna gratia interpretatur, quae est filia Phanuel, qui facies Dei dicitur, unde supra: Signatum est super nos lumen vultus tui, Domine (Psal. IV). De tribu Aser. Id est beati, descendit, qui inter duodecim 251Bpatriarchas ordine nascendi octavus est, propter mysterium resurrectionis. Quinque sunt Annae. Prima mater Samuel, secunda uxor Raguel, tertia mater Tobiae, quarta filia Phanuel, quinta mater Mariae.

Processerat in diebus. (BEDA.) Juxta historiam, Anna, et devotae conversationis, etc., usque ad multiplicantur propter apostolicam doctrinam.

VERS. 38.-- Omnibus qui, etc. Omnibus fidelibus qui jugo Herodis alienigenae gravati, liberationem civitatis et populi exspectabant, promittebat per adventum Christi in proximo redemptionem a tyrannide alieni.

VERS. 40.-- Puer autem. (BEDA.) In eo quod puer erat, etc., usque ad homo fieri coepisset, perfectus 251Cesset ut Deus.

VERS. 41.-- Et ibant parentes, etc. (ID.) Merito Lucas inter quatuor animalia vitulo comparatur, etc., usque ad discipulos Domini in templo laudantes in fine Evangelii sui concludit.

VERS. 45.-- Et non invenientes. (ID.) Si quaeritur quomodo potuit a parentibus obliviscendo relinqui, etc., usque ad cum altero parente reversum.

VERS. 46.-- In medio. (ID.) Quasi fons, medius doctorum sedet, sed quasi exemplar humilitatis, prius interrogat et audit quam instruat, ne parvuli a senioribus doceri erubescant, ne infirmus docere audeat.

VERS. 49.-- Quid est quod me quaerebatis? (ID.) Non quod eum quasi filium quaerunt vituperat, sed 251Dquis sit sibi verus Pater insinuat, et quid potius debeat ei cui est aeternus Filius, mentis oculos attollere cogit. Quia enim Deus et homo est, nunc excelsa Deitatis, nunc infirma praefert humanitatis. Quasi Filius Dei in templo commoratur, quasi Filius hominis cum parentibus quo jubent regreditur.

VERS. 51.-- Et mater ejus conservabat omnia verba haec in corde suo. Omnia quae de Domino, vel a Domino facta cognovit, sive quae intellexit, sive quae nondum intelligere potuit, omnia in memoria recondebat, ut, quando tempus praedicandae seu scribendae incarnationis ejus adveniret, sufficienter universa prout essent gesta posset explicare quaerentibus. Sic nos crebra factorum et dictorum Domini meditatione 252Aimportunos cogitatus repellamus, et alios instruere laboremus.

VERS. 52.-- Proficiebat sapientia. (BEDA.) Sicut est carnis aetate proficere, etc., usque ad utraque pariter salvaretur.

CHAPTER II.

  • 249B

VERS. 1.-- There went out a decree from Caesar. (AUG., BED.) Augustus reigned in peace for twelve years about the nativity of Christ, etc., as far as here first Judaea was made tributary to the Romans.

VERS. 2.-- The first. (BEDA.) Already at various times very many parts of the world are read to have been enrolled, but this is the first of those which took in the whole world; and it is a profession of minds, because no one is shut out. Or certainly it then first began, when Cyrinus was sent by Caesar into Syria, to be an assessor of patrimonies.

VERS. 4.-- Nazareth. (ID.) Daily the Lord is conceived in Nazareth, etc., as far as the insistence of the teacher who speaks avails nothing unless it has received the increase of help from above 249Cthat it may be heard.

VERS. 6.-- And it came to pass. The Lord willed to be conceived in one place and born in another, that he might the more easily escape the fury of the plotting Herod. The humility of Christ is commended, because he willed not only to be made flesh, but even to be born at that time in which, as soon as he was born, he should be made subject to Caesar's census for the sake of our deliverance.

VERS. 7.-- She wrapped him up in swaddling clothes. He is clothed in mean cloths, that we might receive the robe of immortality. His hands and feet are bound fast, that our hands may be directed to working well, and our feet into the way of peace; he was made a little one, that we might be perfect.

VERS. 8.-- And there were shepherds. (BEDA.) The highest shepherd being born, the shepherds from the snares of the night, etc., as far as upon whom the divine grace flashes more abundantly.

249DVERS. 9.-- And behold an angel of the Lord. (ID.) The citizens of heaven bear witness to the Lord both as to be conceived, and as conceived, and as born, that they may both sufficiently instruct mortals and pay their own service to their author.

VERS. 12.-- An infant wrapped in swaddling clothes. (ID.) The infancy of the Saviour has been pressed home by frequent testimonies both of the angels and of the evangelists, that it may be fixed the more deeply in our minds what he was made for our sake: in which his humility is commended: Because, whereas he was rich, he became poor for us, that by his own poverty he might enrich us (II Cor). [Vulg. 2 Cor 8:9 reads propter vos egenus factus est, "for your sakes he became needy," and ut illius inopia vos divites essetis, "that by his poverty you might be rich"; Migne's clause has pauper for egenus and puts in the first person what the Vulgate puts in the second.]

VERS. 14.-- And on earth peace. When by sinning we were strangers to God, the angels reckoned us strangers; but because we have known our king, the angels 250Ahave recognized us as their own fellow citizens. And the angel fears to be adored by a human nature which he beholds in his own king.

To men of good will. (BEDA.) Who receive Christ when born; not to those who at the hearing of his nativity were troubled and persecuted him. There is no peace to the wicked, saith the Lord (Isa. XLVIII).

VERS. 16.-- And they came with haste. (BEDA.) The presence of Christ is not to be sought with sloth, etc., as far as they will find him as it were in a manger.

VERS. 17.-- And seeing. It is the vision or knowledge of God, and this alone is the life of men, that they should know the true Father, and that Jesus Christ, whom he sent, is one God.

VERS. 18.-- They wondered. They wonder both at the mystery of the incarnation and at so great a testimony of shepherds, who 250Bdid not know how to feign what they had heard, but with a simple eloquence proclaimed true things. But the Lord appointed not orators but fishermen to preach the Gospel. So too in the Old Testament he ordained shepherds as the messengers of his dispensation. Therefore the testimony of the shepherds is not to be lightly esteemed.

VERS. 20.-- And returning, etc. (BEDA.) Thus spiritual shepherds, while others sleep, are borne up by contemplating heavenly things, etc., as far as when they say: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace to men of good will.

VERS. 22.-- They carried him. The little ones who from the whole kingdom of the Jews were carried to the temple, if they were of the tribe of Levi, were kept back perpetually for the ministry of the house. If of another tribe, the firstborn were redeemed by a price given by their parents, to be carried back to their own homes.

250CVERS. 23.-- As it is written. (BEDA.) It is written in the law: A woman who, having received, etc., as far as she enters upon the joys of heavenly blessedness with the fruits of good works.

VERS. 24.-- A pair of turtledoves. (ID.) This was the offering of the poor, who were not able to offer a lamb, so that in all things the humility of the Lord might be plain.

VERS. 25.-- And behold there was a man. Not only the angels, but every age and sex render testimony to the child that is born. And as he was foretold by the faithful of all ages, so at his coming he is proclaimed with the praise of all the saints.

VERS. 27.-- The child. Since boyhood begins after the seven years of infancy, Jesus is frequently called a child, 250Dnot so much for his age as for his service; whence the Prophet: Behold my child, [Vulg. Isai 42:1 reads Ecce servus meus, "Behold my servant"; the gloss's whole argument turns on puer.] because the Son of man came not to be ministered unto, but to minister (Isa. XLII).

VERS. 29.-- Now thou dost dismiss thy servant. (AMBR.) He who wishes to be dismissed, let him come into Jerusalem, etc., as far as then he shall be dismissed so that he may not see death, because he had seen life.

VERS. 34.-- Behold this child is set. For the ruin of those who had stood, and the resurrection of those who had fallen; or for the ruin of vices, and for the resurrection of virtues. Not in himself alone, but in his preachers also is he set for ruin and resurrection; whence the Apostle: We are the good odour of Christ unto God, in them that are saved, and in them that perish. For by the good odour some are saved, others perish (II Cor. II).

251AVERS. 35.-- A sword. (BEDA.) The sorrow of the Lord's passion, etc., as far as she beholds the cockle springing up.

That may be revealed. Before, it was uncertain which of the Jews would receive the grace of Christ, and which would spit upon it: but at the hearing of the nativity the thoughts are revealed, and Herod and others are troubled, while certain men come to the teaching of Christ.

VERS. 36.-- And there was Anna, etc. Because through a man and a woman the life of the whole world was lost, in the testimony of life returning through Christ Anna is joined to Simeon. It agrees with the mysteries of the Church that Anna is interpreted grace, who is the daughter of Phanuel, who is called the face of God; whence above: The light of thy countenance, O Lord, is signed upon us (Psal. IV). Of the tribe of Aser. That is, of the blessed, she is descended, from him who among the twelve 251Bpatriarchs is eighth in the order of birth, on account of the mystery of the resurrection. There are five Annas. The first the mother of Samuel, the second the wife of Raguel, the third the mother of Tobias, the fourth the daughter of Phanuel, the fifth the mother of Mary.

She had advanced in days. (BEDA.) According to the history, Anna, and of a devout manner of life, etc., as far as they are multiplied on account of the apostolic teaching.

VERS. 38.-- To all who, etc. To all the faithful who, weighed down by the yoke of the foreigner Herod, were awaiting the deliverance of the city and of the people, she promised through the coming of Christ a redemption close at hand from the tyranny of the stranger.

VERS. 40.-- And the child. (BEDA.) In that he was a child, etc., as far as he had begun to be made man, he was perfect 251Cas God.

VERS. 41.-- And his parents went, etc. (ID.) Deservedly Luke is compared, among the four living creatures, to the calf, etc., as far as at the end of his Gospel he concludes with the disciples of the Lord praising in the temple.

VERS. 45.-- And not finding him. (ID.) If it is asked how he could be left behind by his parents through forgetfulness, etc., as far as returned with the other parent.

VERS. 46.-- In the midst. (ID.) As a fountain, he sits in the midst of the doctors; but as a pattern of humility, he asks and hears before he instructs, lest little ones should blush to be taught by their elders, lest one who is weak should dare to teach.

VERS. 49.-- How is it that you sought me? (ID.) He does not find fault with their seeking him as a son, but 251Dhe suggests who is his true Father, and compels them to lift the eyes of the mind to what is rather owed to him whose eternal Son he is. For because he is God and man, now he sets forth the lofty things of the Godhead, now the weak things of the humanity. As the Son of God he stays in the temple; as the Son of man he goes back with his parents whither they bid.

VERS. 51.-- And his mother kept all these words in her heart. All the things which she knew to have been done concerning the Lord or by the Lord, whether those she understood or those she could not yet understand, she laid up all in her memory, so that, when the time should come for his incarnation to be preached or written, she might be able sufficiently to set forth to those who asked all things just as they had been done. So let us, by frequent meditation on the deeds and words of the Lord, 252Adrive off importunate thoughts and labour to instruct others.

VERS. 52.-- He advanced in wisdom. (BEDA.) As it belongs to the flesh to advance in age, etc., as far as both alike might be saved.

CAPUT III.

VERS. 2.-- Factum est verbum, etc. (BEDA.) Congregaturus Ecclesiam, Dei Filius primum operatur in servulo, etc., usque ad unum itaque dixit, et omnia declaravit.

VERS. 3.-- Praedicans baptismum poenitentiae, etc. (ID.) Joannes baptismum poenitentiae praedicavit, et quibusdam etiam dedit, etc., usque ad praecurretur suo baptismate quo peccata solvi non possunt.

VERS. 4.-- Vox clamantis. Vox, quia praenuntius 252Bverbi. In deserto. Quia desertae et destitutae Judaeae solatium redemptionis annuntiat. Nullum verbum sine voce auditur, nec vox sine verbi intelligentia valet. Parate viam Domini, etc. Qui fidem et bona opera praedicat, quid aliud quam venienti Domino? ad corda audientium viam parat, ut haec vis gratiae illustret, ut rectas Deo semitas faciat, dum mundas cogitationes in animo per sermonem praedicationis format.

VERS. 5.-- Et erunt prava. (BEDA.) Prava, id est, malorum corda per injustitiam distorta ad regulam justitiae dirigentur.

VERS. 6.-- Et videbit. (ID.) In hac vita omnis homo Christum videre non potuit, sed in die judicii, in sede majestatis et electi et reprobi pariter videbunt, ut 252Cjusti remunerentur, et mali in aeternum gemant. Unde et subditur:

VERS. 7.-- Quis ostendit, etc.? (AMBR.) Quis ostendit in prudentia miseratione Dei infusa, etc.? usque ad quam ad gentes transferendam prophetat.

VERS. 8.-- Dico enim vobis. (ID.) Qui amisso sensu rationis, etc., usque ad replentur fructibus, vestiuntur frondibus.

VERS. 11.-- Qui habet duas. (BEDA). Tunica plus sui necessaria quam pallium, etc., usque ad sed quod habet cum paupere partiat.

VERS. 12.-- Publicani. (ID.) Qui vectigalia publica exigunt, etc., usque ad tandem ad propria cum proximis communicanda pertingerent.

VERS. 15.-- Existimante autem. (ID.) Non solum 252Dcogitabant, etc., usque ad per virginem venisse non credunt.

VERS. 16.-- Veniet autem. Ideo baptizo aqua, quia ille venit qui baptizabit spiritu, et oportet, ut illi via praeparetur.

Cujus non sum dignus. (BEDA.) Non sum dignus mysterium incarnationis investigare, etc., usque ad evangelica praedicatio, qua calceati sunt apostoli.

VERS. 19.-- Herodes. Antequam Lucas aliquid narrat de actibus Jesu, dicit Joannem ab Herode captum, ut ostendat se solummodo ea facta descripturum, quae eo anno gesta sunt, quo Joannes vel captus est, vel punitus.

VERS. 21.-- Et Jesu baptizato. (BEDA.) Baptizatur 253ADominus, non mundari indigens, etc., usque ad jam edoctus Dei Filium aperte praedicat.

VERS. 22.-- In te complacui. Non aliena in filio, sed sua laudatur, quasi dicat: Quaecunque habes tu, mea sunt.

VERS. 23.-- Annorum triginta. (BEDA.) Quod tricennalis baptizatur, etc., usque ad ut in Jeremia et Daniele.

VERS. 26.-- Qui fuit Mathathiae, qui fuit Amos, qui fuit Nahum, qui fuit Hesli. (BEDA.) Mathath qui per Salomonem descendit, etc., usque ad qui per carnalem generationem ostenditur factus Filius hominis.

VERS. 29.-- Qui fuit Levi. Non autem hi quator qui hoc loco inferuntur Jacob filii fuerunt, sed quod eorum nominibus participabant.

253B(BEDA.) Quasi nominibus alludens quod in illis figuratum est, etc., usque ad genera virtutum diversa praecederent.

VERS. 31.-- Nathan. (ID.) Quod Matthaeus per Salomonem, etc., usque ad cui judicium a Deo rege defertur: Sacerdos est in aeternum secundum ordinem Melchisedech.

VERS. 37.-- Mathusale. (AMBR.) Anni Mathusale ultra diluvium numerantur, significat Christum, cujus vita aetatem nescit.

Qui fuit Enoch. (BEDA.) A baptizato Dei Filio usque ad Deum Patrem ascendens, septuagesimo gradu Enoch ponit, etc., usque ad quos in resurrectione immutabili Dei sapientiae contemplandae per saecula 253Cmonstrat esse jungendos.

VERS. 38.-- Seth. Seth posterior Adae filius, non siletur, ut cum duae populi sint generationes, significet Christum in posteriore potius quam in priore generatione numerandum.

Qui fuit Dei. A Filio Dei generatio incipit, terminatur in Filium Dei, praecedit creatus in figura, ut sequatur natus in veritate; praeit factus ad imaginem, ut propter eum imago descendat.

CHAPTER III.

VERS. 2.-- The word was made, etc. (BEDA.) About to gather the Church, the Son of God works first in his little servant, etc., as far as he therefore said one thing, and declared all.

VERS. 3.-- Preaching the baptism of penance, etc. (ID.) John preached the baptism of penance, and even gave it to some, etc., as far as there will be a going before by his own baptism, by which sins cannot be loosed.

VERS. 4.-- The voice of one crying. A voice, because he is the herald 252Bof the Word. In the desert. Because he announces the consolation of redemption to a deserted and forsaken Judaea. No word is heard without a voice, nor does a voice avail without the understanding of the word. Prepare ye the way of the Lord, etc. He who preaches faith and good works, what else than for the Lord who comes? He prepares a way to the hearts of his hearers, that this force of grace may give light, that it may make straight paths for God, while by the word of preaching it forms clean thoughts in the mind.

VERS. 5.-- And the crooked shall be. (BEDA.) The crooked, that is, the hearts of the wicked, twisted by injustice, shall be directed to the rule of justice.

VERS. 6.-- And shall see. (ID.) In this life not every man could see Christ, but on the day of judgment, on the seat of majesty, both the elect and the reprobate alike shall see him, that 252Cthe just may be rewarded and the wicked groan for ever. Whence there is also added:

VERS. 7.-- Who hath shewed, etc.? (AMBR.) Who hath shewed, in the prudence poured in by the mercy of God, etc.? as far as which he prophesies is to be transferred to the Gentiles.

VERS. 8.-- For I say to you. (ID.) They who, having lost the sense of reason, etc., as far as they are filled with fruits, they are clothed with leaves.

VERS. 11.-- He that hath two. (BEDA). The tunic is more necessary of itself than the cloak, etc., as far as but let him share what he has with the poor man.

VERS. 12.-- The publicans. (ID.) They who exact the public tolls, etc., as far as they might at length attain to sharing their own goods with their neighbours.

VERS. 15.-- And while they were supposing. (ID.) Not only 252Dwere they thinking, etc., as far as they do not believe that he came through a virgin.

VERS. 16.-- But there shall come. Therefore I baptize with water, because he comes who will baptize with the Spirit, and it is fitting that a way be prepared for him.

Whose I am not worthy. (BEDA.) I am not worthy to search out the mystery of the incarnation, etc., as far as the evangelical preaching, with which the apostles are shod.

VERS. 19.-- Herod. Before Luke relates anything of the acts of Jesus, he says that John was seized by Herod, in order to show that he will describe only those deeds which were done in that year in which John was either seized or punished.

VERS. 21.-- And Jesus being baptized. (BEDA.) The Lord is baptized, 253Anot needing to be cleansed, etc., as far as now taught, he openly preaches the Son of God.

VERS. 22.-- In thee I am well pleased. Not what is another's in the Son, but his own, is praised, as though he should say: Whatsoever things thou hast are mine.

VERS. 23.-- Thirty years of age. (BEDA.) That he is baptized in his thirtieth year, etc., as far as as in Jeremias and Daniel.

VERS. 26.-- Who was of Mathathias, who was of Amos, who was of Nahum, who was of Hesli. (BEDA.) Mathath, who is descended through Solomon, etc., as far as who through carnal generation is shown to have been made the Son of man.

VERS. 29.-- Who was of Levi. But these four who are brought in in this place were not the sons of Jacob, but they shared in their names.

253B(BEDA.) As if alluding by the names to what was figured in them, etc., as far as diverse kinds of virtues might go before.

VERS. 31.-- Nathan. (ID.) That Matthew, through Solomon, etc., as far as to whom judgment is given by God the king: He is a priest for ever according to the order of Melchisedech. [Vulg. Psal 109:4 opens the clause Tu es sacerdos, "Thou art a priest"; Migne reports in the third person what the psalm addresses in the second.]

VERS. 37.-- Mathusale. (AMBR.) The years of Mathusale are numbered beyond the flood; it signifies Christ, whose life knows not age.

Who was of Enoch. (BEDA.) Ascending from the baptized Son of God as far as God the Father, he sets Enoch at the seventieth step, etc., as far as whom he shows to be joined, in the resurrection, through the ages, 253Cto the contemplating of the unchangeable wisdom of God.

VERS. 38.-- Seth. Seth, the later son of Adam, is not passed over in silence, so that, since there are two generations of the people, it may signify that Christ is to be numbered in the later rather than in the earlier generation.

Who was of God. The generation begins from the Son of God, and is ended in the Son of God; the created goes before in figure, that the born may follow in truth; he who was made to the image goes first, that for his sake the image may come down.

CAPUT IV.

VERS. 1.-- Et agebatur. (AMBR.) Agebatur in desertum, hoc consilio, ut diabolum provocaret; nam nisi ille certasset, iste mihi non vicisset; mysterio, 253Dut Adam de exsilio liberaret; exemplo, ut ostenderet nobis diabolum ad meliora tendentibus invidere.

In desertum. Historialiter: in illo deserto quod est inter Hierusalem et Hiericho, ubi figuraliter dixerat Adam a diabolo victum fuisse.

VERS. 2.-- Esuriit. (BEDA.) Non est scriptum de Moyse vel Elia, etc., usque ad esurit autem non tam cibum corporis quam salutem animae.

VERS. 3.-- Si Filius Dei es. (AMBR.) Noverat Dei Filium venturum, sed venisse per infirmitatem corporis non putabat, etc., usque ad qui hoc verbo non vescitur, non vivit.

VERS. 4.-- Scriptum est. Docet magis doctrina quam miraculis pugnare, humilitati quam potentia, 254Anihil pro diaboli arbitrio, nec declarandae virtutis consideratione faciendum.

VERS. 5.-- Et duxit. Lucas sicut res gesta est exsequitur, mediam ponens avaritiam, ultimam superbiam, secundum mores praesentis temporis, quia superbia post omnes virtutes saevit. Matthaeus non sequitur ordinem historiae, sed tentationum Adae.

VERS. 6.-- Tibi dabo. Haec de arrogantia dicit, non quod totus mundus sit suus, sunt enim aliqui boni. Mihi traditur. Non a diabolo est potestas, sed obnoxia tamen insidiis diaboli. Nec ideo mala ordinatio potestatum, quia malo sunt obnoxiae potestates.

VERS. 7.-- Tu ergo. (BEDA.) Dicens diabolus Salvatori: Si procidens adoraveris me, econtrario audit, quod ipse magis adorare eum debeat, Dominum et 254BDeum suum.

VERS. 8.-- Si Filius Dei. In omnibus tentationibus hoc agit, ut intelligat si Filius Dei sit. Sed Christus sic responsum temperat, ut ambiguum relinquat: Mitte te deorsum. Quem nec gula nec avaritia vicit, tentat vana gloria, si forte illum vel ipsa victoriae suae jactantia valeat dejicere. Mitte. Haec vox ei convenit qui omnes praecipitare satagit. In quo infirmus ostenditur qui nulli possit nocere, nisi prius ille se deorsum miserit.

VERS. 9.-- Scriptum est. Quia Scripturarum exemplum praetulerat, Scripturarum vincitur exemplis. Utitur autem diabolus per haereticos testimoniis Scripturarum, non ut doceat, sed ut fallat.

VERS. 10.-- Angelis. (BEDA.) Haec prophetia non 254Cde Christo est, sed de sancto viro. Quae etiamsi de Christo esset, etiam quod sequitur debuerat meminisse: Super aspidem et basiliscum ambulabis, et conculcabis leonem et draconem (Psal. XC). Sed de angelorum auxilio quasi ad infirmum loquitur, de sua conculcatione quasi tergiversator tacet.

VERS. 13.-- Et consummata. (ID.) Tribus tentationibus victis, omnem tentationem dicit consummatam, etc., usque ad tamen formidat instare, ne frequentius triumphetur.

VERS. 14.-- Et fama exiit. Non tantum a praesentibus propter visa miracula honoratur, sed apud absentes fama divulgabatur.

VERS. 15.-- Docebat. Post miracula ecce sapientia, qua docet, qua doctrina per miracula praecedentia 254Dmagnificat.

VERS. 16.-- Et surrexit legere. (BEDA.) Qui non venerat ministrari, sed ministrare, etc., usque ad vel propetiae lectonis attestatione corrigeret.

VERS. 18.-- Spiritus. (ID.) In superioribus prophetiae Salvator de gentium vocatione et Ecclesiae confirmatione loquens, etc., usque ad quia Spiritus Domini est super eum.

Evangelizare pauperibus, etc. Gentes intelligit quae erant vere pauperes. Neque enim Dei cognitionem, neque legem, neque prophetas, neque aliud quidquam in bonis habebant, et caeteris omnibus spiritualibus opibus erant destitutae.

VERS. 20.-- Et cum plicuisset. (BEDA.) Audientibus 255Aillis qui aderant legit, etc., usque ad sed pro capacitate audientium committit doctori dispensandum verbum.

VERS. 23.-- Medice. Sicut fabri filium, ita eadem erroris dementia; medicum vocant, sed in errore illorum veritas latet. Vere enim erat filius fabri, qui per ipsum in principio omnia fecit, qui operatur in Spiritu sancto et igni, qui in magna domo hujus mundi facit vasa diversi generis. Est et medicus, quia per ipsum omnia quae in coelis et quae in terra sunt restaurata. Qui de se dicit: Non est opus valentibus medicus, sed male habentibus (Matth. IX).

VERS. 24.-- Amen dico vobis. (BEDA.) Non solum Dominus et caput prophetarum, etc., usque ad miracula suae virtutis avertit.

VERS. 25.-- In diebus Eliae. (ID.) Quasi dicat: Non 255Best contrarium exemplo prophetarum, etc., usque ad quia nulla erat cognoscendae divinitatis ubertas.

VERS. 26.-- Viduam. (ID.) Vidua ad quam Elias mittitur, gentium figurat Ecclesiam, etc., usque ad in qua nobis vitae aeternae praeparatur panis.

VERS. 27.-- Naaman Syrus. (ID.) Naaman, qui decor interpretatur, populum significat nationum, etc., usque ad quia oportet baptizatos Dominici corporis participatione confirmari.

VERS. 29.-- Et duxerunt. (ID.) Pejores Judaei discipuli, diabolo magistro, etc., usque ad veluti divini Sculptoris expressa manu servantur.

VERS. 31.-- Et descendit, etc. (ID.) Nec indignatione commotus, nec scelere offensus, etc., usque ad nec solvitur lex, si sit renovatio hominis jam labentis.

255CVERS. 33.-- Et in Synagoga. (ID.) In Synagoga est homo habens spiritum immundum, quia Spiritum sanctum amiserat. Introierat enim diabolus, unde Christus spiritualiter exierat.

VERS. 34.-- Scio te. Per opera potuit probari Filius Dei. Sed nonne dixit Apostolis: Majora facietis? Solutio, per opera, id est modum operationis. Alii dicebant: In nomine Jesu, etc. Ipse dicebat: Tibi dixi, etc.

VERS. 35.-- Et cum projecisset. (BEDA.) Curandus permittitur a daemone in medium projici, ut virtus patefacta plures ad salutem invitet.

VERS. 36.-- Et virtute. Non ut homines qui in verbo Dei daemonia ejiciunt, sed propria potestate 255Dvirtutes operatur.

VERS. 38.-- In domum Simonis. Domus Petri, circumcisio est ejus apostolatui commissa. Socrus, est Synagoga quae quodammodo mater est Ecclesiae Petro commissae. Haec febricitat, quia invidiae aestibus laborabat, persequens Ecclesiam. Sed Jesus stans super eam sanat, quia ubi timor Dei incipit esse, talis aestuatio solet recedere.

VERS. 40.-- Cum sol autem occidisset. (BEDA.) Solis occubitus, passio Christi, per quam plures sanantur daemoniaci, quam ante vivens in carne aegrotos sanaverat, quia in carne vivens paucos Judaeorum docuit, post resurrectionem gentibus apparuit.

VERS. 41.-- Tu es Filius Dei. (ID.) Cum jejunio fatigatum 256Adiabolus videret, etc., usque ad quia, si cognovissent Dominum gloriae, nunquam crucifixissent (Matth. I).

VERS. 42.-- Facta autem die. Mystice: manifesta luce resurrectionis a credentium turmis requiritur: et in gentium deserto inventus, ne abeat, retinetur.

CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 1.-- And he was led. (AMBR.) He was led into the desert with this purpose, that he might provoke the devil; for unless that one had contended, this one would not have conquered for me; with this mystery, 253Dthat he might free Adam from exile; with this example, that he might show us that the devil envies those who reach out toward better things.

Into the desert. Historically: into that desert which is between Jerusalem and Jericho, where he had figuratively said that Adam had been overcome by the devil.

VERS. 2.-- He was hungry. (BEDA.) It is not written of Moses or of Elias, etc., as far as but he hungers not so much for the food of the body as for the salvation of the soul.

VERS. 3.-- If thou be the Son of God. (AMBR.) He had known that the Son of God would come, but he did not think that he had come through the weakness of the body, etc., as far as he who is not fed with this word does not live.

VERS. 4.-- It is written. He teaches that one must fight rather with doctrine than with miracles, with humility rather than with power, 254Aand that nothing is to be done at the devil's pleasure, nor out of any regard for displaying his power.

VERS. 5.-- And he brought him. Luke follows the thing as it was done, setting avarice in the middle and pride last, according to the manners of the present time, because pride rages after all the virtues. Matthew does not follow the order of the history, but that of the temptations of Adam.

VERS. 6.-- To thee will I give. He says these things out of arrogance, not because the whole world is his, for there are some good men. It is delivered to me. Power is not from the devil, but it is nevertheless exposed to the snares of the devil. Nor is the ordering of the powers evil on that account, because the powers are exposed to evil.

VERS. 7.-- Thou therefore. (BEDA.) The devil, saying to the Saviour: If falling down thou wilt adore me, hears in return that he himself ought rather to adore him, his Lord and 254Bhis God.

VERS. 8.-- If thou be the Son of God. In all the temptations he does this, that he may learn whether he be the Son of God. But Christ so tempers his answer as to leave it in doubt: Cast thyself down. Him whom neither gluttony nor avarice overcame, vainglory tempts, in case perhaps the very boasting of his own victory might avail to cast him down. Cast. This word befits him who busies himself to hurl all men headlong. In which he is shown to be weak, since he can harm no one unless that one has first cast himself down.

VERS. 9.-- It is written. Because he had put forward an example from the Scriptures, he is overcome by examples from the Scriptures. But the devil uses the testimonies of the Scriptures through the heretics, not to teach, but to deceive.

VERS. 10.-- To the angels. (BEDA.) This prophecy is not 254Cof Christ, but of a holy man. And even if it were of Christ, he ought also to have remembered what follows: Thou shalt walk upon the asp and the basilisk, and thou shalt trample under foot the lion and the dragon (Psal. XC). But of the help of the angels he speaks as though to one who is weak; of his own treading down he keeps silence, like a shuffler.

VERS. 13.-- And being ended. (ID.) The three temptations being overcome, he says that every temptation was ended, etc., as far as yet he dreads to press on, lest he be more often triumphed over.

VERS. 14.-- And the fame went out. He is honoured not only by those present because of the miracles they had seen, but among the absent his fame was being spread abroad.

VERS. 15.-- He taught. After the miracles, behold wisdom, by which he teaches, and by which teaching, through the preceding miracles, 254Dhe magnifies.

VERS. 16.-- And he rose up to read. (BEDA.) He who had come not to be ministered unto but to minister, etc., as far as or he might correct them by the testimony of the reading of the prophecy.

VERS. 18.-- The Spirit. (ID.) In the earlier parts of the prophecy the Saviour, speaking of the calling of the Gentiles and the confirmation of the Church, etc., as far as because the Spirit of the Lord is upon him.

To preach the gospel to the poor, etc. He understands the Gentiles, who were truly poor. For they had neither the knowledge of God, nor the law, nor the prophets, nor anything else among their goods, and they were destitute of all the other spiritual riches.

VERS. 20.-- And when he had folded. (BEDA.) He read in the hearing 255Aof those who were present, etc., as far as but according to the capacity of the hearers he commits the word to the teacher to be dispensed.

VERS. 23.-- Physician. As [they call him] the son of a carpenter, so by the same madness of error; they call him a physician, but in their error the truth lies hidden. For he was truly the son of a carpenter: of one who through him made all things in the beginning, who works in the Holy Ghost and in fire, who in the great house of this world makes vessels of diverse kinds. He is a physician also, because through him all things that are in the heavens and that are on earth have been restored. Who says of himself: They that are in health need not a physician, but they that are ill (Matth. IX).

VERS. 24.-- Amen I say to you. (BEDA.) Not only the Lord and head of the prophets, etc., as far as he turned away the miracles of his power.

VERS. 25.-- In the days of Elias. (ID.) As if he should say: It is not 255Bcontrary to the example of the prophets, etc., as far as because there was no abundance for the knowing of the Godhead.

VERS. 26.-- A widow. (ID.) The widow to whom Elias is sent figures the Church of the Gentiles, etc., as far as in which the bread of eternal life is prepared for us.

VERS. 27.-- Naaman the Syrian. (ID.) Naaman, who is interpreted comeliness, signifies the people of the nations, etc., as far as because it behoves the baptized to be confirmed by partaking of the Lord's body.

VERS. 29.-- And they led him. (ID.) The Jews, worse disciples, with the devil for their master, etc., as far as they are preserved as though shaped by the hand of the divine Sculptor.

VERS. 31.-- And he went down, etc. (ID.) Neither moved by indignation, nor offended by their crime, etc., as far as nor is the law dissolved, if there be a renewal of a man now falling.

255CVERS. 33.-- And in the synagogue. (ID.) In the synagogue there is a man having an unclean spirit, because he had lost the Holy Spirit. For the devil had entered in, whence Christ had spiritually gone out.

VERS. 34.-- I know thee. By his works he could be proved the Son of God. But did he not say to the Apostles: Greater things shall you do? The solution: by works, that is, by the manner of the working. Others were saying: In the name of Jesus, etc. He himself was saying: I have said to thee, etc.

VERS. 35.-- And when he had thrown him. (BEDA.) He who was to be cured is allowed to be thrown into the midst by the demon, so that the power laid open may invite more to salvation.

VERS. 36.-- And with power. Not as men, who cast out demons in the word of God, but by his own authority 255Dhe works mighty deeds.

VERS. 38.-- Into Simon's house. The house of Peter is the circumcision committed to his apostolate. The mother-in-law is the Synagogue, which is in a manner the mother of the Church committed to Peter. She is sick of a fever, because she laboured under the heats of envy, persecuting the Church. But Jesus, standing over her, heals her, because where the fear of God begins to be, such burning is wont to depart.

VERS. 40.-- And when the sun was down. (BEDA.) The setting of the sun is the passion of Christ, through which more possessed by demons are healed than he had healed sick people while living before in the flesh; because living in the flesh he taught few of the Jews, but after the resurrection he appeared to the Gentiles.

VERS. 41.-- Thou art the Son of God. (ID.) When the devil saw him wearied 256Awith fasting, etc., as far as because, if they had known the Lord of glory, they would never have crucified him (Matth. I).

VERS. 42.-- And when it was day. Mystically: in the manifest light of the resurrection he is sought by the companies of believers; and being found in the desert of the Gentiles, he is held back, that he may not go away.

CAPUT V.

VERS. 1.-- Factum est autem. Nec tempore, nec loco turba a studio sanandi cohibetur; vesper incumbit, et tamen sequitur; stagnum occurrit, et turba urget, et ideo ascendit in navim Petri.

Secus stabat. (BEDA.) Stagnum, praesens saeculum designat. Dominus autem jam non in stagno quasi jam non in carne passibili, sed secus stagnum, quia in ea carne in qua passus est stabilitatem perpetuae 256Bquietis adiit.

VERS. 2.-- Vidit duas naves. (ID.) Duae naves, mystice, circumcisionem et praeputium significant quas vidit, quia in utroque populo novit qui sunt ejus, quorum corda a fluctibus saeculi ad futurae vitae tranquillitatem, quasi ad littus misericorditer vivendo, provenit.

VERS. 3.-- Rogavit eum. (ID.) Quod primo rogat navim a terra reduci pusillum, etc., usque ad ad remotiores gentes quibus postea praedicatum est pertinet.

VERS. 4.-- Duc in altum. (AMBR.) Etsi aliis imperatur ut laxent retia, etc., usque ad vel de Spiritu sancti profluentibus donis instruitur Ecclesia, et armatur.

VERS. 5.-- In verbo, etc. (BEDA.) Nisi verbo gratiae 256Claxata fuerint instrumenta disputationum, etc., usque ad etiam inter persequentium scandala.

VERS. 7.-- Et impleverunt. (ID.) Haec impletio usque in finem saeculi crescit, etc., usque ad et talis humilitas meretur piam Domini consolationem.

VERS. 10.-- Ex hoc jam. (ID.) Hoc ad ipsum Petrum specialiter pertinet, cui exponitur quid captura significet piscium. Sed sicut tunc per retia pisces, sic per verba aliquando capiet homines, in quo Petro est typus totius Ecclesiae.

VERS. 12.-- In una civitatum. Hic duo adventus in Capharnaum notantur. Primus, incarcerato Joanne, quando fecit continue illa tria miracula; secundus, quando descendit de monte electis apostolis, quando fit istud miraculum de leproso.

256DVERS. 13.-- Tetigit. Propter humilitatem, ut nos doceret nullum spernere pro aliqua corporis maculatione. Tangit non dedignatus, imperat non diffisus, adhibet operis testimonium. Volo. Dicit propter Fotinum, imperat propter Arium, tangit propter Manichaeum.

VERS. 14.-- Praecepit illi. (BEDA.) Taceri jubet, nec taceri potest. Non quod aliquid voluerit et non potuerit, sed dat exemplum ut sui in magnis quae faciunt latere velint, sed ut prosint aliis, prodantur inviti.

VERS. 15.-- Et conveniebant turbae, etc. (BEDA.) Sanatio hujus multas ad Dominum cogit turbas. Ut enim ipse interius et exterius se sanatum doceret: perceptum beneficium etiam jussus non tacet. Sed 257Aet Marcus ait, evangelico functus officio: Mox egressus, coepit praedicare et diffamare sermonem (Marc. I).

VERS. 16.-- Et orabat. Quando orat, theoricam vitam, quando sanat activam informat.

(BEDA.) Sanat ut Deus, orat ut homo. In urbe, etc., usque ad per interna desideria silenter loquatur.

VERS. 17.-- Et factum est. etc., (ID.) Ubi sedens docuerit Lucas breviandi gratia praeteriit, etc., usque ad quam non nascendo, sed virtutibus illustrando suam fecerat.

Et erant. (AMBR.) Inter caeterorum remedia debilium, convenientibus, etc., usque ad actuumque nostrorum clauda vestigia verbi coelestis remedio reformentur.

VERS. 18.-- Viri portantes. Allegorice: id est, erigentes ad superiora animum hominum, quamvis 257Bexterioris hominis debilitate torpentem; quorum rursus adminiculis, et attollere et humiliare se facilius ante Jesum docetur, Dominico dignus videri aspectu. Humilitatem enim respicit Deus.

Et quaerebant, etc. (BEDA.) Desiderant offerre, sed turba interposita recluduntur, quia saepe anima, etc., usque ad quia ad Christi humilitatem fidei pietate descenditur, et ad Christi notitiam venitur.

VERS. 19.-- Et per tegulas. (ID.) Domus Jesu tegulis tecta describitur, quia sub contemptibili litterarum velamine, si adsit doctor, qui reseret divina, spiritualis gratiae virtus invenitur- Qoud cum lecto deponitur, significat ab homine adhuc in ista carne constituto Christum debere cognosci.

257CVERS. 20.-- Quorum fidem. (BEDA.) Multum valet fides propria cujusque, cum per alienam fidem homo interius et exterius sit salvatus.

(ID.) Haec curatio paralytici significat salvationem animae suspirantis ad Deum, etc., usque ad quae sunt prudentia, fortitudo, temperantia, justitia.

Homo, remittuntur, etc. Tu homo peccator, ego Deus qui peccata remitto, qui aliorum merito tibi peccata relaxo. Qui ergo peccatis gravatur, adhibeat Ecclesiam quae pro eo precetur.

Quia pro culpa animae etiam corpus infirmabatur, ideo sanaturus corpus, prius sanat animam.

VERS. 21.-- Quis potest dimittere? Nemo dimittit peccata nisi Deus, qui per eos quoque dimittit quibus potestatem dimittendi dedit. Cum ergo Christus 257Ddimittat, vere Deus probatur. Verum testimonium Deo reddunt, sed personam Christi negando falluntur. Perfidia enim confiteri potest, credere non potest. Itaque testimonium non deest Divinitati, fides deest saluti. Deus autem volens salvos facere peccatores, et occultorum cognitione se Deum esse demonstrat, et admiratione factorum illis respondens: Quid cogitatis mala in cordibus vestris? Eadem majestate et potentia qua cogitationes vestras intueor, possum hominibus delicta dimittere. Ex vobis intelligite quid paralyticus consequatur. Sed quia spiritualem gratiam non cognoscitis, nec creditis quod interius factum est, probetur signo visibili quod non minoris constet esse potentiae, ut in Filio hominis 258Alatentem cognoscatis potentiam majestatis qua potest peccata dimittere ut Deus.

VERS. 22.-- Ut autem cognovit, etc. (AMBR.) Magnus sacerdos lepram videbat in cordibus Judaeorum, et pejores ostendit illo leproso cui purgato jussit se offerre sacerdoti. Sed hos summus sacerdos repudiat ne alios quoque eorum lepra contaminet.

VERS. 24.-- Tibi dico, surge. (BEDA.) Surgere, est animam a carnalibus desideriis abstrahere. Lectum tollere, etc., usque ad per missionem poenae, remissionem culpae intellige.

VERS. 26.-- Et stupor. (AMBR.) Spectant surgentem increduli, mirantur abeuntem, et divini operis miracula malunt timere quam credere; nam si crederent, non timerent, sed diligerent. Perfecta enim 258Bdilectio foras mittit timorem (I Joan., IV).

VERS. 27.-- Levi. (BEDA.) Idem Levi qui et Matthaeus. Sed Lucas, etc., usque ad de telonario in evangelistam sit mutatus.

(BEDA, AMBR.) Reliquit propria, qui rapiebat aliena. Nec solum lucra reliquit vectigalium, sed et periculum contemnit, quod poterat venire a principibus saeculi, quia rationes vectigalium incompositas reliquerit. Et quia in nullo prorsus hujus vitae respectum vel cogitationem sibi reservavit, dominicorum talentorum fidelis dispensator esse meruit.

(BEDA.) Per Matthaei electionem, fides gentium, quae prius mundanis inhiabant, sed nunc corpus Christi sedula devotione reficiunt, exprimitur. Per superbiam Pharisaeorum insinuatur Judaeorum invidia, 258Cquae de gentium salute torquetur.
