VERS. 30.-- Quare, etc. (AMBR.) Omnis quaestio ex lege quae tenax justitiae, non habet, etc., usque ad et ambulatio et omnia quae gessit, saluti serviunt.

(AMBR., BEDA.) Qui domicilio Christum recipit interno, maximis exuberantium pascitur delectationibus voluptatum. Itaque Dominus ingreditur, et in ejus qui credidit recumbit affectu. Sed rursus accenditur invidia perfidorum, futurae poenae species praefiguratur. Epulantibus enim fidelibus et in regno coelorum recumbentibus, perfidia jejuna torquebitur. Ostenditur etiam quantum intersit inter aemulos legis et gratiae, quia qui legem sequuntur, mentis famem patientur aeternam. Qui vero in interioribus verbum receperint, alimenti coelestis et fontis ubertate 258Drecreati, esurire et sitire non possunt, et ideo qui animo jejunant murmurant, dicentes: Quare cum publicanis, etc.? Filii diaboli, venenum serpentis diffundunt. Serpens primam vocem emisit, dicens Evae: Quid utique Deus dixit: Nolite manducare ex omni ligno? (Gen. III.) Hunc imitantur isti.

VERS. 31.-- Medico. Christus medicus qui, miro medicandi genere, vulneratus est propter iniquitates nostras. Hoc medicamine serpentis venenum excluditur. Qui hoc medicamento utitur, non remanet jejunus.

VERS. 32.-- Vocare justos. (BEDA.) Justos vocat eos qui, Dei justitiam ignorantes et suam volentes constituere, justitiae Dei non sunt subjecti; et de lege 259Apraesumentes, gratiam Evangelii non quaerunt; peccatores qui sua mala attendentes, nec per legem se posse justificari putantes, Christi se gratiae poenitendo submittunt.

VERS. 33.-- Quare discipuli. (BEDA.) Matthaeus dicit ipsos discipulos hoc quaesisse. Sed sciendum, quia utrique quaesierunt.

(ID.) Spiritualiter: discipuli Joannis et Pharisaeorum jejunant, quia qui opera legis, etc., usque ad manducat cum peccatoribus, ut gratiam et potestatem intelligas.

VERS. 34.-- Nunquid enim potestis. (ID.) Quandiu sponsus nobiscum est et in laetitia sumus, nec jejunare possumus, etc., usque ad tunc filii sponsi jejunabunt, desiderantes Judicis adventum.

259BVERS. 35.-- Venient autem. (ID.) Quasi dicat: Facturi estis, ut lugentes jejunent, quia estis, etc., usque ad quandiu cum discipulis Dominus in carne conversatus est.

Tunc jejunabunt, etc. De utroque jejunio quod est in tribulatione vel gaudio respondet: Cum ablatus fuerit sponsus, tunc erunt in moerore et luctu, donec per Spiritum sanctum consolatio tribuatur; quo dono percepto, jejunium quod fit per laetitiam, jam renovati in vitam spiritualem celebrabunt. Et hic prius agit de jejunio quod pertinet ad humilitatem tribulationis. Illud autem quod ad gaudium mentis pertinet (quando mens ad spiritualia suspenditur, et ideo a cibis corporeis alienatur) sequentibus similitudinibus significat, ostendens quod 259Ccarnalibus et ob hoc adhuc veterem sensum trahentibus, hoc genus jejunii non congruit.

VERS. 36.-- A vestimento novo. (BEDA.) Veteri vestimento comparantur discipuli, qui adhuc sunt vetus homo. Quibus novus pannus, etc., usque ad unde Apostolus: Exuite veterem hominem cum actibus suis, et induite novum (Ephes. IV).

VERS. 37.-- Vinum. Vino intus reficimur, veste foris tegimur. Vestis ergo sunt bona opera quae foris agimus, quibus coram hominibus lucemus. Vinum, fervor fidei, spei et charitatis, quo in conspectu Dei in novitate sensus intus reformamur.

In utres. (BEDA.) Veteribus etiam utribus comparantur, qui novo vino, id est, spiritualibus praeceptis, facilius dirumpuntur quam contineant. Erunt autem 259Dnovi utres, cum post ascensionem, accepto Spiritu, desiderio consolationis ejus orando innovabuntur.

Aliter: animae nondum innovatae, sed in vetustate malitiae perseveranti novorum mysteriorum sacramenta non debent committi. Aliter: veteres utres sunt scribae et Pharisaei; novus pannus et novum vinum, praecepta Evangelii, quae non possunt sustinere Judaei, ne major scissura fiat, cum quibus Galatae praecepta legis miscebant in utres veteres vinum novum. Conveniunt autem apostolis non his qui traditionibus majorum depravati, sinceritatem praeceptorum Christi nequeunt custodire; unde subditur: Et nemo, quia Judaeis veteris vitae salivis imbutis novae gratiae praecepta sordebant, quia majorum traditionibus 260Aaccumulati, dulcedinem spiritualium verborum percipere non valebant.

VERS. 38.-- Sed vinum. Fragilitas humanae conditionis aperitur, cum corpora nostra exuviis defunctorum animalium comparantur. Sed renovatis utribus, nova vina conducuntur, cum sacramenta quae accepimus inviolata servamus. Hos utres, si pleni sunt, gratia servat, si vacui, tinea et aerugo consumit: semper ergo sint pleni.

CHAPTER V.

VERS. 1.-- And it came to pass. Neither by time nor by place is the crowd restrained from its zeal for being healed; evening presses on, and yet it follows; the lake meets them, and the crowd presses, and therefore he went up into Peter's ship.

He stood beside. (BEDA.) The lake signifies the present world. But the Lord is now no longer in the lake, as being now no longer in passible flesh, but beside the lake, because in that flesh in which he suffered he came to the stability of perpetual 256Brest.

VERS. 2.-- He saw two ships. (ID.) The two ships, mystically, signify the circumcision and the uncircumcision, which he saw, because in each people he knoweth who are his, whose hearts comes forth from the waves of the world to the tranquillity of the life to come, as it were to the shore, by living mercifully.

VERS. 3.-- He asked him. (ID.) That he first asks the ship to be drawn back a little from the land, etc., as far as it pertains to the more remote Gentiles to whom it was afterwards preached.

VERS. 4.-- Launch out into the deep. (AMBR.) Although it is commanded to others that they let down their nets, etc., as far as or the Church is instructed and armed from the flowing gifts of the Holy Ghost.

VERS. 5.-- At thy word, etc. (BEDA.) Unless by the word of grace 256Cthe instruments of disputations have been let down, etc., as far as even amid the scandals of persecutors.

VERS. 7.-- And they filled. (ID.) This filling grows even unto the end of the world, etc., as far as and such humility deserves the merciful consolation of the Lord.

VERS. 10.-- From henceforth. (ID.) This pertains specially to Peter himself, to whom is expounded what the catch of fishes signifies. But as then he took fishes by nets, so at some time he shall take men by words, wherein Peter is a type of the whole Church.

VERS. 12.-- In one of the cities. Here two comings into Capharnaum are noted. The first, when John had been imprisoned, when he wrought those three miracles in succession; the second, when he came down from the mountain, the apostles having been chosen, when this miracle of the leper is wrought.

256DVERS. 13.-- He touched. Because of humility, that he might teach us to despise no one for any blemish of the body. He touches, not disdaining; he commands, not distrusting; he adds the testimony of the work. I will. He says it because of Photinus, he commands because of Arius, he touches because of Manichaeus.

VERS. 14.-- He charged him. (BEDA.) He bids it be kept silent, and it cannot be kept silent. Not that he willed something and could not, but he gives an example that his own should wish to lie hidden in the great things they do, yet that they may profit others, be published against their will.

VERS. 15.-- And the crowds came together, etc. (BEDA.) The healing of this man drives many crowds to the Lord. For that he might show himself healed within and without: he does not keep silent the benefit received, even when bidden. But 257AMark also says, discharging the evangelist's office: Straightway going forth, he began to publish and to spread abroad the word (Marc. I). [Vulg. Mc 1:45 reads At ille egressus coepit praedicare, et diffamare sermonem, "But he going out began to publish and to spread abroad the word."]

VERS. 16.-- And he was praying. When he prays, he forms the contemplative life; when he heals, the active.

(BEDA.) He heals as God, he prays as man. In the city, etc., as far as let him speak silently through inward desires.

VERS. 17.-- And it came to pass. etc., (ID.) Where he sat and taught Luke passed over for the sake of brevity, etc., as far as which he had made his own not by being born, but by making it illustrious with his powers.

And there were. (AMBR.) Among the remedies of the other weak ones, as they came together, etc., as far as and let the halting steps of our actions be reformed by the remedy of the heavenly word.

VERS. 18.-- Men carrying. Allegorically: that is, raising to higher things the mind of men, though sluggish through the weakness of 257Bthe outward man; by whose supports in turn one is taught both to lift himself up and to humble himself more easily before Jesus, worthy to be seen by the Lord's regard. For God looks upon humility.

And they sought, etc. (BEDA.) They desire to offer, but with the crowd interposed they are shut out, because often the soul, etc., as far as because it is by the devotion of faith that one descends to Christ's humility, and comes to the knowledge of Christ.

VERS. 19.-- And through the tiles. (ID.) The house of Jesus is described as roofed with tiles, because under the contemptible veil of the letter, if there be present a teacher to unlock divine things, the power of spiritual grace is found. And that he is let down with his bed signifies that Christ must be known by a man still set in this flesh.

257CVERS. 20.-- Whose faith. (BEDA.) Each man's own faith avails much, since through another's faith a man has been saved within and without.

(ID.) This healing of the paralytic signifies the salvation of a soul sighing after God, etc., as far as which are prudence, fortitude, temperance, justice.

Man, they are forgiven, etc. Thou art a man, a sinner; I am God, who forgive sins, who by the merit of others release thy sins for thee. Let him therefore who is burdened with sins call in the Church to pray for him.

Because for the fault of the soul the body also was made infirm, therefore, about to heal the body, he first heals the soul.

VERS. 21.-- Who can forgive? No one forgives sins but God, who forgives also through those to whom he has given the power of forgiving. Since therefore Christ 257Dforgives, he is truly proved God. They render true testimony to God, but by denying the person of Christ they are deceived. For faithlessness can confess, it cannot believe. And so testimony is not wanting to the Godhead, faith is wanting to salvation. But God, willing to save sinners, both shows himself God by the knowledge of hidden things, and, answering them by the wonder of his deeds: Why do you think evil things in your hearts? [Vulg. Lc 5:22 reads Quid cogitatis in cordibus vestris?, "What is it you think in your hearts?"; mala, "evil things," stands at Mt 9:4.] By the same majesty and power by which I behold your thoughts, I can forgive men their offences. From yourselves understand what the paralytic obtains. But because you do not know spiritual grace, nor believe what has been done within, let it be proved by a visible sign that it is of no less power, that in the Son of man 258Ayou may know the hidden power of majesty whereby he can forgive sins as God.

VERS. 22.-- But when he knew, etc. (AMBR.) The great priest saw leprosy in the hearts of the Jews, and showed them worse than that leper whom, when cleansed, he bade offer himself to the priest. But these the high priest rejects, lest their leprosy defile others also.

VERS. 24.-- I say to thee, arise. (BEDA.) To arise is to draw the soul away from carnal desires. To take up the bed, etc., as far as by the release of the penalty understand the remission of the fault.

VERS. 26.-- And astonishment. (AMBR.) The unbelieving look on him as he rises, they marvel at him as he goes away, and they choose rather to fear the miracles of the divine work than to believe; for if they believed, they would not fear, but would love. For perfect 258Blove casteth out fear (I Joan., IV). [Vulg. 1 Jo 4:18 reads perfecta caritas foras mittit timorem; Migne prints dilectio, "love", for caritas, and adds enim.]

VERS. 27.-- Levi. (BEDA.) The same Levi who is also Matthew. But Luke, etc., as far as he was changed from a publican into an evangelist.

(BEDA, AMBR.) He left his own, who was seizing what was another's. Nor did he only leave the gains of the tolls, but he also despised the danger that could come from the princes of the world, because he had left the accounts of the tolls unsettled. And because he reserved to himself absolutely no regard or thought of this life, he deserved to be a faithful steward of the Lord's talents.

(BEDA.) By the election of Matthew is expressed the faith of the Gentiles, who before gaped after worldly things, but now refresh the body of Christ with assiduous devotion. By the pride of the Pharisees is intimated the envy of the Jews, 258Cwhich is tormented at the salvation of the Gentiles.

VERS. 30.-- Why, etc. (AMBR.) Every question from the law, which is tenacious of justice, has not, etc., as far as and his walking and all that he did serve for salvation.

(AMBR., BEDA.) He who receives Christ into the inward dwelling is fed with the greatest delights of overflowing pleasures. And so the Lord enters, and reclines in the affection of him who has believed. But again the envy of the faithless is kindled, and a likeness of the punishment to come is prefigured. For while the faithful feast and recline in the kingdom of heaven, faithlessness shall be tormented with fasting. It is shown also how great a difference there is between the rivals of the law and of grace, because they who follow the law shall suffer an everlasting hunger of mind. But they who have received the word in their inward parts, refreshed by the abundance of the heavenly nourishment and fountain, 258Dcannot hunger and thirst; and therefore they who fast in mind murmur, saying: Why with publicans, etc.? Sons of the devil, they pour abroad the venom of the serpent. The serpent uttered the first word, saying to Eve: What indeed did God say: Eat ye not of every tree? (Gen. III.) [Vulg. Gn 3:1 reads Cur praecepit vobis Deus ut non comederetis de omni ligno paradisi?, "Why hath God commanded you that you should not eat of every tree of paradise?"] These men imitate him.

VERS. 31.-- A physician. Christ the physician who, by a wondrous manner of healing, was wounded for our iniquities. By this medicine the venom of the serpent is shut out. He who uses this medicine does not remain fasting.

VERS. 32.-- To call the just. (BEDA.) He calls those just who, not knowing the justice of God and willing to establish their own, are not subject to the justice of God; [Vulg. Rom 10:3 reads Ignorantes enim justitiam Dei, et suam quaerentes statuere, "For not knowing the justice of God, and seeking to establish their own."] and, presuming upon the law, 259Ado not seek the grace of the Gospel; but sinners, who, attending to their own evils and thinking that they cannot be justified by the law, submit themselves by repentance to the grace of Christ.

VERS. 33.-- Why do the disciples. (BEDA.) Matthew says that the disciples themselves asked this. But it must be known that both asked.

(ID.) Spiritually: the disciples of John and of the Pharisees fast, because they who the works of the law, etc., as far as he eats with sinners, that thou mayest understand the grace and the power.

VERS. 34.-- For can you. (ID.) As long as the bridegroom is with us and we are in gladness, neither can we fast, etc., as far as then shall the children of the bridegroom fast, desiring the coming of the Judge.

259BVERS. 35.-- But the days will come. (ID.) As if he should say: You will bring it about that mourners fast, because you are, etc., as far as as long as the Lord dwelt in the flesh with the disciples.

Then they shall fast, etc. Concerning both fasts, that which is in tribulation and that which is in joy, he answers: When the bridegroom shall be taken away, then shall they be in sorrow and in mourning, until consolation be granted through the Holy Ghost; and when that gift has been received, the fast which is made through gladness they, being now renewed unto the spiritual life, shall celebrate. And here he treats first of the fast which pertains to the humility of tribulation. But that which pertains to the joy of the mind (when the mind is suspended toward spiritual things, and is therefore estranged from bodily foods) he signifies by the similitudes that follow, showing that 259Cto the carnal, and for that reason still drawing the old sense with them, this kind of fasting is not fitting.

VERS. 36.-- From a new garment. (BEDA.) To the old garment are compared the disciples, who are still the old man. To whom the new piece, etc., as far as whence the Apostle: Put off the old man with his deeds, and put on the new (Ephes. IV). [Vulg. Col 3:9 reads expoliantes vos veterem hominem cum actibus suis, "stripping yourselves of the old man with his deeds," and Eph 4:24 et induite novum hominem, "and put on the new man"; the quotation joins words from the two places.]

VERS. 37.-- Wine. By wine we are refreshed within, by the garment we are covered without. The garment therefore is the good works which we do outwardly, by which we shine before men. The wine is the fervour of faith, of hope and of charity, by which we are reformed within, in newness of sense, in the sight of God.

Into bottles. (BEDA.) They are compared also to old bottles, which are more easily burst by new wine, that is, by spiritual precepts, than they contain it. But they shall be 259Dnew bottles when, after the ascension, having received the Spirit, they shall be renewed by praying with desire of his consolation.

Otherwise: to a soul not yet renewed, but persevering in the oldness of malice, the sacraments of the new mysteries ought not to be committed. Otherwise: the old bottles are the scribes and Pharisees; the new piece and the new wine, the precepts of the Gospel, which the Jews cannot bear, lest a greater rent be made, with whom the Galatians mingled the precepts of the law, new wine into old bottles. But they suit the apostles, not those who, depraved by the traditions of the elders, are unable to keep the sincerity of Christ's precepts; whence it is subjoined: And no man, because to the Jews, steeped in the spittle of the old life, the precepts of the new grace were loathsome, because, heaped up with the traditions of the elders, 260Athey were not able to perceive the sweetness of spiritual words.

VERS. 38.-- But wine. The frailty of the human condition is laid open, when our bodies are compared to the cast-off skins of dead animals. But when the bottles have been renewed, new wines are brought in, when we keep unviolated the sacraments which we have received. These bottles, if they are full, grace preserves; if empty, moth and rust consume: therefore let them always be full.

CAPUT VI.

VERS. 1.-- Factum est autem, etc. (BEDA.) Dicit quod sabbati littera solvi coeperat, etc., usque ad post secundum, ab eo quo praecedentia sunt dicta vel facta.

260BPer sata, etc. Mystice: ager mundus; agri seges, humani generis fecunditas; spicae, fructus Ecclesiae, quibus saturantur apostoli nostro se alentes profectu.

Vellebant discipuli, etc. Non habentes discipuli spatium manducandi, propter importunitatem turbarum, esuriebant ut homines, sed vellentes spicas inediam consolantur, quod est indicium austerioris vitae, non praeparatas escas, sed cibos simplices quaerere. Et nota quod apostoli litteram sabbati jam destruunt adversus Ebionitas, qui alios apostolos recipiunt, Paulum quasi legis transgressorem repudiant.

(BEDA.) Mystice: discipuli esurientes salutem hominum transeunt per sata, id est per regiones quae, etc., usque ad arguens doctores legis nescire 260Clegem.

VERS. 2.-- Dicebant illis, etc. Alii dicunt ipsi Domino haec fuisse objecta. Sed a diversis et ipsi Domino, et discipulis potuerunt objici, et cuicunque objectum sit ad ipsum maxime respicit. In sabbatis. Sabbata significant feriationes; vel quae in hoc saeculo aguntur a vacantibus superstitionibus Judaeorum; vel quae in futuro agentur, quando in perpetua solemnitate manducabimus bona terrae.

VERS. 3.-- Nec hoc legistis, etc. (BEDA.) Quomodo Domino ad crimen objicitur, quod in servo pro crimine non tenetur? Verus rex, etc., usque ad omnes filii Ecclesiae sunt sacerdotes, uncti Spiritu sancto ut seipsos offerant.

VERS. 4.-- Intravit in domum Dei. Magna hospitalitatis 260Dgratia, proposito mortis periculo, non declinat hospitem animus sacerdotis. Et sic nos debemus aliena pericula in nos transferre. Sed et verum David hospitio mentis nec pro periculo mortis veri excludunt sacerdotes.

VERS. 6.-- Homo. (BEDA.) Mystice homo iste humanum genus significat in fecunditate boni operis arefactum, per manum in primo parente ad lignum extensam, quam sanat manus innocens in cruce extensa. Et bene manus in Synagoga erat arida, quia ubi majus donum scientiae, ibi majori transgressor subjacet culpae.

VERS. 7.-- Observabant. (ID.) Quia destructione sabbati, quam in discipulis arguebant, probabili magistri 261Aexcusavit exemplo, nunc ipsum observando magistrum, calumniari volunt, ut si non curet, crudelitatis vel imbecillitatis, si curet, transgressionis arguant.

VERS. 9.-- Interrogo vos. (BEDA.) Praeveniens calumniam quam sibi parabant, arguit eos, etc., usque ad non est auctor mali Deus, sed quos non salvat, perdere dicitur.

VERS. 10.-- Extende. (ID.) Infructuosae debilitas animae nullo melius ordine curatur, quam eleemosynarum largitate; unde dicitur: Non sit manus tua ad accipiendum porrecta, et ad dandum collecta (Eccles., IV). Quia frustra pro peccatis rogaturus, manus ad Deum extendit, qui has ad pauperes pro posse non extendit.

VERS. 11.-- Insipientia. Quia solo livore permoti 261Bsunt, non enim in culpa fuit vel hominis manum extendere, vel Jesum sanare. Magna insipientia erat de nece ejus tractare cujus beneficiis plurimum indigebant.

VERS. 12.-- Exiit in montem orare. Mons in quo apostolos eligit et docet, altitudinem significat justitiae, quia instituendi erant et praedicaturi, ne infirmis remaneant, sed ad superna erigantur. Sic et lex in monte data fuit. Apostolos praeeminentius adducit, ut a monte prius montes suscipiant pacem populo annuntiandam. Sic et Moyses, solus legem spiritualiter intelligens, montis verticem in quo Deus erat ascendit.

VERS. 13.-- Discipulos. De quibus elegit duodecim quos apostolos, id est suo loco missos, nominavit; unde: Sicut misit me Pater, et ego mitto vos (Joan. XX). In montem ascendens ad se vocat, elegit quos vult, 261Cquia non illorum est studii sed divinae gratiae, ut in apostolatum vocarentur; unde: Non vos me elegistis, sed ego elegi vos (Ibid., XV). Non sapientes, non divites, non nobiles elegit, ne duxisse pro prudentia, ne redemisse divitiis, ne potentiae nobilitatisque auctoritate aliquos ad gratiam traxisse videretur.

VERS. 14.-- Cognominavit. Non modo primum, sed longe prius cum ab Andraea adductus dicitur: Tu vocaberis Cephas, quod interpretatur Petrus. Sed volens Lucas nomina duodecim apostolorum nominare, cum necesse haberet Petrum dicere, breviter voluit innuere, quod non antea vocaretur; sed ita Dominus cognominavit, ut non casu factum putetur, sed ex commutatione sacramentum petrae audientibus commendaretur.

261DVERS. 15.-- Matthaeum. Matthaeus compari suo Thomae, in ordine causa humilitatis se supponit, cum a caeteris evangelistis praelatus sit.

VERS. 17.-- Et descendens, etc. Electurus apostolos Dominus in montana subiit; turbas docturus ad campestria redit, quia non nisi in humili turbae Christum videre sufficiunt, et raro invenitur quod vel turba Dominum ad altiora sequatur, vel quispiam debilis in monte curetur, sed exstincta febre libidinum et succensa luce scientiae, pedetentim quisque subiit culmen virtutum.

Maritima. Non a proximo mari Galilaeae, quia hoc non miraculi loco ponunt, sed a mari magno cognominatur. In quo etiam Tyrus et Sidon comprehendi 262Apoterant. Sed quia sunt civitates gentium, consulte nominatim ponuntur, ut quanta sit virtus Christi intimetur, quae etiam exteras civitates excitat.

VERS. 18.-- Ut audirent. Si dicamus duos esse sermones, ascendit in montem, et habuit ibi discipulos; et post, descendit et fecit istum ad turbam. Si dicimus unum fuisse, ita intelligendum est, quod ascendit in montem, et post aliquantulum descendit, et ibi fecit sermonem, ubi erat planities.

VERS. 19.-- Eum tangere. Tangit Christum qui fideliter credit in eum. Tangitur a Christo qui firmatur in illo. Qui sic tangit et tangitur, spiritus ipsius virtute sanatur.

VERS. 20.-- Et ipse elevatis, etc. Etsi generaliter omnibus loquitur, specialiter in discipulos oculos levat, 262But qui intenta cordis aure verbum Dei percipiunt, latius saporis intimi lumen accipiant, quibus os in monte sedens aperit, ut magna proferret in eos, stans in campo oculos dirigit, ut audita patienter intelligant. Beati pauperes, etc. Qui nondum consummatae virtutis arcem conscendere possunt, generalis interim perfectionis sunt beatitudine perfovendi, ut paulatim ad meliora progressi, dum Domino in planitie stante libenter auscultant, ad hunc quandoque in monte sedentem sublimiter ascendant. Nam quorum cordibus adhuc edomandis insistit, hos quasi stans affatur. Quos longo studio exercitatos et jam dociles invenit, his quasi quietus residens mystica intimat.

(BEDA.) Quod apud Matthaeum plenarie per octo 262Cbeatitudines dicitur in sermone perfectionis ad apostolos in summo montis, hoc apud Lucam, etc., usque ad sed quia fide praestitit, fide prae caeteris meruit principatum.

VERS. 21.-- Qui nunc esuritis. Qui esurit esurienti compatitur: compatiendo largitur, largiendo fit justus; aequalem se inferioribus praestat, dolum excludit, veritatem requirit. Qui nunc fletis. Qui propter divitias haereditatis Christi, propter panem vitae aeternae, propter spem coelestium gaudiorum fletus, esuriem, paupertatemque desiderat, beatus est; multo beatior, qui has virtutes inter adversa servare non trepidat.

VERS. 23.-- Gaudete et exsultate. Inter odia cordium, inter probra linguarum, inter manus persequentium, 262Dlaetiori corde versamini, intuitu supernae mercedis. Secundum haec. Exemplo prophetarum confortat illos, quia vera dicentes persecutionem solent pati.

VERS. 24.-- Verumtamen vae, etc. Matthaeus in monte, beatitudines solummodo proborum; Lucas vero in campo, etiam vae describit improborum, quia rudes adhuc auditores minis et terroribus ad bona sunt compellendi, perfectos satis est praemiis invitari. Verumtamen vae. Cum supra regnum coelorum pauperum esse dicatur: ex opposito apparet, quod ab hoc regno se alienat qui consolationem quaerit in temporalibus. Nec tam divitiae quam amor divitiarum in culpa est.

263AMystice: dives populus Judaicus, vel haereticus, vel philosophus mundi, qui, ubertate verborum et facundiae patrocinio delectati, simplicitatem verae fidei supergressi thesauros inutiles condiderunt, secundum usum saeculi de generatione Christi disputantes, qui in futuro egestatem fidei suae recognoscent, cibumque perfidiae quae in praesenti eructant, aeterno macerati jejunio causam tanti supplicii esse scient. Eritque tempus cum risus suos lugeant. Quibus bene dicitur: Vae, cum benedixerint vobis homines; quia dum hic placent potentibus, maledicto perpetuo se subdunt, quia sicut pauperes esurientes, flentes, improbitate malorum probantur, sic divitiis, epulis, risuique vacantes, mala obsequentium clientela, majorem foventur ad poenam.

263BVERS. 25.-- Qui saturati estis, etc. Qui inter divites amplius abundatis, sicut dives qui induebatur purpura, et bysso, et epulis, qui de digito Lazari quaerit guttam aquae.

Vae vobis, qui nunc ridetis, etc. Salomon: Cor sapientis ubi est tristitia, cor stultorum ubi est laetitia (Eccl. VII); docens stultitiam ridentibus, prudentiam flentibus ascribendam.

VERS. 26.-- Benedixerint vobis, etc. Magna pars poenae est peccatorum sua scelera non modo non argui, sed insuper quasi bene gesta laudari.

VERS. 27.-- Diligite inimicos. Dicto quid ab inimicis possint pati, supponit qualiter ad ipsos se debeant habere inimicos. Et hic Ecclesia non lacte imbuitur, sed validiore charitatis cibo roboratur. Non 263Csufficit non odisse, sed quod ultra humanam naturam est, praecipitur diligere. Et cum lex vicissitudinem imperet ultionis, Evangelium inimicitiis charitatem, odiis benignitatem, maledictis vota, persequentibus patientiam, esurientibus etiam gratiam remunerationis impertit.

VERS. 28.-- Orate pro calumniantibus vos. Quod prophetae videntur imprecari, non vota sunt optantium sed praenuntiationes futurorum: ipsi enim pro inimicis orabant.

VERS. 29.-- Et qui te percutit. Cum charitas patiens sit, debet patientiam verberantis sustinere, cum benigna sit, non debet respondere maledictis. Si non quaerit quae sua sunt, non debet resistere rapienti, si non aemulatur (I Cor. XIII), non debet 263Dodisse inimicum. Praebe illis, etc. Medicus animarum quos ad curandos proximos instruit, omnia quae ad salutem proximorum valere possunt tolerare praecipit. Et pertinet ad misericordiam, ut tanquam a filiis aegrotantibus et phreneticis, si salus illorum hoc exigat, multa patiantur, donec infirmitas transeat. Quid tam mirum quam percutienti maxillam praebere? Imo omnis indignantis impetus frangitur, ira sedatur; et per patientiam ille invitatur ad poenitentiam. Vestimentum. Quod de vestimento et tunica dicitur et in aliis est faciendum, quae aliquo jure temporaliter nostra esse dicimus. Si enim de necessariis hoc imperatur, multo magis superflua contemnere praecipitur.

264AVERS. 30.-- Ne repetas. Quia charitas est patiens, benigna est (I Cor. III); non tantum injurias inimici fortiter sustinet, sed etiam amici quoque gratiam benigne praevenit, ut beneficiis aliis trahantur.

VERS. 31.-- Et prout vultis. Ne videatur legem dissolvere, in beneficiis vicem servat, quam negligit in injuriis. Nec ait prout faciunt, sed prout vultis ut faciant vobis homines, et vos faciatis illis, ut vicissitudo sit cumulatior, cum votis actus aequatur. Et non tantum jam ad amicos opera charitatis extendite, sed etiam ad inimicos, ut amici esse incipiant, et prius affectum dilectionis, et postmodum opera extendite charitatis.

VERS. 32.-- Et si diligitis. Quantum vobis gradus professionis eximior, ita necesse est ut cura 264Bvirtutis sit uberior, ut etiam non amantes charitatis sinu amplectamini.

Duo sunt genera beneficii: vel cum benevole donamus quod damus, vel cum reddituro commodamus.

VERS. 35.-- Verumtamen, etc. Postquam dilectionem et mutuum beneficium peccatorum quasi infructuosa redarguit, nunc qualiter haec a fidelibus fructuose fieri debeant ostendit, quasi dicat: Quamvis peccatoribus non prosit talis dilectio et mutuum beneficium, tamen vos diligite, non tantum ductu naturae, sicut illi amicos, sed altiori gradu virtutis inimicos. Philosophi in tres partes dividunt justitiam: unam in Deum, quae pietas dicitur; alteram in parentes, vel reliquum humanum genus; tertiam 264Cin mortuos, ut his exsequiarum justa solvantur. Sed Dominus legis oraculum et prophetiae fastigium supergressus, in eos quoque qui laeserint pietatis porrexit officium.

Nihil inde sperantes: et erit. Non in hominem spem mercedis figentes. Qui sive reddat quod commodastis, reddet tamen et Deus quo jubente fecistis, sive non reddat, haereditas vestra in aeternum erit.

VERS. 36.-- Estote ergo misericordes, etc. Pluit super malos, proventus terra non negat, idem sol bonos et malos illuminat.

Mystice: populum Judaeorum propheticis pluviis irrigat, et non merentibus radius aeterni solis refulsit. Si ergo illi crediderint, estote eis misericordes, docendo, et extollendo, et nolite temere judicare, 264Dne quis sui conscius delicti, in alterum cogatur ferre sententiam.

VERS. 37.-- Nolite judicare. Sunt quaedam media et incerta quo animo fiant, quia bene et male possunt fieri. Nescimus etiam qualis futurus est, qui nunc apparet malus, de cujus correctione desperare, eumque quasi abjectum reprehendere temerarium est.

De apertis quae bono animo fieri non possunt, permittitur nobis judicare.

VERS. 38.-- Eadem quippe mensura. Generaliter de omnibus potest accipi, quae mente, manu, lingua, aguntur, quia secundum opera singulorum reddet Deus.

265AEadem quippe, (AMBR.) Non iniqua mensura rependet Deus, sed est ac si dicat: Ipsa temeritas judicandi et iniqua mensura tibi nocet, te punit, et saepe non illum de quo hoc agis. Vel si recte judicas et metiris, tibi prodest, te salvat, etsi aliquando non illum cui haec impendis.

VERS. 39.-- Dicebat. Per quam invitat ad eleemosynam dandam et ad dimittendam injuriam. Nunquid potest. Si contra violentum ira te caecaverit: si contra petentem avarus eris, nunquid vitiata mente tua, vitium ejus curare poteris? Nec solum qui tibi injuriam fecit, sed etiam tu qui ferre nescis, reus eris. Sed si improbitas illius te tranquillum invenit, et ille poenitebit, et tu de patientia coronaberis, quia caecum vidente oculo, id est, sereno corde 265Bad lumen ducere curasti.

VERS. 40.-- Sicut magister. Deus non suas ulciscitur injurias, sed tolerando persecutores mitiores reddere voluit; discipuli, qui homines sunt, hanc regulam perfectionis debent imitari.

VERS. 41.-- Quid autem vides, etc. Vere peccans peccantem castigare non valet, quia qui superbia, vel odio, vel alio vitio praeventi, levia haec vel nulla judicantes, graviter increpant illos quos a statu mentis vident, vel ira vel aliquo levi peccato perturbatos, hi tales amant magis vituperare et condemnare, quam corrigere et emendare. Non consideras. Apertus oculus male videt, scilicet jactantiam sui, dum aliis vult mederi, sed caecus se non valet intueri dum in hoc deterius cadit.

265CVERS. 42.-- Hypocrita. Si quem vis reprehendere, primum vide si similis ei sis. Quod si es, pariter ingemisce et noli eum tibi obtemperare, sed pariter conari mone. Quod si non es similis, tamen quia olim fuisti, vel esse potuisti, condescende, et non ex odio sed misericordia argue. Raro ergo non sine magna necessitate sunt objurgationes adhibenda, et nonnisi respectu Dei, remota ab oculo trabe.

Hi enim odio vel livore omnia accusare suscipiunt et volunt videri consultores, sine exemplo suae emendationis. Sed prius debent auferre trabem invidiae, vel malitiae, vel simulationis de occulto sui cordis, ut valeant ejicere festucam irae vel alicujus livoris culpae de oculo fratris.

265DVERS. 43.-- Non est arbor, etc. Arbor bona vel mala, non natura, quae a Deo in omnibus bona creata est, sed bona vel mala voluntas. Fructus, opera, quae nec bona malae voluntatis possunt esse, nec mala bonae voluntatis. Neque arbor. Si veram vis habere justitiam et non fictam, quae verbis ostendis, factis imple, ut sis bona arbor, et bonis fructibus orneris. Quia etsi fingat hypocrita, non est bonus qui facit opera mala, et si reprehendit insontem, non ideo malus est qui facit opera bona. Et nota quod de manifestis hic agitur.

VERS. 44.-- Unaquaeque enim arbor. Mali suo fructu noscuntur, dum bonos opprimunt, et Deum si non verbis, blasphemant factis: et maxime per impatientiam 266Adignoscuntur in tempore adversitatis. Jejunia enim et oratio, et hujusmodi sunt simulatis ut et bonis: sed non debent oves pelles suas deponere, etiam si aliquando lupi eis se contegant. Cognoscitur. Non a veste, sed ab operibus quae fiunt ab eis, cognoscuntur. Quia ut spina non facit uvam, sic bona arbor bonum, mala malum, et non e converso. Neque enim de spinis. Non de spinis, id est sollicitudinibus mundi, ficus, id est dulcedo resurrectionis, neque de rubo, id est punctione vitiorum, uva, id est fructus animae. Quae sicut uva proxima terrenis, corrumpitur, in superioribus maturatur. Vel de carne quae generat spinas et tribulos, non vindemiamus Christum, qui sicut uva pependit in ligno.

Spina et rubus, haereticis, a quibus nullus sapientium 266Bsanctitatem vel virtutem poterit invenire, sed conscindunt et cruentant approximantes. Vel spinae et rubi, curae saeculi et punctiones vitiorum. Uva et ficus, dulcedo novae conversationis quam Christus in nobis esurit, et fervor dilectionis qui laetificat cor hominis.

Non de spinis et rubis uva vel ficus, quia mens adhuc hominis veteris pressa consuetudine, potest quidem simulare, sed non ferre fructum novi hominis. Quod si quando facta vel dicta malorum prosunt bonis, non hoc faciunt mali, sed fit de illis consilio Dei.

VERS. 45.-- Profert bonum. Diligit inimicum. Omni petenti tribuit, et hujusmodi.

Profert malum, etc. Odit amicum, aufert aliena, 266Cnon dat sua, et hujusmodi, quae Deus judicat secundum intentionem cordis. Et vere, quia de bono vel malo corde procedit, bonus vel malus debet judicari fructus, quia ex abundantia cordis quod interius latet loquitur os, id est procedit exterius effectus, tam in verbis quam in factis. Hoc est quantum ad divinum examen cui loquitur os cordis, quia ex qua intentione verba procedunt, non ignorat. Quia etiam verba quae exterius bona videntur, quia ex mala radice procedunt, non bona esse judicat; unde subdit: Quid autem vocatis me Domine? Quasi dicat: Quid folia rectae confessionis vos germinare jactatis, qui nullos boni operis fructus ostenditis? Ex abundantia. Per oris locutionem, universa quae actu vel cogitatu de corde proferuntur, Dominus significat. 266DNam et verbum pro facto solet poni; unde: Non fuit verbum, quod non ostenderet eis (Isa. XXXIX).

VERS. 46.-- Quid autem, etc. Haec vocatio videtur fructus bonae arboris, sed quia non procedit de pinguedine charitatis, Deus non bonum judicat. Quae autem bonorum malorumve fructuum vera discretio sit, sub alia figura supponit:

VERS. 47.-- Omnis qui venit, etc. Postquam de aperte malis, de vere et simulatis bonis diu disputavit, bonos auditores verbi sibi ipsis, malos diabolo assimilat. Quia sicut Christus variis hominum personis unam Ecclesiam construit, erudit, gubernat, in vitam aeternam perducturus; sic bonus auditor variis virtutibus supernam sibi mansionem aedificat 267Ain futuro cum Christo de dedicatione laetaturus. Et sicut diabolus obedientes sibi de coelis ad terrena et ad peccata trahit, ubi non est fundamentum, quia malum est sine substantia, et semper in deteriora praecipitat: sic malus auditor, sive sit initiatus mysteriis Christi, sive ex toto alienus, quasi in puteum sine fundo praecipitatus, non invenit ubi se retineat, sed cum venerit in profundum malorum, contemnit.

VERS. 48.-- Fodit in altum. Fodit in altum qui praeceptis humilitatis terrena omnia de suorum cordibus funditus eruit, ne propter aliquod fluxibile Deo serviat, ut inconcussam in eis habeat mansionem. Posuit fundamentum. Fundamenta pluraliter, doctores; singulariter fundamentum, doctor doctorum 267BChristus.

Moraliter. Fundamenta domus, intentiones bonae conversationis, quas per humilitatem Christianam exhaustis supervacuarum cogitationum ruderibus perfectus auditor in se inserit, hoc in se spiritualiter agens, quod Christus in universa Ecclesia generaliter agit.

Inundatione. Hanc inundationem alibi portas inferi appellat. Et portae inferi non praevalebunt adversus eam (Matth. XVI). Haec inundatio tribus modis fit, quia tentatur quisque a propria concupiscentia, vel falsorum fratrum improbitate, vel aperta exteriorum impugnatione. Illisum. Potest per impetum fluminis, extremi judicii discrimen intelligi, quando Ecclesia utraque est consummata. Omnis qui se exaltat 267Chumiliabitur, et qui se humiliat exaltabitur (Luc. XIV). Domui illi. Singulae nostrae domus, quotidie, vel immundorum spirituum, vel improborum hominum, vel suae ipsius mentis, vel carnis inquietudine pulsantur. Et quantum propriis viribus fidunt, inclinantur: quantum invictissimae petrae adhaerent, labefactari nequeunt.

VERS. 49.-- Supra terram, etc. Diabolus mundum qui in maligno positus est super terram aedificat, quia ad terrenorum amorem trahit. Sine fundamento aedificat, quia peccatum in propria natura non subsistit, quia malum substantia non est, quod tamen ubicunque fit in bona natura coalescit vel sine fundamento, id est sine fundo. Sicut ergo qui in puteo mergitur, putei in fundo retinetur, sic 267Danima lapsa quasi in quodam loco fundi consistit, si in aliqua peccati mensura se retinet. Sed cum peccato in quo labitur non potest esse contenta, dum ad deteriora quotidie ducitur, quasi in puteo quo concidit fundum quo figatur non invenit. Concidit et facta est ruina, etc. Omnis conscientia, quae spe fixa in Deum non permanet, in tentationibus non valet persistere, et tanto plus agitur, quanto plus in illis quae mundi sunt a superioribus disjungitur. Et vere omnes mali vel ficte boni ingruente qualibet tentatione, pejores fiunt.

CHAPTER VI.

VERS. 1.-- And it came to pass, etc. (BEDA.) He says that the letter of the sabbath had begun to be loosed, etc., as far as after the second, from that on which the preceding things were said or done.

260BThrough the corn, etc. Mystically: the field is the world; the crop of the field, the fruitfulness of the human race; the ears, the fruits of the Church, with which the apostles are filled, feeding themselves on our progress.

The disciples plucked, etc. The disciples, not having space to eat because of the importunity of the crowds, hungered as men, but by plucking the ears they solace their want, which is a sign of a more austere life, to seek not prepared dishes but simple foods. And note that the apostles already destroy the letter of the sabbath, against the Ebionites, who receive the other apostles, but reject Paul as a transgressor of the law.

(BEDA.) Mystically: the disciples, hungering for the salvation of men, pass through the corn, that is, through the regions which, etc., as far as convicting the doctors of the law of not knowing 260Cthe law.

VERS. 2.-- They said to them, etc. Others say that these things were objected to the Lord himself. But they could have been objected by different persons both to the Lord himself and to the disciples, and to whomsoever it was objected it looks chiefly to him. On the sabbaths. The sabbaths signify keepings of feast; either those which are kept in this world by those who give themselves to the superstitions of the Jews; or those which shall be kept in the world to come, when in perpetual solemnity we shall eat the good things of the earth.

VERS. 3.-- Have you not read this, etc. (BEDA.) How is that objected to the Lord as a crime, which in a servant is not held for a crime? The true king, etc., as far as all the sons of the Church are priests, anointed with the Holy Ghost that they may offer themselves.

VERS. 4.-- He went into the house of God. Great is the grace 260Dof hospitality: with the peril of death set before it, the mind of the priest does not turn the guest away. And so we ought to take upon ourselves the perils of others. But the true priests do not shut out the true David from the hospitality of the mind, not even for the peril of death.

VERS. 6.-- A man. (BEDA.) Mystically this man signifies the human race dried up in the fruitfulness of good work, through the hand stretched out to the tree in the first parent, which the innocent hand stretched out on the cross heals. And well was the hand withered in the Synagogue, because where there is a greater gift of knowledge, there the transgressor lies under a greater fault.

VERS. 7.-- They watched. (ID.) Because he had excused, by the probable example of the master, the destruction of the sabbath which they were charging against the disciples, 261Anow, watching the master himself, they wish to calumniate him, that if he heal not, they may accuse him of cruelty or of weakness; if he heal, of transgression.

VERS. 9.-- I ask you. (BEDA.) Forestalling the calumny which they were preparing for themselves, he reproves them, etc., as far as God is not the author of evil, but those whom he does not save he is said to destroy.

VERS. 10.-- Stretch forth. (ID.) The weakness of an unfruitful soul is cured by no better order than by the bounty of alms; whence it is said: Let not thy hand be stretched out to receive, and drawn back to give (Eccles., IV). For he stretches out his hands to God in vain, being about to pray for his sins, who does not stretch these out to the poor according to his power.

VERS. 11.-- Folly. Because they were moved by envy alone, 261Bfor there was no fault either in a man's stretching out his hand, or in Jesus' healing. It was great folly to plot the death of him whose benefits they very greatly needed.

VERS. 12.-- He went out into a mountain to pray. The mountain on which he chooses and teaches the apostles signifies the height of justice, because they were to be instructed and to preach, that they might not remain among the weak, but be raised to things above. So also the law was given on a mountain. He leads the apostles up more eminently, that from the mountain the mountains may first receive the peace to be announced to the people. So also Moses, alone understanding the law spiritually, ascended the summit of the mountain on which God was.

VERS. 13.-- Disciples. Of whom he chose twelve, whom he named apostles, that is, sent in his own place; whence: As the Father hath sent me, I also send you (Joan. XX). Ascending into the mountain he calls to himself, he chooses whom he wills, 261Cbecause it is not of their zeal but of divine grace that they were called to the apostleship; whence: You have not chosen me, but I have chosen you (Ibid., XV). He chose not the wise, not the rich, not the noble, lest he should seem to have chosen them for prudence, or to have redeemed them with riches, or to have drawn any to grace by the authority of power and nobility.

VERS. 14.-- He surnamed. Not then for the first time, but long before, when he is said to have been brought by Andrew: Thou shalt be called Cephas, which is interpreted Peter. But Luke, wishing to name the names of the twelve apostles, since he had need to say Peter, wished briefly to hint that he was not so called before; but that the Lord so surnamed him, that it should not be thought done by chance, but that from the change the sacrament of the rock might be commended to the hearers.

261DVERS. 15.-- Matthew. Matthew, for humility's sake, places himself in the order beneath his fellow Thomas, though by the other evangelists he is set before him.

VERS. 17.-- And coming down, etc. The Lord, about to choose apostles, went up into the mountains; about to teach the crowds, he returns to the plains, because the crowds are not able to see Christ except in what is lowly, and rarely is it found either that a crowd follows the Lord to higher things, or that any weak man is cured on the mountain; but when the fever of lusts has been quenched and the light of knowledge kindled, each one step by step goes up to the height of the virtues.

The sea coast. Not from the neighbouring sea of Galilee, because they do not set this in the place of the miracle, but it is named from the great sea. In which Tyre and Sidon also 262Acould be comprehended. But because they are cities of the Gentiles, they are deliberately set down by name, that it may be intimated how great is the power of Christ, which stirs even foreign cities.

VERS. 18.-- That they might hear. If we say that there are two discourses, he went up into the mountain, and had his disciples there; and afterwards he came down and made this one to the crowd. If we say there was one, it must be understood thus, that he went up into the mountain, and after a little came down, and there made the discourse, where there was a level place.

VERS. 19.-- To touch him. He touches Christ who faithfully believes in him. He is touched by Christ who is made firm in him. He who so touches and is touched is healed by the power of his spirit.

VERS. 20.-- And he, lifting up, etc. Although he speaks generally to all, he lifts his eyes specially upon the disciples, 262Bthat they who receive the word of God with the intent ear of the heart may more widely receive the light of the inward savour; to whom, sitting on the mountain, he opens his mouth, that he may utter great things upon them; standing on the plain he directs his eyes, that they may patiently understand what they hear. Blessed are the poor, etc. They who cannot yet climb the citadel of consummated virtue are meanwhile to be cherished with the beatitude of a general perfection, that little by little advancing to better things, while they gladly listen to the Lord standing on the plain, they may at some time loftily ascend to him sitting on the mountain. For those upon whose hearts he still presses to tame them, these he addresses as it were standing. Those whom he finds exercised by long endeavour and now teachable, to these he imparts mystical things as one resting at ease.

(BEDA.) What in Matthew is fully said by eight 262Cbeatitudes in the sermon of perfection to the apostles on the top of the mountain, this in Luke, etc., as far as but because he excelled in faith, by faith he deserved the primacy before the rest.

VERS. 21.-- You that now hunger. He who hungers has compassion on him that hungers: by compassion he gives freely, by giving freely he becomes just; he makes himself equal to his inferiors, he shuts out guile, he seeks the truth. You that now weep. He who for the riches of Christ's inheritance, for the bread of eternal life, for the hope of heavenly joys, desires weeping, hunger and poverty, is blessed; much more blessed he who does not fear to keep these virtues amid adversities.

VERS. 23.-- Rejoice and be glad. Amid the hatreds of hearts, amid the reproaches of tongues, amid the hands of persecutors, 262Dbe conversant with a gladder heart, in view of the reward on high. For according to these things. By the example of the prophets he strengthens them, because those who speak the truth are wont to suffer persecution.

VERS. 24.-- But woe, etc. Matthew on the mountain describes only the beatitudes of the upright; but Luke on the plain describes also the woe of the wicked, because hearers still untrained are to be driven to good things by threats and terrors, while for the perfect it is enough to be invited by rewards. But woe. Since above the kingdom of heaven is said to be the poor's, it appears by the contrary that he estranges himself from this kingdom who seeks his consolation in temporal things. Nor are riches so much in fault as the love of riches.

263AMystically: the rich man is the Jewish people, or the heretic, or the philosopher of the world, who, delighted with the abundance of words and the patronage of eloquence, having gone beyond the simplicity of the true faith, have laid up useless treasures, disputing according to the usage of the world about the generation of Christ; who in the world to come shall recognize the neediness of their faith, and, wasted with eternal fasting, shall know that the food of faithlessness which they belch forth in the present is the cause of so great a punishment. And there shall be a time when they shall bewail their laughter. To whom it is well said: Woe, when men shall bless you; because while here they please the powerful, they subject themselves to a perpetual curse, because as the poor, the hungry, the weeping are proved by the wickedness of the evil, so those given to riches, to feasts and to laughter, by the evil retinue of those who fawn upon them, are cherished unto a greater punishment.

263BVERS. 25.-- You that are filled, etc. You who among the rich abound the more, like the rich man who was clothed in purple and fine linen and with feasts, who seeks a drop of water from Lazarus' finger.

Woe to you that now laugh, etc. Solomon: The heart of the wise man is where there is sadness, the heart of fools where there is gladness (Eccl. VII); teaching that folly is to be ascribed to those who laugh, prudence to those who weep.

VERS. 26.-- Shall bless you, etc. A great part of the punishment of sinners is that their crimes are not only not reproved, but are moreover praised as though well done.

VERS. 27.-- Love your enemies. When it has been said what they can suffer from enemies, he adds how they ought to bear themselves toward those enemies. And here the Church is not fed with milk, but strengthened with the stronger food of charity. It is not 263Cenough not to hate, but what is beyond human nature is commanded, to love. And whereas the law commands a return of vengeance, the Gospel bestows charity for enmities, kindness for hatreds, prayers for curses, patience for persecutors, and even the grace of recompense for those who hunger.

VERS. 28.-- Pray for them that calumniate you. That the prophets seem to imprecate, these are not the wishes of men desiring it, but foretellings of things to come: for they themselves prayed for their enemies.

VERS. 29.-- And to him that striketh thee. Since charity is patient, it ought to sustain the patient bearing of him that beats; and since it is kind, it ought not to answer curses. If it seeketh not her own, it ought not to resist him that plunders; if it envieth not (I Cor. XIII), it ought not 263Dto hate an enemy. Offer them, etc. The physician of souls commands those whom he instructs to heal their neighbours to bear all things that can avail for the salvation of their neighbours. And it pertains to mercy that, as from sick and frenzied sons, they should suffer many things, if their health require it, until the infirmity pass. What is so wonderful as to offer the cheek to him that strikes? Nay, every assault of the angry man is broken, wrath is calmed; and through patience he is invited to repentance. A garment. What is said of the garment and the coat is to be done also in other things which by any right we call temporally our own. For if this is commanded concerning necessary things, much more is it enjoined to despise superfluous things.

264AVERS. 30.-- Ask not again. Because charity is patient, is kind (I Cor. III); it not only bears bravely the injuries of an enemy, but also kindly anticipates the favour of a friend, that they may be drawn by other kindnesses.

VERS. 31.-- And as you would. Lest he should seem to dissolve the law, he keeps in benefits the return which he neglects in injuries. Nor does he say, as they do, but as you would that men should do to you, that you also should do to them, that the return may be the fuller, since the act is matched with the wish. And now extend the works of charity not only to friends, but even to enemies, that they may begin to be friends; and first extend the affection of love, and afterwards the works of charity.

VERS. 32.-- And if you love. The more eminent your degree of profession, the more needful it is that the care 264Bof virtue be more abundant, that you embrace in the bosom of charity even those who do not love.

There are two kinds of benefit: either when we give kindly what we give, or when we lend to one who will repay.

VERS. 35.-- But yet, etc. After he has reproved as unfruitful the love and mutual benefit of sinners, he now shows how these are to be done fruitfully by the faithful, as if he should say: Although such love and mutual benefit profit not sinners, yet do you love, not only by the leading of nature, as they love friends, but by a higher degree of virtue, your enemies. The philosophers divide justice into three parts: one toward God, which is called piety; another toward parents, or the rest of the human race; the third 264Ctoward the dead, that the due rites of burial be paid to them. But the Lord, having gone beyond the oracle of the law and the summit of prophecy, extended the office of piety even to those who have done hurt.

Hoping for nothing thereby: and it shall be. Not fixing the hope of a reward in man. For whether he repay what you have lent, God will nevertheless repay also, at whose bidding you did it; or whether he repay not, your inheritance shall be for ever.

VERS. 36.-- Be ye therefore merciful, etc. He rains upon the evil, the earth does not deny its produce, the same sun enlightens the good and the evil.

Mystically: he waters the people of the Jews with prophetic rains, and upon those not deserving it the ray of the eternal sun has shone. If therefore they have believed, be merciful to them, by teaching and by lifting them up, and do not rashly judge, 264Dlest anyone conscious of his own offence be compelled to pass sentence upon another.

VERS. 37.-- Judge not. There are certain middle and uncertain things, in what mind they are done, because they can be done well and ill. We know not either what he will be like who now appears evil, of whose correction to despair, and to reprove him as though cast off, is rash.

Concerning open things which cannot be done with a good mind, it is permitted us to judge.

VERS. 38.-- For with the same measure. It can be taken generally of all things which are done by mind, by hand, by tongue, because God will render according to the works of each.

265AFor with the same, (AMBR.) God will not repay with an unjust measure, but it is as if he should say: The very rashness of judging and the unjust measure hurts thee, punishes thee, and often not him about whom thou doest this. Or if thou judgest and measurest rightly, it profits thee, it saves thee, even though sometimes not him upon whom thou bestowest these things.

VERS. 39.-- He spoke. By which he invites to the giving of alms and to the forgiving of injury. Can he. If anger has blinded thee against a violent man: if thou shalt be covetous against him that asks, canst thou, with thy mind vitiated, heal his vice? And not only he who has done thee injury, but thou also who knowest not how to bear it, shalt be guilty. But if his wickedness finds thee tranquil, both he shall repent, and thou shalt be crowned for thy patience, because thou hast taken care to lead the blind man with a seeing eye, that is, with a serene heart, 265Bto the light.

VERS. 40.-- As the master. God does not avenge his own injuries, but by bearing with persecutors he willed to make them milder; the disciples, who are men, ought to imitate this rule of perfection.

VERS. 41.-- And why seest thou, etc. Truly a sinner is not able to chastise a sinner, because they who, forestalled by pride, or by hatred, or by some other vice, judging these things light or nothing, gravely rebuke those whom they see disturbed from the state of the mind, either by anger or by some slight sin, such men as these love rather to blame and to condemn than to correct and to amend. Thou considerest not. The open eye sees ill, namely its own boasting, while it wishes to heal others, but being blind it is not able to look upon itself, while in this it falls the worse.

265CVERS. 42.-- Hypocrite. If thou wilt reprove anyone, first see whether thou art like him. And if thou art, groan alike with him, and be not willing that he should obey thee, but admonish him to strive alike. But if thou art not like him, yet because thou once wast, or couldst have been, condescend, and reprove not out of hatred but out of mercy. Rarely therefore, and not without great necessity, are rebukes to be applied, and not save out of regard for God, when the beam has been removed from the eye.

For these men undertake to accuse all things out of hatred or envy, and wish to seem counsellors, without the example of their own amendment. But they ought first to take away the beam of envy, or of malice, or of dissimulation, from the hidden place of their heart, that they may be able to cast out the mote of anger, or of any fault of envy, from their brother's eye.

265DVERS. 43.-- There is no tree, etc. A tree good or bad is not nature, which was created good by God in all things, but a good or a bad will. The fruits are the works, which can neither be good of a bad will, nor bad of a good will. Nor a tree. If thou wilt have true justice and not feigned, fulfil in deeds what thou showest in words, that thou mayest be a good tree, and be adorned with good fruits. Because although the hypocrite feign, he is not good who does evil works; and if he reprove the guiltless, he is not therefore evil who does good works. And note that here the discussion is of manifest things.

VERS. 44.-- For every tree. The evil are known by their own fruit, while they oppress the good, and blaspheme God by their deeds if not by their words: and chiefly they are discerned by their impatience 266Ain the time of adversity. For fasts and prayer and things of this kind belong to the feigning as also to the good: but the sheep ought not to lay aside their own fleeces, even if sometimes wolves cover themselves with them. Is known. Not by the garment, but by the works which are done by them, are they known. Because as the thorn does not make a grape, so a good tree makes what is good, a bad tree what is bad, and not the contrary. For neither of thorns. Not of thorns, that is, of the cares of the world, figs, that is, the sweetness of the resurrection, nor of the bramble, that is, of the pricking of vices, a grape, that is, the fruit of the soul. Which, as a grape near to earthly things is corrupted, in higher places ripens. Or from the flesh, which brings forth thorns and thistles, we do not gather Christ, who hung upon the tree like a grape.

The thorn and the bramble, to the heretics, from whom 266Bno wise man will be able to find holiness or virtue, but they tear and bloody those that draw near. Or the thorns and brambles are the cares of the world and the prickings of vices. The grape and the fig, the sweetness of the new manner of life which Christ hungers for in us, and the fervour of love which gladdens the heart of man.

Not of thorns and brambles a grape or a fig, because the mind still pressed by the custom of the old man can indeed feign, but cannot bear the fruit of the new man. But if at any time the deeds or words of the evil profit the good, the evil do not do this, but it comes of them by the counsel of God.

VERS. 45.-- Bringeth forth good. He loves his enemy. He gives to everyone that asks, and things of this kind.

Bringeth forth evil, etc. He hates his friend, he takes away what is another's, 266Che does not give his own, and things of this kind, which God judges according to the intention of the heart. And truly, because it proceeds from a good or a bad heart, the fruit ought to be judged good or bad, because out of the abundance of the heart the mouth speaks what lies hidden within, that is, the effect proceeds outwardly, as well in words as in deeds. This is as regards the divine examination, to which the mouth of the heart speaks, because he is not ignorant from what intention words proceed. Because even the words which outwardly seem good, because they proceed from an evil root, he judges not to be good; whence he subjoins: And why call you me Lord? As if he should say: Why do you boast that you put forth the leaves of a right confession, who show no fruits of good work? Out of the abundance. By the speech of the mouth the Lord signifies all the things which are brought forth from the heart by act or by thought. 266DFor a word is wont to be put for a deed; whence: There was no word which he did not show them (Isa. XXXIX).

VERS. 46.-- And why, etc. This calling seems the fruit of a good tree, but because it does not proceed from the fatness of charity, God judges it not good. But what the true discernment of good or of evil fruits may be, he sets forth under another figure:

VERS. 47.-- Everyone that cometh, etc. After he has long disputed of the openly evil, and of the truly and the feignedly good, he likens good hearers of the word to himself, the evil to the devil. Because as Christ builds, instructs and governs one Church out of the various persons of men, being about to lead it into life eternal; so the good hearer builds for himself by various virtues a dwelling on high, 267Abeing about to rejoice in the world to come with Christ at its dedication. And as the devil draws those who obey him from the heavens to earthly things and to sins, where there is no foundation, because evil is without substance, and always casts them down into worse things: so the evil hearer, whether he be initiated in the mysteries of Christ, or wholly a stranger, cast down as it were into a well without a bottom, finds not where to hold himself, but when he has come into the depth of evils, he despises.

VERS. 48.-- He digged deep. He digs deep who by the precepts of humility roots out utterly from the hearts of his own all earthly things, that he may not serve God for the sake of anything fleeting, that he may have in them an unshaken dwelling. He laid a foundation. Foundations in the plural, the doctors; in the singular, the foundation, the doctor of doctors 267BChrist.

Morally. The foundations of the house are the intentions of a good manner of life, which through Christian humility, the rubbish of superfluous thoughts having been drawn off, the perfect hearer sets within himself, doing spiritually in himself what Christ does generally in the whole Church.

A flood. This flood he elsewhere calls the gates of hell. And the gates of hell shall not prevail against it (Matth. XVI). This flood happens in three ways, because each man is tempted by his own concupiscence, or by the wickedness of false brethren, or by the open assault of those without. Beaten upon. By the rush of the river can be understood the crisis of the last judgment, when each Church has been consummated. Everyone that exalteth himself 267Cshall be humbled, and he that humbleth himself shall be exalted (Luc. XIV). That house. Our several houses are daily beaten by the unquiet of unclean spirits, or of wicked men, or of one's own mind, or of the flesh. And in so far as they trust in their own strength, they are bowed down: in so far as they cleave to the most unconquered rock, they cannot be shaken.

VERS. 49.-- Upon the earth, etc. The devil builds upon the earth the world which is set in wickedness, because he draws it to the love of earthly things. He builds without a foundation, because sin does not subsist in its own nature, since evil is not a substance, which nevertheless, wherever it happens, grows together in a good nature, or without a foundation, that is, without a bottom. As therefore he who is plunged into a well is held at the bottom of the well, so 267Dthe fallen soul stands as it were in a certain place of the bottom, if it holds itself within some measure of sin. But since it cannot be content with the sin into which it falls, while it is daily led to worse things, as in a well into which it has fallen it does not find a bottom whereon it may be fixed. It fell down, and there was made a ruin, etc. [Vulg. Lc 6:49 reads et continuo cecidit: et facta est ruina domus illius magna, "and immediately it fell, and the ruin of that house was great."] Every conscience which does not remain with its hope fixed in God is not able to persist in temptations, and is the more driven the more it is disjoined from things above in those things which are of the world. And truly all the evil, or the feignedly good, become worse when any temptation presses in.

CAPUT VII.

VERS. 1.-- Cum autem implesset. Pulchre ubi praecepta 268Acomplevit, formam docet suorum exsequendi praeceptorum. Nam statim gentilis centurionis servus Domino sanandus offertur, in quo gentiles qui mundana servitute aegri tenebantur, beneficio Dei salvandi exprimuntur. Intravit Capharnaum. Centurionis, etc. Non ante intravit quam verba terminasset, etsi statim post haec verba non intravit. Priusquam enim intraret, in ipso intervallo mundatus est leprosus, ut Matthaeus ponit.

VERS. 3.-- Misit ad eum seniores, etc. Divina providentia seniores Judaeorum mittuntur, et eis praesentibus languidus sanatur, ut inexcusabiles sint si credere noluerint.

Centurio amicos mittit, ne praesentia sua verecundiam Domini gravare videretur, et officium officio 268Bprovocasse. Significat autem, quia nos qui de gentibus credimus ad Dominum, non ipsi venire possumus quem in carne videre non possumus, sed ad residentem in dextera Patris. Gentilis populus quasi centurio stipatur milite virtutum et spiritualium perfectione, quae in centum significatur. Sublimis, nihil terrenum a Domino sibi suisque requirens, sed sola aeternae salutis gaudia: et hi pro servis, id est, pro his qui adhuc spiritu servitutis in timore premuntur, Domino supplicant, ut eis paulatim ad superiora provectis, perfecta dilectio foras mittat timorem (I Joan. IV).

Matthaeus dicit centurionem accessisse, quod per fidem intelligendum est. Per fidem enim vere ad Deum acceditur, quae in centurione a Domino commendatur: 268CNon inveni tantam fidem in Israel. Porro Lucas ordine quo gestum est exsequitur, ut intelligere cogeremur, quomodo eum accessisse dicit Matthaeus.

VERS. 5.-- Gentem nostram. Mystice. Gentilis populus (cujus figuram gestat centurio) non longe habet a domo Jesum, tametsi sub tecto invitare non audet, quia, prope timentes eum salutare ipsius. Qui naturali lege recte utitur, ut bona quae novit operatur, eo illi qui vere bonus est appropiat. At illis quos errore gentilitatis crimina vinxerant, aptari potest, quod alibi dicitur: Quidam enim ex his de longe venerunt (Marc. VIII).

VERS. 6.-- Jesus autem ibat. Solo verbo potenter curaturus, ad humilitatis commendationem visitare 268Ddignatur languentem; et qui ad sanandum reguli filium ire noluit, ne divitias honorasse videretur, ad servum vadit, ne servilem conditionem sprevisse putaretur. Elucet fides in operibus dum sanat, sed plus operatur humilitas in affectibus dum vadit. Non sum dignus. Propter vitae gentilis conscientiam, gravari se magis dignatione putavit Domini quam juvari, nec posse habere hospitem Christum, cujus etsi fide praeditus, nondum tamen sacramentis imbutus. Sed quia quod infirmitas humana non praesumit, divina gratia dare novit in figuram gentilis populi, et suam fidem nondum catechizatus a Domino laudari et famulum sanari promeruit.

VERS. 8.-- Nam et ego, etc. Si ego homo sub alio 269Apossum imperare minoribus, quanto magis tu, qui super omnia habens famulantes angelos, potes per eorum ministeria sine corporis praesentia dicere infirmitati ut recedat, et recedet, et sanitati ut veniat, et veniet.

VERS. 9.-- Nec in Israel. Fides istius etiam electioribus et Deum videntibus antefertur. Etiam in isto uno fides gentium praefertur Israeli.

VERS. 10.-- Et reversi sunt. Matthaeus planius explicat, quod dicente Domino: Sicut credidisti fiat tibi, sanatus sit puer (Matth. VIII). Sed mos est beato Lucae ab aliis plane exposita breviare, vel etiam de industria praeterire quae breviter dicta ab aliis, vel omissa solertius dilucidare. Invenerunt servum. Credente Domino sanatur servus, quia potest meritum Domini 269Betiam servis suffragari, non tantum fidei merito, sed etiam studio disciplinae.

VERS. 11.-- Et factum est, etc. Post sanatum infirmum suscitat mortuum, quia infirmitas praecedit mortem.

Quae vocatur Naim. Naim est civitas Galilaeae in secundo milliario a Thabor contra meridiem juxta Endor, qui est vicus grandis. Naim autem interpretatur fluctus vel commotio.

VERS. 12.-- Cum autem appropinquaret, etc. (BEDA.) Cum Verbum caro factum, gentilem populum per portas fidei, etc., usque ad quod et interim in paucis Judaeorum conversis, et tandem in plenitudine imperat.

269CEcce defunctus efferebatur, etc. Defunctus, qui coram multis extra portam effertur, significat criminaliter peccantem, et peccatum non cordis cubili tegentem, sed indicio operis vel locutionis, quasi per ostia suae civitatis aliis propalantem. Quem sicut unicum deflet mater Ecclesia, quae licet ex multis collecta personis, una est tamen virgo mater Ecclesia, singuli autem filii. Porta qua effertur, aliquis est de sensibus quo aliquis in peccatum corruit. Ut qui videt ad concupiscendum, qui autem otiosis vel turpibus audiendis, qui linguam commodat litigiis.

Filius unicus matri suae. (AMBR.) Adam de terra portatur a quatuor elementis, unde homo constat. Tangitur loculus, etc., usque ad quando avaritiam 269Dcogitat, vel quando pro nimia febre tangi non potest.

VERS. 13.-- Et turba. Multi cum Domino, multi cum vidua, ut viso miraculo multi Dei laudatores et testes fiant.

VERS. 15.-- Et resedit. Tres mortuos suscitavit Dominus: filiam archisynagogi in domo, id est, cogitatione; filium unicum matris in porta, id est in verbo; Lazarum in monumento, id est in opere.

VERS. 16.-- Visitavit. Dum et Verbum semel incorporari constituit, et quotidie Spiritum sanctum in corda hominum, ut suscitentur mittit.

VERS. 18.-- Et nuntiaverunt. (AMBR.) Non simplici corde, sed invidia stimulante, etc., usque ad 270Aet correcti credant in Jesum et magistro interrogante sibi dicant: Tu es qui venturus es.

(BED., GREG.) Non ait, qui venisti, etc., usque ad quia lapsus amoris fidem non impedit, talis enim lapsus est religiosus.

VERS. 22.-- Et respondens dixit illis: Euntes renuntiate Joanni quae vidistis, etc. Sciens Jesus neminem sine Evangelio plene posse credere, quia sicut fides a Veteri incipit Testamento, ita impletur in Novo. Interrogatus de se, non verbo aliquo, sed factis se esse signavit, non respondens ad ea quae interrogabantur, sed ad scandalum nuntiorum.

VERS. 23.-- Beatus. Quasi dicat: Mira quidem facio, sed abjecta pati non dedignor, et cavendum est hominibus ne in me despiciant mortem, qui 270Bsigna venerantur.

Qui non. (GREG.) Qui de potentia divinitatis non dubitaverit. Hoc non contra Joannem, quem mox adeo commendat, sed non credentes a perfidia revocat, et Joanni exponit quod quaerebat, quia Deus salvos faciendi, et Domini Domini exitus mortis (Psal. LXVII). Visis tot signis et virtutibus, fidelis non potuit de morte ejus dubitare, sed admirari, sed infidelium mens scandalum in illo pertulit, cum et post miracula morientem vidit.

VERS. 24.-- Quid. Ubi monuit discipulos Joannis in crucem Dominicam esse credendum: conversus ad turbas, ad virtutem pauperes provocat, ne exaltati corde, mente instabiles, consilio infirmi, speciosa utilibus aeternis caduca praeferrent, sed crucem potius 270Cquam mundi phaleras tollant. Et ideo laudat personam Joannis, qui posthabito amore vitae etiam justitiae formam nec mortis timore mutavit. Deserto mundus comparatur, quia adhuc incultus et sterilis in quem non est ita prodeundum, ut homines mente carnis inflatos et virtutis vacuos, et de fragilis mundi gloria jactantes, putemus esse imitandos.

Arundinem vento. (BED.) Per arundinem carnalis animus, qui cum favore vel detractione tangitur, in quamlibet partem inclinatur. Sed Joannes non est arundo vento agitata, quia illum nec gratia blandum nec cujuslibet ira faciebat asperum, nec prospera erigebant, nec adversa inclinabant.

VERS. 25.-- Mollibus vestimentis indutum. Per 270Dvestem, corpus humanum intelligitur. Prophetico ergo exemplo ad virtutem subeundae virtutis hortatur. Ecce quae in veste pretiosa. Quibus fluida divitiis membra solvuntur, exteros coelestis regni, sub jure daemonum (qui sunt reges tenebrarum) intra habitacula hujus mundi consenescunt.

VERS. 26.-- Plusquam prophetam. Quia angelus: non natura, sed officio, qui supernum judicem nuntiare mittitur.

VERS. 28.-- Dico autem. In hoc non praefertur patriarchis et cunctis hominibus, sed aequalis caeteris sanctis ostenditur. Qui autem. Omnis sanctus qui jam est cum Deo, major est eo qui adhuc est mundo. Vel, ego quem minorem facit multorum opinio, 271Ain Ecclesia sanctorum illi praelatus sum. Vel novissimus angelus coeli melior est quovis homine.

VERS. 29.-- Et omnis populus. Si hoc a Domino dictum esse intelligitur: audiens Joannem, populus intelligitur esse designatus. Si ab evangelista dicitur interpositum: audiens ipsum Dominum, de Joannis magnitudine disputantem, restat intelligi.

VERS. 31.-- Cui ergo, etc. Laudato Joanne, transit ad increpandum illos qui nec praedicatione Joannis moti sunt, vel ipsius Christi praedicationem despiciunt.

VERS. 32.-- Similes sunt pueris. Pueri, doctores sunt spiritu humiles. Forum, synagoga, vel ipsa Jerusalem, in qua jura praeceptorum Dei condebantur, quia contribulibus solebant exprobrare quod nec 271Bpsalmis allecti ad laudes Dei assurrexerunt corde devoto, membrorum agilitate (quod notat saltus) nec threnis (id est lamentationibus prophetarum pro excidiis jam factis vel faciendis) ad poenitentiam sunt conversi.

VERS. 34.-- Venit Filius hominis manducans et bibens. Venit sicut tunc, ita et nunc, utramque viam salutis respuitis. Lamentavimus ad Joannem, cantavimus ad Christum.

VERS. 35.-- Justificata est sapientia. Ostendit filios sapientiae intelligere, nec in abstinendo, nec in manducando esse justitiam, sed in aequanimitate tolerandi inopiam et temperantiam, non se corrumpendi per abundantiam. Non est regnum Dei esca et potus (Rom. XIV). Quorum non usus, sed concupiscentia 271Creprehenditur. Justificata, etc. Quidquid me putetis, ego tamen Dei virtus et Dei sapientia juste facere intelligor ab apostolis, quibus Pater revelavit quae prudentibus abscondit.

VERS. 36.-- Rogabat. Sicut proposuerat verbis, ita factis etiam astruit justificatam sapientiam ab omnibus filiis, quia etiam a Maria poenitente post culpam.

VERS. 37.-- In domo. In cujuscunque domo interiore cognoveris sapientiam et justitiam recumbentem, recurre ad pedes, id est, ad extremam sapientiae partem, inquire lacrymis, confitere peccata, expande capillos, id est, sterne ante eum cunctas corporis tui dignitates, osculare, id est, nihil nisi sapientiam loqueris. Attulit. Quot habuit in se oblectamenta, 271Dtot de se invenit holocausta.

(HIER.) Alabastrum unguenti, id est, corpus cum fide, etc., usque ad id est, peccata Judaeorum.

VERS. 38.-- Coepit rigare. Maria soror Lazari bis eodem functa est officio: semel in Galilaea cum primo accedit cum humilitate et lacrymis, ubi remissionem peccatorum accepit. Secundo, in Bethania, non jam peccatrix nominata, sed casta, et ideo devota: ibi pedes, hic caput inungit.

VERS. 43.-- Is cui plus. Secundum homines plus fortasse offendit, cui plus debuerit. Sed per misericordiam Domini causa mutatur, ut amplius diligat qui amplius debuit, tamen gratiam consequatur. Recte judicasti. Sua sententia Pharisaeus convincitur, 272Aquia sicut phreneticus funem portat ex quo ligetur.

VERS. 44.-- Intravi in domum. Intravi carne assumpta in populum Judaeorum.

VERS. 46.-- Oleo caput meum non unxisti. Et si populus ille in Deum se credere et non in hominem fatebatur, tamen ipsam potentiam divinitatis quae per miracula apparebat, digna laude praedicare neglexit. Sed gentilitas dum mysterium incarnationis credit, summa laude etiam infirma ejus praedicat.

VERS. 47.-- Remittuntur ei peccata. In domo Pharisaei, id est, in custodia legis et prophetarum, non Pharisaeus incredulus, sed peccatrix poenitens glorificatur, quia ardor charitatis in ea rubiginem 272Bdelictorum combussit.

VERS. 49.-- Et coeperunt qui simul. Sanata aegra, de salute ejus aliis aegrotant.

VERS. 50.-- Fides tua te salvam fecit. Fides salvam fecit, quia quod petiit, posse accipere non dubitavit, quia jam spem ab illo acceperat, a quo salutem quaerebat.

CHAPTER VII.

VERS. 1.-- When he had fulfilled. Beautifully, where he has completed his precepts, 268Ahe teaches the form of carrying out his precepts. For at once the servant of a Gentile centurion is offered to the Lord to be healed, in whom the Gentiles, who were held sick in worldly servitude, are set forth as those to be saved by the benefit of God. He entered into Capharnaum. Of a centurion, etc. He did not enter before he had brought his words to an end, although he did not enter immediately after these words. For before he entered, in that very interval the leper was cleansed, as Matthew sets it down.

VERS. 3.-- He sent to him the elders, etc. By divine providence the elders of the Jews are sent, and in their presence the sick man is healed, that they may be without excuse if they have refused to believe.
