The centurion sends friends, lest by his own presence he should seem to burden the Lord's modesty, and to have challenged 268Bservice with service. But it signifies that we who from among the Gentiles believe in the Lord cannot ourselves come to him whom we cannot see in the flesh, but to him who sits at the Father's right hand. The Gentile people, like a centurion, is thronged about with the soldiery of the virtues and with the perfection of spiritual things, which is signified in the hundred. Lofty, seeking nothing earthly from the Lord for himself and for his own, but only the joys of eternal salvation: and these make supplication to the Lord for servants, that is, for those who are still pressed down in fear by the spirit of servitude, that, as they are little by little advanced to higher things, perfect love may cast out fear (I Joan. IV).

Matthew says that the centurion came near, which is to be understood of faith. For by faith one truly draws near to God, and that faith is commended in the centurion by the Lord: 268CI have not found so great faith in Israel. But Luke follows out the order in which it was done, that we might be compelled to understand in what manner Matthew says he came near.

VERS. 5.-- Our nation. Mystically. The Gentile people (whose figure the centurion bears) has Jesus not far from the house, although it does not dare to invite him under the roof, because, near to them that fear him is his salvation. He who rightly uses the natural law, so as to work the good things he knows, draws near thereby to him who is truly good. But to those whom the crimes of Gentile error had bound, that can be fitted which is said elsewhere: for some of these came from afar (Marc. VIII).

VERS. 6.-- And Jesus went. Being about to heal powerfully by his word alone, he deigns, for the commendation of humility, to visit 268Dthe sick man; and he who would not go to heal the ruler's son, lest he should seem to have honoured riches, goes to a servant, lest he should be thought to have despised the servile condition. Faith shines out in his works while he heals, but humility works more in the affections while he goes. I am not worthy. Because of the conscience of his Gentile life, he thought himself to be burdened rather than helped by the Lord's condescension, and that he could not have Christ as a guest, since, although endowed with faith, he was nevertheless not yet steeped in the sacraments. But because divine grace knows how to give what human weakness does not presume, as a figure of the Gentile people he deserved, not yet catechized, both that his faith should be praised by the Lord and that his servant should be healed.

VERS. 8.-- For I also, etc. If I, a man set under another, 269Acan command those beneath me, how much more thou, who, having angels serving thee over all things, canst through their ministries, without the presence of thy body, say to sickness that it depart, and it will depart, and to health that it come, and it will come.

VERS. 9.-- Not even in Israel. This man's faith is set even before the more chosen and those who see God. Even in this one man the faith of the Gentiles is preferred to Israel.

VERS. 10.-- And they returned. Matthew explains it more plainly, that when the Lord said: As thou hast believed, so be it done to thee; let the boy be healed (Matth. VIII) [Vulg. Matt 8:13 "Et sanatus est puer in illa hora," and the boy was healed in that hour; the printed reading has the subjunctive]. But it is the custom of blessed Luke to abridge what has been plainly set out by others, or even of set purpose to pass over what has been briefly said by others, or to elucidate more skilfully what has been omitted. They found the servant. The master believing, the servant is healed, because the merit of the master 269Bcan avail even for servants, not only by the merit of faith, but also by zeal for discipline.

VERS. 11.-- And it came to pass, etc. After the sick man healed he raises the dead, because sickness goes before death.

Which is called Naim. Naim is a city of Galilee at the second milestone from Thabor toward the south, next to Endor, which is a large village. And Naim is interpreted a wave or a commotion.

VERS. 12.-- And when he drew near, etc. (BEDA.) When the Word made flesh, the Gentile people through the gates of faith, etc., as far as which he commands both meanwhile in the few of the Jews converted, and at last in the fulness.

269CBehold a dead man was carried out, etc. The dead man, who is carried out before many outside the gate, signifies him who sins criminally, and who does not cover his sin in the chamber of the heart, but by the evidence of deed or of speech publishes it abroad to others, as it were through the doors of his own city. Whom mother Church bewails as an only son, for although she is gathered out of many persons, yet mother Church is one virgin, while the sons are single. The gate by which he is carried out is some one of the senses, by which a man falls into sin. As he who looks in order to lust, and he who [lends himself] to hearing idle or filthy things, and he who lends his tongue to quarrels.

The only son to his mother. (AMBR.) Adam is carried from the earth by the four elements, of which man consists. The bier is touched, etc., as far as when he thinks upon avarice, or when for excess of fever 269Dhe cannot be touched.

VERS. 13.-- And a crowd. Many with the Lord, many with the widow, so that, the miracle being seen, many may become praisers and witnesses of God.

VERS. 15.-- And he sat up. The Lord raised three dead: the daughter of the ruler of the synagogue in the house, that is, in thought; the only son of his mother at the gate, that is, in word; Lazarus in the tomb, that is, in deed.

VERS. 16.-- He hath visited. Since he both appointed the Word to be embodied once, and daily sends the Holy Spirit into the hearts of men, that they may be raised up.

VERS. 18.-- And they told. (AMBR.) Not with a simple heart, but at the prompting of envy, etc., as far as 270Aand, being corrected, they may believe in Jesus and, at their master's questioning, say for themselves: Thou art he that is to come.

(BED., GREG.) He does not say, who hast come, etc., as far as because a lapse of love does not hinder faith, for such a lapse is religious.

VERS. 22.-- And answering he said to them: Go and relate to John what you have seen, etc. [Vulg. Luke 7:22 "quae audistis et vidistis," what you have heard and seen] Jesus, knowing that no one can fully believe without the Gospel, because as faith begins from the Old Testament, so it is fulfilled in the New. Being questioned about himself, he signified what he was, not by any word, but by his deeds, answering not to the things about which he was questioned, but to the scandal of the messengers.

VERS. 23.-- Blessed. As if he should say: I do indeed marvellous things, but I do not disdain to suffer abject ones, and men must beware lest they despise death in me, who 270Bvenerate the signs.

He that shall not. (GREG.) He who shall not have doubted of the power of the godhead. This is not against John, whom he presently commends so highly, but he calls back from faithlessness those who believe not, and he sets forth to John what he was asking, because God of saving; and of the Lord, of the Lord are the issues from death (Psal. LXVII). So many signs and mighty works having been seen, the faithful man could not doubt concerning his death, but only wonder; but the mind of the unbelieving bore a scandal in him, when it saw him dying even after the miracles.

VERS. 24.-- What. When he had admonished John's disciples that they must believe in the Lord's cross, turning to the crowds he summons the poor to virtue, lest, lifted up in heart, unstable in mind, weak in counsel, they should prefer specious and fleeting things to things eternally useful, but that they should rather take up the cross 270Cthan the world's trappings. And therefore he praises the person of John, who, setting aside the love of life, did not change the form even of justice through fear of death. The world is compared to a desert, because it is still untilled and barren, into which one must not so go forth as to think that men puffed up with the mind of the flesh and empty of virtue, and boasting of the glory of a frail world, are to be imitated.

A reed by the wind. (BED.) By the reed is meant the carnal mind, which, when it is touched by favour or by detraction, is bent to whatever side. But John is not a reed shaken by the wind, because neither did favour make him soft nor any man's anger make him harsh, nor did prosperity lift him up, nor did adversity bow him down.

VERS. 25.-- Clothed in soft garments. By the 270Dgarment the human body is understood. Therefore by the prophetic example he exhorts to the virtue of a virtue to be undergone. Behold they who are in costly apparel. Those whose limbs are loosed and made flabby by riches, strangers to the heavenly kingdom, grow old under the sway of demons (who are the kings of darkness) within the dwellings of this world.

VERS. 26.-- More than a prophet. Because he is an angel: not by nature, but by office, who is sent to announce the judge from on high.

VERS. 28.-- But I say. In this he is not preferred before the patriarchs and all men, but is shown equal to the rest of the saints. But he that. Every saint who is already with God is greater than he who is still in the world. Or: I, whom the opinion of many makes the lesser, 271Aam in the Church of the saints set before him. Or, the least angel of heaven is better than any man whatsoever.

VERS. 29.-- And all the people. If this is understood to have been said by the Lord, the people is understood to be designated as hearing John. If it is said to have been interposed by the evangelist, it remains to be understood as hearing the Lord himself, discoursing upon John's greatness.

VERS. 31.-- To whom then, etc. John being praised, he passes to the rebuking of those who neither were moved by John's preaching, or who despise the preaching of Christ himself.

VERS. 32.-- They are like to children. The children are teachers humble in spirit. The marketplace is the synagogue, or Jerusalem itself, in which the ordinances of God's precepts were established, because they were wont to reproach their fellow tribesmen that neither, 271Bdrawn by psalms, did they rise up to the praises of God with a devout heart and with agility of limbs (which the dancing denotes), nor by threnodies (that is, by the lamentations of the prophets over the destructions already wrought or yet to be wrought) were they turned to penance.

VERS. 34.-- The Son of man is come eating and drinking. He comes as then, so now also; you spurn both ways of salvation. We have lamented to John, we have sung to Christ.

VERS. 35.-- Wisdom is justified. He shows that the sons of wisdom understand that justice lies neither in abstaining nor in eating, but in the evenness of mind that bears want and temperance, and does not corrupt itself through abundance. The kingdom of God is not meat and drink (Rom. XIV). Of which not the use but the 271Cconcupiscence is reproved. Justified, etc. Whatever you think me, I nevertheless, the power of God and the wisdom of God, am understood to act justly by the apostles, to whom the Father revealed the things he hid from the prudent.

VERS. 36.-- He asked. As he had set it forth in words, so in deeds also he establishes that wisdom is justified by all her sons, because she is justified even by Mary repenting after her fault.

VERS. 37.-- In the house. In whosesoever inner house thou shalt have known wisdom and justice reclining, run back to the feet, that is, to the furthest part of wisdom; seek with tears, confess thy sins, spread out thy hair, that is, strew before him all the dignities of thy body; kiss, that is, thou shalt speak nothing but wisdom. She brought. As many delights as she had in herself, 271Dso many holocausts she found out of herself.

(HIER.) The alabaster box of ointment, that is, the body with faith, etc., as far as that is, the sins of the Jews.

VERS. 38.-- She began to water. Mary the sister of Lazarus performed the same office twice: once in Galilee, when she first draws near with humility and tears, where she received the remission of sins. The second time, in Bethania, no longer named a sinner, but chaste, and therefore devout: there she anoints the feet, here the head.

VERS. 43.-- He to whom more. According to men, he perhaps offended more who owed more. But by the Lord's mercy the case is changed, so that he who owed more should love more, and yet obtain grace. Thou hast judged rightly. The Pharisee is convicted by his own sentence, 272Abecause, like a frenzied man, he carries the rope by which he is to be bound.

VERS. 44.-- I entered into the house. I entered, flesh being assumed, into the people of the Jews.

VERS. 46.-- My head with oil thou didst not anoint. And although that people confessed that it believed in God and not in a man, yet it neglected to proclaim with worthy praise that very power of the godhead which appeared through the miracles. But the Gentile world, while it believes the mystery of the incarnation, proclaims with the highest praise even his weak things.

VERS. 47.-- Her sins are forgiven her. In the house of the Pharisee, that is, in the keeping of the law and the prophets, it is not the unbelieving Pharisee but the repentant sinful woman who is glorified, because the ardour of charity burned away in her the rust 272Bof her offences.

VERS. 49.-- And they that were together began. The sick woman being healed, they sicken over her salvation, to others.

VERS. 50.-- Thy faith hath made thee safe. Faith made her safe, because she did not doubt that she could receive what she asked, since she had already received hope from him from whom she was seeking salvation.

CAPUT VIII.

VERS. 1.-- Et factum est. More aquilae provocantis ad volandum pullos suos, paulatim Dominus discipulos suos in nido fidei plumis virtutum induit, quibus postea sublimius volare, et alios instruere valeant. Prius docet in synagogis, miracula facit, famam ubique dispergit, turbas suscipit, curat, instruit, hic discipulos facit, ex quibus duodecim elegit, 272Cquos primo praesente turba docet, et miseris solita praestat beneficia, jam vero solos ipsos secum retinet, ut eum familiarius audiant solis illis occultiora exponit mysteria, et sic demum virtutum suarum ostensione quasi alarum protectione firmatis, dat ipsis potestatem curandi, et mittit praedicare regnum Dei.

VERS. 2.-- Maria quae vocatur Magdalene. Cum Maria iter faceret cum Domino, et ei ministrare commemoratur, celebri eam vocabulo Magdalenam vocat: ubi eamdem peccatricem describit, reverenter eam generali nomine mulierem dicit, ne nomen tantae famae quod hodie veneramur, prisci erroris nota fuscaret. Maria, amarum mare, propter insitum poenitentiae rugitum, quo ipsa vel nos singula 272Dmala deflemus. Daemonia septem. Eamdem Ecclesiam de gentibus a sordibus vitiorum mundatam, quam Maria significat, significat et Joanna prius cultibus idolorum deditam, sed jam Christi pietate redemptam, et gratia impletam, quae quasi uxor erat prius diaboli.

VERS. 3.-- Et Joanna, etc. Joanna, Dominus gratia ejus, vel Dominus misericors. cujus est omne quod vivimus. Procuratoris Herodis. Cum quilibet malignus spiritus pro regno diaboli ad decipiendum laborat, quasi Herodis impiissimi procurator existit. De facultatibus, etc. Antiquus mos erat Judaeorum, nec ducebatur in culpam, ut mulieres sua substantia pascerent et vestirent doctores. Hic quia hoc in 273Agentibus scandalum poterat facere, Paulus se memorat abstinuisse. Ministrabant ergo Domino carnalia, cujus metebant spiritualia, ut formam daret praedicatoribus quod victu et vestitu deberent esse contenti accipiendo a plebibus.

VERS. 4.-- Cum autem turba, etc. Quotiens in Evangelio turbae vocabulum interseritur, sicut diversitas hominum, ita etiam diversitas intimatur voluntatum. Non enim omnes eadem voluntate, sed diversis ex causis Christum sequebantur: quidam pro bono, quidam pro malo, omnibus tamen Dominus beneficia praestat, docendo, alendo, sanando. Exiit qui seminat, etc. Exiit Filius Dei de sinu Patris, quo creaturae non erat accessus, ut seminaret semen suum, venit in mundum, ut testimonium 273Bperhiberet veritati. Unde secundum alios evangelistas hanc parabolam dicturus de domo exiisse, mare adiisse, navem conscendisse memoratur, id ipsum situ corporis quod processu sermonis insinuans.

VERS. 5.-- Secus viam. Via, est cor sedulo malarum cogitationum transitu attritum et arefactum, ne semen verbi possit accipere vel germinare, sed a pessimis cogitationibus conculcatur, et a daemonibus rapitur. Qui ideo volucres coeli dicuntur, quia coelestis et spiritualis sunt naturae, vel quia per aerem discurrunt.

VERS. 6.-- Supra petram. Petra indomitum cor nullo verae fidei vomere penetratum, in quo non est verus amor et perseverantiae virtus.

273CVERS. 8.-- Centuplum, etc. Cum denarius pro perfectione soleat accipi (quia in decem legis praeceptis custodia continetur), centenarius qui per multiplicatum denarium surgit, pro magna perfectione ponitur: cor igitur centuplum facit fructum quod per amorem proximi activam, et per amorem Dei contemplativam adipiscitur vitam, et spiritualium ornatur perfectione virtutum.

VERS. 9.-- Haec parabola. Nota hanc esse primam parabolam quae cum interpretatione sua posita sit. Et cavendum est ubicunque Dominus rogatus a discipulis intrinsecus sermones suos disserit, ne vel aliud vel plus vel minus intelligere velimus, quam ab eo expositum est. Ideo autem hanc parabolam per seipsum dignatur ostendere, ut se figurate 273Dloqui innotesceret, et rerum significationes etiam in his quae exponere noluit, quaerendas esse doceret.

VERS. 10.-- Videntes. Doctores Judaeorum qui cum merito perversitatis suae excaecati sunt, mendaciter se videntes putant.

VERS. 12.-- Quod autem secus viam. Secus viam seminantur, qui nulla fide, nullo intellectu, nulla saltem tentante utilitatis occasione, percipere dignantur. Porro super petram et in spinis seminantur, qui audiunt quidem et utilitatem verbi probant et desiderium gustant, sed ne perveniant ad id quod gestiunt: vel per adversa terrendo, vel prospera blandiendo retardant. His tribus generibus 274Aquicunque verbum auditum non faciunt designantur.

VERS. 13.-- Ad tempus credunt. Multi audientes disputationem contra avaritiam vel luxuriam, dicunt contemptores saeculi et castos homines esse beatos: sed ubi species concupiscibiles eorum obtutibus praesentantur, mox recedunt ab eis quidquid recte cogitaverant.

VERS. 14.-- Suffocantur. Quia voluptates et divitiae suis importunis cogitationibus guttur mentis, ne bonum desiderium ad cor possit intrare quasi aditum vitalis strangulant flatus.

VERS. 15.-- In terram bonam, etc. In bonos auditores qui verbum audire satagunt, et in ventrem memoriae recondunt, et sic opportuno tempore fructum 274Boperationis proferunt, inter prospera et adversa saeculi patientes. Et bona terra cum patientia fructum reddit, quia bona agit, et mala proximorum aequanimiter tolerat, et dum humiliter flagella suspiciunt, post flagella in requiem suscipiuntur.

VERS. 16.-- Nemo autem. Haec verba ponuntur secundum similitudinem humanae consuetudinis.

VERS. 17.-- Non enim, etc. Quasi dicat. Timor carnalium non vos retardet, sed nec mala opinio, quae de vobis modo habetur apud incredulos quia etsi viles apparetis, quandoque claritas vestra apparebit, cum Dominus illuminabit abscondita tenebrarum, tunc et vos coronabimini, et punientur vestri adversarii.

274CVERS. 18.-- Qui enim habet, etc. Haec specialiter apostolis, quibus ex charitate nosse mysteria datur, et perfidis Judaeis (qui per parabolas quod non intelligunt audiunt) dicta videntur. Potest et generaliter accipi, quia et ingeniosus, negligendo, sapientia vera se privat et simplex studiosus laborando degustat. Quia qui amorem verbi habet, dabitur et sensus intelligendi quod amat. Sed qui non habet amorem audiendi, etiamsi naturali ingenio vel labore studioso se intelligere putat, nullam tamen verae scientiae dulcedinem gustabit. Et quicunque non. Ideo desidiosus ingenium accipit saepe, ut de negligentia jutius puniatur, qui scire contempsit quod sine labore assequi potuit, et aliquando studiosus tarditate intelligentiae premitur, ut eo majora 274Dpraemia inveniat, quo studiosius elaborat.

VERS. 24.-- Et cessavit, et facta est tranquillitas. Nota quod omnis creatura sentit Creatorem. Imperat, increpat, sentiunt imperantem, non quod secundum haeresim quorumdam sint animantia, sed majestati conditoris sunt sensibilia, quae apud nos sunt insensibilia.

VERS. 25.-- Mirati sunt ad invicem. Non discipuli, sed nautae et alii qui in navi erant mirantur. Unde Matthaeus: Porro homines illi mirati sunt, dicentes: Qualis est hic? (Matth. VIII.) Quod si discipulos dicimus miratos fuisse, merito et ipsi homines sunt appellati.

Ad regionem Gerasenorum. Gerasa civitas Arabiae 275Ajuxta montem Galaad in tribu Manasse, non longe a stagno Tiberiadis, significat gentes quas post passionem et resurrectionem Dominus per praedicatores visitavit, unde Gerasa sive Gergesi colonum ejiciens, id est diabolum a quo prius incolebatur vel advena propinquans, qui longe erat in tempore.

VERS. 27.-- In monumentis. Quid aliud sunt perfidorum corpora, nisi quaedam defunctorum sepulcra, in quibus non habitant Dei verba?

VERS. 28.-- Jesu Fili Dei. Arius contendit esse creaturam, quem daemon confitetur esse Filium Dei. Judaei dicunt: In principe daemoniorum ejicit daemonia, quem daemonia negant aliquid secum commune habere. Tormentum est diaboli ab hominis laesione cessare, et quantum diutius possederat, tantum 275Bdifficilius dimittit. Caveat ergo aliquis laesus a diabolo, ut cito ejus jugum dirumpat, quia tanto facilius quanto citius ejus dominium evitatur.

VERS. 29.-- Vinciebatur catenis. Catenae et compedes, durae et graves sunt gentium leges, quibus, et in eorum republica cohibebantur peccata. His tamen legibus ruptis, ad ea scelera cupiditate ducebantur, quae vulgarem consuetudinem excedebant.

VERS. 30.-- Quia intraverunt daemonia multa, etc. Multa daemonia quae unum premunt, signant multos cultus idololatriae in uno gentium populo. E contra dicitur: Multitudinis credentium erat cor unum et anima una (Act. IV). Unde bene in Babylone unitas linguarum scissa, in Jerusalem est adunata.

275CVERS. 31.-- Et rogabant. Sciebant daemones per adventum Domini se aliquando in abyssum mergendos: non ipsi futura praedivinantes, sed prophetarum de se dicta recolentes.

VERS. 32.-- Grex porcorum. Porci sunt homines vocis et rationis expertes, lutulentis actibus dediti, in monte superbiae pascentes. Et nisi quis more porci vixerit, diabolus in eo potestatem non accipit, vel ad probandum tantum, non autem ad perdendum accipit.

VERS. 34.-- Fugerunt et nuntiaverunt in civitatem, et in villas. Pastores fugerunt, quia nec philosophi gentium, nec principes Synagogae pereuntibus possunt conferre medicinam, et licet Christianam fugiant legem, potentiam tamen ejus stupendo et mirando praedicant.

275DVERS. 35.-- Exierunt autem videre quod factum est, et venerunt ad Jesum, et invenerunt hominem, etc. Significat multitudinem sua venusta vita delectatam, honorare quidem, sed nolle pati religionem Christianam, dum dicunt quod eam implere non possunt. Mirantur et fidelem populum a pristina conversatione sanatum, et sedere ad pedes Domini, id est, sano mentis examine vestigia Salvatoris quae sequatur intueri, et vestitum studiis virtutum, quae deceptus amiserat.

VERS. 37.-- Et rogaverunt. Conscii fragilitatis, praesentia Domini se indignos judicant, non capientes verbum Dei, et in firma mente pondus sapientiae non sustinentes, et ideo diutius molestus non fuit, sed 276Aregreditur quia non dignatur curare invitos, sed cito deserit infirmos, quibus oneri videt esse suam praesentiam.

VERS. 39.-- Redi in domum, etc. Allegorice. Post remissionem peccatorum redeundum est in bonam conscientiam, et serviendum Evangelio, propter aliorum etiam salutem, ut deinde cum Christo requiescat, ne cum jam prope vult esse cum Christo, negligat mysterium praedicationis accommodatum fraternae liberationi, unde Apostolus: Cupio dissolvi, et esse cum Christo (Philip. I), multo melius, manere autem in carne necessarium propter vos.

VERS. 43.-- In medicos. Medici, daemones, qui quasi consulentes hominibus, pro Deo se coli exigunt, quibus servierat gentilitas.

276BVERS. 44.-- Tetigit fimbriam. Fimbriam tangit, qui Incarnationis mysteria perfecte amat et credit, donec ad majora capienda perveniat. Si consideremus quanta sit fides nostra, et intelligamus quantus sit Filius Dei, videmus quia, comparatione ejus, fimbriam tantummodo tangimus, superiorem vestimenti ejus partem nequimus attingere.

Et confestim, etc. Suscitaturus mortuam rediit ad fidem faciendam, prius curat hemorroidam. Sic Elisabeth sterilis paritura indicatur ut conceptura virgo credatur.

VERS. 45.-- Quis est. Non quaerit ut doceatur quae nesciat, sed ut fides mulieris appareat.

VERS. 46.-- Nam et ego novi. Non latet quicunque 276Ctangit: Deus non indiget oculis ut videat, nec corporaliter sentit, sed in se habet cognitionem omnium. Etiam virtus quae propitiatur omnibus iniquitatibus nostris, et sanat omnes infirmitates nostras, non ex nobis, sed ex Deo exit.

VERS. 47.-- Videns autem mulier. Haec mulier verecunda fimbriam tangit, fidelis accessit, religiosa credidit: sapiens se esse sanatam cognoscit: sic plebs gentium quae Deo credidit, peccatum erubuit ut desereret, fidem detulit ut crederet, devotionem exhibuit ut rogaret, sapientiam induit ut sanitatem suam et ipsa sentiret, fiduciam sumpsit ut fateretur quod praeripiebat alienum.

VERS. 51.-- Patrem puellae. Pater puellae, coetus doctorum legis, scilicet Scribae et Pharisaei, qui si 276Dcredere voluerint etiam subjecta Synagoga salva erit.

VERS. 52.-- Flebant autem. Flent qui Synagogam a Deo, qui vita est, vident alienam et dolent, nec amplius posse vivificari putant, et illa quae sponsi praesentiam qua vivere debet, amisit, quasi inter plangentes mortua jacens, nec hoc ipsum quidem quare plangatur, intelligit. Nolite flere. Fleant mortuos suos, qui putant mortuos, ubi est fides resurrectionis non est mors, sed spes quietis. Non est mortua, etc. Mortua est puella hominibus qui suscitare non possunt: non Deo, cui anima vivit, et caro suscitando quiescit.

VERS. 53.-- Deridebant, etc. Merito exclusi sunt, 277Aqui deridere quam credere malebant, indigni videre resurrectionis miraculum.

VERS. 54.-- Tenens manum. Tenet manum, quia nisi manus Judaeorum (quae sanguine plenae) prius fuerint mundatae, Synagoga non surget. Puella, surge. Quisquis a morte animae, a Christo manum sibi confortante resipiscit, a sordibus vitiorum debet exsurgere, et (sicut Marcus ait) in bonis operibus ambulare, et coelesti pane necesse est ut mox satietur, id est, verbi Dei et altaris.

VERS. 56.-- Quibus praecepit. Sicut publica noxa publico eget remedio, ita levia peccata leviori et secreta queunt deleri poenitentia. Unde puella in domo jacens, paucis arbitris exsurgit, eisdemque ne miraculum vulgarent interdicitur.

CHAPTER VIII.

VERS. 1.-- And it came to pass. After the manner of an eagle stirring up her young to fly, the Lord little by little clothes his own disciples in the nest of faith with the feathers of the virtues, by which afterwards they may be able to fly more loftily and to instruct others. First he teaches in the synagogues, works miracles, spreads his fame everywhere, receives the crowds, heals, instructs; here he makes disciples, out of whom he chose twelve, 272Cwhom at first he teaches with the crowd present, and bestows on the wretched his accustomed benefits; but now he keeps them alone with him, that they may hear him more familiarly; to them alone he expounds the more hidden mysteries; and so at last, when they have been strengthened by the showing of his powers as by the protection of wings, he gives them the power of healing, and sends them to preach the kingdom of God.

VERS. 2.-- Mary who is called Magdalen. When Mary was making the journey with the Lord, and is recorded to minister to him, he calls her by the celebrated name of Magdalen: where he describes the same woman as a sinner, he reverently calls her by the general name of woman, lest the mark of her former error should darken a name of such renown as we venerate today. Mary, a bitter sea, because of the ingrafted roaring of penitence, by which she herself, or we, bewail our several 272Devils. Seven demons. That same Church of the Gentiles, cleansed from the filth of vices, which Mary signifies, Joanna also signifies: formerly given over to the worship of idols, but now redeemed by the mercy of Christ and filled with grace, and which was before as it were the wife of the devil.

VERS. 3.-- And Joanna, etc. Joanna, the Lord her grace, or the Lord merciful. whose is all that we live. Of Herod's steward. When any malignant spirit labours to deceive on behalf of the devil's kingdom, he stands as it were the steward of the most impious Herod. Of their substance, etc. It was an ancient custom of the Jews, and it was not accounted a fault, that women should feed and clothe their teachers out of their own substance. Here, because this could give 273Ascandal among the Gentiles, Paul records that he abstained from it. They ministered therefore carnal things to the Lord, whose spiritual things they reaped, that he might give a form to preachers, that they ought to be content with the food and clothing they receive from the peoples.

VERS. 4.-- And when a crowd, etc. As often as the word "crowd" is inserted in the Gospel, just as a diversity of men, so also a diversity of wills is intimated. For not all followed Christ with the same will, but for different causes: some for good, some for evil; yet to all the Lord bestows his benefits, by teaching, by feeding, by healing. The sower went out, etc. The Son of God went out from the bosom of the Father, to which there was no access for a creature, that he might sow his own seed; he came into the world, that he might give testimony 273Bto the truth. Whence, according to the other evangelists, when about to speak this parable he is recorded to have gone out of the house, to have gone to the sea, and to have gone up into a ship, insinuating by the posture of his body the very thing he insinuated by the course of his discourse.

VERS. 5.-- By the wayside. The way is the heart worn down and dried up by the constant passage of evil thoughts, so that it cannot receive the seed of the word nor make it germinate, but it is trodden down by the worst thoughts, and snatched away by the demons. Who are therefore called the birds of the air, because they are of a heavenly and spiritual nature, or because they course through the air.

VERS. 6.-- Upon the rock. The rock is the untamed heart, pierced by no ploughshare of true faith, in which there is not true love nor the virtue of perseverance.

273CVERS. 8.-- A hundredfold, etc. Since the number ten is wont to be taken for perfection (because the keeping of the law is contained in its ten precepts), the number a hundred, which arises from ten multiplied, is set for great perfection: therefore that heart bears fruit a hundredfold which by the love of its neighbour attains the active life, and by the love of God the contemplative, and is adorned with the perfection of spiritual virtues.

VERS. 9.-- This parable. Note that this is the first parable which is set down with its own interpretation. And one must beware, wherever the Lord, being asked by the disciples privately, expounds his own discourses, lest we should wish to understand either something else, or more, or less, than what has been set forth by him. And therefore he deigns to show this parable by himself, that he might make known that he speaks figuratively, 273Dand might teach that the significations of things are to be sought even in those which he was unwilling to expound.

VERS. 10.-- Seeing. The teachers of the Jews, who, although they have been blinded by the desert of their own perversity, falsely think themselves to be seeing.

VERS. 12.-- But that which by the wayside. They are sown by the wayside who deign to receive it with no faith, with no understanding, not even with any occasion of profit tempting them. But they are sown upon the rock and among thorns who do indeed hear, and approve the profit of the word, and taste the desire of it; but, lest they should come to that which they long for, they hold them back either by terrifying through adverse things or by flattering with prosperous ones. By these three kinds 274Aare designated all who do not do the word they have heard.

VERS. 13.-- For a while they believe. Many, hearing a discourse against avarice or luxury, say that despisers of the world and chaste men are blessed: but when desirable forms are set before their gaze, presently whatever they had rightly thought departs from them.

VERS. 14.-- They are choked. Because pleasures and riches, by their importunate thoughts, strangle the throat of the mind, as it were the entrance of the vital breath, so that a good desire cannot enter to the heart.

VERS. 15.-- On the good ground, etc. Upon good hearers who take pains to hear the word, and lay it up in the belly of memory, and so in due time bring forth the fruit 274Bof working, being patient amid the prosperity and adversity of the world. And the good ground renders fruit with patience, because it does good things, and bears with an even mind the evils of its neighbours; and while they humbly look up to the scourges, after the scourges they are received into rest.

VERS. 16.-- And no man. These words are set down according to the likeness of human custom.

VERS. 17.-- For there is not, etc. As if he should say. Let not the fear of carnal men hold you back, nor yet the evil opinion which is now held of you among unbelievers; because although you appear vile, one day your brightness will appear, when the Lord will bring to light the hidden things of darkness, then you also will be crowned, and your adversaries will be punished.

274CVERS. 18.-- For he that hath, etc. These things seem to be said specially to the apostles, to whom out of charity it is given to know the mysteries, and to the faithless Jews (who through parables hear what they do not understand). It can also be taken generally, because both the clever man, by being negligent, deprives himself of true wisdom, and the simple diligent man, by labouring, tastes it. Because to him who has the love of the word, the sense also of understanding what he loves will be given. But he who has not the love of hearing, even though by natural talent or by studious labour he thinks that he understands, will yet taste no sweetness of true knowledge. And whosoever hath not. Therefore the slothful man often receives talent, that he may be the more justly punished for his negligence, who scorned to know what he could have attained without labour; and sometimes the studious man is pressed by slowness of understanding, that he may find the greater 274Drewards the more studiously he labours.

VERS. 24.-- And it ceased, and there was a calm. Note that every creature perceives its Creator. He commands, he rebukes, and they perceive him commanding; not that, according to the heresy of certain men, they are living things, but that things which with us are insensible are sensible to the majesty of their maker.

VERS. 25.-- They wondered one to another. Not the disciples, but the sailors and the others who were in the ship wonder. Whence Matthew: But those men wondered, saying: What manner of man is this? (Matth. VIII.) But if we say that the disciples wondered, they too are rightly called men.

To the country of the Gerasens. Gerasa, a city of Arabia 275Abeside Mount Galaad in the tribe of Manasses, not far from the lake of Tiberias, signifies the Gentiles whom after his passion and resurrection the Lord visited through his preachers; whence Gerasa, or Gergesi, casting out the settler, that is, the devil by whom it was formerly inhabited, or, a stranger drawing near, who was far off in time.

VERS. 27.-- In the sepulchres. What else are the bodies of the faithless but certain tombs of the dead, in which the words of God do not dwell?

VERS. 28.-- Jesus, Son of God. Arius contends that he is a creature, whom the demon confesses to be the Son of God. The Jews say: In the prince of demons he casteth out demons, whom the demons deny to have anything in common with themselves. It is a torment to the devil to cease from injuring a man, and by how much the longer he had possessed him, by so much 275Bthe more hardly does he let him go. Let anyone therefore who has been injured by the devil take care to break his yoke quickly, because his dominion is escaped the more easily the more quickly it is escaped.

VERS. 29.-- He was bound with chains. The chains and fetters are the hard and heavy laws of the Gentiles, by which, even in their commonwealth, sins were restrained. Yet when these laws were broken, they were led by desire to those crimes which exceeded common usage.

VERS. 30.-- Because many demons entered, etc. The many demons which oppress one man signify the many cults of idolatry in one people of the Gentiles. On the contrary it is said: The multitude of believers had one heart and one soul (Act. IV). Whence fittingly in Babylon the unity of tongues was rent, and in Jerusalem it was made one.

275CVERS. 31.-- And they besought. The demons knew that by the Lord's coming they were one day to be plunged into the abyss: not themselves foreseeing things to come, but recalling the things said of them by the prophets.

VERS. 32.-- A herd of swine. The swine are men devoid of speech and of reason, given over to filthy deeds, feeding on the mountain of pride. And unless a man has lived after the manner of a swine, the devil receives no power over him, or receives it only for proving him, and not for destroying him.

VERS. 34.-- They fled and told it in the city and in the villages. The herdsmen fled, because neither the philosophers of the Gentiles nor the rulers of the Synagogue can bring medicine to the perishing; and although they flee the Christian law, yet by being astonished and by wondering they proclaim its power.

275DVERS. 35.-- And they went out to see what was done, and they came to Jesus, and found the man, etc. It signifies the multitude, delighted with its own comely life, indeed honouring the Christian religion, but unwilling to endure it, while they say that they cannot fulfil it. They wonder also at the faithful people healed of its former manner of life, and sitting at the Lord's feet, that is, with a sound judgment of mind beholding the footsteps of the Saviour which it is to follow, and clothed with the pursuits of the virtues which, being deceived, it had lost.

VERS. 37.-- And they besought him. Conscious of their frailty, they judge themselves unworthy of the Lord's presence, not receiving the word of God, and not sustaining the weight of wisdom in a firm mind; and therefore he was not troublesome to them any longer, but 276Areturns, because he does not deign to heal the unwilling, but quickly leaves the weak, to whom he sees his own presence to be a burden.

VERS. 39.-- Return to thy house, etc. Allegorically. After the remission of sins one must return into a good conscience, and must serve the Gospel, for the salvation of others also, that thereafter he may rest with Christ; lest, when he now wishes to be near with Christ, he neglect the mystery of preaching that is fitted to the deliverance of the brethren. Whence the Apostle: I desire to be dissolved and to be with Christ (Philip. I), a thing much better, but to abide in the flesh is needful for you.

VERS. 43.-- Upon physicians. The physicians are the demons, who, as though taking counsel for men, demand to be worshipped in God's stead, and whom the Gentile world had served.

276BVERS. 44.-- She touched the hem. He touches the hem who perfectly loves and believes the mysteries of the Incarnation, until he comes to the grasping of greater things. If we consider how great our faith is, and understand how great the Son of God is, we see that, in comparison with him, we touch only the hem, and cannot reach the upper part of his garment.

And immediately, etc. Being about to raise the dead girl, he returned to make faith; first he heals the woman with the issue of blood. So Elizabeth, barren, is declared as about to bear, that the Virgin about to conceive may be believed.

VERS. 45.-- Who is it. He does not ask that he may be taught what he does not know, but that the woman's faith may appear.

VERS. 46.-- For I also know. Whoever touches is not hidden: 276CGod does not need eyes in order to see, nor does he perceive bodily, but has in himself the knowledge of all things. The power also which is merciful to all our iniquities, and heals all our infirmities, goes forth not from us, but from God.

VERS. 47.-- And the woman seeing. This woman, modest, touches the hem; faithful, she drew near; religious, she believed; wise, she knows herself to be healed: so the people of the Gentiles, which believed in God, blushed at sin that it might forsake it, brought faith that it might believe, showed devotion that it might ask, put on wisdom that it too might perceive its own healing, took confidence that it might confess that it was seizing beforehand what belonged to another.

VERS. 51.-- The father of the girl. The father of the girl is the assembly of the teachers of the law, namely the Scribes and Pharisees, who, if they 276Dshall have been willing to believe, the Synagogue that is subject to them will also be saved.

VERS. 52.-- And they wept. They weep who see the Synagogue estranged from God, who is life, and who grieve, and think that she can no longer be quickened; and she, who has lost the bridegroom's presence by which she ought to live, lying as it were dead among the mourners, does not understand even this very thing, why she is mourned. Weep not. Let them bewail their dead who think them dead; where there is faith of the resurrection there is not death, but the hope of rest. She is not dead, etc. The girl is dead to men who cannot raise her: not to God, to whom the soul lives, and to whom the flesh rests in order to be raised.

VERS. 53.-- They laughed him to scorn, etc. Rightly were they shut out, 277Awho preferred to mock rather than to believe, unworthy to see the miracle of the resurrection.

VERS. 54.-- Holding her hand. He holds her hand, because unless the hands of the Jews (which are full of blood) shall first have been cleansed, the Synagogue will not rise. Maid, arise. Whosoever comes to himself from the death of the soul, Christ strengthening his hand for him, ought to rise from the filth of vices, and (as Mark says) to walk in good works, and it is necessary that he be presently filled with heavenly bread, that is, with the bread of the word of God and of the altar.

VERS. 56.-- Whom he charged. As a public offence needs a public remedy, so light and secret sins can be blotted out by a lighter penance. Whence the girl, lying in the house, rises with few witnesses, and these same are forbidden to publish the miracle abroad.

CAPUT IX.

  • 277B

VERS. 1.-- Convocatis autem Jesus. Concessa primum potestate signorum mittuntur praedicare regnum Dei, ut magnitudini promissorum attestetur etiam magnitudo factorum, et fidem verbis daret virtus ostensa, et nova facerent qui nova praedicarent.

VERS. 3.-- Nihil tuleritis. In Marco legitur, ut nihil tollerent in via nisi virgam tantum (Marc. VI). Sed sciendum est, quia utrumque praeceptum est a Domino, ut nihil nisi virgam ferrent, et ut nec virgam ferrent. Non ferre virgam, est non esse sollicitum de his quae necessaria sunt humanae sustentationi, quia haec debentur praedicatoribus ab his quibus praedicant, quando sine scandalo possunt accipi. Nihil 277Cferre nisi virgam, est uti illa potestate accipiendi necessaria quae data est praedicatoribus. Duas tunicas. Simplex vestimentum est quidquid est necessarium alicui secundum qualitatem loci vel temporis. Duplex est, quod ultra necessitatem est. Allegorice, in duabus tunicis, duplicitatem prohibet.

VERS. 4.-- Ibi manete. Ne excursetis per domos et inviolabilis hospitii jura mutetis. Unde secundum Matthaeum, domus in qua manere debeant prius eligenda est, ut mutandi hospitii necessitas auferatur.

VERS. 5.-- Pulverem pedum vestrorum. Pulvis excutitur in testimonium laboris sui quod ingressi sunt in civitatem, et usque ad illos praedicatio pervenit. 277DVel, excutitur, ut nihil ab eis accipiant qui Evangelium spreverunt, nec etiam necessaria vitae.

VERS. 8.-- Joannes surrexit. Qui Joannem, cujus nullum signum viderant, resurrexisse dicebant, per summam malitiam Jesum resurrexisse negabant, multa mirabilia opera viderant, in cujus passione multa miranda contigerant.

VERS. 10.-- Narraverunt. Narrant apostoli Domino quae fecerint, et quomodo docuerint, quomodo Joannes dum ipsi docerent passus sit. Unde sequitur: Et assumptis. Non otiosis, non in civitate Synagogae, id est, saecularis dignitatis residentibus, sed inter deserta Christum quaerentibus coelestis gratiae alimonia impertitur. Qui enim non fastidiunt, a Christo suscipiuntur, et spiritualiter et corporaliter, 278Aetiam si indigent curantur. Et nota, quia postquam perfecta est mentio passionis Joannis, Christus reficit, quia post defectum legis, evangelicus cibus incipit. Denique postquam curata est mulier quae gestat figuram Ecclesiae, et postquam apostoli ad praedicandum sunt missi, fit mentio refectionis, quia per apostolos in Ecclesia vera refectio fit.

VERS. 11.-- Secutae sunt illum. Non jumentis, non vehiculis, sed proprio labore pedum, ut ardorem mentis ostenderent. Sanabat. Nemo cibum Christi accipit nisi antea sanatus fuerit, quia post remissionem peccatorum tribuitur alimonia coelestis.

VERS. 12.-- Dies autem coeperat. Declinata die reficit, quia vel fine saeculorum propinquante, vel cum sol justitiae occubuit, a spirituali fame sumus 278Bsalvati. Et nota ordinem mysterii: prius per remissionem peccatorum vulneribus medicina tribuitur, postea alimonia mensae coelestis expenditur.

VERS. 13.-- Nisi forte. Nondum intellexerant apostoli fidem populi credentis non esse venalem, sed noverant nos potius esse redimendos per suas escas gratuitas.

VERS. 14.-- Quinque millia. Per quinque millia significantur, qui in saeculari adhuc habitu per quinque sensus corporis exterioribus quae possident, bene uti noverunt. Qui quinque panibus aluntur, quia tales necesse est legalibus adhuc praeceptis institui, et legali timore comprimi. Nam qui mundo integre renuntiant et quatuor sunt millia propter evangelicam 278Cdoctrinam, et septem panibus, id est, septiformis spiritus reficiuntur gratia.

VERS. 15.-- Et discumbere omnes. Secundum alium evangelistam quinque millia super fenum discumbunt, quia illi quibus adhuc sunt corporales sensus, melioribus delectantur. Omnis enim caro fenum. Quatuor millia super terram recumbunt, plus est enim terra premere quam super feno jacere.

VERS. 16.-- Distribuit discipulis suis. Provocat apostolos ad fractionem panis, ut illis non habere testantibus magnitudo signi notior fiat. Insinuat quod per eorum doctrinam, jejuna corda quotidie sunt pascenda.

VERS. 17.-- Et saturati sunt. Quod manducans 278Dpopulus satiatur, significat famem in perpetuum ab electis repellendam, quia non esuriet qui acceperit Christi cibum. Quod apostoli ministrant, futuram divisionem Dominici corporis et sanguinis pronuntiat. Cophini duodecim. Cur quinque millibus hominum plus redundat, quatuor millibus minus? Quia quatuor millia triduo cum Christo fuerunt, et ideo amplius coelestis pabuli receperunt.

VERS. 18.-- Et factum est. Post miraculum cum turbae vellent eum facere regem, dimissa turba, fugit et ascendit in montem solus orare, et antequam discipuli ascenderent in naviculam, aderant cum ipso. Ipse autem solus orat Patrem. Possunt enim sancti fide et amore Domino conjungi, et eum hominibus excellentiorem intueri, et conversationem 279Aejus in carne passibus humilitatis subsequi, sed incomprehensibilia paternae positionis arcana solus penetrat, et solus quod orandum novit, orat. Cum discipulis autem non reperitur orasse, sed solus obsecrat, quia Dei consilium humana corda non capiunt, nec quisquam potest interiorum particeps esse cum Christo. Quem me dicunt esse turbae? Exploraturus fidem discipulorum, prius vulgi sententiam inquirit, ne apostolorum fides firmata videatur vulgari opinione, sed veritatis agnitione. Merito turbae nominantur quae diversas de Domino ferunt sententias, quorum sermo et sensus instabilis est et vagus, a quibus suos distinguens ait. Vos autem quem me esse dicitis?

VERS. 19.-- Joannem Baptistam. Opinio de Joanne 279Bideo forte erat, quia in utero matris positus, Domini praesentiam sentiebat. De Elia, quia raptus est in coelum, et venturus creditur. De Jeremia, quia in matris utero sanctificatus est.

VERS. 20.-- Vos autem. Qui tractat debet audientium considerare personas, ne prius irrideatur quam audiatur. Christum Dei. Complexus est omnia, qui et naturam et nomen expressit. In nomine enim Christi et divinitatis et incarnationis est expressio et fides passionis. Haereticus etsi nomen Christi non negat Christum tamen negat, qui non omnia quae Christi sunt confitetur.

VERS. 21.-- Praecepit ne, etc. Jubet tacere discipulos ut fallat principem mundi, et ut declinet jactantiam, doceat humilitatem. Simul ne adhuc rudes 279Cet imperfecti discipuli majoris praedicationis mole opprimerentur.

VERS. 23.-- Dicebat autem. Quae ad fidem Dominicae nativitatis vel passionis pertinent, solis seorsum discipulis aperuit, sed haec ad omnes. Abneget semetipsum. Nisi quis a seipso deficiat, ad eum qui super se est non appropinquat: et novitatem ad quam vocatur, non apprehendit, nisi mactet vetustatem, in qua natus fuit; et cum cessat a vitiis exquirat etiam virtutes, unde subjungitur:

VERS. 24.-- Nam qui. (GREG.) Sicut persecutionis tempore ponenda est anima, ita in pace frangenda sunt desideria terrena, ut quanto videtur homo securior, tanto sit ad sui custodiam sollicitior.

VERS. 26.-- Hunc filius hominis. Non sufficit ad 279Dprobationem fidei vox professionis, quam defendit a verecundia professio generalitatis.

VERS. 27.-- Aliqui hic stantes. Qui stat cum Christo non gustat mortem, quia nec tenuem mortis aeternae sensum habebit, qui Christi consortia meruerit, cui nec in morte interrumpitur ordo vivendi. Donec videant regnum. Quia arduum erat animam periculis, corpus morti offerre, sustentat infirmitatem humanae mentis remuneratione praesentium, ne frangatur desperatione vel taedio. Visa enim aeterna gloria, etsi transitu et ad breve momentum, fortiores tamen contra mundi adversa redduntur. Promittit itaque futuram gloriam in transitu videndam in terra, ut certius in coelo speretur aeterna. Vel 280Apromittit aliquos de discipulis visuros dilationem Ecclesiae antequam per mortem recedant de mundo, ut in praesenti contra mundi gloriam erecti, certius credantur in coelo regnaturi.

VERS. 28.-- Post haec verba. Alii qui sex dies tantum numerant post sex aetates saeculi sanctis a labore quiescendum signant. Lucas qui et primum quo promittitur, et ultimum quo promissio impletur connumerat, octava innuit resurgendum. Nam sicut Christus post sextam sabbati qua crucem ascendit, et post septimam sabbati, qua in sepulcro quievit, octava die resurrexit: sic nos post sex saeculi aetates quibus pro Domino patimur et laboramus, et post septimam quietis animarum quae interim in alia vita geritur, octava aetate resurgemus. Assumpsit Petrum. 280BTres tantum dicit, quia multi vocati, pauci electi (Matth. XX), et qui fidem Trinitatis inviolatam servant, aeterna merentur visione laetari. Scit meritorum cognitor perfectiores fuisse caeteris tres apostolos. Signant illos qui habent fidem, spem, charitatem. Vel per Petrum, praepositi sive conjugati, per Jacobum poenitentes vel actuales, per Joannem, virgines vel theorici.

VERS. 29.-- Et factus est. Transfiguratus non substantiam verae carnis amisit, sed gloriam, vel suae vel nostrae resurrectionis ostendit, qui qualis tunc apostolis, talis post judicium cunctis apparebit electis. Nam in ipso judicio et bonis et malis in forma servi apparebit ut videant in quem pupugerunt. 280CEt vestitus ejus. Vestes Domini sancti sunt qui Christum induerunt. Quae vestis Domino in terris consistente despicabilis, et aliorum similis visa est, sed Domino montem ascendente fulget, quia nondum apparuit quid erimus, sed tandem similes ei erimus, quando videbimus eum sicuti est. Vel vestimenta verbi sermones sunt Scripturarum, et quaedam indumenta divini intellectus. Quia sicut illis apostolis in altera species apparuit ipse et vestis ejus refulsit, ita et oculis mentis tuae jam divinarum sensus albescit lectionem.

VERS. 30.-- Ecce duo viri. Notandum est, non corpora vel animas Eliae vel Moysi apparuisse, sed ex aliqua subjecta creatura illa corpora formata fuisse: potest etiam credi angelico ministerio illud 280Dfactum esse, ut angeli eorum personas assumerent. Mortuus et vivus apparent, ut significent Christum moriturum et post victurum, et ut vivamus Christo, et moriamur mundo. Etiam apostolis dat signum de coelo, ut fides eorum augeatur. Sed scribis tentantibus dare noluit, ut indurarentur. Erant autem Moyses, et Elias visi. Per Moysen, infernales; per Eliam, coelestes; per apostolos, terrestres ad judicium venturi significantur.

VERS. 31.-- Dicebant excessum. Moyses, id est legislator, et Elias prophetarum eximius apparent cum verbo, ut ostendant ipsum esse qui locutus est in lege et prophetis, et quem lex et prophetae promiserunt. Qui non in infimis, sed in monte visi sunt, 281Aquia soli mente excelsi majestatem Scripturae, quae in Domino impleta est, perspiciunt.

VERS. 32.-- Gravati erant somno. Somno gravantur, quia splendore incomprehensibilis deitatis premitur sensus humani corporis. Vel somno gravantur, ut resurrectionis gloriam post corporis quietem viderent, et sancti eo verius majestatem Domini videbunt, quo carnis suae in qua mortem vicerant immortalitate gaudebunt. Tunc Moysen et Eliam speculabuntur in gloria, quia melius intelligent quomodo unum iota aut unus apex non praeteribit a lege (Matth. V), et quomodo Dominus legem et prophetas non solverit, sed adimpleverit.

VERS. 33.-- Bonum est nos hic esse. Qui ad montana conscenderat non vult ad terrena descendere, 281Bsed semper in sublimi perseverare, et est Petrus in persona ingredientium regnum Dei. Bonum est nos hic esse. Bonum est in regno Dei esse. Quasi dicat: Nos soli hac luce fruemur, non Judaei invidi. Tria tabernacula. Id est, verba, cogitationes, opera. Unum Moysi. Quomodo noscunt quos antea non viderunt? Per majestatem super eos venientem, sicut in futura resurrectione unusquisque alterum cognoscet, et desiderata in altero alter per charitatem intelliget.

VERS. 34.-- Facta est nubes, et obumbravit eos. Etsi Petrus ex infirmitate ignorat, placet tamen obsequii devotio. Unde et nubes obumbrat, divini Spiritus est ista obumbratio quae non caligat affectibus hominum, sed revelat occulta, cujus perfectus 281Costenditur audita voce dicentis: Hic est Filius meus. Quia materiale quaesivit tabernaculum, nubis accepit obumbraculum, ut discat in resurrectione, non regimine domorum, sed Spiritus sancti gloria sanctos esse protegendos. Et timuerunt. Timent, quia humana fragilitas conspectum majoris gloriae ferre non sustinet, et quanto quis ampliora quaesierit, tanto magis ad inferiora collabitur, si mensuram suam ignoraverit.

VERS. 36.-- Et vox. Vox Patris Petrum sublato terrore docet veritatem. Et nota quod, sicut Domino baptizato, sic et transfigurato mysterium Trinitatis declaratur, quia gloriam quam in baptismate confitemur, in resurrectione videbimus. Spiritus in columba, 281Dhic in nube lucida, quia qui nunc simplici corde fidem servat, tunc aperta luce visionis contemplabitur quod credidit, ipsaque qua illustrabitur gratia, protegetur. Inventus est Jesus solus. Non Elias, non Moyses, sed quem solum videtis, huic figendum est tabernaculum in corde. Recedunt ergo illi ubi Dominus coepit designari, ne ad servos vox Patris missa putetur.

VERS. 37.-- Factum est autem in sequenti die, descendentibus illis de monte. Pro qualitate meritorum, quotidie Dominus aliis ascendit, dum perfectos, quorum conversatio in coelis est, sublimius extollendo glorificat, et de aeternis instruit et docet quae a turba non audiri valeant. Aliis descendit, dum terrenos et incipientes confortat et docet et castigat. Occurrit 282Aillis turba. In monte orat, docet, majestatem suam demonstrat, Patris vocem apostolis aperit, descendens a turba excipitur, miserorum fletu pulsatur, infidelitatis peccata exprobrat, malos spiritus expellit.

VERS. 39.-- Et ecce spiritus apprehendit. Matthaeus lunaticum, Marcus surdum et mutum describit. Significat autem illos qui ut luna mutantur, nunquam in eodem statu manentes, sed per diversa vitia crescunt et decrescunt, qui nec fidem confitentur, nec ipsius fidei audire volunt sermonem.

VERS. 42.-- Et cum accederet. Dum puer ad Dominum accedit, eliditur, quia conversi ad Dominum plerumque a daemonio gravius pulsantur, ut vel ad vitia reducantur, vel de sua expulsione diabolus se vindicet. Sicut in principio nascentis Ecclesiae multa 282Bet gravia opposuit certamina illis quos suo regno subtrahi videbat.

VERS. 43.-- Et increpavit. Non puer vim patiens, sed diabolus vim inferens increpatur, quia qui peccantem vult sanare, vitium debet arguere et depellere, sed hominem amando refovere donec sanatum spiritualibus reddat patribus Ecclesiae.

VERS. 44.-- Ponite vos. Vos qui meo discipulatui adhaeretis, quibus arcana mea manifestius aperui, pretiosi sanguinis quo mundus redimendus est, eventum mente recondite, dum caeteri mirantur tanta facta divinae altitudinis. Futurum est ut tradatur. Inter magnalia potentiae divinae saepius replicat abjecta passionis humanae, ne subito veniens terreat, sed levius feratur praecogitata.

282CVERS. 45.-- At illi ignorabant. Haec ignorantia non tam de tarditate, quam de amore nascitur, quia adhuc carnales, et mysterii crucis ignari, quem Deum cognoverunt, moriturum credere nequeunt, sed sicut per figuras loquentem audire solebant, sic etiam quod de sua traditione loquebatur, figuraliter aliud significare putabant.

VERS. 46.-- Intravit autem cogitatio. Quia viderant Petrum et Jacobum et Joannem in montem seorsum ductos, secretumque aliquod eis esse traditum. Sed et Petro superius claves regni coelorum esse promissas, Ecclesiamque super eum esse aedificandam rati sunt; vel, istos tres caeteris, vel, omnibus apostolis Petrum esse praelatum: vel quia in tributi solutione Petrum ipsi Domino parificatum viderant, 282Dipsum caeteris praeferendum arbitrabantur. Sed sciendum est istam quaestionem et ante tributum et post tributum solutum esse factam.

VERS. 48.-- Quicunque susceperit puerum. Vel simpliciter: pauperes Christi ab his qui volunt esse majores, pro ejus docet esse honore suscipiendos. Vel parvulos malitia docet esse, ut puerilis innocentiae simplicitatem conservent, ut formam virtutis quam natura duce puer observat, ipsi pro nomine Christi sequantur ex industria. Me recipit. Qui imitatorem Christi recipit, Christum recipit, et qui imaginem Dei recipit, Deum recipit.

VERS. 49.-- Joannes dixit. Joannes diligens Deum et a Domino dilectus, excludendum putat a beneficio 283Aqui non utatur obsequio, ideo docetur neminem a bono quod ex parte habet, esse arcendum, sed ad hoc potius quod nondum habet esse provocandum. Tales etiam ob aliorum salutem admittendi censentur. Igitur in haereticis vel in malis catholicis, non sacramenta communia in quibus nobiscum sunt, et adversum nos non sunt, sed divisionem paci veritatique contrariam, qua adversum nos sunt, et Dominum non sequuntur nobiscum, detestari et prohibere debemus.

VERS. 50.-- Nolite prohibere. Qui enim. Non reprehenditur Joannes quia amore faciebat, sed docetur ut noverit infirmorum esse firmorumque distantiam, quia Dominus, etsi remunerat fortiores, non excludit infirmos.

283BVERS. 54.-- Domine, vis. (BED.) Apostoli adhuc rudes et modum vindicandi ignorantes, etc., usque ad talis potestas sanctis viris a Deo collata est.

Dicimus ut ignis descendat de coelo. Ad sermonem istorum ignis de coelo descenderet, quia sunt filii tonitrui. Et consumat illos? Et conversus increpavit illos et dixit. In similitudine Eliae, qui et propria manu et igne divinitus impetrato, presbyteros Baal morte affecit. Et in hoc quod apostoli potestatem talis prophetae sibi praesumunt, ostenditur in ipsis fuisse meritum prophetarum.

(AMBR.) Non peccant apostoli, qui legem sequuntur, etc., usque ad vindictam non quaerit qui non timet.

VERS. 55.-- Nescitis cujus spiritus estis. Cujus 283Cspiritu signati estis, cujus acta imitamini, nunc pie consulentes, sed in futuro juste judicantes.

VERS. 56.-- Filius hominis non venit animas perdere, sed salvare, etc. Perfecta virtus non habet studium ultionis, nec ulla est iracundia, ubi est charitatis plenitudo.

(BED.) Non semper in eos qui peccant est vindicandum, etc., usque ad a quibus hoc loco ignis arcetur.

VERS. 57.-- Dixit quidam. (ID.) Iste signorum magnitudine motus vult sequi, etc., usque ad et finxit obsequium discipuli, quae fictio per vulpem figuratur.

VERS. 58.-- Vulpes foveas habent. (ID.) Vulpes animal fallax, insidiis intentum, rapinas fraudis exercens, 283Detiam inter ipsa hominum hospitia habitans in foveis: ita haereticus domum fidei non habens alios in suam fraudem trahit et a fide seducit.

VERS. 59.-- Ait autem ad alterum. Attende Dominum non culpas aspernatum, sed fraudes, quia qui repudiavit fraudulentum, elegit innocentem, sed eum cujus patrem sciebat mortuum, illum patrem de quo dicitur: Obliviscere domum patris tui (Psal. XLIV), etc.

VERS. 60.-- Sine ut mortui. (BED.) Mystice. Non revocatur ab officio patris filius, etc., usque ad diaboli perfidias in se sepelire non debet.

VERS. 61.-- Renuntiare his. Si discipulus Deum secuturus arguitur, quod renuntiare domui velit, quid 284Afiet illis qui nulla utilitate saepe visitat domos suorum quas dereliquerunt.

CHAPTER IX.

  • 277B

VERS. 1.-- And Jesus, having called them together. The power of signs being first granted, they are sent to preach the kingdom of God, that the greatness of the deeds might also bear witness to the greatness of the promises, and that the power shown might give credit to the words, and that they who preached new things might also do new things.

VERS. 3.-- Take nothing. In Mark it is read, that they should take nothing for the way but a staff only (Marc. VI). But it must be known that both were commanded by the Lord: that they should carry nothing but a staff, and that they should not carry even a staff. Not to carry a staff is not to be anxious about the things which are necessary for human sustenance, because these are owed to preachers by those to whom they preach, when they can be received without scandal. To carry 277Cnothing but a staff is to use that power of receiving necessities which has been given to preachers. Two coats. A single garment is whatever is necessary to a man according to the character of the place or the time. A double one is what is beyond necessity. Allegorically, in the two coats he forbids duplicity.

VERS. 4.-- There abide. Do not run about through the houses and change the rights of an inviolable hospitality. Whence according to Matthew, the house in which they ought to remain must first be chosen, that the necessity of changing lodging may be taken away.

VERS. 5.-- The dust of your feet. The dust is shaken off in testimony of their labour, that they entered into the city, and that the preaching reached as far as them. 277DOr, it is shaken off, that they may receive nothing from those who have spurned the Gospel, not even the necessaries of life.

VERS. 8.-- John is risen. They who said that John, of whom they had seen no sign, had risen again, by the utmost malice denied that Jesus had risen again; they had seen many marvellous works, in whose passion many wonderful things had happened.

VERS. 10.-- They told. The apostles tell the Lord what they had done, and how they had taught, and how John suffered while they themselves were teaching. Whence it follows: And taking them. The nourishment of heavenly grace is imparted not to the idle, not to those residing in the city of the Synagogue, that is, of secular dignity, but to those seeking Christ amid the deserts. For those who do not turn away in loathing are received by Christ, and both spiritually and bodily, 278Aif they have need, they are healed. And note, that after the mention of John's passion is completed, Christ refreshes, because after the failing of the law the food of the Gospel begins. Finally, after the woman who bears the figure of the Church has been healed, and after the apostles have been sent to preach, mention is made of the refreshment, because through the apostles true refreshment takes place in the Church.

VERS. 11.-- They followed him. Not on beasts, not in carriages, but by the proper labour of their feet, that they might show the ardour of their mind. He healed. No one receives the food of Christ unless he has first been healed, because after the remission of sins the heavenly nourishment is bestowed.

VERS. 12.-- Now the day began. He refreshes them when the day has declined, because, either as the end of the ages draws near, or when the sun of justice had set, we were 278Bsaved from spiritual famine. And note the order of the mystery: first medicine is applied to the wounds by the remission of sins; afterwards the nourishment of the heavenly table is dispensed.

VERS. 13.-- Unless perhaps. The apostles had not yet understood that the faith of a believing people is not for sale, but they had known that we are rather to be redeemed by his own free foods.

VERS. 14.-- Five thousand. By the five thousand are signified those who, being still in a secular habit, know how to use well, through the five senses of the body, the outward things which they possess. Who are fed with five loaves, because such must still be instructed by the precepts of the law, and restrained by legal fear. For those who renounce the world entirely both are four thousand, because of the evangelical 278Cdoctrine, and are refreshed with seven loaves, that is, with the grace of the sevenfold Spirit.

VERS. 15.-- And all to sit down. According to another evangelist the five thousand sit down upon the grass, because those who still have bodily senses are delighted with better things. For all flesh is grass. The four thousand recline upon the earth, for it is more to press the earth than to lie upon grass.

VERS. 16.-- He distributed to his disciples. He calls forth the apostles to the breaking of bread, that, while they testify that they have none, the greatness of the sign may become the better known. He insinuates that through their teaching fasting hearts are daily to be fed.

VERS. 17.-- And they were filled. That the people, eating, 278Dis filled signifies that hunger is to be driven away for ever from the elect, because he who has received Christ's food will not hunger. That the apostles minister announces the future distribution of the Lord's body and blood. Twelve baskets. Why is more left over to the five thousand men, and less to the four thousand? Because the four thousand were with Christ for three days, and therefore received more of the heavenly pasture.

VERS. 18.-- And it came to pass. After the miracle, when the crowds wished to make him king, the crowd being dismissed, he fled and went up into the mountain alone to pray; and before the disciples went up into the boat, they were present with him. But he alone prays to the Father. For the saints can be joined to the Lord by faith and love, and can behold him as more excellent than men, and can follow his manner of life 279Ain the flesh with the steps of humility; but he alone penetrates the incomprehensible secrets of the Father's position, and he alone prays what he knows must be prayed. But he is not found to have prayed with the disciples, but prays alone, because human hearts do not grasp the counsel of God, nor can anyone be a partaker of the inner things with Christ. Whom do the crowds say that I am? Being about to search out the faith of the disciples, he first inquires the opinion of the common people, lest the faith of the apostles should seem to be established by common opinion, but by the acknowledgment of the truth. Rightly are they named crowds who bring forward different opinions about the Lord, whose speech and understanding is unstable and wandering; and distinguishing his own from them he says. But whom do you say that I am?

VERS. 19.-- John the Baptist. The opinion about John 279Bwas perhaps for this reason, because, placed in his mother's womb, he perceived the Lord's presence. About Elias, because he was caught up into heaven, and is believed to be about to come. About Jeremias, because he was sanctified in his mother's womb.

VERS. 20.-- But you. He who discourses ought to consider the persons of his hearers, lest he be derided before he is heard. The Christ of God. He embraced all things, who expressed both the nature and the name. For in the name of Christ there is the expression both of the godhead and of the incarnation, and the faith of the passion. The heretic, although he does not deny the name of Christ, nevertheless denies Christ, since he does not confess all the things that are Christ's.

VERS. 21.-- He charged them lest, etc. He bids the disciples be silent, that he may deceive the prince of the world, and that he may avoid boasting and teach humility. At the same time, lest the disciples, still rude 279Cand imperfect, should be crushed by the weight of a greater preaching.

VERS. 23.-- And he said. The things that pertain to faith in the Lord's nativity or passion he opened to the disciples alone, apart; but these things to all. Let him deny himself. Unless a man fall away from himself, he does not draw near to him who is above himself: and he does not lay hold of the newness to which he is called, unless he slay the oldness in which he was born; and when he ceases from vices let him also seek out virtues. Whence there is subjoined:

VERS. 24.-- For he that. (GREG.) As in time of persecution the soul must be laid down, so in peace earthly desires must be broken, that by how much a man seems the more secure, by so much he may be the more careful for his own keeping.

VERS. 26.-- Him the Son of man. The voice of profession does not suffice for the 279Dproof of faith, a voice which the profession of the generality defends from shame.

VERS. 27.-- Some standing here. He who stands with Christ does not taste death, because he who has merited fellowship with Christ will not have even the slightest sense of eternal death, and for him the order of living is not interrupted even in death. Till they see the kingdom. Because it was hard to offer the soul to dangers and the body to death, he sustains the weakness of the human mind by the reward of present things, lest it be broken by despair or weariness. For when the eternal glory has been seen, even in passing and for a brief moment, they are nevertheless rendered stronger against the adversities of the world. He promises therefore that the glory to come is to be seen in passing upon earth, that the eternal glory may be the more surely hoped for in heaven. Or 280Ahe promises that some of the disciples will see the spreading of the Church before they depart from the world by death, so that, raised up in the present against the glory of the world, they may be the more surely believed to be about to reign in heaven.

VERS. 28.-- After these words. Others, who count only six days, signify that after the six ages of the world the saints must rest from labour. Luke, who counts in both the first day on which it is promised and the last on which the promise is fulfilled, hints that we shall rise on the eighth. For as Christ, after the sixth day of the week on which he ascended the cross, and after the seventh day of the week on which he rested in the sepulchre, rose on the eighth day, so we, after the six ages of the world in which we suffer and labour for the Lord, and after the seventh of the rest of souls which meanwhile is passed in another life, shall rise in the eighth age. He took Peter. 280BHe names three only, because many are called, few chosen (Matth. XX), and those who keep the faith of the Trinity inviolate deserve to rejoice in the eternal vision. He who knows merits knows that the three apostles were more perfect than the rest. They signify those who have faith, hope, charity. Or by Peter, prelates or the married; by James, the penitent or the active; by John, virgins or the contemplative.

VERS. 29.-- And he was made. Being transfigured, he did not lose the substance of true flesh, but showed the glory either of his own or of our resurrection; and such as he then appeared to the apostles, such will he appear after the judgment to all the elect. For in the judgment itself he will appear both to the good and to the evil in the form of a servant, that they may see him whom they pierced. 280CAnd his raiment. The Lord's garments are the saints who have put on Christ. Which garment, while the Lord dwelt on earth, seemed contemptible and like that of others, but shines when the Lord ascends the mountain, because it hath not yet appeared what we shall be, but at length we shall be like to him, when we shall see him as he is. [Vulg. 1 John 3:2 "Scimus quoniam cum apparuerit, similes ei erimus: quoniam videbimus eum sicuti est," We know that, when he shall appear, we shall be like to him, because we shall see him as he is] Or the garments of the Word are the discourses of the Scriptures, and certain clothings of the divine understanding. Because as he himself appeared to those apostles in another form and his garment shone, so also to the eyes of thy mind the sense of the divine readings already grows white.

VERS. 30.-- Behold two men. It is to be noted that not the bodies or the souls of Elias or Moses appeared, but that those bodies were formed out of some underlying creature: it can also be believed that this was done 280Dby angelic ministry, so that angels took upon them their persons. A dead man and a living man appear, that they may signify that Christ was to die and afterwards to live, and that we should live to Christ and die to the world. To the apostles also he gives a sign from heaven, that their faith may be increased. But to the scribes tempting him he would not give one, that they might be hardened. And they were Moses and Elias, seen. By Moses are signified those of the lower regions; by Elias, the heavenly; by the apostles, the earthly, who shall come to the judgment.

VERS. 31.-- They spoke of his decease. Moses, that is the lawgiver, and Elias, the most eminent of the prophets, appear with the Word, that they may show that he is the one who spoke in the law and the prophets, and whom the law and the prophets promised. Who were seen not in the lowest places, but on the mountain, 281Abecause only those lofty in mind perceive the majesty of Scripture, which was fulfilled in the Lord.

VERS. 32.-- They were heavy with sleep. They are weighed down with sleep, because the sense of the human body is oppressed by the splendour of the incomprehensible godhead. Or they are weighed down with sleep, that they might see the glory of the resurrection after the rest of the body; and the saints will see the majesty of the Lord the more truly in proportion as they rejoice in the immortality of their flesh, in which they had conquered death. Then they shall behold Moses and Elias in glory, because they will better understand how one iota or one tittle shall not pass from the law (Matth. V), and how the Lord did not destroy the law and the prophets, but fulfilled them.

VERS. 33.-- It is good for us to be here. He who had climbed to the mountains does not wish to descend to earthly things, 281Bbut always to persevere on high; and Peter stands in the person of those entering the kingdom of God. It is good for us to be here. It is good to be in the kingdom of God. As if he should say: We alone shall enjoy this light, not the envious Jews. Three tabernacles. That is, words, thoughts, works. One for Moses. How do they know those whom they had not seen before? By the majesty coming upon them, as in the resurrection to come each one will know the other, and the one will understand through charity the things desired in the other.

VERS. 34.-- There came a cloud, and overshadowed them. Although Peter is ignorant out of weakness, yet the devotion of his service pleases. Whence also the cloud overshadows; this overshadowing is of the divine Spirit, which does not darken the affections of men, but reveals hidden things, whose perfect one is 281Cshown when the voice is heard saying: This is my Son. Because he sought a material tabernacle, he received the shade of a cloud, that he might learn that in the resurrection the saints are to be protected not by the covering of houses, but by the glory of the Holy Spirit. And they were afraid. They fear, because human frailty does not endure to bear the sight of a greater glory; and by how much the greater the things a man has sought, by so much the more does he sink down to lower things, if he has been ignorant of his own measure.

VERS. 36.-- And the voice. The voice of the Father, the terror being taken away, teaches Peter the truth. And note that, as when the Lord was baptized, so also when he was transfigured, the mystery of the Trinity is declared, because the glory which we confess in baptism we shall see in the resurrection. The Spirit in a dove, 281Dhere in a bright cloud, because he who now keeps the faith with a simple heart will then contemplate in the open light of vision what he believed, and by that very grace by which he shall be illuminated he shall be protected. Jesus was found alone. Not Elias, not Moses, but he whom alone you see: for him the tabernacle must be fixed in the heart. They therefore withdraw when the Lord began to be pointed out, lest the Father's voice should be thought to have been sent to the servants.

VERS. 37.-- And it came to pass on the next day, as they came down from the mountain. According to the quality of merits, the Lord daily ascends for some, while by lifting them up more loftily he glorifies the perfect, whose conversation is in heaven, and instructs and teaches them of eternal things which cannot be heard by the crowd. For others he descends, while he comforts and teaches and chastens the earthly and the beginners. There met 282Athem a crowd. On the mountain he prays, teaches, shows forth his majesty, opens the Father's voice to the apostles; coming down, he is received by the crowd, is beset by the weeping of the wretched, reproaches the sins of unbelief, drives out the evil spirits.

VERS. 39.-- And behold a spirit seizeth him. Matthew describes him as a lunatic, Mark as deaf and dumb. But it signifies those who are changed like the moon, never remaining in the same state, but who wax and wane through diverse vices, who neither confess the faith, nor are willing to hear the discourse of that faith.

VERS. 42.-- And as he was coming. While the boy draws near to the Lord he is dashed down, because those converted to the Lord are for the most part more grievously assailed by the demon, that either they may be brought back to vices, or that the devil may avenge himself for his own expulsion.
