As in the beginning of the Church's birth he set many 282Band grievous struggles against those whom he saw being withdrawn from his kingdom.

VERS. 43.-- And he rebuked. Not the boy suffering violence, but the devil doing violence is rebuked, because he who wishes to heal a sinner ought to reprove and drive out the vice, but to cherish the man by loving him, until he render him healed to the spiritual fathers of the Church.

VERS. 44.-- Lay you up. You who cleave to my discipleship, to whom I have more openly disclosed my secrets, lay up in mind the issue of the precious blood by which the world is to be redeemed, while the rest wonder at such great deeds of the divine loftiness. It shall come to pass that he be delivered. Among the great works of the divine power he the more often repeats the abject things of his human passion, lest, coming suddenly, it should terrify, but that, thought upon beforehand, it may be more lightly borne.

282CVERS. 45.-- But they knew not. This ignorance arises not so much from slowness as from love, because, being still carnal and ignorant of the mystery of the cross, they cannot believe that he whom they had known to be God was to die; but as they were accustomed to hear him speaking through figures, so also they thought that what he spoke concerning his own betrayal signified figuratively something else.

VERS. 46.-- And a thought entered. Because they had seen Peter and James and John led apart into the mountain, and that some secret had been delivered to them. And they thought that above the keys of the kingdom of heaven had been promised to Peter, and that the Church was to be built upon him; or that these three were set before the rest, or that Peter was set before all the apostles: or, because they had seen Peter made equal to the Lord himself in the payment of the tribute, 282Dthey judged that he was to be preferred to the rest. But it must be known that this question was raised both before the tribute and after the tribute was paid.

VERS. 48.-- Whosoever shall receive a child. Or simply: he teaches that Christ's poor are to be received for his honour by those who wish to be greater. Or he teaches that they should be little ones in malice, that they may keep the simplicity of childish innocence, so that the form of virtue which a child observes under nature's leading they themselves may follow of set purpose for the name of Christ. Receiveth me. He who receives an imitator of Christ receives Christ, and he who receives the image of God receives God.

VERS. 49.-- John said. John, loving God and beloved of the Lord, thinks that he is to be shut out from the benefit 283Awho does not use the service; therefore he is taught that no one is to be kept back from the good which he has in part, but is rather to be called forward to that which he has not yet. Such men are judged to be admitted even for the salvation of others. Therefore in heretics or in bad Catholics we ought to detest and forbid, not the sacraments held in common, in which they are with us and are not against us, but the division contrary to peace and to truth, by which they are against us and do not follow the Lord with us.

VERS. 50.-- Forbid not. For he that. John is not reproved, because he acted out of love, but he is taught, that he may know that there is a distance between the weak and the strong, because the Lord, although he rewards the stronger, does not exclude the weak.

283BVERS. 54.-- Lord, wilt thou. (BED.) The apostles, still rude and ignorant of the measure of vengeance, etc., as far as such a power has been conferred by God upon holy men.

We say that fire come down from heaven. At the word of these men fire would come down from heaven, because they are the sons of thunder. And consume them? And turning he rebuked them and said. In the likeness of Elias, who both by his own hand and by fire divinely obtained put the elders of Baal to death. And in this, that the apostles presume to themselves the power of such a prophet, it is shown that the merit of the prophets was in them.

(AMBR.) The apostles do not sin, who follow the law, etc., as far as he does not seek vengeance who does not fear.

VERS. 55.-- You know not of what spirit you are. By whose 283Cspirit you have been sealed, whose deeds you imitate, now taking counsel mercifully, but in the future judging justly.

VERS. 56.-- The Son of man came not to destroy souls, but to save, etc. Perfect virtue has no zeal for vengeance, nor is there any anger where there is the fulness of charity.

(BED.) Vengeance is not always to be taken upon those who sin, etc., as far as from whom in this place the fire is kept back.

VERS. 57.-- A certain man said. (ID.) This man, moved by the greatness of the signs, wishes to follow, etc., as far as and he feigned the service of a disciple, which feigning is figured by the fox.

VERS. 58.-- The foxes have holes. (ID.) The fox is a deceitful animal, intent upon ambushes, practising the robberies of fraud, 283Ddwelling in holes even amid the very lodgings of men: so the heretic, not having the house of faith, draws others into his own fraud and seduces them from the faith.

VERS. 59.-- And he said to another. Note that the Lord spurned not faults but frauds, because he who rejected the fraudulent man chose the innocent one; but him whose father he knew to be dead, that father of whom it is said: Forget thy father's house (Psal. XLIV), etc.

VERS. 60.-- Suffer that the dead. (BED.) Mystically. The son is not called back from his duty to his father, etc., as far as he ought not to bury the devil's treacheries in himself.

VERS. 61.-- To take leave of those. If a disciple about to follow God is rebuked because he wishes to take leave of his house, what 284Awill become of those who often, with no profit, visits the houses of their own people which they had forsaken.

CAPUT X.

VERS. 1.-- Post haec autem, etc. (BED.) Sicut in apostolis est, forma episcoporum, etc., usque ad sicut sol triduanum suae lucis ambitum per septuaginta duas horas efficere solet.

Misit illos binos. Per hoc quod binos mittit, innuitur quod nemo praedicationis officium debet suscipere, qui erga alium charitatem non habet. Vel bini mittuntur, secundum quod bina animalia missa sunt in arcam, id est masculus et femina, immunda prius in carnali generatione, sed mundata Ecclesiae sacramento per spiritualem gratiam in discipulorum praedicatione.

284BVERS. 2.-- Messis quidem multa, operarii autem pauci. Messis, turba credentium, operarii apostoli et sequaces eorum. Et licet messis verbo Dei sit sata, tamen culturae laborem et sollicitum munus operarii requirit, ne aves coeli sparsa semina dissipent.

VERS. 3.-- Sicut agnos inter lupos. Sicut lupus ovibus, sic haereticus insidiatur fidelibus. Et sicut lupus ovile circumit nocte, non audens intrare, canis somnum pastoris absentiam vel desidiam explorans, sic haereticus nocte suae interpretationis fideles decipere conatur Ecclesiam non intrans, pastores Ecclesiae vel vitare, vel necare, vel in exsilium mittere contendit. Lupus natura corporis rigidus, se inflectere facile non potest, sic haereticus intentionem 284Cduri cordis non solet ab errore revocare.

VERS. 4.-- Nolite ergo portare sacculum neque peram. Tanta praedicatori debet esse fiducia in Deo, ut praesentis vitae sumptus etsi non praevideat, tamen sibi non defecturos certissime sciat, ne dum occupatur mens ad temporalia, minus praedicet aeterna. Neminem per viam salutaveritis. Festinare jubet contendere, ne alicujus obvii confabulatione reflectantur ab injuncto itinere. Nec simpliciter ait: Neminem salutaveritis cum sit usus humanitatis alios salutare, sed addidit, in via, quia non salutationis sedulitas aufertur, sed obstaculum impediendae devotionis aboletur, ut quando divina mandantur, paulisper sequestrentur humana.

VERS. 7.-- In eadem autem domo. Si pax vestra 284Drecipitur, dignum est ut in ea maneatis, carnalia stipendia ab eis accipientes, quibus coelestia offertis. Ecce qui saccum et peram prohibuit, sumptus ex praedicatione concedit, sic tamen ut oblato cibo et potu sint contenti. Manete. Non est de domo in domum vaga facilitate demigrandum, ut in hospitali amore servetur constantia, ne coalita amicitiae necessitudo facile resolvatur. Dignus est enim. Nota quod uni operi praedicatorum duae mercedes debentur: una in via, quae nos in labore sustentat; alia in patria, quae nos in resurrectione remunerat.

VERS. 8.-- Et in quamcunque civitatem. Descripto diverso domus hospitio, quid etiam in civitatibus agere debeant docet, piis scilicet, in omnibus 285Acommunicare, ab impiorum per omnia societate secerni.

VERS. 11.-- Etiam pulverem. Pulverem excutere jubet: vel ad contestationem terreni laboris, quem pro illis inaniter susceperunt; vel ut ostendant usque adeo se ab ipsis nihil terrenum quaerere, ut etiam pulvere de terra eorum non sibi patiantur adhaerere.

VERS. 12.-- Quia Sodomis in die illa. Sodomitae, etsi in vitiis exardebant, et inhospitales erant, tamen apud illos nulli hospites tales quales apud Judaeos prophetae, quales apostoli reperti sunt. Loth inter eos etsi justus, non tamen aliquid docuit, nulla signa fecit. Et ideo cui multum donatum est, multum ab eo quaeritur, et potentes potenter tormenta 285Bpatientur (Sap. VI).

VERS. 13.-- Vae tibi, Corozaim. Corozaim, Bethsaida, Capharnaum, et Tyberias civitates sunt Galileae, in littore laci Genesareth. Has plangit Dominus, quia post tanta miracula et virtutes non poenituerunt: et sunt pejores gentilibus, naturale solummodo jus dissipantibus, quia post descriptae legis contemptum, Filium quoque Dei conculcare, gratiamque ingrati spernere non timuerunt. Et cinere sedentes. Impleta videmus verba Salvatoris, quia cum illae civitates praedictae, Domino praesente credere noluerunt, Tyrus et Sidon olim fuerunt amicae David et Salomoni, et postea evangelizantibus Christi discipulis, devote fidem susceperunt. Quare autem sit praedicatum non credituris, et non praedicatum 285Ccredituris, novit ille qui omnia novit.

VERS. 16.-- Qui vos audit me audit. In audiendo vel spernendo Evangelii praedicatorem, quisque sciat se non viles personas, sed ipsum Salvatorem spernere vel audire, quia in discipulo magister auditur, et in Filio Pater honoratur.

VERS. 17.-- Domine, etiam. Bene confitentur deferentes honorem nomini Christi: sed quia infirma adhuc fide, gaudent in virtutibus, et de signorum operatione efferuntur, exemplo terrentur, et ad humilitatem revocantur. Quia si diabolus propter superbiam de coelo est praecipitatus, multo magis isti de terra editi, si superbierunt, humiliabuntur.

VERS. 19.-- Supra serpentes et scorpiones. Serpentes 285Dsunt mali homines vel daemones qui inchoandis virtutibus venena pravae persuasionis objiciunt. Scorpiones, qui consummandas virtutes ad finem vitiare contendunt.

VERS. 20.-- Nolite gaudere, quia spiritus vobis, etc. Malos spiritus ejicere et alias virtutes facere, aliquando non est meritum illius qui illa operatur, sed invocatio nominis Christi hoc agit ad condemnationem eorum qui invocant, vel ad utilitatem eorum qui vident et audiunt: ubi licet homines despiciant signa facientes, Deum tamen honorant, ad cujus invocationem miracula fiunt. Prohibet gaudere de humiliatione diaboli, qui propter superbiam cecidit, sed gaudeant de sua sublimatione: 286Aut unde illi ceciderunt sublevati, isti ascendant humiliati. Gaudete autem. Si quis coelestia sive terrestria opera gesserit, per haec quasi litteris adornatus apud Dei memoriam aeternaliter est affixus.

VERS. 21.-- In ipsa hora. Cum de subjectione immundorum spirituum, de nominibus scriptis in coelo loqueretur, ostendit per quid haec tanta celsitudo data sit hominibus, scilicet per humilitatem fidei Christianae. Confiteor tibi, Domine. Gratias ago, quia apostolis adventus mei aperuisti sacramenta, quae ignorant scribae et Pharisaei, qui sibi sapientes videntur. Revelasti ea. Non ait, insipientibus et hebetibus, sed parvulis, id est humilibus, ut probet se tumorem damnasse, non acumen. Quia sic. Non rationem reddit cur hos 286Belegit, illos reprobaverit, sed sic Deo placitum dicit. In quo accipimus exemplum humilitatis, ne temere discutiamus de supernis consiliis.

Omnia mihi, etc. Commendata humilitate, per quam ad fidem venitur, pulchre de ipsa fide locus connectitur, cum omnia sibi tradita dicit, non elementa quae ipse creavit, sed illos parvulos, quibus Pater sacramenta Filii revelavit. Cum audis omnia, agnoscis omnipotentem, non decolorem, non degenerem Patris. Cum audis tradita, confiteris Filium cui per naturam unius substantiae omnia jure sunt propria, non dono collata per gratiam.

VERS. 24.-- Quod multi prophetae. Prophetae et justi a longe Domini gloriam viderunt per speculum 286Cet in aenigmate; sed apostoli praesentem Dominum habentes, et ab ipso quae volebant interrogantes, non per angelos vel varias visionum species docebantur.

VERS. 25.-- Legisperitus surrexit. Legis doctor qui sine fide in lege jacebat, surgit ut stet: stat dum magistrum vocat, sed et stando tentat Deum tacendo quod erat.

VERS. 27.-- Ille respondens dixit. Dixit quod legerat, sed non implevit quod scriptum erat, quia in praesentia habebat Dominum quem diligere debebat, sed hunc magistrum, non Deum, vocat.

VERS. 29.-- Ille autem volens. Legisperitus ut sapienter respondisse dicatur, primum se legis fatetur ignorare mandatum, quia humiliari recusans, et seipsum justificare volens, arcana non potest videre 286Dquae revelantur parvulis Christi; cui Christus ita responsum temperat, ut et omnis qui misericordiam facit proximus intelligatur, et specialiter ipse Dei Filius qui nobis per humanitatem proximus factus est designetur. Quis est meus. Nemo proximior homini quam Deus, qui intrinsecus et extrinsecus novit, et omnia curare potest, sed omnis incredulus vel tentator nec Deum nec hominem proximum habet.

Homo quidam, etc. (AUG.) Homo iste Adam intelligitur in genere humano, etc., usque ad proximior fuit quam sacerdos vel Levita ejusdem gentis.

VERS. 31.-- Accidit autem, etc. Sacerdos Dei legem annuntiat: descendit quidem lex per Moysen 287Ain mundum, et nullam sanitatem contulit hujusmodi. Descendit Levita, qui typum ostendit prophetarum, sed et hic nullum sanat, quia lex peccata arguit, sed pertransit, quia indulgentiam non largitur.

VERS. 33.-- Misericordia motus. Hoc de sacerdote et Levita non est dictum, quia lex non habet misericordiam, sed judicium et vindictam.

VERS. 34.-- Duxit in stabulum. Non domum, sed stabulum vocat, quo nomine miserias, et fetores hujus vitae signat, ne homo in hoc exsilio tanquam in patria gaudeat.

VERS. 37.-- Qui fecit. Cognatio non facit proximum, sed misericordia, quae misericordia est secundum naturam. Nihil enim tam secundum naturam quam naturae juvare consortem.

287BVERS. 38.-- Factum est, dum, etc. Habito sermone de dilectione Dei et proximi, supponitur exemplum utriusque dilectionis, non tam in solo verbo, quam in operis exhibitione. Per istas enim duas sorores, duae significantur vitae spirituales. Per Martham, operibus actuosa devotio, qua proximo in charitate sociamur. Per Mariam religiosa mentis intentio in Dei verbo, qua in Dei amore suspiramus. Activa panem vel corporalem esurienti, vel doctrinae ignoranti tribuit, errantem corrigit, superbum ad humilitatem revocat, quae singulis expediant dispensat: contemplativa, charitatem Dei et proximi retinet, sed ab exteriori actione quiescit, soli conditoris desiderio inhaeret, et calcatis omnibus curis ad videndum faciem Creatoris inardescit, et desiderat misceri 287Csupernis civibus, de aeterna in conspectu Domini incorruptione gaudentibus. In domum suam. Intrante Jesu in domum, vita iniqua, etsi aliquando fuerat, aufugit, remanent duae vitae innocentes, laboriosa et otiosa, inter eas medius est ipse fons vitae.

VERS. 39.-- Sedens secus. Quanto humilius sedet, tanto amplius cadit. Aqua confluit ad convallem de tumoribus collis. Maria sedet, quia contemplativa pacatis vitiorum tumultibus, optata jam in Christo mentis quiete fruitur. Martha stat, quia activa laboriosa desudat in certamine.

VERS. 40.-- Martha autem. Intenta erat Maria quomodo pasceretur a Domino, intenta erat Martha: quomodo pasceret Dominum: haec convivium parat Domino, in convivio Domini illa jam jucundatur.

287DVERS. 41.-- Et respondens. Maria non respondet, sed causam suam tanquam otiosa committit judici. Si enim pararet respondendi sermonem remitteret audiendi intentionem. Martha, Martha. Repetitio indicium est dilectionis, vel forte monendae intentionis, ut audiret attentius.

VERS. 42.-- Porro unum est. Praeponitur unum multis. Non enim a multis unum, sed multa ab uno, et tamen multa sunt necessaria tendentibus ad unum. Maria optimam. Non reprehenditur pars Marthae, quia et ipsa bona, sed laudatur pars Mariae, quae quare sit optima, subinfertur.

CHAPTER X.

VERS. 1.-- And after these things, etc. (BED.) Just as in the apostles there is the form of bishops, etc., as far as just as the sun is wont to accomplish the three-day circuit of its light in seventy-two hours.

He sent them two and two. By this, that he sends them two and two, it is intimated that no one ought to take up the office of preaching who has not charity toward another. Or they are sent two by two according as the animals were sent into the ark two by two, that is, male and female, unclean before in carnal generation, but cleansed by the sacrament of the Church through spiritual grace in the preaching of the disciples.

284BVERS. 2.-- The harvest indeed is great, but the labourers are few. The harvest is the crowd of believers; the labourers are the apostles and those who follow them. And although the harvest has been sown by the word of God, still it requires the toil of tillage and the careful service of a labourer, lest the birds of heaven scatter the seeds that have been sown.

VERS. 3.-- As lambs among wolves. As the wolf lies in wait for the sheep, so does the heretic for the faithful. And as the wolf goes about the sheepfold by night, not daring to enter, scouting out the dog's sleep, the shepherd's absence or sloth, so the heretic tries to deceive the faithful by the night of his own interpretation, not entering the Church, and strives either to avoid the pastors of the Church, or to kill them, or to send them into exile. The wolf, stiff by the nature of its body, cannot easily bend itself; so the heretic is not wont to call back the purpose 284Cof his hard heart from error.

VERS. 4.-- Carry therefore neither purse nor scrip. So great ought a preacher's confidence in God to be, that even if he does not foresee the means of the present life, he yet knows most certainly that they will not fail him, lest, while his mind is taken up with things temporal, he preach the eternal things less. Salute no man by the way. He bids them make haste and press on, lest they be turned aside from the journey enjoined them by conversation with someone they meet. Nor does he say simply, Salute no man, since it is a usage of human kindness to salute others, but he added, by the way, because it is not the diligence of greeting that is taken away, but the obstacle that hinders devotion is done away with, so that, when divine things are commanded, human things may be set aside for a little.

VERS. 7.-- And in the same house. If your peace be 284Dreceived, it is fitting that you remain in it, receiving carnal wages from those to whom you offer heavenly things. Behold, he who forbade purse and scrip grants means from preaching, yet in such a way that they be content with the food and drink set before them. Remain. There must be no removing from house to house with a roving readiness, so that constancy may be preserved in the love of hospitality, lest the close bond of friendship once grown up be easily dissolved. For he is worthy. Note that two wages are owed to the one work of preachers: one on the way, which sustains us in labour; another in the fatherland, which rewards us in the resurrection.

VERS. 8.-- And into whatsoever city. Having described the various lodging of a house, he teaches what they ought to do in cities as well: namely, to have fellowship in all things with the godly, 285Aand to be kept apart in all things from the company of the ungodly.

VERS. 11.-- Even the dust. He bids them shake off the dust: either as a testimony of the earthly labour which they undertook for them in vain; or that they may show that to such a degree do they seek nothing earthly from them, that they do not even suffer the dust from their land to cleave to them.

VERS. 12.-- That for Sodom in that day. The Sodomites, although they burned in vices and were inhospitable, yet among them no guests such as the prophets among the Jews, such as the apostles, were found. Lot, though just among them, still taught nothing, wrought no signs. And therefore to whom much has been given, much is required of him, and the mighty shall mightily 285Bsuffer torments (Sap. VI) [Vulg. Lc 12:48 cui multum datum est, multum quaeretur ab eo, future; the second clause is Sap 6:7, and the two are joined here under one citation].

VERS. 13.-- Woe to thee, Corozaim. Corozaim, Bethsaida, Capharnaum and Tiberias are cities of Galilee, on the shore of the lake of Genesareth. These the Lord laments, because after so many miracles and mighty works they did not repent: and they are worse than the Gentiles, who squander only the natural law, because after their contempt of the written law they did not fear also to trample upon the Son of God and, ungrateful, to spurn his grace. And sitting in ashes. We see the words of the Saviour fulfilled, for whereas those foresaid cities, with the Lord present, would not believe, Tyre and Sidon were once friends of David and Solomon, and afterwards, when the disciples of Christ preached the Gospel, devoutly received the faith. But why it was preached to those who would not believe, and not preached 285Cto those who would believe, he knows who knows all things.

VERS. 16.-- He that heareth you heareth me. In hearing or in spurning a preacher of the Gospel, let each man know that he spurns or hears not persons of no account, but the Saviour himself, because in the disciple the master is heard, and in the Son the Father is honoured.

VERS. 17.-- Lord, even. They confess well, offering honour to the name of Christ: but because, their faith being still weak, they rejoice in mighty works and are lifted up by the working of signs, they are made afraid by an example and called back to humility. For if the devil was cast headlong from heaven for pride, much more shall these, brought forth from the earth, be humbled if they have been proud.

VERS. 19.-- Upon serpents and scorpions. Serpents 285Dare evil men or demons who cast the venom of wicked persuasion against virtues at their beginning. Scorpions are those who strive to corrupt at the end virtues that are to be brought to completion.

VERS. 20.-- Rejoice not in this, that spirits unto you, etc. To cast out evil spirits and to work other mighty deeds is sometimes not the merit of him who works them, but the invocation of the name of Christ does this, either to the condemnation of those who invoke it, or to the profit of those who see and hear: where, though men despise those who work the signs, they yet honour God, at whose invocation the miracles come to pass. He forbids them to rejoice over the humbling of the devil, who fell through pride, but let them rejoice over their own exaltation: 286Aso that whence those, being lifted up, fell, these, being humbled, may ascend. But rejoice. If anyone has done heavenly or earthly works, by these he is, as though adorned with letters, fastened eternally in the memory of God.

VERS. 21.-- In that same hour. When he was speaking of the subjection of unclean spirits and of the names written in heaven, he showed by what means so great a loftiness was given to men, namely by the humility of the Christian faith. I confess to thee, O Lord. I give thanks, because thou hast opened to the apostles the sacraments of my coming, which the scribes and Pharisees, who seem wise to themselves, do not know. Thou hast revealed them. He does not say, to the unwise and dull, but to little ones, that is, to the humble, to prove that he condemned swelling, not sharpness. For so. He does not render a reason why he chose 286Bthese and reprobated those, but says that it so pleased God. In which we receive an example of humility, that we should not rashly discuss the counsels above.

All things are delivered to me, etc. Humility being commended, through which one comes to faith, a passage concerning that same faith is beautifully joined on, when he says that all things are delivered to him, not the elements which he himself created, but those little ones to whom the Father revealed the sacraments of the Son. When thou hearest all things, thou acknowledgest one almighty, not discoloured, not degenerate from the Father. When thou hearest delivered, thou confessest the Son, to whom by the nature of one substance all things are by right his own, not conferred as a gift through grace.

VERS. 24.-- That many prophets. The prophets and the just saw the glory of the Lord from afar, through a glass 286Cand in a dark manner; but the apostles, having the Lord present and asking of him what they would, were not taught through angels or through various kinds of visions.

VERS. 25.-- A lawyer stood up. The doctor of the law, who was lying in the law without faith, rises that he may stand: he stands while he calls him Master, but even in standing he tempts God by keeping silent about what he was.

VERS. 27.-- He answering said. He said what he had read, but did not fulfil what was written, because he had present with him the Lord whom he ought to love, yet he calls him Master, not God.

VERS. 29.-- But he, willing. The lawyer, that he may be said to have answered wisely, first confesses that he is ignorant of the commandment of the law, because, refusing to be humbled and willing to justify himself, he cannot see the hidden things 286Dthat are revealed to the little ones of Christ; to whom Christ so tempers his answer that both everyone who does mercy may be understood to be a neighbour, and especially the Son of God himself, who through his humanity has been made neighbour to us, may be designated. Who is my. No one is nearer to man than God, who knows him within and without and can care for all things; but every unbeliever or tempter has neither God nor man for a neighbour.

A certain man, etc. (AUG.) That man is understood to be Adam in the human race, etc., as far as he was nearer than the priest or the Levite of the same nation.

VERS. 31.-- And it happened, etc. The priest announces the law of God: the law indeed came down through Moses 287Ainto the world, and conferred no health upon such a one. The Levite came down, who shows the type of the prophets, but he too heals no one, because the law reproves sins, but passes by, since it does not bestow pardon.

VERS. 33.-- Being moved with mercy. This is not said of the priest and the Levite, because the law has not mercy, but judgment and vengeance.

VERS. 34.-- He brought him to an inn. He calls it not a house but a stable, by which name he marks the miseries and the stenches of this life, lest a man rejoice in this exile as though in his fatherland.

VERS. 37.-- He that did. Kinship does not make a neighbour, but mercy, which mercy is according to nature. For nothing is so according to nature as to help a partner of one's nature.

287BVERS. 38.-- It came to pass, as, etc. A discourse having been held about the love of God and of neighbour, an example of both loves is set down, not so much in word alone as in the showing forth of work. For by these two sisters two spiritual lives are signified. By Martha, a devotion busy in works, by which we are joined to our neighbour in charity. By Mary, the religious intention of the mind upon the word of God, by which we sigh in the love of God. The active life gives bread, either bodily to one that hungers, or of doctrine to one that is ignorant; it corrects the erring, calls back the proud to humility, dispenses what is expedient for each. The contemplative holds fast the charity of God and of neighbour, but rests from outward action, cleaves to the desire of the Creator alone, and, all cares being trodden underfoot, burns to see the face of the Creator, and longs to be mingled 287Cwith the citizens above, who rejoice in eternal incorruption in the sight of the Lord. Into her house. When Jesus enters into the house, the wicked life, even if once it had been there, flees away; there remain two innocent lives, the laborious and the leisured, and between them, in the midst, is the very fount of life.

VERS. 39.-- Sitting beside. The more humbly she sits, the more abundantly does it fall. Water flows together into the valley from the swellings of the hill. Mary sits, because the contemplative life, the tumults of the vices being stilled, now enjoys the longed-for quiet of the mind in Christ. Martha stands, because the laborious active life sweats in the contest.

VERS. 40.-- But Martha. Mary was intent on how she might be fed by the Lord; Martha was intent on how she might feed the Lord: the latter prepares a banquet for the Lord, in the Lord's banquet the former already takes her delight.

287DVERS. 41.-- And answering. Mary does not answer, but, as one at leisure, commits her cause to the judge. For if she were preparing words to answer, she would slacken her intentness on hearing. Martha, Martha. The repetition is a token of love, or perhaps of an intention to admonish, that she might listen more attentively.

VERS. 42.-- But one thing is. The one is set before the many. For the one is not from the many, but the many from the one; and yet many things are necessary to those who tend toward the one. Mary the best. Martha's part is not blamed, since it too is good, but Mary's part is praised, and why it is best is added below.

CAPUT XI.

VERS. 1.-- Et factum est. Post historiam sororum 288A(quae duas vitas significant) inducit Dominum orasse, et discipulos ad orandum informasse, quia et oratio quam docuit utriusque vitae in se continet mysterium, et ipsarum perfectio vitarum non nostris viribus, sed precibus est obtinenda. Ut cessavit. Ideo saepe Salvator orans inducitur, ut discipulos ad orationis studium inducat. Unde finita oratione, a discipulis sciscitatur, ut doceat qualiter debeant orare.

VERS. 5.-- Quis vestrum habet amicum. Postquam formam orationis rogantibus proposuit, instantiam etiam et frequentiam orandi illis injungit, ut non solum diebus, sed etiam noctibus oratio deferatur, qui enim media nocte panes ab amico petit, et in petendi intentione persistit, non fraudatur orans.

VERS. 7.-- Noli mihi. Non aufert spem impetrandi, 288Bsed vehementius accendit desiderium orandi, ostensa difficultate consequendi. Jam ostium. Ostium quod Paulus sibi aperiri petit ad loquendum mysterium Christi, et quod Joannes sibi apertum vidit, intelligentia est divini sermonis, quod est clausum tempore famis verbi Dei, cum intelligentia non datur. Et pueri, id est, illi qui evangelicam sapientiam tanquam panem erogantes per orbem terrarum praedicaverunt, jam cum Domino sunt in aeterna quiete, et tamen orando efficitur, ut orans accipiat intellectum ab ipso Domino, etiamsi homo desit per quem sapientia praedicetur. Non possum surgere. (AMBR.) Nemo timet ne excitet dormientem, quem scit semper orare vigilantem.

VERS. 8.-- Propter improbitatem tamen ejus, etc. 288CMultae sunt insidiae, et gravis corporis somnus, quia si dormire mens incipit, vigorem suae virtutis amittit. Excita ergo somnum tuum, ut pulses Christi ostium, ut cum intraveris aperiantur tibi thesauri absconditi, et tenebrosi in Christo Jesu.

VERS. 9.-- Petite et dabitur vobis. Si amicus homo surgit, et dat non amicitia, sed taedio compulsus, quanto magis dat Deus qui sine taedio largissime donat quod petitur. Ne ergo animus ab erroris vanitate conversus spirituali inopia diutius tabescat, petamus epulas verbi, quaeramus amicum qui det, pulsemus ostium ubi servantur abscondita. Magnam enim spem tribuit, qui promittendo non decipit. Omnis enim qui petit accipit. Opus est perseverantia, ut consequamur. Quod si petenti datur, et quaerens 288Dinvenit, et pulsanti aperitur, cui ergo non datur, et qui non invenit, et cui non aperitur, apparet quod non bene quaesierit, petierit et pulsaverit.

VERS. 10.-- Quaerite et invenietis.--Prope est Dominus omnibus invocantibus eum in veritate, et voluntatem timentium se faciet (Psal. XLIV). Alios qui non ita petunt redarguit Jacobus dicens: Petitis et non accipitis, eo quod male petatis (Jac. IV), quia perseveratis in peccatis.

VERS. 11.-- Quis autem ex vobis patrem petit panem, nunquid lapidem, etc. (BED.) Mystice. Panis signat charitatem propter majorem appetitum et necessarium usum, etc., usque ad in ea quae ante sunt se extendat.

289AVERS. 13.-- Quanto magis. Aperta comparatio. Si enim homo peccator adhuc fragili carne gravatus, petentibus filiis temporalia dare non abnegat, multo magis Pater coelestis filiis suo timore et amore praeditis, non deficientia bona largitur in coelis.

VERS. 14.-- Et erat ejiciens. Post praedicationis verba quae humilibus proposuit etiam miracula operatur, ut qui praedicanti non credunt, visis miraculis credant. Matthaeus hunc daemoniacum etiam caecum fuisse dicit. In quo tria miracula a Domino fiunt: videt, loquitur, a daemone liberatur. Quod autem tunc in uno carnaliter factum est, quotidie fit in conversione gentium, ut primum expulso daemone, et omni spurcitia idololatriae, fidei lumen videant, deinde tacentia prius ora ad laudem Dei laxarentur.

289BVERS. 15.-- In Beelzebub. Ninus rex, conditor Ninive, Belo patri suo statuam consecravit, eique divinos honores instituit, cujus simulacri similitudinem suscipientes Chaldaei Bel vocabant. Palaestini suscipientes, Baal dicebant, Moabitae Beelphegor. Judaei autem unius Dei cultores propter derisionem gentilium Beelzebub appellabant. Baal enim vir dicitur, Zebub musca, inde Beelzebub, id est, vir muscarum, sive habens muscas, propter sordes cruoris, qui in templo ejus immolabatur. Asserebant etiam Judaei in hoc simulacro principem daemoniorum habitare, per cujus potestatem dicebant Jesum daemonia pellere.

VERS. 18.-- Ipse autem ut vidit cogitationes eorum. Hoc sibi proprium Dominus reservavit, ut cogitationes 289Cintueatur et judicet. Proprium est Judaeorum quaerere signa, unde Paulus: Judaei signa petunt, gentes sapientiam (I Cor. I). Ipse autem ut vidit. Non ad verba, sed ad cogitata respondet: in quo dat certum potentiae suae signum qua secreta cordium rimatur. Juxta litteram patet, quod omne regnum solidum, et firmum si per partes dividitur, in solitudinem redigitur, vacuatum ab habitatoribus, et domus supra domum cadet cum habitatoribus unius domus ab alterius domus familia vel opprimuntur vel puniuntur. Quo autem haec spiritualiter tendant, exponit cum adjungit: Si autem et Satanas in seipsum divisus est. Si ego, ut vos dicitis, in principe daemoniorum ejicio daemonia, ergo regnum diaboli est divisum contra se, et ita potestas malitiae ejus 289Dstare non potest. Si autem daemon daemonem non potest expellere, falso dicitis me in principe daemoniorum daemonia ejicere. Si vero potest sicut dicitis, prospicite vobis, ut de regno ejus quod divisum est exeatis, ne in ruina ejus pariter involvamini. In quo autem Dominus ejiciat daemones, ne daemoniorum principem existiment, attendant quod sequitur.

VERS. 19.-- Si autem ego in Beelzebub ejicio daemonia, filii. Filios Judaeorum apostolos vocat, qui inter alia miracula quae a Domino acceperant, etiam daemones pellebant. Quam expulsionem non diabolo, sed Deo assignabant, qui sibi conscii erant nihil malarum artium a Domino didicisse. Ideo ipsi ignobilia et contemptibilia mundi eligentes, in quibus 290Anulla malitia artificiosa fuit, sedebunt super sedes duodecim judicantes duodecim tribus Israel. Vel, filii, id est, exorcistae illius gentis, qui invocato nomine Domini daemones expellebant. Quasi dicat: Si expulsio daemonum in illis, non diabolo sed Deo deputatur, cur idem opus in me non habeat eamdem causam? Ideo ipsi judices vestri erunt. Non potestate, sed comparatione, dum illi expulsionem, non diabolo sed Deo assignant. Si autem. Quasi dicat: Regnum divisum stare non potest, sed regnum Satanae est divisum, ut vos dicitis, quod Satanas Satan expellat, ergo, etc.

VERS. 20.-- Porro, si in digito. Filius dicitur manus vel brachium Patris: Spiritus sanctus dicitur digitus propter differentiam variorum donorum, quae per 290BSpiritum sanctum hominibus dantur, sicut digiti inter se discreti sunt: nec nocet inaequalitas membrorum. Licet enim brachium majus sit digito, tamen brachium cum digito unum est corpus. Hoc digito scripta est lex in tabulis. Hunc digitum magi confessi sunt in Aegypto. Profecto pervenit, etc., regnum. Id est, judiciaria potestas Dei: qua bonos a malis secernet in die judicii. Vel, Regnum, id est, superna beatitudo. Quasi dicat: Si in Spiritu Dei ejicio daemonia, sine dubio sciatis aditum regni coelestis patefactum credentibus.

VERS. 21.-- Cum fortis atrium. Quasi dicat: Si ego in Beelzebub, sicut dicitis, ejicerem daemonia, tunc in aliquo operibus diaboli consentirem, sed nullo modo cum illo consentio, sed potius illum superare et ejicere 290Ca meis fidelibus festino. Cum diabolus, fortis. Ad nocendum, armatus, multis spiritualibus nequitiis: custodit, in sua servitute, atrium suum, id est mundum, qui in maligno positus est, in quo usque ad adventum Filii Dei diabolus sine contradictione principabatur, et male pacato utebatur imperio in cordibus infidelium.

VERS. 22.-- Si autem fortior. Fortiorem se vocat, qui non concordi pace et operatione, sicut calumniabantur, sed fortiori potentia ipsum stravit, mundumque ab ipsius dominatione liberavit. Arma diaboli quae abstulit, calidissimae sunt versutiae spiritualis nequitiae. Spolia, sunt animae ab eo deceptae, quae victor Christus distribuit, quod est insignium triumphantis, quia captivam ducens captivitatem, dedit dona hominibus, 290Dquosdam quidem apostolos, alios evangelistas (Ephes. IV), etc.

VERS. 23.-- Qui non est, etc. Et vere opera mea non conveniunt cum operibus Satanae, quia ego ipse in nullo convenio cum illo, sed omnino contrarii sumus, quia ego humilis, benignus, animas volens salvare: ille superbus, invidus cupit perdere. Ego praedicatione mea virtutes congrego; ille spargit et separat ab unitate Ecclesiae. Et qui non colligit. Haec de omnibus haereticis vel schismaticis ex superfluo possunt intelligi, qui omnes excluduntur a regno Dei.

VERS. 24.-- Cum immundus spiritus. Quia subtrahitis vos a meo regno, attribuentes opera mea diabolo, 291Aideo diabolus habitat in vobis, sicut in suo regno gravius quam in principio. Exierit. Exivit diabolus a Judaeis, quando legem et cultum unius Dei populus iste suscepit, et transivit ad gentes quae erant a pinguedine Spiritus sancti et dilectione proximi aridae et steriles. In quibus jam non invenit requiem, quia jam a cordibus gentium suscepta fide Christi expellitur, quando dicit: Repetam Judaeos quos ante dimisi. Et invenit scopis mundatam illam plebem, id est superfluis observationibus Pharisaeorum et caeremoniis legis, quae jam nihil valent post adventum Christi. Et ut firmius possideat, assumit universitatem daemonum. Et modo deteriores Israelitae sunt, blasphemantes Christum in suis conventiculis, quam olim fuissent in Aegypto ante perceptam legem, quia major infidelitas 291Best venientem non suscipere, quam venturum non credere.

Quaerens requiem et non invenit. Quia qui in pravis cogitationibus et actibus pascitur, in mentibus fidelium omnia sua tentamenta cessare perpendit. Tunc dicit: Revertar in domum meam unde exivi. Revertar ad illius conscientiam, unde in baptismo ejectus fueram, et pristinam possessionem consueto dominio mihi subjiciam.

VERS. 25.-- Et cum venerit invenit eam scopis mundatam. Quia cum hujusmodi reprobum delictis cogentibus repetierit, invenit conscientiam peccatoris a vitiis in baptismo mundatam, sed nullo bono opere cumulatam.

VERS. 26.-- Tunc assumit septem. Plenitudinem 291Comnium vitiorum. Qui scilicet tales spiritus, nequiores, diabolo dicuntur, quia cum diabolus malus sit, illi tamen qui suis meritis impellentibus, vitiis mancipantur, non solum mali sunt, sed etiam speciem bonitatis affectatis virtutibus per hypocrisim ostentare nituntur. Et fiunt novissima hominis. Melius erat viam veritatis ignorare, quam post agnitionem converti retrorsum.

VERS. 27.-- Beatus. Hic Maria laudatur quae Dominum portavit. Et pravitas tam praesentium Judaeorum quam futurorum haereticorum qui veram Christi humanitatem negant, confutatur, quia verum Dei Filium adversus blasphemos Judaeos confitetur, et verum hominis filium testatur, matri consubstantialem. Nisi enim esset una caro cum matre, frustra 291Dventer ille virginis et ubera beatificarentur. Et ubera. Secundum physicos ex eodem fonte et lacte nutriendis et semen procreandis pueris emanat, ergo de semine virginis potuit concipi, qui ejus lacte potuit nutriri.

VERS. 28.-- Quinimo. Quasi dicat: Non solum laudanda Maria, quia Verbum Dei portavit in utero, sed maxime beata est, quia praecepta Dei servavit in opere. Sic etiam omnes beati sunt qui verbum Dei auditu fidei concipiunt, et boni operis custodiam in suo vel proximorum corde pariunt et nutriunt. Qui audiunt. In laude Ecclesiae. E contrario damnantur Pharisaei, qui verbum Dei nec audire nec implere, sed blasphemare quaerebant.

292AVERS. 29.-- Coepit dicere. Quidam calumniabantur opera ejus, quibus hucusque respondit. Alii tentantes, signum quaerebant de coelo, et illis hic respondet. Signum quaerit et signum non dabitur illi, etc. Discipulis signum dedit divinitatis, et prius transfiguratus in monte, et postea ipsis videntibus sublevatus in coelum. Sed incredulis de inferiori humanitatis passione.

VERS. 31.-- Regina austri surget. Hic condemnata plebe Judaeorum, Ecclesiae mysterium exprimitur, quae in Ninivitis per poenitentiam (quae peccatum abolet) in regina austri studium percipiendae sapipientiae (quae peccatum cavet) de totis finibus orbis congregatur, ut veri Pacifici verba cognoscat. Ex duobus enim constat Ecclesia, ut aut peccare desistas, 292Baut peccare nescias. In judicio. Non mille annis ante judicium sicut Judaei fingunt, sed in ipso judicio surget. Cum viris. (BED.) Ecce communis ostenditur resurrectio, tam bonorum quam malorum. Condemnabit. Non potestate judicii sed comparatione melioris facti, quia, relicto imperio, venit in Judaeam audire hominem sapientia famosum, sed Judaei veram Dei sapientiam cum ipsis conversantem, non audiunt sed blasphemant.

In praedicatione. Jonas paucis diebus, Christus longo tempore praedicavit. Ille peregrinis, Christus suis civibus. Ille signum non fecit, Christus multa fecit.

VERS. 33.-- Nemo. (BED.) Qui in figura reginae et Ninivitarum Ecclesiam praetulit Synagogae, etc., usque ad apertam lucis januam nolunt ingredi credendo.

292CVERS. 35.-- Vide ergo ne, etc. Post institutionem rectae fidei etiam de bonis operibus et bona intentione instruit, ut non tantum opera, sed etiam cogitationes et cordis intentiones mundentur, quasi dicat: Ego lucernam fidei in vestris sensibus accendo, et in Ecclesiam pono. Vos autem, quantum ex vobis est, lucernas vestras accendite, id est intentiones cordis vestri mundate, ut ex intentione munda placeant etiam opera. Haec contra hypocrisim Pharisaeorum subdole signa quaerentium specialiter dicta, nos juxta moralem sensum generaliter instruunt.

VERS. 37.-- Et cum loqueretur. Non ait, cum loqueretur haec, sed cum loqueretur. In quo innuitur, non statim finitis his verbis, sed aliis interpositis ad Pharisaeum divertisse, quia finito hoc 292Dsermone adhuc eo loquente, ecce mater et fratres quaerebant loqui ei. De quibus postquam nuntianti sibi respondit: Qui facit voluntatem Patris mei, hic frater meus et soror et mater est, postea intravit ad convivium Pharisaei.

Quare non baptizatus. Judaei frequenter lavant manus antequam manducent, tenentes traditionem seniorum, et a foro revertentes nisi baptizentur non comedunt.

VERS. 39.-- Calicis. Marcus refert, Judaeos baptismata calicis et urceorum lectorumque et aeramentorum solitos observare. Calix, vas vitreum; catinus, fictile de terra. Per hoc figuratur fragilitas humani corporis, in quo exterius praetendunt sanctitatem 293Aet simulant justitiam, quod foris erat lavabant, intus nequissimi. Quod autem intus est vestrum. Nemo putet fornicationem et hujusmodi corporalia vitia solummodo gravia esse, et spiritualia vitia esse levia, sicut avaritiam, iram, superbiam, etc., sicut Pharisaei putabant.

VERS. 41.-- Verumtamen quod superest. Jam bonus doctor, quomodo contagium corporis mundari debeat, docet. Et hic latius explicatur quod supra breviter dixerat de mundando cordis oculo. Totus enim hic locus ad hoc dirigitur, ut nos ad studium simplicitatis invitet, et superflua Judaeorum et terrena condemnet, qui secundum corpus intelligendo legem vitro et catino propter fragilitatem comparantur. In quo etsi graviter peccant, ipsis tamen 293Babsolutio peccati promittitur, si misericordiam facere nitantur. Quod necessario victui et vestimento superest, date pauperibus. Vel do consilium, quod restat solummodo post tanta scelera, date eleemosynam. Prima eleemosyna est mederi animabus vestris credendo in me qui corda mundo, et per fidem mundatis cordibus omnia etiam exteriora erunt munda. Qui ordinate vult dare eleemosynam, a semetipso incipit, et eam sibi primum dat. Est enim eleemosyna opus misericordiae, unde dicitur: Miserere animae tuae placens Deo.

VERS. 42.-- Praeteritis judicium. Breviter multa eorum vitia perstrinxit, qui ad vilia decimanda intendentes, futuri judicii metum, et Dei charitatem non habent. Praetereunt judicium, quia non omnia 293Cquae agunt in judicium referunt. Charitatem praetereunt, quia non ex affectu Deum diligunt. Sed rursus, ne fidei vos studiosos faciat operum negligentes, perfectionem fidelis viri supponit, ut post fidem operetur.

VERS. 44.-- Qui estis ut monumenta. Hic superstitionem ipsorum doctorum redarguit, qui foris speciem rectae doctrinae praetendunt, foeditatem vero quam intus gerunt, occultant.

VERS. 45.-- Respondens autem. Audito verbo Dei Pharisaeus ex mala conscientia sibi contumeliam fieri putat, et commemorata poena perfidorum, intelligit se dammandum.

VERS. 47.-- Vae vobis. Non arguuntur quod exornant monumenta prophetarum, sed quia interfectores 293Dprophetarum imitantur, aedificando sepulcra, accusant paterna facinora. Sed persequendo Christum et apostolos imitantur scelera patrum, et sententiam damnationis quam in patres proferebant, in seipsos retorquent.

VERS. 48.-- Profecto. Propter vulgi favorem captandum aedificatis, quasi exhorreatis scelera eorum, sed ipso opere ostenditis, quod perfidiae eorum, consentitis, quia hoc bonum non bene facitis sed pro vana gloria. Vos autem. Propterea quia estis imitatores illorum, ut impleatis quod patribus defuit, scilicet ut me et meos persequamini, quem illi persecuti sunt in prophetis.

VERS. 49.-- Prophetas et apostolos, et ex illis occident, 294Aet persequentur. Mentiuntur haeretici, qui alium Veteris, alium Novi Testamenti dicunt institutorem.

VERS. 50.-- A generatione. Omnes mali una generatio sunt, una civitas, unum corpus diaboli, sicut omnes boni una generatio, et unum corpus sunt dicti.

VERS. 51.-- Usque ad sanguinem Zachariae. Cur usque ad hunc, cum post hunc multi occisi ante natale Christi, et nato Christo mox pueri ab hac generatione sunt perempti? Sed quia Abel pastor ovium fuit, necatusque in agro, Zacharias sacerdos necatus in atrio templi, duos ordines martyrum signant, laicos, et eos qui altaris officio mancipantur.

294BVERS. 52.-- Quia tulistis clavem. Clavis, humilitas Christi, quam qui habent, Scripturas intelligunt. Hanc legisperiti, nec ipsi lege et prophetis intelligunt, nec ab aliis intelligi volunt. Intrare clavem, est non esse contentum superficie litterae sed usque ad arcana intelligenda penetrare. Vel doctor si illos quos verbo aedificat exemplo scandalizat, nec ipse regnum Dei intrat et illos qui intrare poterant excludit.

VERS. 53.-- Cum autem haec ad illos diceret, coeperunt Pharisaei, et legisperiti. Quam vera perfidiae simulationis et impietatis suae crimina audierint, ipsi testantur qui tanto intonante turbine non resipiscunt, sed doctorem veritatis aggrediuntur.

CHAPTER XI.

VERS. 1.-- And it came to pass. After the history of the sisters 288A(who signify the two lives) he brings in the Lord as having prayed, and as having instructed the disciples to pray, because both the prayer which he taught contains in itself the mystery of either life, and the perfection of those lives is to be obtained not by our own strength but by prayers. When he ceased. For this reason the Saviour is often brought in praying, that he may lead the disciples on to the pursuit of prayer. Whence, the prayer being ended, he is asked by the disciples, that he may teach how they ought to pray.

VERS. 5.-- Which of you hath a friend. After he had set forth the form of prayer to those who asked, he lays upon them also instancy and frequency in praying, so that prayer may be offered not only by day but also by night; for he who at midnight asks loaves of his friend, and persists in the intention of asking, is not cheated of his prayer.

VERS. 7.-- Trouble me not. He does not take away the hope of obtaining, 288Bbut the more vehemently kindles the desire of praying, by showing the difficulty of obtaining. The door is now. The door which Paul asks to be opened to him for the speaking of the mystery of Christ, and which John saw opened to him, is the understanding of the divine word, which is shut in the time of a famine of the word of God, when understanding is not given. And the children, that is, those who, dealing out evangelical wisdom like bread, preached it through the whole world, are now with the Lord in eternal rest; and yet by praying it is brought about that he who prays receives understanding from the Lord himself, even if there be lacking a man through whom wisdom may be preached. I cannot rise. (AMBR.) No one fears that he may waken a sleeper, whom he knows to be always watching in prayer.

VERS. 8.-- Yet because of his importunity, etc. 288CMany are the snares, and heavy is the sleep of the body, because if the mind begins to sleep, it loses the vigour of its own strength. Shake off therefore thy sleep, that thou mayest knock at the door of Christ, that when thou hast entered there may be opened to thee the treasures hidden and in darkness in Christ Jesus.

VERS. 9.-- Ask, and it shall be given you. If a man who is a friend rises and gives, compelled not by friendship but by weariness, how much more does God give, who without weariness most bountifully bestows what is asked. Lest therefore the soul, turned back from the vanity of error, waste away any longer in spiritual want, let us ask the banquet of the word, let us seek the friend who may give, let us knock at the door where hidden things are kept. For he gives great hope, who in promising does not deceive. For every one that asketh receiveth. There is need of perseverance, that we may obtain. But if it is given to him that asks, and he that seeks 288Dfinds, and to him that knocks it is opened, then as for him to whom it is not given, and who does not find, and to whom it is not opened, it appears that he has not sought and asked and knocked well.

VERS. 10.-- Seek, and you shall find.--The Lord is nigh unto all them that call upon him in truth, and he will do the will of them that fear him (Psal. XLIV). Others, who do not so ask, James rebukes, saying: You ask and receive not, because you ask amiss (Jac. IV), because you persevere in your sins.

VERS. 11.-- And which of you, if he ask his father bread, will he give him a stone, etc. (BED.) Mystically. Bread signifies charity, on account of the greater appetite for it and its necessary use, etc., as far as let him stretch himself forth to those things which are before.

289AVERS. 13.-- How much more. An open comparison. For if a sinful man, still weighed down with frail flesh, does not refuse to give temporal things to his sons when they ask, much more does the heavenly Father bestow in heaven goods that do not fail upon sons endowed with his fear and his love.

VERS. 14.-- And he was casting out. After the words of preaching which he set before the humble, he works miracles also, so that those who do not believe him preaching may believe when they have seen miracles. Matthew says that this demoniac was blind as well. In whom three miracles are wrought by the Lord: he sees, he speaks, he is freed from the demon. And what was then done carnally in one man is done daily in the conversion of the Gentiles, so that first, the demon being cast out along with all the filth of idolatry, they may see the light of faith, and then mouths that were silent before may be loosed to the praise of God.

289BVERS. 15.-- In Beelzebub. King Ninus, the founder of Nineveh, consecrated a statue to Belus his father, and appointed divine honours for him; and the Chaldeans, receiving the likeness of that image, called it Bel. The Palestinians, receiving it, called it Baal, the Moabites Beelphegor. But the Jews, worshippers of the one God, called it Beelzebub for a derision of the Gentiles. For Baal means man, Zebub a fly; whence Beelzebub, that is, the man of flies, or one having flies, on account of the filth of the gore that was sacrificed in his temple. The Jews also asserted that the prince of demons dwelt in this image, by whose power they said that Jesus drove out demons.

VERS. 18.-- But he, when he saw their thoughts. This the Lord has reserved as proper to himself, that he should behold 289Cand judge thoughts. It is proper to the Jews to seek signs, whence Paul: The Jews require signs, the Gentiles wisdom (I Cor. I) [Vulg. 1 Cor 1:22 et Judaei signa petunt, et Graeci sapientiam quaerunt, "and the Greeks seek after wisdom"]. But he, when he saw. He answers not to their words but to their thoughts: in which he gives a sure sign of his own power, by which he searches out the secrets of hearts. According to the letter it is plain that every kingdom, solid and firm, if it is divided into parts, is reduced to a wilderness, emptied of its inhabitants; and house shall fall upon house, when the inhabitants of one house are either oppressed or punished by the household of another house. But whither these things tend spiritually he sets forth when he adds: And if Satan also be divided against himself. If I, as you say, cast out demons in the prince of demons, then the kingdom of the devil is divided against itself, and so the power of his malice 289Dcannot stand. But if a demon cannot drive out a demon, you say falsely that I cast out demons in the prince of demons. But if he can, as you say, look to yourselves, that you go out from his kingdom which is divided, lest you be involved alike in its ruin. But by what power the Lord casts out demons, lest they think him the prince of demons, let them attend to what follows.

VERS. 19.-- But if I in Beelzebub cast out demons, your sons. He calls the apostles the sons of the Jews, who among the other miracles they had received from the Lord drove out demons also. Which casting out they assigned not to the devil but to God, being conscious to themselves that they had learned nothing of evil arts from the Lord. Therefore they themselves, choosing the ignoble and contemptible things of the world, in which 290Athere was no crafty malice, shall sit upon twelve seats, judging the twelve tribes of Israel. Or, sons, that is, the exorcists of that nation, who by invoking the name of the Lord drove out demons. As though he said: If the casting out of demons in them is imputed not to the devil but to God, why should not the same work in me have the same cause? Therefore they shall be your judges. Not by the power of judgment, but by comparison, since they assign the casting out not to the devil but to God. But if. As though he said: A divided kingdom cannot stand, but the kingdom of Satan is divided, as you say, in that Satan drives out Satan; therefore, etc.

VERS. 20.-- But if by the finger. The Son is called the hand or the arm of the Father; the Holy Ghost is called the finger, on account of the difference of the various gifts which are given 290Bto men through the Holy Ghost, as the fingers are distinct one from another; nor does the inequality of the members do harm. For although the arm is greater than the finger, yet the arm with the finger is one body. By this finger the law was written on the tables. This finger the magicians confessed in Egypt. Assuredly is come, etc., the kingdom. That is, the judiciary power of God: by which he will separate the good from the evil in the day of judgment. Or, Kingdom, that is, the beatitude above. As though he said: If I cast out demons in the Spirit of God, know without doubt that the entrance of the heavenly kingdom is laid open to them that believe.

VERS. 21.-- When the strong man his court. As though he said: If I, as you say, were casting out demons in Beelzebub, then I should be consenting in something to the works of the devil; but in no way do I consent with him, but rather I hasten to overcome him and to cast him out 290Cfrom my faithful ones. When the devil, the strong man, to do harm, armed, with many spiritual wickednesses, keepeth, in his own servitude, his court, that is the world, which is set in wickedness, in which, until the coming of the Son of God, the devil bore rule without contradiction, and used an ill-pacified sovereignty in the hearts of the unbelieving.

VERS. 22.-- But if a stronger. He calls himself the stronger, who laid him low not by a concordant peace and working, as they slanderously alleged, but by a mightier power, and freed the world from his dominion. The arms of the devil which he took away are the most crafty wiles of spiritual wickedness. The spoils are the souls deceived by him, which Christ the victor distributes, which is the mark of one triumphing, because leading captivity captive, he gave gifts to men, 290Dsome indeed apostles, others evangelists (Ephes, IV), etc. [Vulg. Eph 4:8 captivam duxit captivitatem, a finite verb; and Eph 4:11 reads quosdam quidem apostolos, quosdam autem prophetas, alios vero evangelistas — the prophets are absent here].

VERS. 23.-- He that is not, etc. And truly my works do not agree with the works of Satan, because I myself in nothing agree with him, but we are altogether contrary, because I am humble, kindly, willing to save souls; he is proud, envious, desirous to destroy. I by my preaching gather in the virtues; he scatters and severs from the unity of the Church. And he that gathereth not. These things may over and above be understood of all heretics or schismatics, who are all shut out from the kingdom of God.

VERS. 24.-- When the unclean spirit. Because you withdraw yourselves from my kingdom, attributing my works to the devil, 291Atherefore the devil dwells in you, as in his own kingdom, more grievously than at the beginning. Is gone out. The devil went out from the Jews, when that people received the law and the worship of the one God, and he passed over to the Gentiles, who were dry and barren of the fatness of the Holy Ghost and of the love of neighbour. In whom now he does not find rest, because now he is driven out from the hearts of the Gentiles when the faith of Christ has been received, when he says: I will go back to the Jews whom I formerly left. And he finds that people swept with brooms, that is, with the superfluous observances of the Pharisees and the ceremonies of the law, which now avail nothing after the coming of Christ. And that he may possess it the more firmly, he takes to himself the whole company of demons. And the Israelites are now worse, blaspheming Christ in their little assemblies, than they had once been in Egypt before the law was received, because it is a greater unbelief 291Bnot to receive him when he comes than not to believe in him who is to come.

Seeking rest, and he findeth not. Because he who feeds upon depraved thoughts and deeds perceives that in the minds of the faithful all his temptations come to nothing. Then he says: I will return into my house whence I came out. I will return to that man's conscience, whence I had been cast out in baptism, and I will subject my former possession to my accustomed lordship.

VERS. 25.-- And when he is come he findeth it swept with brooms. Because when he has gone back to a reprobate of this kind, his offences compelling it, he finds the conscience of the sinner cleansed from vices in baptism, but heaped up with no good work.

VERS. 26.-- Then he taketh seven. The fulness 291Cof all the vices. Which spirits, namely such as these, are called more wicked than the devil, because, though the devil is evil, yet those who, driven on by their own deserts, are made over to vices, are not only evil, but strive besides to make a display of the appearance of goodness through hypocrisy by affected virtues. And the last state of the man becometh. It were better to be ignorant of the way of truth than after acknowledging it to be turned back again.

VERS. 27.-- Blessed. Here Mary is praised, who bore the Lord. And the perversity both of the Jews then present and of the heretics to come, who deny the true humanity of Christ, is confuted, because she confesses the true Son of God against the blaspheming Jews, and testifies him a true son of man, consubstantial with his mother. For unless he were one flesh with his mother, in vain 291Dwould that womb of the Virgin and those breasts be called blessed. And the paps. According to the natural philosophers, from the same fount there flows both milk for children to be nourished and seed for children to be begotten; therefore he could be conceived of the seed of the Virgin who could be nourished by her milk.

VERS. 28.-- Yea rather. As though he said: Not only is Mary to be praised, because she bore the Word of God in her womb, but she is most of all blessed, because she kept the precepts of God in her work. So also all are blessed who conceive the word of God by the hearing of faith, and bring forth and nourish the keeping of good work in their own heart or in their neighbours'. Who hear. In praise of the Church. On the contrary the Pharisees are condemned, who sought neither to hear nor to fulfil the word of God, but to blaspheme it.

292AVERS. 29.-- He began to say. Certain men were slandering his works, to whom he has replied thus far. Others, tempting him, were seeking a sign from heaven, and to these he replies here. A sign is sought, and a sign shall not be given to it, etc. To the disciples he gave a sign of his divinity, both when he was transfigured before on the mountain, and afterwards when, as they looked on, he was lifted up into heaven. But to unbelievers, a sign from the lower passion of his humanity.

VERS. 31.-- The queen of the south shall rise. Here, the people of the Jews being condemned, the mystery of the Church is set forth, which is gathered together from all the ends of the world: in the Ninevites through penance (which does away with sin), in the queen of the south the zeal of receiving wisdom (which guards against sin), that she may know the words of the true Peaceable One. For the Church stands upon two things: that thou either cease to sin, 292Bor know not how to sin. In the judgment. Not a thousand years before the judgment, as the Jews feign, but in the judgment itself shall she rise. With the men. (BED.) Behold, a common resurrection is shown, of the good as well as of the evil. Shall condemn. Not by the power of judgment, but by the comparison of a better deed, because, leaving her empire behind, she came into Judea to hear a man famed for wisdom, whereas the Jews do not hear but blaspheme the true Wisdom of God dwelling among them.

In the preaching. Jonas preached for a few days, Christ for a long time. He to strangers, Christ to his own fellow-citizens. He wrought no sign, Christ wrought many.

VERS. 33.-- No man. (BED.) He who in the figure of the queen and of the Ninevites set the Church before the Synagogue, etc., as far as they will not enter by believing the open gate of the light.

292CVERS. 35.-- Take heed therefore lest, etc. After the instruction of right faith he instructs also concerning good works and good intention, so that not only works but also the thoughts and intentions of the heart may be made clean; as though he said: I kindle the lamp of faith in your senses and set it in the Church. But you, so far as in you lies, kindle your own lamps, that is, cleanse the intentions of your heart, that from a clean intention your works also may be pleasing. These things, spoken particularly against the hypocrisy of the Pharisees who craftily sought signs, instruct us generally according to the moral sense.

VERS. 37.-- And as he was speaking. He does not say, as he was speaking these things, but as he was speaking. In which it is intimated that he turned aside to the Pharisee not immediately when these words were finished, but with other things coming between, because when this 292Ddiscourse was finished, while he was still speaking, behold his mother and his brethren were seeking to speak to him. Concerning whom, after he had answered the man who announced them: He that doth the will of my Father, he is my brother and sister and mother, [Vulg. Mt 12:50 Quicumque enim fecerit voluntatem Patris mei, qui in caelis est, ipse meus frater — Migne's opening and hic frater meus follow Mc 3:35, which however reads voluntatem Dei] he afterwards went in to the Pharisee's banquet.

Why not washed. The Jews frequently wash their hands before they eat, holding the tradition of the elders, and returning from the market place they do not eat unless they be washed.

VERS. 39.-- Of the cup. Mark relates that the Jews were wont to observe the washings of cups and pitchers and beds and brazen vessels. The cup is a glass vessel; the dish is earthenware, of clay. By this is figured the frailty of the human body, in which outwardly they make a show of holiness 293Aand feign justice: what was without they washed, within most wicked. But that which is within you. Let no one think that fornication and bodily vices of this kind are alone grievous, and that spiritual vices are light, such as avarice, anger, pride, and the rest, as the Pharisees thought.

VERS. 41.-- But yet that which remaineth. Now the good teacher teaches how the contagion of the body ought to be cleansed. And here is more fully set out what he had said briefly above about cleansing the eye of the heart. For this whole passage is directed to this end, that it may invite us to the pursuit of simplicity, and condemn the superfluous and earthly things of the Jews, who by understanding the law according to the body are likened to the glass and the dish because of their frailty. In which, although they sin grievously, yet to them the absolution of sin is promised, if they strive to do mercy. 293BWhat remains over from necessary food and clothing, give to the poor. Or, I give counsel: what alone is left after so great crimes, give alms. The first alms is to heal your own souls by believing in me who cleanse hearts; and when hearts have been cleansed through faith, all outward things also will be clean. He who wishes to give alms in an ordered way begins from himself, and gives it first to himself. For alms is a work of mercy, whence it is said: Have mercy on thy own soul, pleasing God.

VERS. 42.-- You pass over judgment. He has briefly touched upon many of their vices, who, intent upon tithing worthless things, have not the fear of the judgment to come nor the charity of God. They pass over judgment, because they do not refer all 293Cthat they do to the judgment. They pass over charity, because they do not love God from affection. But again, lest it make you, zealous for faith, negligent of works, he sets down the perfection of the faithful man, that after faith he may work.

VERS. 44.-- Who are as monuments. Here he rebukes the superstition of those same teachers, who outwardly make a show of the appearance of right doctrine, but hide the foulness which they carry within.

VERS. 45.-- Answering, however. Hearing the word of God, the Pharisee out of an evil conscience thinks that an insult is being done to him, and, the punishment of the faithless being called to mind, understands that he is to be condemned.

VERS. 47.-- Woe to you. They are not reproved because they adorn the monuments of the prophets, but because they imitate the slayers 293Dof the prophets; by building sepulchres they accuse their fathers' crimes. But by persecuting Christ and the apostles they imitate the crimes of their fathers, and the sentence of damnation which they were pronouncing upon their fathers they turn back upon themselves.

VERS. 48.-- Truly. You build for the sake of catching the favour of the common people, as though you shuddered at their crimes; but by the very work you show that you consent to their treachery, because you do not do this good thing well, but for vainglory. But you. For this reason: because you are imitators of them, that you may fill up what was wanting to your fathers, namely that you may persecute me and mine, whom they persecuted in the prophets.

VERS. 49.-- Prophets and apostles, and some of them they will kill, 294Aand persecute. Heretics lie, who say that the founder of the Old Testament is one and of the New another.

VERS. 50.-- From the generation. All the evil are one generation, one city, one body of the devil, as all the good are called one generation and one body.

VERS. 51.-- As far as the blood of Zacharias. Why as far as this man, when after him many were slain before the birth of Christ, and, Christ being born, the children were straightway destroyed by this generation? But because Abel was a shepherd of sheep, and was slain in the field, and Zacharias a priest slain in the court of the temple, they signify two orders of martyrs, the laity and those who are made over to the service of the altar.

294BVERS. 52.-- Because you have taken away the key. The key is the humility of Christ, and they who have it understand the Scriptures. This the lawyers neither themselves understand by the law and the prophets, nor do they wish it to be understood by others. To enter the key is not to be content with the surface of the letter but to penetrate as far as the hidden things that are to be understood. Or: a teacher, if he scandalizes by his example those whom he builds up by his word, neither himself enters the kingdom of God and shuts out those who were able to enter.

VERS. 53.-- And as he was saying these things unto them, the Pharisees and the lawyers began. How true were the charges of their treachery and dissimulation and impiety which they heard, they themselves bear witness, who at so great a thundering whirlwind do not come to their senses, but assail the teacher of truth.

CAPUT XII.

294CVERS. 1.-- Attendite. Ad hoc fermentum pertinent omnia quae superius in domo Pharisaei disputavit, unde et Apostolus: Non in fermento veteri neque in fermento malitiae (I Cor. V), etc. Attendite a fermento. Pulcher locus tenendae simplicitatis et aemulandae fidei, ne more Judaeorum aliud promamus affectu, aliud voce simulemus, cum sciamus in die judicii non posse esse latebram fraudis.

VERS. 2.-- Neque absconditum quod non sciatur. Ideo ne aemulemini simulatores, quia profecto veniet tempus in die judicii in quo et vestra virtus et illorum hypocrisis omnibus revelabitur, unde sequitur. Quoniam quae in tenebris dixistis, in lumine dicentur. Non solum in futuro omnia patebunt, sed etiam in praesenti tempore in tota Ecclesia per orbem terrae 294Dpublice praedicabuntur, quae in tenebris, id est, in umbra pressurarum et carcerum locuti estis, quando actus vestri legentur in excelsis. Quoniam quae in tenebris dixistis, in lumine dicentur. (AMBR.) Quod in tenebris, id est in timore dixistis, dicetur in lumine, id est in fiducia veritatis accepto Spiritu sancto. Et quod in aure, id est secreto locuti, super tecta, id est calcato carnis domicilio praedicabitur.

VERS. 4.-- Dico autem vobis. (ID.) Cum gemina sit causa perfidiae, etc., usque ad ita fidei fundamentum est fortitudo.

VERS. 5.-- Ostendam autem vobis. Quia duo sunt genera persecutorum, unum aperte persequentium, alterum fraudulenter blandientium. Sicut supra 295Acontra hypocrisim, ita hic armat contra carnifices. Totus hic locus ad subeundam pro confessione Domini passionem vel contemptu mortis, vel spe praemii, vel mansuros in denuntiatione supplicii instruit.

VERS. 6.-- Nonne quinque. (AMBR.) Quinque passeres, quinque corporis sensus; vel duo secundum Matthaeum, corpus et anima, etc., usque ad non dubium fidelium contemplationem apud illum valituram.

Veneunt dipondio. Dipondius est pondus ex duobus assibus compositum. Quod autem in numeris est unum, hoc in ponderibus as: quod duo, hoc dipondius est. Et unus ex, etc. Simplicitatis affectum inspiraverat, virtutem mentis erexerat, sola fides nutabat, hanc de vilioribus roborat. Quomodo dicit 295BApostolus: Nunquid de bobus cura est Deo? (I Cor. IX.) Sed aliud est cura, aliud est scientia.

VERS. 7.-- Sed et capilli. Non actu computationis, sed facultate cognitionis, a similitudine pecuniae quae numeratur ut servetur. Ubi magna providentia Dei, quod nec parva nec otiosa dicta eum lateant.

VERS. 8.-- Omnis quicunque. Ne quis tempore persecutionis sufficere sibi putaret fidem cordis, dicit: Omnis quicunque confessus fuerit. Confitetur quis Jesum ea charitate quae per dilectionem operatur, negat qui praeceptis non obedit. Filius hominis. Confitetur Filius apud Patrem illum, qui per Filium habet accessum ad Patrem. Negatur a Filio apud Patrem, cui non manifestatur in divinitatis potentia Filius et Pater. Et ne putetur una conditio omnium 295Cet eorum qui studio et eorum qui infirmitate vel ignorantia negant, subdit: Et omnis qui dicit verbum in Filio hominis remittetur.

VERS. 10.-- Ei autem. Ei etiam qui scandalizatus in infirmitate carnis me purum hominem vel voratorem suspicatus fuerit, remissibile erit, sed qui gratiam Spiritus sancti (qua poenitentia inspiratur, et ad congregationem reditur) non cognoscens impoenitenti corde permanserit, cum hoc jam non sit humanum sed diabolicum, irremissibile erit.

VERS. 11.-- Cum autem inducent vos in synagogas, et ad magistratus, et potestates. Terror talium conventuum solet auferre libertatem loquendi. Nolite solliciti esse. Voluntatem vestram pro Christo offerte. Christus vero loquetur pro se.

295DVERS. 13.-- Magister. Commendanti gratiam supernae pacis et unitatis, vult iste ingerere molestiam terrenae divisionis, unde et homo dicitur, id est terrenus. Inter quos enim hujusmodi zelus et contentio est, homines sunt et secundum hominem ambulant. Inter fratres, patrimonium, non judex medius, sed pietas debet sequestra dividere, quamvis immortalitatis patrimonium non sit pecunia hominibus petendum.

VERS. 14.-- Quis me constituit. Non sum dissensionis Deus, sed pacis et unitatis, qui veni homines pacificare cum angelis, ut multi unum cor et unam animam habeant. Non ut dividantur, sed ut habeant omnia communia, nec sit aliquis egens inter eos. 296AIlle qui non colligit mecum, est divisor fraternitatis et dissensionis auctor.

VERS. 15.-- Ab omni avaritia. Nemo putaret crimen esse, haereditatem dividere, fructus in horrea congregare, sed et hoc avaritiam indicat supernus judex, unde et subjungit: hominis cujusdam divitis. Sicut supra contra Pharisaeos et hypocritas, ita hic occasione stultae petitionis contra avaritiam disputat. Non in abundantia. Non in solo pane vivit homo, sed in verbo Dei (Deut. VIII), nec temporalis vita multitudine divitiarum protenditur.

VERS. 16.-- Dixit autem, etc. Post praeceptum subdit exemplum ad declinandam avaritiam temporalium. Hominis cujusdam divitis uberes fructus. Non reprehenditur dives quod terram coluerit, vel fructus 296Bcondiderit in horrea, sed quod fiduciam vitae posuerit in illis, nec pauperibus erogaverit, ut ab eis reciperetur in aeternis tabernaculis.

VERS. 17.-- Quid faciam, etc. Fructus suos, et sua bona esse computans non erogat pauperibus quae supersunt, sed suae luxuriae reservare in futurum studet, et ignarus quis congregatis sit usurus.

VERS. 19.-- Bona mea et dicam. (AMBR.) Non sunt hominis bona, quae secum auferre non potest. Sola enim misericordia comes est defunctorum.

Requiesce. Ecce cum pesti avaritiae jungitur pestis desidiae, ut nec pro temporalibus Deum interpellaret.

VERS. 20.-- Dixit autem. Dicere Dei, est ad hominem pravas ejus machinationes subita animadversione compescere. Stulte. Quia longa tibi tempora 296Cdivitiarum promittebas, hac nocte praereptus aliis congregata relinques. Repetunt, etc. In nocte aufertur anima, quae in obscuritate cordis quod poterat pati, praevidere noluit.

VERS. 21.-- Et non est in Deum dives. In Deum dives est qui transitoria contemnens pauperibus distribuit, cujus exspectatio Dominus est, cujus substantia, id est conscientiae possessio qua sustentatur et pascitur, est apud Deum, non in sacculis terrae.

VERS. 22.-- Dixitque ad discipulos suos. Apostolos per fidem in spe aeternorum confirmat, quia plures sunt quos torpentes facit amor praesentium et desperatio futurorum. Nolite solliciti. Perfectius esset pauperibus omnia dare, et de mammona iniquitatis amicos facere; sed quia plerumque devotio fidei infirmitate 296Drevocatur, non prohibet providentiam, per quam in sudore vultus panis praeparatur, sed vetat sollicitudinem quae mentem perturbat et ab aeternis revocat.

VERS. 24.-- Considerate. Sancti merito avibus comparantur, qui nihil in mundo habentes, nec laborantes, sola contemplatione aeterna petunt, jam similes angelis. Quanto magis. Si volatilibus non laborantibus, nec communes fructus aliquo sibi dominatu specialius vindicantibus providentia Dei necessaria largitur, verum est causam inopiae nostrae avaritiam indicare, qui communia quasi propria vindicamus et aliis invidemus.

VERS. 26.-- Si ergo neque. Si nec in augmentatione 297Acorporis vestri operari potestis, quae operatio est Deo minima ad comparationem spiritualis creaturae, quid solliciti estis de caeteris majoribus, id est de animae conservatione.

VERS. 27.-- Considerate lilia. Aperta comparatio. Lilia non sicut caeteri fructus aliquem agricolarum cultum requirunt. Solet etiam per lilium coelestis beatitudo significari, ad quam nos invitat Dominus. Lilia non silvis sed in hortis nascuntur, sic et nos in hortis virtutum nasci debemus. Nec Salomon. (AMBR.) Salomon et hic vestitus, et alibi coopertus gloria dicitur, qui infirmitatem corporeae naturae veluti quadam virtute mentis adopertam, operum gloria vestiebat.

Vestiebat sicut unum. Quae purpura regum, quae 297Bpictura textricum potest floribus comparari? Ipse color dicitur vestimentum floris, sicut dicitur: Operuit istum rubor.

VERS. 28.-- Cras in clibanum. Cras in Scripturis pro futuro ponitur, dicente Jacob: Exaudiet me cras justitia mea (Gen. XXX).

VERS. 29.-- Nolite quaerere quid manducetis, aut quid bibatis. Non ait: Nolite quaerere vel solliciti esse de cibo vel vestimento, sed expressius quid manducetis aut quid bibatis. In quo videntur argui hi qui, spreto victu vel vestitu communi, lautiora vel austeriora prae his cum quibus vivunt, alimenta vel indumenta requirunt. Nolite in sublime. Prohibita sollicitudine victus vel vestitus, quae sunt ad necessitatem, prohibet etiam superbiam quae solet de istis superabundantibus 297Cconsequi: ideo haec ne requiratis, quia talis sollicitudo facit infidelibus similes.
