VERS. 30.-- Et haec omnia. Quia omnia sunt filiorum, ideo haec omnia adjicientur vobis, etiam non quaerentibus, ut nec in praesenti nec in futuro desit aliqua gratia fidelibus, quibus si haec subtrahuntur, ad probationem est, si dantur ad gratiarum actionem, quia omnia cooperantur in bonum bonis.

VERS. 32.-- Nolite timere. Regnum quaerite, et de regno ne diffidatis; quia Pater complacita bonitate dabit vobis regnum, non vestris meritis. Pusillus. Vel ob comparationem majoris numeri reproborum, vel potius ob humilitatis devotionem, per quam humilitatem crescit Ecclesia, per quam pervenitur ad regnum.

297DVERS. 33.-- Vendite. Non tantum non timeatis ne propter regnum militantibus vitae necessaria desint, sed etiam possessa vendite propter eleemosynam, quae est via ad regnum, dum per eam peccata redimuntur. Non tantum cibos vestros communicate pauperibus, sed etiam vendite vestras possessiones, ut omnibus vestris semel pro Domino spretis postea labore manuum vestrarum operemini, unde vivatis vel eleemosynam faciatis. Date eleemosynam. Qui pro coelo omnia mundana spernit vendat quae habet et distribuat: qui non est tantae virtutis, de his quae habet eleemosynam det. Non deficientem. Temporalia, vel ex sua fragilitate deficiunt, vel si quid solidum superest, ut lapides pretiosi, a furibus possunt 298Atolli. Sed data pro Christo aeternum fructum conferunt in coelis.

VERS. 34.-- Ubi enim thesaurus. Universaliter, et de pecunia et de omnibus voluptatibus. Gulosi thesaurus est venter, ubi habet cor, sic et de aliis. Nam si in terra, cor est deorsum; si in coelestibus, est in Christo fixum. Necesse est enim, ut quo praecesserit dilectionis thesaurus, illic et cogitationis sequatur affectus.

VERS. 35.-- Sint lumbi, etc., et lucernae. Deo non placet unum sine altero cum quis vel bona agit nondum refrenata luxuria, vel caste vivit, non exercens se per bona opera. Et ut ab amore temporalium possitis removeri, et bona quae agitis, simplici intentione aeternorum agatis, cingite lumbos, id est motus 298Bcarnalium desideriorum refrenate, et habete lucernas ardentes, ut in operatione vestra solo Dei amore ardeatis et aliis exemplum praebeatis.

VERS. 36.-- Exspectantibus. Sive vitatis mala sive facitis bona, nihil mundamini, sed solum adventum Redemptoris exspectetis. Quando revertatur. Ad nuptias Dominus ivit, cum post resurrectionem novus homo angelorum multitudinem sibi copulavit: a quibus revertitur, cum nobis per judicium manifestatur. Unde et bene de exspectantibus subdit: Ut cum venerit, confestim aperiant ei. Non vult aperire judici pulsanti, qui timens videre iratum quem contempsit, de corpore exire metuit. Aperit qui judicem laetus et securus sustinet, et de propinqua morte gaudet, unde subditur: Beati servi illi, quos cum 298Cvenerit, etc.

VERS. 37.-- Vigilantes. Vigilat qui oculos apertos in vero lumine tenet, ut tenebras negligentiae evitet; qui etiam quod credidit operatur, qui sollicitus est in cura gregis sibi commissi. Quid vero vigilantibus debeatur subdit: Amen dico vobis.

VERS. 38, 39.-- Et si venerit in secunda, etc. Vigilias vocat a similitudine excubantium. In nocte hujus mundi semper debemus esse contra hostem solliciti, et exspectare lucem venturam, id est adventum judicis. Prima ergo vigilia custodia pueritiae est, secunda juventutis, tertia senectutis. Si quis vero in pueritia vigilare neglexerit, non tamen desperet, sed etiam in juventute vel saltem tandem in senectute resipiscat, quia pius judex moras nostras 298Dpatienter exspectat. Qui ad excutiendam mentis desidiam exteriorum damnatorum similitudinem inducit, ut per hoc animus ad custodiam sui suscitetur. Unde, Hoc autem scitote quoniam si sciret paterfamilias. Nesciente patrefamilias fur domum perfodit, quia dum a sui custodia spiritus dormit, improvisa mors carnis habitaculum irrumpit, et ad supplicia trahit. Furi autem resisteret si vigilaret, quia adventum judicis occulte venientem praecavens, poenitendo occurreret.

VERS. 40.-- Qua hora non putatis Filius hominis veniet? Ultimam horam semper ignorari voluit Dominus, ut semper sit suspecta, et ad eam semper praeparetur.

299AVERS. 41.-- Ait autem ei Petrus. Cum Dominus duo proposuerit, scilicet et se subito venturum, et illos exspectando paratos esse debere, quaerit Petrus si illa sublimia instituta coelestis vitae, quae sunt necessaria se praeparantibus, ad apostolos solum et similes eorum, an ad omnes salvandos pertineant.

VERS. 42.-- Dixit autem Dominus. Respondens ad interrogata Dominus, primum et secundum judicium cunctis affuturum, et singulos pro meritis vel remunerandos vel damnandos dicit. Deinde, quod maxime quaesierat, supponit scilicet gratiam virtutum quam mundo attulerat, a singulis quantum potest esse sectandam, quod est ibi: Ignem veni mittere in terram. Quis putas esse fidelis dispensator? Quasi dicat: Vobis, qui dispensatores estis instituti, hoc 299Bspecialius convenit de custodia et praeparatione, ut et vos et alios praeparetis in adventum judicis, quia pauci sunt qui commissam sibi bene administrant dispensationem. Et prudens quem constituit Dominus super familiam suam, ut, etc. Quam rarus est Domino propter Dominum serviens, oves Christi non ad lucrum, sed amore Christi pascens, prudens in futuro, sibi providens quem constituit; id est, quam rarus qui sit vocatus a Deo tanquam Aaron, et non magis se ingesserit, et qui non magis se pascat quam oves!

VERS. 43.-- Beatus. Quasi dicat: Pauci sunt fideles. Iste autem qui repertus fuerit fideliter annonam verbi ministrat, et in curam gregis vigilans, aeterna beatitudine glorificabitur.

299CVERS. 44.-- Super omnia quae possidet. Quanta inter bonos auditores et bonos doctores est distantia meritorum, tanta et praemiorum. Auditores bonos facit discumbere et transiens ministrat, dispensatores constituit super omnia quae possidet, non ut soli, sed ut prae caeteris habeant aeterna, tum pro sua vita, tum pro gregis custodia.

VERS. 45.-- Quod si dixerit servus. Nota inter vitia servi ascriptum, quod tardum putat domini reditum. In virtutibus boni non annumerantur, quod hunc cito speraverit, sed tantum quod fideliter ministravit. Nihil ergo est melius, quam ut patienter sustineamus ignorare quod sciri non potest, sed tamen laboremus ut idonei inveniamur.

Et edere. Id est, cunctis sceleribus et illecebris 299Dsaeculi, quae mentem errare faciunt, occupari, vel ad litteram, si sit deditus gulae et ebrietati.

VERS. 46.-- Partemque. Mali cum infidelibus punientur, sed differenter. Illi enim qui scienter peccant, gravius punientur. Minus graviter, qui peccant ex ignorantia. Et inter injustos tanto quisque tolerabiliorem habebit damnationem, quanto minorem habuit iniquitatem.

VERS. 48.-- Paucis. Mitissima omnium poena est eorum qui praeter originale nullum insuper addiderunt delictum.

(AMBR.) Multi existimantes se minus vapulaturos, si nesciant, etc., usque ad sed contemptores judicantur.

300AOmni autem. Merito malus cognoscens plus, et non cognoscens minus vapulabit quia illi plus, isti minus, commissum est: et ab omni cui plus committitur, plus exigitur.

VERS. 49.-- Ignem, etc. Quasi dicat: Tu quaerebas, an omnes moneam accinctis lumbis, ardentibus lucernis adventum Domini praestolari? Sed ego qui tantum ob hoc veni in mundum ut a cupiditatibus mundi ad coelestia homines accenderem, nil aliud volo, nisi ut incendii hujus jubar usque ad finem saeculi cunctas mundi partes illustret, et ut in cordibus fidelium ita ignescat, ne aliquo impetu fluctuum vel flatuum exstinguatur.

Baptismo autem habeo, etc. Et ut ignis iste in fidelium cordibus queat accendi, ita ut omnia temporalia 300Bperfecte et seipsos possint despicere, prius habeo baptismo baptizari, hoc est unctione proprii sanguinis perfundi: non enim daretur spiritus, nisi Jesus primum glorificaretur. Et quomodo. Tanta est dignatio Domini, ut infundendae nobis devotionis et consummandae perfectionis in nobis, et maturandae pro nobis studium passionis sibi inesse testetur, qui cum in se nil haberet unde doleret, nostris tamen angebatur aerumnis, et tempore mortis moestitiam praetendebat, quam non ex metu mortis suae, sed mora redemptionis nostrae assumpserat. Usque dum perficiatur. Qui usque ad perfectionem angitur de perfectione est securus, nec de ipsa angitur, sed potius mors corporis absolutio est anxietatis, non coacervatio doloris.

300CVERS. 51.-- Putatis. Quomodo post baptisma passionis, et post adventum ignis terra sit arsura, dicit: Quia ad fidem Christi totus orbis contra se est divisus, unaquaeque domus habuit fideles et infideles, his contra fidem, illis pro fide pugnantibus.

VERS. 52.-- In domo una. Unusquisque homo, domus una est, vel Dei, vel diaboli. Itaque homo spiritualis domus spiritualis est. Divisi. Prima causa est religionis ad Deum, secunda pietatis ad proximum. Oportet enim ut divinis humana posthabeas. Non est ergo pignoribus renuntiandum, sed Deus omnibus praeferendus. Etenim naturae pignora sunt Dei beneficia, nec debet plus amari beneficium quod accipitur, quam Deus a quo beneficium acceptum servatur. Tres in duos. Id est, filius et filia et nurus, in patrem et 300Dmatrem, et duo in tres, id est, pater et mater, ubi etiam socrus intelligitur, in filium et filiam et nurum.

VERS. 54.-- Dicebat autem. Cum apostolos etiam de suo primo adventu informasset, insinuans quid in eo factum sit, et de futuro, qualiter eum exspectare debeant, admonuisset, vertit sermonem ad turbas, in quibus Pharisaei erant, ut Matthaeus indicat: Quia nec primum adventum factum credentes, nec secundum recte exspectantes, signum de coelo quaerebant tentantes. Hos ergo arguit, quia si vellent attendere, possent ex verbis prophetarum certum utriusque adventus habere indicium. Cum videritis. Mystice. Nubes ab occasu oriens, carnem 301AChristi a morte resurgentem significat, ex quo tempore imber Evangelicae praedicationis omnibus terris est infusus. Auster flans ante aestum, leviores tribulationes ante judicium significat.

Nimbus venit. Ad litteram manifestus est sensus: Qui ex elementorum immutatione aurarum statum possunt praenoscere, possent etiam tempus utriusque adventus scire, tum ex dictis prophetarum, tum ex magnificentia operum.

VERS. 56.-- Hypocritae, etc. Et ne aliqui rudes in turba de imperitia sua sibi blandientes se excusarent, dicentes se legem et prophetas ignorare, supponit eos per naturalem rationem posse discernere, vel eum qui opera facit quae nullus alius fecerat, supra hominem et Deum esse: vel post tot saeculi injustitias, 301Bjustum Creatoris judicium esse venturum.

VERS. 58.-- Cum autem vadis. Cum in hac vita praeparas te ad placatam faciem judicis videndam, quamvis sermo Dei sit contrarius tuae carni, tamen eo utere sicut custodia data tibi in hoc itinere, ne contemptus te accuset. Cum adversario. Adversarius noster in via est sermo Dei, contrarius nostris carnalibus desideriis in praesenti vita, a quo liberatur qui praeceptis ejus humiliter subditur: alioquin ex sermonis contemptu reus, in examine judicis tenebitur peccator, quem judex exactori, id est diabolo, tradet; quia permittet ut diabolus animam ejus ad poenam trahat, et ipse exactor qui poenam exigit pro reatu quem suggessit, eam retrudet in gehennam.

301CDa operam, etc. Haec etiam ad calcandas mundi illecebras et ad judicis adventum praestolandum pertinent. Quasi dicat: Quandoquidem constat districtum judicem esse venturum, ergo hic ita vivite, ne in illo judicio damnemini.

VERS. 59.-- Reddas. Reddit semper poenas pro peccatis patiendo, sed nunquam veniam consequendo.

CHAPTER XII.

294CVERS. 1.-- Beware. To this leaven belong all the things which he disputed above in the house of the Pharisee; whence also the Apostle: Not with the old leaven, nor with the leaven of malice (I Cor. V), etc. Beware of the leaven. A beautiful passage for the holding of simplicity and the emulating of faith, that we may not after the manner of the Jews put forth one thing in affection and feign another with the voice, since we know that in the day of judgment there can be no hiding-place for fraud.

VERS. 2.-- Nor hidden, that shall not be known. Therefore do not emulate dissemblers, because assuredly the time will come, in the day of judgment, in which both your virtue and their hypocrisy shall be revealed to all; whence there follows. For the things which you have spoken in darkness shall be said in the light. Not only in the world to come shall all things lie open, but even in the present time they shall be publicly preached in the whole Church throughout the world 294D: the things which in darkness, that is, in the shadow of oppressions and of prisons, you have spoken, when your acts shall be read on high. For the things which you have spoken in darkness shall be said in the light. (AMBR.) What you have spoken in darkness, that is, in fear, shall be said in the light, that is, in the confidence of truth when the Holy Ghost has been received. And what you have spoken in the ear, that is, in secret, shall be preached upon the housetops, that is, when the dwelling-place of the flesh has been trodden underfoot.

VERS. 4.-- And I say to you. (ID.) Since the cause of faithlessness is twofold, etc., as far as so fortitude is the foundation of faith.

VERS. 5.-- But I will show you. Because there are two kinds of persecutors, one of those who persecute openly, the other of those who fraudulently flatter. As above 295Aagainst hypocrisy, so here he arms against the executioners. This whole passage instructs unto the undergoing of suffering for the confession of the Lord, either by contempt of death, or by hope of reward, or those who will abide, in the denunciation of punishment.

VERS. 6.-- Are not five. (AMBR.) Five sparrows, the five senses of the body; or two according to Matthew, the body and the soul, etc., as far as there is no doubt that the contemplation of the faithful will prevail with him.

They are sold for two farthings. A dipondius is a weight composed of two asses. What in numbers is one, that in weights is the as; what is two, that is the dipondius. And one of, etc. He had breathed in the disposition of simplicity, he had raised up the strength of the mind, faith alone was wavering; this he strengthens from the meaner things. As the Apostle says: 295BDoth God take care for oxen? (I Cor. IX.) But care is one thing, knowledge another.

VERS. 7.-- Yea, and the hairs. Not by the act of counting, but by the faculty of knowing, from the likeness of money which is numbered that it may be kept. Wherein is the great providence of God, that neither small nor idle words lie hidden from him.

VERS. 8.-- Every one whosoever. Lest anyone in the time of persecution should think the faith of the heart sufficient for himself, he says: Every one whosoever shall confess. A man confesses Jesus with that charity which works through love; he denies who does not obey the commandments. The Son of man. The Son confesses before the Father him who through the Son has access to the Father. He is denied by the Son before the Father, to whom the Son and the Father are not made manifest in the power of the Godhead. And lest one condition be thought to belong to all, 295Cboth to those who deny of set purpose and to those who deny out of weakness or ignorance, he adds: And every one that speaketh a word in the Son of man, it shall be forgiven him. [Vulg. Lc 12:10 reads in Filium hominis, the accusative — "against the Son of man". The plate sets the ablative in Filio, read at 400 dpi on leaf n153, so the sentence Migne prints forgives a word spoken in the Son rather than against him.]

VERS. 10.-- But to him. To him also who, being scandalized in the weakness of the flesh, shall have suspected me to be a mere man or a glutton, it shall be remissible; but he who, not acknowledging the grace of the Holy Ghost (by which penance is inspired and a return is made to the congregation), shall have remained with an impenitent heart, since this is now not human but devilish, for him it shall be irremissible.

VERS. 11.-- And when they shall bring you into the synagogues, and to magistrates and powers. The terror of such assemblies is wont to take away freedom of speech. Be not solicitous. Offer your will for Christ. But Christ will speak for himself.

295DVERS. 13.-- Master. Upon one who was commending the grace of the peace and unity above, this man wishes to thrust the trouble of an earthly division; whence also he is called a man, that is, earthly. For those among whom there is a zeal and contention of this kind are men, and walk according to man. Among brethren it is not a judge in the midst but family affection as arbitress that ought to divide a patrimony, although the patrimony of immortality is not money to be sought by men.

VERS. 14.-- Who hath appointed me. I am not the God of dissension but of peace and unity, who came to make men at peace with the angels, that many may have one heart and one soul. Not that they may be divided, but that they may have all things in common, and that there be not anyone in need among them. 296AHe who does not gather with me is a divider of brotherhood and an author of dissension.

VERS. 15.-- Of all covetousness. No one would think it a crime to divide an inheritance, to gather fruits into barns; but the judge above declares that this too is covetousness, whence also he adds: of a certain rich man. As above against the Pharisees and hypocrites, so here, on the occasion of a foolish petition, he disputes against covetousness. Not in abundance. Man liveth not by bread alone, but in the word of God (Deut. VIII) [Vulg. Deut 8:3 non in solo pane vivat homo, sed in omni verbo quod egreditur de ore Dei, "in every word that goeth forth from the mouth of God"], nor is temporal life prolonged by a multitude of riches.

VERS. 16.-- And he spoke, etc. After the precept he subjoins an example for the avoiding of covetousness for temporal things. The plentiful fruits of a certain rich man. The rich man is not blamed because he tilled the ground, or laid up his fruits 296Bin barns, but because he placed the confidence of his life in them, and did not lay them out upon the poor, that he might be received by them into everlasting dwellings.

VERS. 17.-- What shall I do, etc. Reckoning the fruits to be his own and the goods his own, he does not lay out upon the poor what is over and above, but is eager to keep it for his own luxury against the time to come, and knows not who will make use of what he has gathered.

VERS. 19.-- My goods, and I will say. (AMBR.) Those are not a man's goods which he cannot carry away with him. For mercy alone is the companion of the dead.

Take thy rest. Behold, to the plague of covetousness is joined the plague of sloth, so that he did not even call upon God for temporal things.

VERS. 20.-- But he said. For God to speak is to check a man's depraved contrivances by a sudden chastisement. Thou fool. Because thou wast promising thyself long seasons 296Cof riches, this night, snatched away, thou shalt leave to others what thou hast gathered. They require, etc. In the night the soul is taken away, which in the darkness of the heart would not foresee what it could suffer.

VERS. 21.-- And is not rich toward God. He is rich toward God who, despising things transitory, distributes to the poor; whose expectation is the Lord; whose substance, that is, the possession of a conscience by which he is sustained and fed, is with God, not in the purses of earth.

VERS. 22.-- And he said to his disciples. He strengthens the apostles through faith in the hope of things eternal, because there are many whom the love of things present and the despair of things to come make sluggish. Be not solicitous. It would be more perfect to give all to the poor, and to make friends of the mammon of iniquity; but because for the most part the devotion of faith is called back by weakness, 296Dhe does not forbid foresight, by which bread is prepared in the sweat of the face, but he forbids the solicitude which disturbs the mind and calls it back from things eternal.

VERS. 24.-- Consider. The saints are deservedly compared to the birds, who, having nothing in the world and not labouring, seek eternal things by contemplation alone, being already like the angels. How much more. If to the birds, which do not labour, nor claim the common fruits for themselves more particularly by any lordship, the providence of God bestows what is needful, it is true that the cause of our want is shown to be covetousness, in that we claim common things as though our own, and grudge them to others.

VERS. 26.-- If then neither. If you cannot work even in the increase 297Aof your own body, an operation which is the least thing for God in comparison with the spiritual creature, why are you solicitous about the rest, which are greater, that is, about the preservation of the soul.

VERS. 27.-- Consider the lilies. An open comparison. Lilies do not, like other fruits, require any tillage of husbandmen. By the lily also the heavenly beatitude is wont to be signified, to which the Lord invites us. Lilies are born not in the woods but in gardens; so we too ought to be born in the gardens of the virtues. Nor Solomon. (AMBR.) Solomon is here said to be clothed, and elsewhere to be covered with glory: he who used to clothe with the glory of works the weakness of bodily nature, covered over as it were with a certain strength of mind.

Was clothing as one. What purple of kings, what 297Bpainting of weavers can be compared to flowers? The colour itself is called the garment of the flower, as it is said: Redness has covered it.

VERS. 28.-- To-morrow into the oven. To-morrow in the Scriptures is put for the future, as Jacob says: My justice shall hear me to-morrow (Gen. XXX) [Vulg. Gen 30:33 Respondebitque mihi cras justitia mea, "and my justice shall answer for me"].

VERS. 29.-- Seek not what you shall eat, or what you shall drink. He does not say: Seek not, or be not solicitous about food or clothing, but more expressly what you shall eat or what you shall drink. In which those seem to be reproved who, despising the common food or clothing, seek finer or harsher meats or garments than those among whom they live. Be not lifted up on high. Solicitude for food or clothing, which are for necessity, being forbidden, he forbids also the pride which is wont to follow from these things when they are superabundant: therefore do not seek these things, because such solicitude 297Cmakes men like the unbelieving.

VERS. 30.-- And all these things. Because all things belong to the sons, therefore all these things shall be added to you, even when you do not seek them, so that neither in the present nor in the future may any grace be lacking to the faithful; from whom, if these things are taken away, it is for a testing; if they are given, it is for a giving of thanks, because all things work together unto good for the good. [Vulg. Rom 8:28 diligentibus Deum omnia cooperantur in bonum, "to them that love God"]

VERS. 32.-- Fear not. Seek the kingdom, and do not distrust concerning the kingdom; because the Father with a well-pleased goodness will give you the kingdom, not by your own merits. Little. Either on account of the comparison with the greater number of the reprobate, or rather on account of the devotion of humility, through which humility the Church grows, through which one comes to the kingdom.

297DVERS. 33.-- Sell. Not only should you not fear that the necessaries of life may be lacking to those who fight for the kingdom's sake, but sell also what you possess for the sake of alms, which is the way to the kingdom, since through it sins are redeemed. Not only share your food with the poor, but sell also your possessions, so that, when all that is yours has once been despised for the Lord's sake, you may afterwards work with the labour of your hands, whence you may live or may give alms. Give alms. He who for heaven's sake despises all worldly things, let him sell what he has and distribute it; he who is not of so great virtue, let him give alms out of what he has. That faileth not. Temporal things either fail of their own frailty, or, if anything solid remains, such as precious stones, it can be taken away by thieves. 298ABut things given for Christ's sake bring an eternal fruit in the heavens.

VERS. 34.-- For where your treasure is. Universally, both of money and of all pleasures. The glutton's treasure is his belly, where he has his heart; and so of the rest. For if it is on earth, the heart is below; if in things heavenly, it is fixed in Christ. For it is necessary that where the treasure of love has gone before, there the affection of thought should follow.

VERS. 35.-- Let your loins be, etc., and lamps. The one without the other does not please God, when a man either does good things while luxury is not yet bridled, or lives chastely without exercising himself in good works. And that you may be able to be drawn away from the love of things temporal, and may do the good things you do with a simple intention of things eternal, gird your loins, that is, bridle the motions 298Bof carnal desires, and have your lamps burning, that in your working you may burn with the love of God alone and may offer an example to others.

VERS. 36.-- Waiting. Whether you avoid evils or do good, you are cleansed in nothing, but only await the coming of the Redeemer. When he shall return. The Lord went to the marriage when, after the resurrection, the new man joined to himself the multitude of angels: from whom he returns when he is made manifest to us through the judgment. Whence too he fitly subjoins concerning those that wait: That when he cometh, they may open to him immediately. [Vulg. Lc 12:36 reads ut, cum venerit et pulsaverit, confestim aperiant ei, "when he cometh and knocketh"; the gloss at once expounds the knocking (pulsanti) that the lemma as printed does not carry.] He does not wish to open to the judge who knocks, who, fearing to see angry the one whom he despised, dreads to go forth from the body. He opens who bears with the judge glad and untroubled, and rejoices at death drawing near; whence it is subjoined: Blessed are those servants whom, when 298Che cometh, etc.

VERS. 37.-- Watching. He watches who keeps his eyes open in the true light, that he may avoid the darkness of negligence; who also works what he has believed; who is solicitous in the care of the flock committed to him. But what is owed to those that watch he subjoins: Amen I say to you.

VERS. 38, 39.-- And if he shall come in the second, etc. He calls them watches from the likeness of men keeping guard. In the night of this world we ought always to be solicitous against the enemy, and to await the light that is to come, that is, the coming of the judge. The first watch, therefore, is the guarding of boyhood, the second of youth, the third of old age. But if anyone has neglected to watch in boyhood, let him not despair, but let him come to himself even in youth, or at least at last in old age, because the merciful judge 298Dpatiently awaits our delays. He, to shake out the sloth of the mind, brings in the likeness of outward condemned men, that by this the soul may be roused to the guarding of itself. Whence, But this know ye, that if the householder knew. While the householder knows not, the thief digs through the house, because, while the spirit sleeps from the guarding of itself, unforeseen death bursts into the dwelling of the flesh and drags it to punishments. But he would withstand the thief if he watched, because, taking precaution against the coming of the judge who comes secretly, he would meet him by repenting.

VERS. 40.-- At what hour you think not, the Son of man will come? The Lord willed the last hour always to be unknown, that it may always be suspected, and that men may always be prepared for it.

299AVERS. 41.-- And Peter said to him. Since the Lord had set forth two things, namely both that he would come suddenly, and that they by waiting ought to be ready, Peter asks whether those lofty ordinances of the heavenly life, which are necessary for men preparing themselves, pertain to the apostles alone and to such as they, or to all who are to be saved.

VERS. 42.-- And the Lord said. Answering the things asked, the Lord says that the first and the second judgment will be present to all, and that each severally is to be rewarded or condemned according to his merits. Then he adds what he had chiefly asked, namely that the grace of the virtues which he had brought to the world is to be followed by each as far as he can, which is in that place: I came to send fire upon the earth. Who dost thou think to be the faithful steward? As if he said: To you, who have been appointed stewards, this 299Bmore especially belongs concerning watchfulness and preparation, that you may prepare both yourselves and others for the coming of the judge, because few are they who well administer the stewardship committed to them. And prudent, whom the Lord hath set over his household, that, etc. How rare is he that serves the Lord for the Lord's sake, feeding the sheep of Christ not for gain but out of love of Christ, prudent for what is to come, providing for himself, whom he hath appointed; that is, how rare is he who is called by God as Aaron was, and has not rather thrust himself forward, and who feeds not himself more than the sheep!

VERS. 43.-- Blessed. As if he said: Few are the faithful. But he who shall have been found faithfully ministers the allowance of the word, and, watching in the care of the flock, shall be glorified with eternal blessedness.

299CVERS. 44.-- Over all that he possesseth. As great as is the distance of merits between good hearers and good teachers, so great is that of rewards also. The good hearers he makes to sit down, and passing by he ministers to them; the stewards he sets over all that he possesses, not that they alone, but that beyond the rest they may have things eternal, both for their own life and for the guarding of the flock.

VERS. 45.-- But if the servant shall say. Note that it is reckoned among the vices of the servant that he thinks his lord's return slow. Among the virtues of the good it is not counted that he hoped for it soon, but only that he ministered faithfully. Nothing therefore is better than that we patiently bear to be ignorant of what cannot be known, yet labour that we may be found fit.

And to eat. That is, to be taken up with all the crimes and enticements 299Dof the world, which make the mind to go astray; or literally, if he be given over to gluttony and drunkenness.

VERS. 46.-- And his portion. The wicked shall be punished with the unfaithful, but with a difference. For those who sin knowingly shall be punished more grievously; less grievously, those who sin out of ignorance. And among the unjust each shall have a damnation the more tolerable, the less iniquity he had.

VERS. 48.-- With few. The mildest punishment of all is that of those who beyond original sin have added no offence besides.

(AMBR.) Many, thinking that they shall be beaten less if they know not, etc., as far as but they are judged as despisers.

300AAnd to everyone. Deservedly the wicked man who knows shall be beaten more, and he who knows not, less, because to the one more, to the other less, has been committed: and of everyone to whom more is committed, more is required.

VERS. 49.-- Fire, etc. As if he said: Thou wast asking whether I admonish all men, with loins girt and lamps burning, to await the coming of the Lord? But I, who came into the world only for this, that I might kindle men from the desires of the world to heavenly things, will nothing else save that the radiance of this burning may light up all the parts of the world as far as the end of the age, and that it may so blaze in the hearts of the faithful that it be not quenched by any onset of waves or of blasts.

But I have a baptism, etc. And that this fire may be able to be kindled in the hearts of the faithful, so that they may be able perfectly to despise all temporal things 300Band themselves also, I must first be baptized with a baptism, that is, be steeped in the anointing of my own blood: for the Spirit would not be given unless Jesus were first glorified. And how. So great is the condescension of the Lord that he testifies there to be in him an eagerness for devotion to be poured into us, and for perfection to be consummated in us, and for the passion to be hastened for us: he who, though he had in himself nothing whereat to grieve, was yet straitened by our troubles, and at the time of death put forward a sadness which he had taken on not from the fear of his own death, but from the delay of our redemption. Until it be accomplished. He who is straitened as far as the accomplishment is untroubled about the accomplishment, nor is he straitened about it; but rather the death of the body is the loosing of anxiety, not the heaping up of grief.

300CVERS. 51.-- Think ye. How, after the baptism of the passion and after the coming of the fire, the earth is to burn, he says: Because for the faith of Christ the whole world is divided against itself, and every house had faithful and unfaithful, these fighting against the faith, those for the faith.

VERS. 52.-- In one house. Each single man is one house, either God's or the devil's. And so a spiritual man is a spiritual house. Divided. The first cause is that of religion toward God, the second that of dutifulness toward one's neighbour. For thou oughtest to set human things after divine. Kindred pledges are not therefore to be renounced, but God is to be preferred to all. For indeed the pledges of nature are God's benefits, nor ought the benefit that is received to be loved more than God, by whom the benefit received is preserved. Three against two. That is, the son and the daughter and the daughter-in-law, against the father and 300Dthe mother; and two against three, that is, the father and the mother, where the mother-in-law also is understood, against the son and the daughter and the daughter-in-law.

VERS. 54.-- And he said. When he had instructed the apostles also concerning his first coming, intimating what was done in it, and had admonished them concerning the coming to be, how they ought to await it, he turned his discourse to the crowds, among whom were the Pharisees, as Matthew indicates: because, neither believing the first coming to have taken place nor rightly awaiting the second, they were seeking a sign from heaven, tempting him. These therefore he reproves, because if they were willing to attend, they could have from the words of the prophets a sure sign of both comings. When you see. Mystically. The cloud rising from the west signifies the flesh 301Aof Christ rising again from death, from which time the shower of the Gospel preaching has been poured out upon all lands. The south wind blowing before the heat signifies the lighter tribulations before the judgment.

A shower cometh. Literally the sense is plain: they who from the changing of the elements can foreknow the state of the breezes could also know the time of both comings, both from the sayings of the prophets and from the magnificence of his works.

VERS. 56.-- Hypocrites, etc. And lest any unlearned men in the crowd, flattering themselves upon their want of skill, should excuse themselves, saying that they knew not the law and the prophets, he adds that they can discern by natural reason either that he who does works which no other had done is above man and is God, or that after so many injustices of the age 301Bthe just judgment of the Creator will come.

VERS. 58.-- And when thou goest. When in this life thou preparest thyself to see the appeased face of the judge, although the word of God be contrary to thy flesh, yet use it as a guard given thee on this journey, lest, being despised, it accuse thee. With the adversary. Our adversary in the way is the word of God, contrary to our carnal desires in the present life, from which he is set free who is humbly subject to its precepts: otherwise the sinner, guilty from his contempt of the word, will be held at the judge's examination, and the judge will deliver him to the exactor, that is, to the devil; because he will permit the devil to drag his soul to punishment, and the exactor himself, who exacts the punishment for the guilt which he suggested, will thrust it back into hell.

301CGive diligence, etc. These things too pertain to the treading down of the world's enticements and to the awaiting of the judge's coming. As if he said: Since it is certain that a strict judge will come, live therefore here in such wise that you be not condemned in that judgment.

VERS. 59.-- Thou repay. He repays always by suffering punishments for his sins, but never by obtaining pardon.

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 1.-- Aderant autem. Dum magister veritatis praesentes instrueret qualiter se praeparare deberent ad adventum judicis, et qualiter qui se non praeparant sunt damnandi, nuntiatur ei de Galilaeis, quod quasi juste ideo petierunt, quod non bene se praeparaverunt: et respondet Dominus, et illos vere 301Dfuisse peccatores et juste periisse, et similiter alios peccatores perituros, si se peccatores non attendunt. Nuntiantes. Quasi dicant: Apparet istos fuisse peccatores et damnandos in aeternum, qui tam scelerata morte ab impio praeside inter sua sacrificia occisi sunt. De Galilaeis. Galilaei impias scelerata morte scelerum suorum poenas solvunt, quibus tamen non ipsa mors obfuit, ut in secundam mortem mitterentur, sed improba vita. Puniti sunt autem ad correctionem viventium, ut flagellato pestilente stultus sapientior fiat, vel ad exemplar corrigi nolentium, ideoque pessime periturorum.

VERS. 3.-- Omnes, etc. Hoc impletum est post passionem Domini quando Romani impoenitentes 302AJudaeos incipiendo a Galilaea adeo devastaverunt, ut non solum atria templi, sed etiam interiora domus, quo Galilaeorum accessus non erat, humano sanguine foedarentur.

VERS. 4.-- Sicut illi decem. Isti Hierosolymitae sicut et Galilaei non soli peccatores fuerunt, sed in terrorem reliquorum puniti sunt; qui ruina turris pressi significant Judaeos qui poenitere noluerunt, cum suis moenibus esse perituros. Nec frustra decem et octo, qui numerus apud Graecos ex iota et ita exprimitur, hoc est ex eisdem litteris quibus nomen Jesus incipit. Significat autem Judaeos hinc maxime perituros, qui nomen Salvatoris recipere noluerint. Illa turris significat illum qui est turris fortitudinis, quae merito in Siloa, quia Siloa, quod interpretatur 302Bmissus, significat eum qui missus a Patre venit in mundum, qui omnes super quos ceciderit conteret.

VERS. 6.-- Arborem fici. In origine humani generis Adam et Eva cum peccassent de foliis ficulneae succinctoria sibi fecerunt. Per folia ergo ficulneae intelliguntur peccata. Erant autem sub arbore fici, tanquam sub umbra mortis, de quibus dictum est: Qui sedebant sub umbra mortis lumen ortum est eis (Isa. XI).

VERS. 7.-- Quaerens fructum. Vinea Domini sabaoh, domus Israel est, in qua domo Synagoga condita est sicut ficus in vinea. Sed qui in vinea peccantem a viatoribus permisit diripi, hanc etiam ficum jussit excidi. In qua quaerit fructum, non qui adesse nesciret, sed ostendit in figura, quia in Synagoga 302Cjam fructum habere deberet. Nec ante tempus quaerit qui per triennium venit. Qui per Moysen Synagogam instituit, ipse in carne apparens in Synagoga crebris docet, fructum fidei quaerit, sed in Pharisaeis non reperit.

Dixit autem ad cultorem vineae. Cultor vineae, doctores sunt et apostoli quorum precibus et monitis assidua Dei cura suggeritur, quibus Dominus saepe de infructuosa gente Judaeorum conqueritur, quod per tres annos suae visitationis, id est, in legalibus edictis, propheticis contestationibus, Evangelii gratia, negligens exstiterit.

Ut quid etiam terram, etc. Potest per terram sterili ficu occupatam Judaica plebs signari, quae noxia praepositorum umbra premitur, ne lumen veritatis 302Dvideat et exemplis eorum impeditur, ne sole dilectionis calescat; unde (Matth. XXIII): Vae vobis, Scribae et Pharisaei, etc. Terram occupat, qui locum quem tenet bonis operibus non exercet, qui exemplo pravitatis impedimentum praestat caeteris.

VERS. 8.-- Usquedum fodiam, etc. Donec radicem infructuosae mentis humilem, et praesentium pressurarum, et aeternae damnationis incutiendo metum. Fossa quippe in imo est, et increpatio, dum mentem sibi demonstrat, humiliat. Novit cultor terram excolere, qui novit vitia reprehendere. Intellexit duritiam et superbiam Judaeorum causam esse sterilitatis. Pollicetur itaque dura corda apostolicis ligonibus esse fodienda, et superbientia ad humilitatem 303Arevocanda. Mittam stercora. Id est, malorum quae fecit abominationem ad animum reducam, et compunctionis gratiam cum fructibus boni operis quasi de pinguedine stercoris exsuscitem. Peccata enim carnis stercora dicuntur, quae immittuntur ad radicem arboris, quando pravitatis conscientiae tangitur cogitationis memoria, et dum inde poenitet quasi per tactum stercoris redit ad fecunditatem operis.

VERS. 9.-- Et si quidem. Cum poenitentiae ordo exigeret, ut apponeret, reservabitur vel aliquid tale, nihil tamen subjunxit, sed suspendit sententiam.

Sin autem. Scilicet, non fecerit fructum, continue judicium venturae damnationis annexuit, quia procliviores 303Bad negandum quam ad credendum videbat.

VERS. 10.-- Erat autem docens. Hanc similitudinem de ficulnea dicitur Dominus docuisse in Synagoga, ut intimetur ad ipsam Synagogam pertinuisse parabolam. Quod enim fructum quaerit in ficulnea nec invenit, significat quod verbum commodat Synagogae, nec illa recipit, quam tamen non ex toto exstirpandam, sed reliquias per fidem salvandas intelligens, mox ibidem primitivae Ecclesiae sanatio sub specie incurvae mulieris subsequitur. Idem enim significat ficulnea reservata quod mulier erecta. Sabbatis. Sabbatis docet et curvam erigit, quia per verbum praedicationis a temporalibus ad aeterna erectos ad quietem resurrectionis perducit.

303CVERS. 11.-- Ecce mulier, etc. Haec mulier non nisi post decem et octo annos erigi potuit, quia humana natura hanc corruptionem deponere et ad induendam incorruptionem aeternae beatitudinis erigi non poterit, nisi prius impleverit legem quae in decem praeceptis continetur, et gratiam in qua Christi resurrectio facta est et nostra exspectatur.

Annis, etc. Senarius in quo mundi creatura perfecta est, operum perfectionem signat, qui per tres multiplicatos decem et octo reddit, et signat eos qui in tribus temporibus Dominicae visitationis, scilicet ante legem et sub lege et sub gratia, perfectionem operum habent. Hoc ergo tempore Judaea a suae mentis rectitudine curvata fuit, quae magis terrena quam coelestia noverat, operatur.

303DVERS. 14.-- Quia Sabbato. Deus ab operibus mundi Sabbato requievit. Unde et Moyses Sabbato a servili et noxia actione feriandum praecepit, praefigurans tempus quo nostra saecularia opera, non vero religiosa, id est laudandi Deum cessabunt. In signum hujus rei etiam Sabbato Dominus curat. Hoc autem non intelligens archisynagogus fallitur et fallit, quia lex in Sabbato non hominem curare, sed servilia opera facere, id est peccatis gravari prohibuit.

VERS. 15.-- Hypocritae. Merito hypocritas, id est simulatores vocat, qui cum magistri plebium videri appetant, sanationem hominis non reverentur postponere curae pecoris. Non solvit bovem. Mystice. Bos qui cognovit possessorem suum et asinus qui novit 304Apraesepe domini sui, Judaicum et Gentilem populum significant, qui uterque peccati vinculis absolutus, sitim aestumque hujus mundi per haustum Dominici fontis deposuit. In his ergo duobus animalibus vocationem duorum populorum, adversantibus Judaeis, praenuntiat Dominus.

VERS. 16.-- Hanc autem, etc. Filia Abrahae, quaeque fidelis anima filia Abrahae, Ecclesia de utroque populo ad fidem collecta, quae tempore legis et Dominicae resurrectionis impleto, per septiformem gratiam Spiritus sancti vincula longae captivitatis erupit: et sic idem est bovem vel asinum a praesepio solutos ad potum agi, quod est filiam Abrahae a vinculo noxiae inclinationis erigi

VERS. 17.-- Erubescebant omnes. Qui de dictis 304BSalvatoris erubescunt, ficulneae sterili se similes ostendunt. Qui ergo gaudent de miraculis, pertinent ad filiam Abrahae, id est Ecclesiam, Deum de sua erectione glorificantem. Adversarii. Scribae et Pharisaei, qui nec aperte veritati contradicere possunt, nec propter invidiam credere volunt. Omnis populus gaudebat. Simplices, humiles verba veritatis et miracula amantes, et isti sunt figura gentium, quae excaecatis Judaeis congregandae sunt ad fidem per humilitatem. Unde et Salvator fidem eorum commendat sub comparatione grani sinapis.

VERS. 18.-- Dicebat ergo. Quandoquidem aderant gaudentes et glorificantes, dicebat ad commendationem et instructionem illorum qui per humilitatem et fidei fervorem exaltandi sunt in Ecclesia.

304CVERS. 19.-- Simile est, etc. Granum sinapis, res vilis et simplex, sed, si teritur, vim suam fundit. Regnum ergo coelorum, id est fides per quam venitur ad regnum, comparatur grano sinapis: quia primo humilis et quasi abjecta et amari saporis propter infirmitatem Christi et scandalum crucis; sed trita adversis, gratiam suae virtutis effundit et odore suo circumstantes adimplet, dum gloriam resurrectionis et ascensionis etiam post mortem praedicavit toto orbe terrarum. Vel corpus Christi granum sinapis, quod accipiens Joseph in horto sepelivit, quod postea in resurrectione et ascensione crevit. Expandit ramos, praedicatores per totum mundum mittens, in quorum dictis et consolationibus fideles a fatigatione mundi respirant.

304DVERS. 21.-- Quod acceptum, etc. Mulier Ecclesia, cujus farina sumus, quotquot timoris et spei exercitio quasi mola superiore et inferiore conterimur, ut unus panis multi simus in Christo. Abscondit ergo Ecclesia fermentum dilectionis in farinae sata tria, quia praecipit ut diligamus Deum toto corde, tota anima, tota virtute.

VERS. 22.-- Et ibat, etc. Ubique indifferenter praedicabat, non personas discernens, sed salutem credentibus quaerens.

VERS. 24.-- Contendite intrare, etc. Quasi diceret: Vere pauci, et illi cum magnis laboribus, quia nisi mentis intentio ferveat, unda mundi non vincitur, per quam anima ad ima semper revocatur.

305AVERS. 25.-- Cum autem, etc. Paterfamilias Christus, cum ubique ex Divinitate sit, illis jam intus esse dicitur, quos in coelo praesens sua visione laetificat, sed quasi foris est his quos in hac peregrinatione certantes occultus adjuvat. Intrabit ergo cum totam Ecclesiam ad sui contemplationem perducet. Claudet ostium, cum reprobis locum poenitentiae tollet, qui foris stantes pulsabunt, id est a justis segregati, misericordiam quam contempserunt frustra implorabunt. Incipietis foris stare. Modo tempore misericordiae contendite, quia tempore judicii reprobis tollitur locus poenitentiae, qui nunc aperitur cunctis pie quaerentibus.

Nescio vos. Non novit lux tenebras, id est non aspicit, quia, si aspiceret, tenebrae non essent.

305BVERS. 26.-- Manducavimus, etc. Non festivitatum epulatio juvat, quae fidei pietas non commendat. Non scientia Scripturarum notum Deo facit, quem operum iniquitas indignum ostendit. Vel de Judaeis simpliciter intelligendum est, qui respuentes mysteria fidei, gratos se Deo arbitrantur, quod in templo victimis oblatis coram Domino epulantur, et lectionem prophetarum auscultant. Vel mystice: Manducat coram Domino et bibit, qui verbi pabulum avide suscipit.

In plateis, etc. Scriptura in obscurioribus cibus est, quia quasi exponendo frangitur, et manducando glutitur. Potus enim in apertioribus, ubi ita absorbetur sicut invenitur. Et occulta ergo et aperta se 305Cintellexisse testantur, qui, reprobante judice, quod manducaverunt conqueruntur.

VERS. 28.-- Ibi erit fletus, etc. Fletus de ardore, stridor dentium de frigore solet excitari, quia ibi transibunt ab aquis nivium ad calorem nimium. Vel stridor prodit indignantis affectum, quod sero poenitet, quod deliquit pertinaciter. Vel stridebunt dentibus, qui hic de edacitate gaudebunt. Flebunt oculi qui hic per concupiscentias vagabuntur. Et nota per fletum qui est oculorum, et stridorem dentium, veram impiorum resurrectionem.

VERS. 29.-- Et venient. Multi prius ferventes postea torpent. Multi prius frigidi, subito ardescunt. Multi in saeculo despecti, in futuro sunt glorificandi. Alii apud homines gloriosi, in futuro sunt damnandi. 305DVel gentiles, qui diu sine fide vixerant, ad fidem sunt vocandi, Judaei exsecrandi.

VERS. 32.-- Dicite vulpi, etc. Vulpis animal dolosum, in fovea ad insidias latens, odore fetens, nunquam rectis itineribus incedens. Haec omnia haereticis (quorum typum tenet Herodes) congruunt, qui Christum, id est humilitatem Christianae fidei, in credentibus conantur exstinguere. Ecce ejicio daemonia, etc. Quasi diceret: Nec Herodes, nec haeretici quorum ipse figura est, poterunt perturbare quod intendo facere. Intendo autem ejicere daemonia de cordibus hominum, ut relictis vanitatibus in me credant; et perficere sanitates, ut secundum mea praecepta vivant; postea consummor in meo corpore, 306Aquod est Ecclesia, quam in die resurrectionis glorificatam ad consortium angelorum perducam.

VERS. 33.-- Verumtamen oportet. Non poterit perturbare Herodes quin ita agam et ita consummar, sed nec mortem corporis mei poterit operari, quam in Jerusalem oportet fieri, ubi non ipse sed Pilatus dominatur. Hodie et cras, etc. Prima dies, est per Dei gratiam abrenuntiare vanitatibus. Secunda, concordare veritati moribus et vita. Tertia, est ultima glorificatio.

VERS. 34.-- Jerusalem, etc. Ubi prophetae mei, ibi ego caput prophetarum debeo immolari, pro quo peccato juste ipsa destruetur, cui destructioni eorum sicut pius pater condoleo. Quoties volui congregare. Ac si dicat: Ego volui et tu noluisti: et quotquot 306Bcongregavi mea voluntate semper efficaci, te nolente feci, quia ingrata semper fuisti. Filios tuos. In hoc quod dicit: Quoties volui congregare filios tuos, etc., omnes retro prophetas a se missos testatur. Quemadmodum avis, etc. Pulchre qui Herodem de sua nece tractantem vulpem vocaverat, se avi comparat, quia fraudulenta vulpes semper avibus insidias tendit.

VERS. 35.-- Domus vestra. Templum vel civitas, quia, occiso Domino, Romani quasi vacuum nidum diripientes, tulerunt locum et gentem. Non videbitis me, etc. Nisi poenitentiam egeritis et confessi fueritis me Filium omnipotentis Patris, in secundo adventu faciem meam non videbitis. Cum dicetis, etc. Hoc quidem turbae dixerunt Domino veniente in Jerusalem. 306CSed quia Lucas non dicit Dominum ad Galilaeos postea reversum, ut ab illis hoc ei decantaretur, cogit mystice hoc de adventu claritatis intelligi.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 1.-- And there were present. While the master of truth was instructing those present how they ought to prepare themselves for the coming of the judge, and how they that do not prepare themselves are to be condemned, tidings are brought him of the Galileans, who as it were justly sought this for the cause that they had not well prepared themselves: and the Lord answers both that they truly 301Dhad been sinners and had justly perished, and that other sinners shall likewise perish, if they take no heed that they are sinners. Bringing tidings. As if they said: It appears that these men were sinners and to be condemned for ever, who by so wicked a death were slain by an impious governor in the midst of their own sacrifices. Of the Galileans. The Galileans pay the impious penalties of their own crimes by a wicked death; yet it was not that death itself that harmed them, so that they should be sent into the second death, but their wicked life. And they were punished for the correction of the living, that when the pestilent man is scourged the fool may become wiser, or for an example to those who will not be corrected, and who therefore shall perish most wretchedly.

VERS. 3.-- All, etc. This was fulfilled after the Lord's passion, when the Romans so laid waste the impenitent 302AJews, beginning from Galilee, that not only the courts of the temple but even the inner parts of the house, whither the Galileans had no access, were defiled with human blood.

VERS. 4.-- As those eighteen. These men of Jerusalem, like the Galileans also, were not the only sinners, but were punished for the terror of the rest; and they, crushed by the fall of the tower, signify that the Jews who would not repent were to perish together with their own walls. Nor is it without purpose that they are eighteen, which number among the Greeks is expressed by iota and ita, that is, by the same letters with which the name Jesus begins. And it signifies that those Jews chiefly were to perish who would not receive the name of the Saviour. That tower signifies him who is the tower of strength, which is rightly in Siloe, because Siloe, which is interpreted 302Bsent, signifies him who, sent by the Father, came into the world, who shall crush all upon whom he shall fall.

VERS. 6.-- A fig tree. In the beginning of the human race Adam and Eve, when they had sinned, made themselves aprons of fig leaves. By the leaves of the fig tree, therefore, sins are understood. And they were under the fig tree as under the shadow of death, of whom it is said: They that sat under the shadow of death, light is risen to them (Isa. XI). [Vulg. Is 9:2 reads habitantibus in regione umbrae mortis, lux orta est eis, "to them that dwelt in the region of the shadow of death, light is risen"; the qui sedebant form belongs to Mt 4:16, and neither witness has sub umbra or lumen.]

VERS. 7.-- Seeking fruit. The vineyard of the Lord sabaoh is the house of Israel, in which house the Synagogue was founded as a fig tree in a vineyard. But he who permitted the one sinning in the vineyard to be plundered by wayfarers commanded this fig tree also to be cut down. In it he seeks fruit, not as one who did not know that it was there, but he shows in a figure that in the Synagogue 302Cit ought by now to have fruit. Nor does he seek before the time, who comes through three years. He who through Moses established the Synagogue, himself appearing in the flesh teaches often in the Synagogue, seeks the fruit of faith, but finds it not in the Pharisees.

And he said to the dresser of the vineyard. The dresser of the vineyard: these are the teachers and the apostles, by whose prayers and admonitions the unceasing care of God is supplied; to whom the Lord often complains of the unfruitful nation of the Jews, that through the three years of his visitation, that is, in the edicts of the law, in the testimonies of the prophets, in the grace of the Gospel, it has been negligent.

Why doth it also the ground, etc. By the ground occupied by the barren fig tree may be signified the Jewish people, which is pressed by the harmful shadow of its rulers, that it may not see the light of truth, 302Dand is hindered by their examples, that it may not grow warm with the sun of love; whence (Matth. XXIII): Woe to you, Scribes and Pharisees, etc. He occupies the ground who does not work with good works the place that he holds, who by the example of his crookedness affords a hindrance to the rest.

VERS. 8.-- Until I dig, etc. That is, until I make the root of the unfruitful mind humble, by striking into it the fear both of present afflictions and of eternal damnation. For a ditch is in the lowest place, and rebuke, while it shows the mind to itself, humbles it. He knows how to till the ground who knows how to reprove vices. He understood that the hardness and pride of the Jews was the cause of their barrenness. And so he promises that hard hearts are to be dug with apostolic mattocks, and proud hearts to be called back to humility. 303AAnd put on dung. That is, I will bring back to their mind the abomination of the evils they have done, and will rouse up the grace of compunction, with the fruits of good work, as it were from the richness of the dung. For the sins of the flesh are called dung, which are laid to the root of the tree when the memory of the thought touches the conscience of its own crookedness; and while a man repents thereof, he returns, as it were by the touch of dung, to the fruitfulness of work.

VERS. 9.-- And if indeed. Although the order of repentance required that he should add "it shall be spared," or something of the kind, yet he subjoined nothing, but left the sentence hanging.

But if not. That is, if it shall not bear fruit, he at once annexed the judgment of the damnation to come, because he saw men more prone 303Bto deny than to believe.

VERS. 10.-- And he was teaching. This similitude of the fig tree the Lord is said to have taught in the synagogue, that it might be intimated that the parable belonged to the Synagogue itself. For that he seeks fruit in the fig tree and finds none signifies that he lends the word to the Synagogue and she does not receive it; yet understanding that she is not to be rooted out wholly, but that a remnant is to be saved through faith, straightway in the same place there follows the healing of the primitive Church under the figure of the bowed woman. For the fig tree spared signifies the same thing as the woman set upright. On the sabbaths. On the sabbaths he teaches and raises up her that was bowed, because through the word of preaching he leads those raised up from temporal things to eternal into the rest of the resurrection.

303CVERS. 11.-- And behold a woman, etc. This woman could not be raised up save after eighteen years, because human nature will not be able to lay aside this corruption and be raised up to put on the incorruption of eternal blessedness, unless it first fulfil the law which is contained in the ten precepts, and the grace in which the resurrection of Christ has been wrought and our own is awaited.

Years, etc. The number six, in which the creature of the world was perfected, signifies the perfection of works, and multiplied by three it renders eighteen; and it signifies those who in the three times of the Lord's visitation, namely before the law, and under the law, and under grace, have perfection of works. In this time therefore Judea was bent away from the uprightness of her mind, she who had known earthly things rather than heavenly, works.

303DVERS. 14.-- Because on the sabbath. God rested on the sabbath from the works of the world. Whence Moses also commanded that on the sabbath men should keep holiday from servile and harmful action, prefiguring the time in which our secular works, but not indeed the religious, that is, of praising God, shall cease. In sign of this thing the Lord heals on the sabbath also. But the ruler of the synagogue, not understanding this, is deceived and deceives, because the law forbade not to heal a man on the sabbath, but to do servile works, that is, to be weighed down with sins.

VERS. 15.-- Hypocrites. He rightly calls them hypocrites, that is, dissemblers, who, while they desire to be seen as masters of the people, are not ashamed to put the healing of a man after the care of a beast. Doth he not loose his ox. Mystically. The ox that knew its owner and the ass that knew 304Aits master's crib signify the Jewish and the Gentile people, each of which, loosed from the bonds of sin, laid aside the thirst and the heat of this world by a draught from the Lord's fountain. In these two animals, therefore, the Lord foretells the calling of the two peoples, the Jews withstanding it.

VERS. 16.-- And this woman, etc. A daughter of Abraham: every faithful soul is a daughter of Abraham, the Church gathered to the faith out of both peoples, which, the time of the law and of the Lord's resurrection being fulfilled, broke the bonds of a long captivity through the sevenfold grace of the Holy Spirit: and so it is the same thing for the ox or the ass to be loosed from the manger and led to drink, as for the daughter of Abraham to be raised up from the bond of a harmful bowing down

VERS. 17.-- All were ashamed. They who are ashamed of the sayings 304Bof the Saviour show themselves like to the barren fig tree. They therefore who rejoice at the miracles belong to the daughter of Abraham, that is, to the Church, glorifying God for her raising up. The adversaries. The Scribes and Pharisees, who can neither openly gainsay the truth nor, because of envy, are willing to believe. All the people rejoiced. The simple and the humble, who love the words of truth and the miracles; and these are a figure of the Gentiles, who, the Jews being blinded, are to be gathered to the faith through humility. Whence the Saviour also commends their faith under the comparison of the grain of mustard seed.

VERS. 18.-- He said therefore. Since there were present those who rejoiced and glorified, he spoke to the commendation and the instruction of those who through humility and fervour of faith are to be exalted in the Church.

304CVERS. 19.-- It is like, etc. The grain of mustard seed is a cheap and simple thing, but if it be crushed it pours out its own strength. The kingdom of heaven, therefore, that is, the faith through which one comes to the kingdom, is compared to a grain of mustard seed: because at first it is humble and as it were abject and of bitter savour, because of the weakness of Christ and the scandal of the cross; but crushed by adversities it pours forth the grace of its own power and fills those standing about with its odour, since even after death it preached the glory of the resurrection and of the ascension through the whole world. Or the body of Christ is the grain of mustard seed, which Joseph took and buried in the garden, and which afterwards grew in the resurrection and the ascension. It spread out its branches, sending preachers through the whole world, in whose words and consolations the faithful take breath from the weariness of the world.

304DVERS. 21.-- Which being taken, etc. The woman is the Church, whose meal we are, as many of us as are ground, by the exercise of fear and of hope, as by an upper and a nether millstone, that we many may be one bread in Christ. The Church therefore hides the leaven of love in three measures of meal, because she bids us love God with the whole heart, with the whole soul, with the whole strength.

VERS. 22.-- And he went, etc. Everywhere he preached without distinction, not discerning persons, but seeking salvation for them that believe.

VERS. 24.-- Strive to enter, etc. As if he said: Truly they are few, and those with great labours, because unless the intention of the mind be fervent, the wave of the world is not overcome, by which the soul is always called back to the depths.

305AVERS. 25.-- But when, etc. Christ the householder, though by his Godhead he is everywhere, is said to be already within to those whom, present in heaven, he gladdens by the sight of himself; but he is as it were without to those whom, hidden, he helps as they strive in this pilgrimage. He will enter therefore when he shall bring the whole Church to the contemplation of himself. He will shut the door when he shall take away from the reprobate the place of repentance, who, standing without, shall knock; that is, being separated from the just, they shall implore in vain the mercy which they despised. You shall begin to stand without. Strive now, in the time of mercy, because in the time of judgment the place of repentance is taken from the reprobate, which is now open to all that seek it dutifully.

I know you not. The light knows not the darkness, that is, it does not look upon it, because if it looked upon it, there would be no darkness.

305BVERS. 26.-- We have eaten, etc. The feasting of festivals does not profit, which the piety of faith does not commend. The knowledge of the Scriptures does not make him known to God whom the iniquity of his works shows to be unworthy. Or it is to be understood simply of the Jews, who, spurning the mysteries of the faith, think themselves acceptable to God because they feast before the Lord in the temple upon the victims offered, and give ear to the reading of the prophets. Or mystically: he eats and drinks before the Lord who eagerly receives the food of the word.

In the streets, etc. Scripture in its more obscure places is food, because it is broken as it were by expounding and is swallowed by eating. For it is drink in the more open places, where it is drunk down just as it is found. Both the hidden things, therefore, and the open they testify that they have 305Cunderstood, who, when the judge reproves them, complain of what they have eaten.

VERS. 28.-- There shall be weeping, etc. Weeping is wont to be stirred up by heat, the gnashing of teeth by cold, because there they shall pass from the waters of snow to excessive heat. Or the gnashing betrays the feeling of one indignant, that he repents too late of what he stubbornly did amiss. Or they shall gnash with their teeth who here shall rejoice in gluttony. Those eyes shall weep which here shall wander through concupiscences. And note, through the weeping which belongs to the eyes and the gnashing of the teeth, the true resurrection of the wicked.

VERS. 29.-- And they shall come. Many who are fervent at first grow torpid afterwards. Many who are cold at first suddenly take fire. Many despised in this world are to be glorified in the world to come. Others glorious among men are to be condemned in the world to come. 305DOr the Gentiles, who had long lived without faith, are to be called to the faith, and the Jews to be execrated.

VERS. 32.-- Say ye to the fox, etc. The fox is a crafty animal, lurking in a hole for ambush, foul of smell, never going by straight paths. All these things suit the heretics, whose type Herod holds, who strive to quench Christ, that is, the humility of the Christian faith, in them that believe. Behold I cast out demons, etc. As if he said: Neither Herod nor the heretics, of whom he himself is the figure, shall be able to disturb what I purpose to do. And I purpose to cast out demons from the hearts of men, that, leaving vanities behind, they may believe in me; and to perfect healings, that they may live according to my precepts; afterwards I am consummated in my body, 306Awhich is the Church, which, glorified on the day of the resurrection, I shall bring to the fellowship of the angels.

VERS. 33.-- Nevertheless I must. Herod shall not be able to disturb it but that I so act and am so consummated; nor shall he be able to work the death of my body, which must come to pass in Jerusalem, where not he but Pilate bears rule. To-day and to-morrow, etc. The first day is to renounce vanities through the grace of God. The second, to be in accord with the truth in manners and in life. The third is the final glorification.

VERS. 34.-- Jerusalem, etc. Where my prophets are, there I, the head of the prophets, must be sacrificed; for which sin she herself shall justly be destroyed, and with that destruction of theirs I grieve as a devoted father. How often would I have gathered. As if he said: I would, and thou wouldest not: and as many as 306BI have gathered by my will, which is always effectual, I did it while thou wast unwilling, because thou hast always been ungrateful. Thy children. In this, that he says: How often would I have gathered thy children, etc., he bears witness to all the prophets sent by him in time past. Even as a bird, etc. Beautifully does he who had called Herod a fox, while he was plotting his death, compare himself to a bird, because the deceitful fox always lays ambushes for birds.

VERS. 35.-- Your house. The temple or the city, because, the Lord being slain, the Romans, plundering as it were an empty nest, took away both the place and the nation. You shall not see me, etc. Unless you do penance and confess me to be the Son of the almighty Father, you shall not see my face at the second coming. When you shall say, etc. This indeed the crowds said when the Lord was coming into Jerusalem. 306CBut because Luke does not say that the Lord afterwards returned to the Galileans, so that this might be chanted to him by them, he compels it to be understood mystically of the coming of his brightness.

CAPUT XIV.

Et factum est, etc. Ὕδωρ , aqua, inde hydropis, morbus aquosus subcutaneus, de vitio vesicae natus cum inflatione turgente et anhelitu fetido. Et est proprium hydropici, quanto plus bibit, tanto plus sitit, et ideo comparatur ei quem fluxus carnalium voluptatum exuberans aggravat, comparaturque diviti avaro.

VERS. 3.-- Si licet, etc. Sabbatis praecipue docet et operatur Jesus, non solum propter spirituale sabbatum, sed et propter populi celebriorem conventum, 306Dquaerens salutem omnium.

VERS. 4.-- At illi tacuerunt. Merito tacent, qui contra se dicturum quidquid dixerat vident. Si enim licet, cur observant? Si non licet, cur pecora curant? Ipse vero. Ideo ante Pharisaeos hydropicum sanat, et mox contra avaritiam disputat, ut ipsos avaritiae arguat per quam sabbatum violant, et legem male interpretantur. In sabbato enim non a bonis, sed a malis operibus feriandum est, unde dicitur: Omne opus servile non facietis in eo (Lev. XXIII), id est peccatum. Sic in aeterna requie a malis tantum feriabitur, non a bonis.

VERS. 5.-- Cujus vestrum. Competenti exemplo solvit quaestionem, ut ostendat eos sabbatum violare 307Ain opere cupiditatis, qui eum violare arguunt in opere charitatis. Bos in puteum. Congrue animali quod decidit in puteum hydropicum comparat, quia noxio humore peribat. Et bene bovem et asinum nominat, ut vel sapientes vel hebetes, vel utrumque populum signat, scilicet Judaeum pressum jugo legis, et gentilem nulla ratione domitum, sed stolidum et brutum, quos omnes a puteo concupiscentiae demersos extrahit: Omnes enim peccaverunt, et egent gratia Dei (Rom. XXXV).

VERS. 7.-- Dicebat autem. Postquam convicerat illos de avaritia et violatione sabbati, incipit docere de Evangelica gratia illos qui aderant idonei, invitans illos ad humilitatem contra tumorem quem habebant in lege Pharisaei, vel intendens in doctrina 307Bsua quomodo debeant accubitus in Ecclesia eligere.

VERS. 8.-- Cum invitatus fueris: Cum per gratiam fidei vocatus a praedicatore, membris Ecclesiae te junxeris, non te de meritis gloriando, quasi caeteris sublimior extollas. Ne forte honoratior. Honoratiori post invitato locum dat, qui de suae conversationis confidentia securior factus, cito illorum qui se in Christo secuti sunt agilitate praeitur. Et cum rubore novissimum locum tenet, etc., cum de aliis meliora cognoscens, quidquid de sua operatione altum senserat, humiliat, dicens cum Propheta: Exaltatus autem humiliatus sum et confessus (Psal. LXXXVII).

VERS. 9.-- Et tunc incipias, etc. Et tunc, erubescens quod de te aliquando majora praesumpsisti, incipies ad poenitentiam humiliari, et te minoris 307Ccognoscere meriti, ne nunc incipias quaerere quod tibi servatur in fine, quia haereditas ad quam festinatur in principio, in novissima benedictione deficiet.

VERS. 10.-- Tunc erit tibi gloria, etc. Quotidie Dominus, dum convivas suos visitat et dijudicat, tanta spiritus sui humilibus dona praestat, ut eos coetus fidelium cum admiratione glorificet; stupensque in auctoris sui laudem prosiliat, dicens: Mihi autem nimis honorificati sunt amici tui, Deus (Psal. CXXXVIII).

VERS. 12.-- Dicebat autem. Secundum ordinem litterae. Postquam monuit invitatos qualiter debeant accumbere, monet et invitatorem quos debeat invitare, ne temporalis expensio bonorum quam facit 307Dcedat in vacuum, vel forsitan augmentet peccatum: sed ita expendat temporalia, ut per haec pateat via ad aeterna. Et ei qui se invitaverat. Principi Pharisaeorum loquitur, qui forsitan superbire poterat, quia tot ad convivium quasi ex misericordia vocaverat; vel sicut ex verbis Domini potest concipi, ideo potius invitaverat, ut ab illis iterum invitaretur, et sic nulla specialis retributio sequeretur. Noli vocare amicos. Fratres, amicos, et divites alterutrum convivia celebrare, non quasi scelus interdicit, sed ostendit non valere ad praemia vitae. Sunt autem et quaedam talium divitum convivia, quae non solum in praesenti retributiones, sed etiam damnationem percipiunt futuro; unde Apostolus: Non in comessationibus 308Aet ebrietatibus (Rom. VIII), id est in luxuriosis conviviis, quae aut collatione omnium celebrantur, aut vicibus contubernalibus solent exhiberi, ubi neminem pudeat aliquid inhonestum facere vel dicere. Ne forte. Quasi dicat: Si intendis invitare, ut inviteris, ibi potes falli; sed si distribueris pauperibus, de mercede non falleris.

VERS. 13.-- Voca pauperes. Qui pauperes vocat in futuro praemium percipiet. Qui amicos et fratres et divites vocat, recepit mercedem suam. Sed si hoc propter Deum facit, in exemplum filiorum Job, sicut caetera fraternae dilectionis officia, ipse qui jussit remunerat. Qui luxuriosos propter lasciviam vocat, poena non carebit.

VERS. 14.-- In resurrectione. Cum omnes resurrecturi 308Bsint, tamen singulariter et quasi propria dicitur resurrectio justorum, qui in ea sunt immutandi.

VERS. 15.-- Haec cum audisset, etc. Illectus dulcedine auditae resurrectionis in laudem ejus prorumpit, et in ea vere beatos fore asserit, etsi non bene intelligat quibus meritis ad illam beatitudinem possit attingi. Beatus, etc. Si quis manducaverit ex hoc pane, vivet in aeternum (Joan. VI). Si quis incarnationis sacramento perfecte incorporatus, divinae majestatis visione frui meruerit, hic aeterna beatitudine gaudebit. Sed quia multi fide tenus quasi orando percipiunt, dulcedinem vero ejus veraciter attingere fastidiunt, torpor eorum redarguitur.

VERS. 16.-- Homo quidam, etc. Verbum incarnatum 308Csatietatem internae dulcedinis multis praeparavit, sed pauci veniunt, quia multi qui per fidem ei subjiciuntur male vivendo ei continuo contradicunt. Coenam magnam. Hoc convivium, non prandium sed coena dicitur, quia post prandium de quo Matthaeus agit, coena restat, post coenam nullum convivium restat: de prandio multi, et de coena nullus ejicitur.

VERS. 17.-- Et misit servum. Hora coenae finis saeculi; unde Apostolus: Nos scimus in quos fines saeculorum devenerunt (I Cor. X). In hoc fine mittitur servus, id est ordo praedicatorum ad invitatos per legem et prophetas, ut repulso fastidio ad gustandam coenam se praepararent, quia omnia jam sunt parata. Christo enim immolato, introitus regni 308Dpatet.

VERS. 18.-- Et coeperunt, etc. Ideo excusant, quia nemini intercluditur regnum, nisi quem suae vocis professio excluserit. Excusat autem se omnis qui plus terrena quam coelestia diligit, etiamsi ad coelestia se tendere dicat. Villam emi. Villa, id est terrena substantia. Exit ergo videre villam, qui sola exteriora cogitat propter substantiam, et hoc est quod ab amatoribus mundi prius quaeritur, scilicet substantia corporis.

(AUG.) In empta villa, dominatio notatur et superbia. Habere enim villam, etc., usque ad amor rerum terrenarum viscus est spiritualium pennarum.

309ARogo te. Dum dicit: Rogo te, habe me excusatum, humilitas sonat in voce dum venire contemnit, superbia in actione.

VERS. 19.-- Juga boum. Quinque juga boum, quinque corporis sensus, qui recte juga dicuntur, qui in utroque sexu geminantur; qui quoniam non interna, sed sola exteriora cognoscunt et tangunt, recte per eos curiositas designatur, quae dum exterius investigat vitam proximi, sua intima nescit. Et curiosi animus quanto est peritus alieni, tanto ignarus sui. Nec discrepant verba excusantis a significatione sui vitii, dum dicit: Eo probare illa. Quia probatio ad curiositatem solet pertinere.

VERS. 20.-- Uxorem duxi. Quia multi non propter fecunditatem, sed propter desideria carnis uxores 309Bducunt, ideo per rem istam carnis voluptas designatur, propter quam ad coenam Dei fastidiosus venire recusat.

VERS. 22.-- Factum est, etc. Multos tales ex Judaeis ad coenam tuam collegimus, sed isti locum convivii tui non implent, superest locus ubi gentium numerositas suscipiatur.

VERS. 23.-- Exi in vias et sepes, etc. Nota quod in hac tertia invitatione non dicitur: Invita, sed Compelle intrare. Qui enim hujus mundi adversitatibus fracti ad Dei amorem redeunt, atque a praesentis vitae desideriis corriguntur, illi non nisi compulsi intrant. Quidam autem vocantur et contemnunt, qui domum intellectus accipiunt, sed opere non implent. Quidam vocantur et veniunt, qui acceptam 309Cintelligentiam opere adimplent.

VERS. 25.-- Ibant autem turbae, etc. Turbae quae sequuntur Jesum iter facientem in Jerusalem, signant eos qui ad coenam invitati vel compulsi veniunt, et tota intentione Jesum imitantur, praecedentem ad aeternae pacis visionem. Hos ergo instruit qui sincero amore patriam illam debeant appetere, et pro ea carnalis pietatis affectibus renuntiare.

VERS. 26.-- Si quis venit. Simul et odisse et diligere possumus: ut et cognitione conjunctos, et divino praecepto diligamus, et quos adversarios in via Dei patimur, odiendo et fugiendo nesciamus. Adhuc. Tunc bene animam odimus, cum ejus carnalibus desideriis non acquiescimus, sed reluctamur et frangimus.

309DVERS. 27.-- Et qui non bajulat. Crux duobus modis bajulatur: cum vel per abstinentiam caro afficitur, vel per compassionem proximi affligitur animus. Et venit post me. Aliquis enim carnem lacerat pro inani gloria, vel proximo carnaliter compatitur ut culpae faveat, hic crucem ferre videtur, sed Deum non sequitur.

VERS. 28.-- Quis enim ex vobis. Postquam Dominus sequentibus se in Jerusalem dedit sublimia praecepta de contemptu mundi, et de abjectione corporis, supponit praeceptum de perseverantia, ad quam necessaria est cum humilitate patientia. Et hoc per simile aedificantis. Turrim aedificare. Quia sublimia praecepta dedit, aedificandae sublimitatis 310Acomparationem subjungit. Computat sumptus. Ille computat sumptus, qui discernit esse erogandam temporalem pecuniam, et cor avertendum a cupiditatibus mundi, et animam praeparandam contra adversa mundi.

VERS. 29.-- Omnes qui vident. Sicut ait Apostolus: Facti sumus spectaculum mundo, angelis et hominibus (II Cor. IV). Et in omni quod agimus, considerare occultos adversarios debemus, qui nostris operibus insidiantur, et nisi contra eos vigilemus, patimur irrisores quos habemus ad malum suasores.

VERS. 31.-- Aut quis, etc. Post similitudinem de turre aedificanda, supponit similitudinem majoris rei, de rege pugnaturo. In quo ostenditur per impletionem praecedentium mandatorum nullum ad gloriam 310Bposse pertingere, nisi severi judicis sententia temperetur per misericordiam. In conspectu enim Dei omnis homo imperfectus. Si possit. Quasi cum decem millibus occurrit Deo, qui offert opera decem mandatorum quae exterius gessit. Sed Deus quasi cum duplicato numero contravenit, cum vix illum praeparatum in solo opere, discutit simul de opere et cogitatione. Et ideo, dum adhuc longe est per dilationem judicii, nos fragilitatis nostrae insufficientiam attendentes, ad impetrandam severi judicis pacem debemus praemittere legationem lacrymarum, bonorum operum, piorum affectum.

VERS. 33.-- Sic ergo, etc. Infert a praemissis similitudinibus. Quandoquidem non bene potest aedificare qui non computat sumptus, nec secure potest occurrere 310Cqui non praemittit legationem, ergo nec meus potest esse discipulus qui non renuntiat omnibus.

VERS. 34.-- Bonum. Vere qui vult meus esse discipulus oportet ut renuntiet omnibus, quia ita erit bonum sal, id est dignus constitui in numero apostolorum, ad condienda corda auditorum spiritualia sapientia.

VERS. 35.-- Neque in sterquilinium. Sal sterilem facit terram. Unde ira victorum destructas urbes sale seminabat, ne in eis germen oriretur. Ita apostoli, destructo peccati regno, germen vitiorum compescunt. Sal cibos condit, carnes siccat. Praedicatio a vermibus et putredine vitiorum illaesam servat suo Creatori humanam naturam.

CHAPTER XIV.
