VERS. 24.-- For as, etc. Do not believe them, because the second coming will be sudden, and so manifest that none will be able to be ignorant of it from under heaven, 320Dbecause the crisis of the judgment is carried on under heaven, that is, in the midst of the air.

VERS. 25.-- But first, etc. Speaking of the glory of his coming, he inserts something also of the passion, so that when they saw dying him whom they had heard was to reign, they might soften the grief of the passion by the hope of the promised brightness.

VERS. 26.-- And as it came to pass. He builds up the sudden day of his coming by many examples, and that which he had compared to lightning (which swiftly flies across all things) he makes equal to the days of Noe or of Loth, when a sudden destruction came upon mortals.

VERS. 27.-- They did eat, etc. It is not marriages or foods that are condemned, since in these are set the supports of succession, in those the supports of nature, but the immoderate 321Ause of things lawful is reproved. For because by giving themselves wholly to these they were despising the judgments of God, they perished by fire or by water. Until the day. Allegorically: Noe builds the ark, when the Lord gathers the fideres into the Church. And when it is finished he enters it, when on the day of judgment he lights it up with his presence. But while the ark is being built the wicked are wanton, and while it is being entered they are struck with eternal damnation.

VERS. 28.-- In like manner, etc. Passing over the greatest crime of the Sodomites, he mentions those things alone which might be thought either light or nothing at all, that thou mayest understand with what punishment things unlawful are struck, if things lawful and those without which life is not led, when done more immoderately, shall be punished with fire and brimstone.

Loth. Allegorically: Loth, who is interpreted declining, is the people of the elect, who while they dwell in Sodom, 321Bthat is, among the reprobate as a stranger, decline their crimes as far as they can. When Loth goes out, Sodom perishes, because in the consummation of the world the angels shall go out, and shall separate the wicked from among the just, and shall cast them into the furnace of fire (Matth. XIII).

VERS. 30.-- The Son of man. He who, appearing in the meantime, sees all things, then appearing will judge all things, when he shall have beheld all men, forgetful of his judgments, made bondsmen to this world, so that they may deservedly be blotted out together with the very world they inhabit.

VERS. 31.-- In that day. [Vulg. Lc 17:31 In illa hora, "in that hour"] Thus far concerning this, that the kingdom, that is, Christ, comes not with observation but unlooked for: now, by what sort of men that coming ought to be awaited. He is on the housetop who, going beyond carnal things, lives spiritually as it were in the open air. His vessels 321Cin the house are the carnal concupiscences which he had loved before, but, seeking things above, had left behind in the world, and which, with the judgment imminent, he must needs not seek again.

And he that shall be in the field. What he had said, that he who is in the field ought not to turn back, lest it should seem to have been said only of those who are openly to return from the field, that is, who are to deny the Lord openly; he shows that there are many who, while they seem to keep their face toward the things before, yet look backward in mind.

VERS. 32.-- Be mindful. Loth's wife signifies those who look backward in tribulation, and turn themselves away from the hope of the divine promise, and therefore she was made a statue of salt, because by admonishing men not to do the like, she seasons their hearts that they be not foolish.

VERS. 33.-- Whosoever. He who shall wish to save the carnal life (by denying 321DChrist) in the world, shall lose it in the world to come; or he who seeks the eternal salvation of his soul, let him not hesitate to give it up unto the death of this present time.

VERS. 34.-- In that night. That is, in that tribulation so dark that they may be led into error, if it be possible, even the elect (Matth. XXIV). Two in a bed. Those, namely, who choose leisure, occupied neither with secular nor with ecclesiastical business.

VERS. 35.-- The other shall be left. He who, whether by human praise or by any corruption whatever, forsakes the state of life in which he was steeped. Of whom Jeremias says: The enemies saw her, and mocked at her sabbaths (Thren. I). There shall be two, etc. Two differences, of those who follow the round of moveable things. Which are designated by the feminine gender, 322Abecause they are ruled by the counsel of their elders, as women by men; which nevertheless grind together in so far as out of their own business they serve the uses of the Church. But some only for the sake of things eternal, who have as though not having, [Vulg. 1 Cor 7:29 et qui habent uxores, tamquam non habentes sint, "and they that have wives, as though they had none"] some for the sake of things temporal, who hope in the uncertainty of riches (I Tim VI).

VERS. 37.-- Who said to them. To those asking two things he answers one, namely that the saints are to be taken up with him; the other he intimates by subjoining, namely that the evil, driven back from him, are to be damned with the devil. Wheresoever, etc. Wheresoever the Lord shall be bodily, thither shall the elect be gathered, who by humbly imitating his passion are filled as it were from his flesh, whose youth shall be renewed as the eagle's through the resurrection.

CAPUT XVIII.

322BVERS. 2.-- Judex quidam. Haec parabola ad orandum et credendum, et non de nobis, sed de Deo praesumendum hortatur.

VERS. 3.-- Vidua autem. Haec vidua potest habere similitudinem Ecclesiae, quae desolata videtur donec veniat Dominus, qui tamen in secreto etiam nunc curam ejus gerit. Vindica me. Cum justi pro persecutoribus orare moneantur, tamen de malis salva justitia hanc vindictam expetunt: ut pereant omnes mali, vel conversi ad justitiam, vel amissa per supplicium potestate quam nunc contra bonos quandiu hoc ipsum bonis expedit vel temporaliter aliquid possunt. Quem finem cum justi venire desiderant, quamvis pro inimicis orent, tamen non absurde vindictam desiderare dicuntur.

322CVERS. 8.-- Verumtamen. Dico me facturum vindictam justorum, sed illi erunt valde pauci, quia cum in die judicii in forma Filii hominis apparuero, tanta erit raritas justorum, ut non tam ob clamorem fidelium injuste damnatorum, quam ob eorum torporem juste damnandorum totius jam mundi sit acceleranda ruina. Putas. In verbo increpatur dubitationis infidelitas, non opinatur Divinitas. Scit enim omnia per quem sunt omnia, sed dubitando increpat infidelium corda.

VERS. 10.-- Duo homines. Quia parabola qua ad orandum invitavit, ita conclusit, ut diceret, veniente Judice, difficile fidem in terra reperiendam. Ne quis sibi de supervacua fidei cognitione, vel etiam confessione blandiretur, in hac parabola, diligentius 322Dostendit a Deo non fidei verba, sed opera examinanda, inter quae maxime regnat humilitas, quam quia non habent superbi de sua justitia praesumentes, et infirmos despicientes, quasi vacui fide, cum orant non exaudiuntur.

VERS. 11.-- Deus, gratias, etc. Quatuor sunt species tumoris: cum vel quis bonum quod habet, a se habere aestimat: aut si datum a Deo credit, pro meritis datum putat; aut cum jactat se habere quod non habet; aut cum despectis caeteris appetit singulariter videri habere quae habet. Hac Pharisaeus peste laborabat, qui ideo non justificatus descendit, quia merita bonorum operum sibi singulariter tribuens, publicano se praetulit.

323AVERS. 12.-- Jejuno bis. Ecce Pharisaeus ad exhibendam abstinentiam, ad impetrandam misericordiam, ad referendas Deo gratias oculum habet, ad humilitatis custodiam non habet. Et quid prodest si tota civitas custodiatur, et unum foramen per quos hostes intrent relinquatur?

VERS. 13.-- Et publicanus. Sicut humilis, non audet appropinquare, ut ad eum Deus appropinquet: non aspicit ut aspiciatur; premit conscientiam, sed spem sublevat; percutit pectus, poenas de se exigit ut Deus parcat; confitetur ut Deus ignoscat; ignoscit Deus quod ille agnoscit. Deus, propitius. Publicanus humiliter orans ad illos pertinet de quibus dicitur: Deus non faciet vindictam electorum ad se clamantium. Pharisaeus merita jactans, ad illos de quibus subditur: 323BFilius hominis veniens, putas inveniet fidem in terra?

VERS. 14.-- Quia omnis, etc. Posita Pharisaei et publicani controversia, ponitur judicis sententia, ut caveatur superbia.

VERS. 15.-- Afferebant autem. Post dictam controversiam, post datam sententiam humili magistro, offeruntur infantes, ut ostendatur quod innocens et simplex aetas ad gratiam pertineat. Increpant apostoli, non quod nollent infantes manu et voce Salvatoris benedici, sed quia nondum perfecti in fide, putabant eum in similitudinem hominum lassari importunitate offerentium.

Increpabant. Hoc fortasse ideo, quia prius populum Judaeorum ex quo secundum carnem erant, salvari volebant, sed et pro Chananaea muliere supplicaverunt. 323CSciebant ergo mysterium, quod utriusque populi vocatio deberetur, sed fortasse adhuc nesciebant ordinem.

VERS. 16.-- Sinite pueros. Nemo prohibeat languidos venire ad medicum. Isti in ramo nihil adhuc commiserunt, sed in radice perierunt. Qui pro se agere non possunt, causam suam majoribus commiserunt. Tangat medicus, et benedicat pusillis cum majoribus (Psal. CXIII). Talium est enim. Non ait istorum, sed qui habent ex studio innocentiam et simplicitatem quam isti habent per naturam, quia non aetas regnat, sed mores. Non enim est virtus non posse peccare, sed nolle. Sicut puer non perseverat in ira, laesus non meminit, non concupiscit mulierem, non cogitat vel loquitur malum, sic vos nisi talem innocentiam et animi puritatem habueritis, 323Dnon intrabitis in regnum coelorum.

VERS. 17.-- Regnum Dei. Id est doctrinam Evangelii sicut puer jubemur accipere. Puer in discendo non contradicit doctoribus. Non rationes et verba componit ad resistendum, sed fideliter suscipit, obtemperat et quiescit.

VERS. 18.-- Et interrogavit eum. (BEDA.) Audierat hic princeps similes puerorum dignos esse, etc., usque ad his qui perfecti esse volunt, adjungit: Omnia vende et da pauperibus (Marc. X).

Magister bone. Quia bonum magistrum dixit, et non Deum vel Dei Filium confessus est dicit quamvis sanctum hominem comparatione Dei bonum non esse. Christus autem non renuit testimonium bonitatis, 324Ased magistri absque Deo excludit bonitatem.

VERS. 19.-- Dixit autem. (AMBR.) Versuta interrogatio, et ideo arguta responsio. Tentator enim, etc., usque ad cum his verbis definiatur contra naturam esse dives misericors.

Mandata nosti. Haec est puerilis innocentiae castitas, quae nobis imitanda proponitur, si regnum Dei ingredi volumus.

VERS. 23.-- Dives erat. Nullus dives, id est confidens divitiis suis, intrabit, ut Jesus secundum Matthaeum exponit. Non ergo divites manentes, sed qui esse desierunt intrabunt.

VERS. 25.-- Facilius est enim. Mystice. Facilius est Christum pati pro dilectoribus saeculi, quam dilectores saeculi ad Christum converti. Camelus dicitur 324BChristus, quia sponte humiliatus, infirmitatis nostrae onera sustulit. Acus punctio, per quam angustias passionis significat, qua passione velut acu, naturae nostrae quasi scissa vestimenta resarcire dignatus est, id est reparare post lapsum. Aliter: facilius est gentilem populum gibbo peccatorum deformem per angustam vitae viam ingredi, quam Judaeum de meritis gloriantem, et ad subeundum leve onus Christi se humiliare nolentem.

VERS. 26.-- Quis potest? Intelligunt in numero divitum deputandos omnes qui amant divitias, etsi non habent: aliter enim, cum sit turba major pauperum, non dicerent: Quis potest salvus fieri?

VERS. 29.-- Nemo. Qui carnalia pro Domino dimiserit, spiritualia accipiet. Marcus ait: Centies tantum 324Cnunc, etc. (Marc. X), id est, in hac vita persecutionibus plena gaudium regni degustant, et omnium electorum sincera dilectione fruuntur.

VERS. 30.-- Recipiat, etc. Qui pro Domino omnes divitias dimiserit, et carnis affectus contempserit, multo plura recipiet in praesenti, et a fratribus et sororibus propositi sui glutino spirituali colligatis, multo gratiosiorem in hac vita recipiet charitatem.

VERS. 31.-- Assumpsit. Praevidens ex passione sua discipulos perturbandos, praedicit et passionis poenam, et resurrectionis gloriam, ut cum morientem cernerent, resurrecturum non dubitarent. Et consummabuntur. In hoc ostendit quidquid prophetae praedixerunt ad suae dispensationis mysterium intendisse. In quo percutit haereticos, qui dicunt 324DChristum legi et prophetis contrarium praedicasse, et alium Veteris, alium Novi esse Deum Testatamenti. Confutat etiam paganorum dementiam, quia ejus crucem derident cum proximae suae passionis, et tempus quasi futurorum praescius ostendit, et locum quasi mortem non timens adiit.

VERS. 34.-- Et ipsi, etc. Discipuli sermonem Dominicae passionis toties replicatum ideo intelligere non valent, quia quem maxime videre desiderant, ejus mortem audire non possunt, et quem non solum hominem innocentem, sed etiam verum Deum esse sciunt, mori posse non putant.

VERS. 35.-- Caecus. Caecus significat humanum genus, quod a superna claritate exclusum damnationis 325Asuae patitur tenebras, sed a Domino appropinquante Jericho curatur. Jericho luna interpretatur, per quam defectus mortalitatis intelligitur. Dum ergo Verbum Dei infirmitatem nostrae carnis suscipit, homo ad cognoscenda redit. Caecus sedet juxta viam dum incipit credere in ipsum, qui dicit: Ego sum via (Joan. XIV). Mendicat, dum rogat.

VERS. 39.-- Et qui praeibant, etc. Qui Jesum praeeunt venientem, significant caecum carnalium vitiorum, quae dissipant cogitationem hominis et perturbant vocem orationis, ne Jesus ad illuminandum venire possit cor hominis, sed jam sentiens se gravari phantasmate priorum vitiorum, et vocem orationis suae impediri, ne pro se exorari possit, ardentius clamat.

VERS. 40.-- Stans autem, etc. Transiens, caecum audit, stans illuminat, quia per humanitatem suam nostrae 325Bcaecitatis vocibus compatitur, sed per potentiam Divinitatis lumen nobis gratiae infundit. Adduci ad se. Qui praetereuntem aliquatenus sentiebat, dum in oratione patitur phantasmatum turbas, vehementius insistens orationi ducitur ad stantem, et recipit lumen, quia Deus in corde firmo figitur, et sic lux amissa reparatur.

VERS. 41.-- Quid tibi, etc. Non ex ignorantia quaerit, sed ideo ut rogetur, ut mentem ad orationem excitet. Quod intelligens, caecus subjungit: Domine, ut videam. Non petit caecus aurum vel aliquid temporale, sed tantum lumen. Imitemur ergo eum, quem mente et corpore audivimus sanatum; petamus a Domino lucem aeternae visionis, et dicet nobis: Respice, fides tua salvum te fecit.

VERS. 43.-- Et confestim vidit. Videt et sequitur, 325Cqui bonum quod intelligit operatur, et Jesum praesentem vel praetereuntem imitatur. Et hic talis non solum pro impetrato in Deum proficit, sed etiam alios ad laudem Dei accendit. Et omnis plebs. Laudat plebs Deum non solum pro impetrato munere lucis, sed et pro merito fidei impetrantis, quia non solum laudat quod potenter et misericorditer illuminat, sed quia clamorem fidei firmiter clamantis exaudit.

CHAPTER XVIII.

322BVERS. 2.-- A certain judge. This parable exhorts to praying and to believing, and to presuming not upon ourselves but upon God.

VERS. 3.-- And a widow. This widow can bear a likeness of the Church, which seems desolate until the Lord come, who nevertheless even now bears care for her in secret. Avenge me. Although the just are admonished to pray for their persecutors, yet, righteousness being safe, they seek this vengeance upon the evil: that all the evil may perish, either by being converted to righteousness, or by the loss through punishment of that power by which they now, against the good, so long as this very thing is expedient for the good, are able to do somewhat even temporally. And when the just desire this end to come, although they pray for their enemies, yet they are not absurdly said to desire vengeance.

322CVERS. 8.-- But yet. I say that I will execute the vengeance of the just, but they will be exceedingly few, because when on the day of judgment I shall appear in the form of the Son of man, so great will be the rarity of the just that the ruin of the whole world must then be hastened, not so much for the cry of the faithful unjustly condemned, as for the torpor of those justly to be condemned. Thinkest thou. In the word the unbelief of doubting is rebuked; the Divinity does not form an opinion. For he through whom all things are knows all things, but by doubting he rebukes the hearts of the unbelieving.

VERS. 10.-- Two men. Because the parable by which he invited to prayer he so concluded as to say that, when the Judge comes, faith would hardly be found on earth. Lest anyone should flatter himself with a superfluous knowledge of faith, or even with a confession of it, in this parable he shows more diligently 322Dthat not the words of faith but the works are to be examined by God, among which humility chiefly reigns; and because the proud do not have it, presuming upon their own righteousness and despising the weak, they, as though empty of faith, are not heard when they pray.

VERS. 11.-- God, I give thee thanks, etc. There are four kinds of swelling: when either a man reckons that the good which he has he has of himself; or, if he believes it given by God, he thinks it given for his merits; or when he boasts that he has what he has not; or when, despising the rest, he seeks to be seen singularly to have what he has. With this plague the Pharisee laboured, who therefore went down not justified, because ascribing to himself singularly the merits of good works, he set himself before the publican.

323AVERS. 12.-- I fast twice. Behold, the Pharisee has an eye to the showing of abstinence, to the obtaining of mercy, to the returning of thanks to God; to the keeping of humility he has none. And what does it profit if the whole city be guarded, and one opening be left through which the enemies may enter?

VERS. 13.-- And the publican. As a humble man, he does not dare to draw near, that God may draw near to him: he does not look up, that he may be looked upon; he presses down his conscience, but lifts up his hope; he strikes his breast, he exacts penalties of himself that God may spare; he confesses that God may pardon; God pardons what he acknowledges. God, be merciful. The publican praying humbly belongs to those of whom it is said: Shall not God revenge his elect crying to him? The Pharisee boasting of his merits belongs to those of whom it is added: 323BThe Son of man coming, thinkest thou, shall he find faith on earth?

VERS. 14.-- For every one, etc. The controversy of the Pharisee and the publican being set down, the sentence of the judge is set down, that pride may be shunned.

VERS. 15.-- And they brought. After the controversy spoken, after the sentence given by the humble master, infants are offered, that it may be shown that an innocent and simple age belongs to grace. The apostles rebuke, not because they were unwilling that infants should be blessed by the hand and voice of the Saviour, but because, not yet perfect in faith, they thought that he was wearied, after the likeness of men, by the importunity of those who offered them.

They rebuked. This perhaps for the reason that they wished the people of the Jews, from whom they were according to the flesh, to be saved first; yet they also made supplication for the Chanaanite woman. 323CThey knew therefore the mystery, that the calling of both peoples was due, but perhaps they did not yet know the order.

VERS. 16.-- Suffer the children. Let no one forbid the sick to come to the physician. These have as yet committed nothing in the branch, but they have perished in the root. They who cannot plead for themselves have committed their cause to their elders. Let the physician touch, and let him bless the little ones with the greater (Psal. CXIII). For of such is. He does not say, of these, but of those who have from endeavour the innocence and simplicity which these have by nature, because it is not age that reigns, but manners. For it is not a virtue not to be able to sin, but not to will it. As a child does not persevere in anger, when hurt does not remember it, does not covet a woman, does not think or speak evil, so you, unless you have such innocence and purity of mind, 323Dshall not enter into the kingdom of heaven.

VERS. 17.-- The kingdom of God. That is, we are bidden to receive the teaching of the Gospel as a child does. A child in learning does not contradict his teachers. He does not compose reasonings and words to resist, but faithfully receives, obeys and rests.

VERS. 18.-- And he asked him. (BEDA.) This ruler had heard that those like children were worthy, etc., as far as to those who wish to be perfect he adds: Sell all and give to the poor (Marc. X).

Good master. Because he said a good master, and did not confess him God or the Son of God he [Christ] says that even a holy man, in comparison with God, is not good. But Christ does not refuse the testimony of goodness, 324Abut excludes the goodness of a master apart from God.

VERS. 19.-- And he said. (AMBR.) A crafty question, and therefore a keen answer. For the tempter, etc., as far as since by these words it is defined that a merciful rich man is against nature.

Thou knowest the commandments. This is the chastity of childish innocence, which is set before us for imitation, if we wish to enter the kingdom of God.

VERS. 23.-- He was rich. No rich man, that is, none trusting in his riches, shall enter in, as Jesus expounds according to Matthew. Not therefore those who remain rich, but those who have ceased to be so, shall enter in.

VERS. 25.-- For it is easier. Mystically. It is easier for Christ to suffer for the lovers of the world, than for the lovers of the world to be converted to Christ. Christ is called a camel, 324Bbecause, humbled of his own will, he took up the burdens of our infirmity. The needle is a pricking, by which he signifies the straits of the passion, by which passion, as by a needle, he deigned to sew up again the as it were rent garments of our nature, that is, to repair them after the fall. Otherwise: it is easier for the Gentile people, deformed by the hump of sins, to enter by the narrow way of life, than the Jew who glories in his merits and is unwilling to humble himself to take up the light burden of Christ.

VERS. 26.-- Who can? They understand that all who love riches, even if they have them not, are to be reckoned in the number of the rich: for otherwise, since the crowd of the poor is greater, they would not say: Who can be saved?

VERS. 29.-- No man. He who shall have let go carnal things for the Lord shall receive spiritual things. Mark says: A hundred times as much 324Cnow, etc. (Marc. X), that is, in this life full of persecutions they taste the joy of the kingdom, and enjoy the sincere love of all the elect.

VERS. 30.-- He shall receive, etc. He who shall have let go all riches for the Lord, and shall have despised the affections of the flesh, will receive much more in the present, and from brethren and sisters bound together by the spiritual glue of his own purpose he will receive in this life a much more gracious charity.

VERS. 31.-- He took. Foreseeing that the disciples would be troubled by his passion, he foretells both the penalty of the passion and the glory of the resurrection, that when they beheld him dying they might not doubt that he would rise again. And they shall be accomplished. In this he shows that whatever the prophets foretold had regard to the mystery of his dispensation. In which he strikes the heretics, who say that 324DChrist preached a thing contrary to the law and the prophets, and that the God of the Old Testatamenti is one and of the New another. He confutes also the madness of the pagans, because they deride his cross, since he both showed the time of his approaching passion as one foreknowing things to come, and approached the place as one not fearing death.

VERS. 34.-- And they, etc. The disciples are not able to understand the discourse of the Lord's passion, so often repeated, for this cause, that of him whom they most desire to see they cannot bear to hear the death, and him whom they know to be not only an innocent man but also true God, they do not think able to die.

VERS. 35.-- A blind man. The blind man signifies the human race, which, shut out from the brightness above, suffers the darkness of its own 325Adamnation, but is healed by the Lord drawing near to Jericho. Jericho is interpreted moon, by which the failing of mortality is understood. When therefore the Word of God takes up the infirmity of our flesh, man returns to the things that are to be known. The blind man sits by the way when he begins to believe in him who says: I am the way (Joan. XIV). He begs, when he asks.

VERS. 39.-- And they that went before, etc. They who go before Jesus as he comes signify the blind man of carnal vices, which scatter the thought of man and disturb the voice of prayer, that Jesus may not be able to come to enlighten the heart of man; but he, now feeling himself weighed down by the phantasm of his former vices, and the voice of his prayer hindered, lest [the Lord] be entreated on his behalf, cries out the more ardently.

VERS. 40.-- And standing, etc. Passing by, he hears the blind man; standing, he enlightens him, because through his humanity he suffers with the voices of our 325Bblindness, but through the power of his Divinity he pours into us the light of grace. To be brought unto him. He who somewhat perceived him passing by, while in prayer he suffers crowds of phantasms, pressing the more vehemently upon prayer is led to him as he stands, and receives light, because God is fixed in a firm heart, and so the lost light is repaired.

VERS. 41.-- What wilt thou, etc. He does not ask out of ignorance, but for this cause, that he may be asked, that he may rouse the mind to prayer. Understanding which, the blind man subjoins: Lord, that I may see. The blind man does not ask for gold or anything temporal, but only for light. Let us therefore imitate him whom we have heard was healed in mind and in body; let us ask of the Lord the light of the eternal vision, and he will say to us: Receive thy sight, thy faith hath made thee whole.

VERS. 43.-- And immediately he saw. He sees and follows, 325Cwho works the good which he understands, and imitates Jesus present or passing by. And such a one not only profits toward God for what he has obtained, but also kindles others to the praise of God. And all the people. The people praises God not only for the obtained gift of light, but also for the merit of the faith that obtained it, because it praises not only that he enlightens powerfully and mercifully, but because he hears the cry of a faith that cries firmly.

CAPUT XIX.

VERS. 1, 2.-- Et ingressus, etc. Ecce camelus deposita sarcina gibbi per foramen acus transit, dum contempto censu fraudium, angustam portam quae ducit ad vitam ascendit. Et quia devotione fidei ad videndum Salvatorem, quod natura minus habebat, 325Dascensu arboris supplet, juste quod rogare non audebat, Dominicae susceptionis benedictionem accipit quam desiderabat. Mystice, Zachaeus, qui interpretatur justificatus, significat credentes ex gentibus qui per occupationem temporalium depressi erant, et minimi, sed a Domino sanctificati.

Et ipse dives. Quis de se desperet, cum venit iste ad Deum cui census ex fraude? Per hunc apparet, quia non omnis dives avarus.

VERS. 3.-- Prae turba. Quae turba, nisi imperitae confusio multitudinis, quae verticem sapientiae videre nequit? Zachaeus ergo non in turba, sed plebem transgressus Messiam quem desiderat aspicit.

VERS. 4.-- Sycomorum. Sycomorus arbor est, 326Amoro foliis similis, sed altitudine praestans, et interpretatur ficus fatua, id est crux. Quae credentes ut ficus pascit, ab infidelibus irridetur ut fatua. Quam pusillus ascendit, dum quilibet humilis et propriae infirmitatis conscius, in virtute crucis gloriatur, et per hanc laudabilem fatuitatem transeuntem prope Dominum cernit, quia si nondum ita solide ut est jam, tamen raptim, et quasi in transitu luci sapientiae coelestis intendit.

Quia inde erat transiturus. (ISID.) Illa parte ubi sycomorus, vel ubi crediturus, etc., usque ad velut in fecunda altitudine operis eminebat.

VERS. 5.-- Et cum venisset. Per praemissos praecones verbi sui, in quibus erat Jesus et loquebatur, venit ad populum nationum, qui passionis ejus fide jam sublimis, etiam divinitatem ejus agnoscere ardet, 326Bsuspiciens videt, quia per fidem a terrenis elevatum elegit eligentem se, et amat amantem se. Descende. Quod descendere de sycomoro, et sic in domo parare mansionem jubetur, hoc est quod Apostolus ait: Etsi cognovimus secundum carnem Christum, sed jam non novimus. Si enim mortuus est ex infirmitate, sed jam vivit ex virtute Dei (II Cor. V, XIII). Manere. Manebat aliquando Dominus in domo principis Pharisaeorum, id est in Judaeorum Synagoga docebat.

VERS. 7.-- Murmurabant. Manifestum est Judaeos semper gentium odisse, vel non intellexisse salutem. Unde et adversus Petrum dicitur: Quare introisti ad gentes praeputium habentes (Act. IX)?

VERS. 8.-- Stans autem, etc. Aliis calumniantibus hominem peccatorem, ipse et Zachaeus stans, 326Cid est in ea quam coeperat fidei veritate persistens, non solum se ex peccatore conversum, sed etiam inter innocentes probat conversatum, cum etiam ante conversionem innocenter vixit, et qui ante conversionem dimidium, post conversionem potest dare et totum. Si quid, etc. Primum est secundum legem reddere, deinde quod remanet pauperibus erogare. Hunc fructum vitae publicanus de sycomoro per sapientem stultitiam legit quod rapta reddit, propinqua relinquit, visibilia contemnit, ejus quem nondum videt sequi vestigia concupiscit.

VERS. 9.-- Filius sit. Non carne, sed fide, quia sicut Abraham jussu Dei patriam ob spem futurae haereditatis deseruit, sic et iste qui thesaurum in coelis acquirit spe.

326DVERS. 10.-- Venit, etc. Etiam murmurantibus sua mysteria non dedignatur exponere, quod propter poenitentiam peccatorum suspiciendam maxime ipse in terram venerit, ubi et se appellat Filium hominis, ut insinuet nobis dispensationem suae pietatis.

VERS. 11.-- Haec illis, etc. Suscepta et commendata poenitentia publicani divitis, per parabolam docet sibi magis poenitentiam peccatorum, quam justitiam placere superborum, latiusque in gentibus de ignorantia legis humilibus, quam in Judaeis de justitia legis superbientibus se esse regnaturum.

Eo quod. Non solum qui justi perseverant, sed etiam qui de injustitia convertuntur ad filios promissionis pertinent.

327AVERS. 12.-- Nobilis abiit. Hic est nobilis cui caecus clamabat: Miserere mei, fili David, et cui tubae clamabant: Hosanna filio David: benedictus qui venit in nomine Domini rex Israel, qui constitutus est a Patre rex, cui datae sunt gentes in haereditatem. Longinquam. Excaecatos Judaeos relinquens, transit ad illuminandas gentes, quae longe (quia a finibus terrae) clamant ad Dominum, vel quia longe a peccatoribus salus (Psal. CXVIII): Deus, qui ubique praesens, longe ab his qui idola colunt, sed et ipsi prope facti sunt in sanguine.

VERS. 13.-- Vocatis autem decem. Vocat decem servos, dum elegit discipulos littera Decalogi imbutos. Dat decem mnas, dum post resurrectionem surgit, et aperit sensum, ut spiritualiter intelligat 327Blegis dicta Mna decem drachmas appendit. Et omnis Scripturae sermo, quia vitae coelestis perfectionem suggerit, quasi centenarii numeri pondere fulget. Negotiamini. Verba legis mystica interpretatione discussa populis offerte, et ab eis confessionem fidei, morumque probitatem recipite.

VERS. 15.-- Et factum est. Quotidie Christus accepto regno a Patre redit, quia peregrinantis in terra Ecclesiae semper statum conspicit. Huic servo pecuniam negotiaturo commodat, in altero modum consummati operis examinat. Hunc prudenter laborantem ampliori gratia donat, illum luxuriosa sectantem, etiam quod dederat privat. In judicio vero multi qui ad docendum idonei videbantur, ob negligentiam 327Csuam inter indoctos reputabuntur. Quidam simplicioris observationis devotionem inter apostolicos summa praemia percipient. Qui enim recipit prophetam in nomine prophetae, mercedem prophetae accipiet (Matth. X).

VERS. 16.-- Venit autem. Primus servus, ordo doctorum in circumcisione missus, qui unam mnam negotiaturus accepit, quia unum Deum, unam fidem, unum baptisma missus est praedicare. Sed decem acquirit dum populum sub lege constitutum docendo sibimet associat.

VERS. 17.-- In modico fuisti fidelis. Modicum est quidquid in praesenti percipimus donorum ad comparationem futurorum. Ex parte enim cognoscimus, et ex parte prophetamus (I Cor. XIII). Decem 327Dcivitates. Decem civitates sunt animae, per legis verba ad gratiam Evangelii pervenientes, quibus tunc glorificandus praeponitur, qui eis pecuniam verbi digne Deo commendaverit; unde Apostolus: Quae est spes nostra, aut gaudium, aut corona gloriae? Nonne vos ante Dominum Jesum (I Thes. II)?

VERS. 18.-- Et alter, etc. Bonus ordo, vocaturus gentes, et Judaeos missurus interfici, qui noluerunt super se Christum regnare, hanc praemisit comparationem, ne diceretur: Nihil dederat populo Judaeorum unde melior possit fieri, ut quod exigeretur ab eo qui nihil recipit? Ex una mna quinque facit, qui moralia habet propter quinque sensus.

VERS. 20.-- Et alter, etc. Qui ad praeputium missus 328Aest, cui Dominus mnam unam eamdemque fidem, quae circumcisioni credita est, ad praedicandum commisit. Quae quinque mnas facit, quia gentes quinque sensibus corporis mancipatas, ad gratiam fidei evangelicae convertit. In sudario, etc. In sudario pecuniam reponit, qui ad praedicandum idoneus, officium praedicandi vel suscipere renuit, vel susceptum non bene gerit, sed percepta dona sub otio temporis abscondit secum, dicens: Sufficit mihi ut de me rationem reddam, cur aliis praedicem, vel de aliis cogar reddere rationem? cum etiam apud Deum illi sunt inexcusabiles quibus lex data non est, neque audito Evangelio dormierunt, quia per creaturam poterant Creatorem cognoscere.

VERS. 21.-- Metis quod. Metit enim Deus ubi non 328Bseminavit, id est etiam eos impietatis reos tenet quibus legis verbum vel Evangelii non ministravit. Timens ergo periculum judicii, requiescam ab administratione verbi.

VERS. 22.-- Sciebas, etc. Quod putaverat pro excusatione dixisse vertitur in culpam. Si me ita durum et crudelem noveras, quare ista cogitatio non incussit tibi timorem, ut scires me mea diligentius quaesiturum?

VERS. 23.-- Pecuniam. Id est evangelicam praedicationem paratisque fidelium cordibus non intimasti. Meam ad mensam. Non tuam. Unde Apostolus: Si quis loquitur, quasi sermones Dei (I Petr. IV). Cum usuris. Qui verbi pecuniam a doctore emit credendo, necesse est ut cum usuris solvat operando. Vel, 328Cde accepto verbi fenore usuras solvit, qui ex eo quod audit, etiam alia studet intelligere.

VERS. 24.-- Auferte, etc. Collatam gratiam amittit, qui praedicando aliis communicare neglexit. Augetur vero illi qui in ea laboravit. Sicut regium chrisma quod superbiendo Saul amisit, David obediendo promeruit. Spiritus enim Domini qui a Saul recessit, directus est a die illa et deinceps in David.

VERS. 26.-- Et abundabit. Mystice: cum omnis Israel salvus erit, tunc plenitudo spiritualis gratiae quam modo torpenter exercemus, doctoribus illius temporis abundanter conferetur.

VERS. 28.-- Et his dictis. Finita parabola, vadit Hierosolymam, ut ostenderet de ejusdem maximae civitatis eventu parabolam fuisse praemissam, quae 328Dnon longe post, et ipsum occisura ob odium regni ejus hostili clade sit peritura.

VERS. 29.-- Ad Bethphage. Bethphage, viculus sacerdotum in monte Oliveti, et interpretatur domus buccae. Bethania villa vel civitas in latere ejusdem montis, et dicitur domus obedientiae. Has Dominus Hierosolymam venturus praesentia sua sublimavit, quia multos ante passionem suam docendo piae confessionis donis et obedientiae spiritualis implevit. Quae bene in monte Oliveti sunt positae, id est in ipso Domino qui nos unctione spiritualium chrismatum, et conscientiae pietatisque luce refovet. Et quia non vult abscondi civitatem supra se positam, mittit discipulos in castellum, quod est contra eos, ut per 329Aeos totius contra positi orbis munitiones penetret.

VERS. 30.-- Introeuntes invenietis. Introeuntes in mundum praedicatores inveniunt populum nationum, perfidiae vinculis irretitum, liberum et lascivum. Cui nemo, etc., id est, nullus rationalis doctor fraenum correctionis posuit, qui vel linguam a malo prohiberet, vel in arctam viam vitae ire cogeret. Nemo indumenta salutis quibus calefieret, utilia suadendo contulit. Nemo sedit, quia nullus stultitiam ejus deprimendo correxit. Alligatum. Alligatus vinculis perfidiae, in quo addictus vel astritus Domino errore famulabatur, sed dominatum sibi vindicare non poterat, quem Dominum fecerat non natura, sed culpa. Et ideo cum Dominus dicitur, unus agnoscitur. Nam, 329Betsi multi dii et domini, generaliter tamen unus Deus et unus Dominus.

VERS. 33.-- Solventibus autem, etc. Non despicias hunc asellum, quia sicut in vestitu ovium sunt lupi rapaces, ita sub exuviis corporis (quod nobis est commune cum belluis) mens Deo plena vegetatur. Dixerunt. Multos habebat dominos qui non uni superstitioni deditus, sed pro libitu immundorum spirituum raptabatur ad varios errores. Quid solvitis. Qui solvendo pullo contradixerant, audito nomine quiescunt, quia magistri errorum qui doctoribus ad salutem gentium venientibus obsistunt, suas tenebras defendunt, donec miraculis attestantibus veri possessoris virtus emineat. Sed postquam Dominicae fidei potestas apparet, cedentibus adversariorum 329Cquerelis, coetus qui Deum corde portet adducitur.

VERS. 35.-- Vestimenta. Vestimenta apostolorum doctrina virtutum, vel discretio Scripturarum, et gratia spiritualiter, quibus illi corda hominum prius nuda et frigida contegunt, ut mereantur habere sessorem Christum. Imposuerunt. Dum populus gentium portat Christum, discipuli in via vestimenta sternunt, quia corporis sui exuentes amictum simplicibus sanguine suo viam parant, ut inoffenso gressu mentis, Hierosolymam, quo Christus ducit, tendant, id est ad veram pacis visionem.

VERS. 37.-- Ad descensum. Descendente Domino de monte Oliveti, id est, humiliante se ad infirmitatem nostrae carnis illo qui erat in forma Dei, cum eo 329Ddescendunt turbae, id est, humiliatur qui ejus misericordia indigent, ut ab eo exaltentur. Gaudentes. Frequenter ingressus est civitatem Hierusalem, sed non rex vocatus, quod semper fugerat, nisi modo cum ascendit passurus. Quod ideo factum est, ut amplius eorum adversum se excitaret invidiam, quia jam tempus passionis instabat. Super. Maxime de resurrectione Lazari, quae nuper facta erat. Nam et propterea obviam venit ei turba, quia hoc signum factum audierant ab eo. Laudant etiam de signis et doctrina, quae fecit cum ascendit ad diem scenopegiae, quod Joannes narrat.

VERS. 38.-- Benedictus. Turba post modicum crucifixura, regem appellat, exspectatum secundum 330Acarnem David filium venisse declarat, ut adversum se testimonium extorqueatur invitis. Pax. Sicut nascente Domino hominum et angelorum agmina coelestia decantabant: Gloria in excelsis Deo. Et in terra pax hominibus, etc. Sic eidem mox triumphaturo homines concinunt: Pax in coelo, et gloria in excelsis.

VERS. 39.-- Magister. Mira invidentium dementia, quem vera docentem noverant, cum magistrum appellari audiunt, discipulos ejus quasi melius edocti redarguendos aestimant, quos ille instituerat, quem Deum apparet esse per miracula.

VERS. 40.-- Lapides. Crucifixo Domino apostolis prae timore tacentibus, petrae quae scissae sunt, apertis monumentis Deum et mundi Dominum aperte 330Bclamabant.

Mystice: si caecitas contigerit in Israel, ut a laude Dei conticeat, populus gentium mollito corde lapideo Creatorem suum credet et annuntiabit.

VERS. 44.-- Et non relinquent. Perversus enim cum perversionem adjicit, quasi lapidem super lapidem struit. Sed cum anima ad ultionem rapitur, talis cogitationum constructio dissipatur, et hoc ideo, quia non cognoveris tempus. Perversam enim animam Deus visitat, aliquando praecepto, aliquando miraculo. Sed quia superbiens contemnit, nec de malis suis erubescit, in extremo inimicis tradetur, cum quibus in aeterno judicio damnationis societate colligitur. Ad pacem, etc. Dum carnem das voluptatibus mala ventura non prospiciens, habes bona praesentia 330Cquae ad pacem possunt esse temporalem. Et cur hoc, subdit, scilicet, quia non praevidet mala quae ei eventura sunt. Si enim praevideret, in praesentibus prosperis laetata non fuisset.

VERS. 45.-- Et ingressus. Post enumerata mala, ingressus templum ejecit vendentes et ementes, per hoc innuens, quod ruina populi, maxime ex sacerdotum culpa fuit; dum enim vendentes et ementes percutit, ostendit unde radix perditionis processit.

VERS. 47.-- Et erat docens. Domus Dei est conscientia fidelium, quam facit speluncam latronum qui, relicta simplicitate sanctitatis, illud meditatur agere, unde proximum innocentem possit laedere. Sed econtra Dominus mentes fidelium ad cavenda mala erudit. Quaerebant illum perdere. Ideo non poterant 330Dillum perdere, quia timebant tumultum populi, vel ideo volebant perdere, quia, neglecto, suo magisterio, totum populum videbant ad ejus doctrinam confluere.

CHAPTER XIX.

VERS. 1, 2.-- And entering in, etc. Behold, the camel, having laid down the burden of its hump, passes through the eye of the needle, while, despising the revenue got by frauds, it climbs up to the narrow gate which leads to life. And because by the devotion of faith he supplies, by the climbing of a tree, what he had too little of by nature for seeing the Saviour, 325Djustly he receives what he did not dare to ask, the blessing of the Lord's welcome which he had desired. Mystically, Zacchaeus, who is interpreted justified, signifies those believing from among the Gentiles, who had been pressed down by the business of temporal things, and were least, but were sanctified by the Lord.

And he was rich. Who should despair of himself, when this man came to God whose revenue was from fraud? By him it appears that not every rich man is covetous.

VERS. 3.-- Because of the crowd. What crowd, but the confusion of an unskilled multitude, which cannot see the summit of wisdom? Zacchaeus therefore, not in the crowd, but having passed beyond the common people, beholds the Messiah whom he desires.

VERS. 4.-- A sycomore. The sycomore is a tree, 326Alike the mulberry in its leaves, but surpassing it in height, and it is interpreted the foolish fig, that is, the cross. Which, as a fig, feeds believers; as foolish, is mocked by unbelievers. The little man climbs it when anyone humble and conscious of his own weakness glories in the virtue of the cross, and through this praiseworthy foolishness discerns the Lord passing near; because if not yet so solidly as he now is, yet hastily, and as it were in passing, he strains toward the light of heavenly wisdom.

Because he was to pass that way. (ISID.) In that part where the sycomore was, or where he was to believe, etc., as far as he stood out as in the fruitful height of a work.

VERS. 5.-- And when he was come. Through the heralds of his word sent before, in whom Jesus was and was speaking, he came to the people of the nations, which, already lifted on high by faith in his passion, burns to acknowledge his divinity also; 326Blooking up he sees, for he chose him who through faith had been raised from earthly things and who was choosing him, and loves him who loves him. Come down. That he is bidden to come down from the sycomore, and so to make ready a lodging in his house, this is what the Apostle says: Although we have known Christ according to the flesh, yet now we know him not. For if he died of weakness, yet now he lives by the power of God (II Cor. V, XIII) [Vulg. 2 Cor 13:4 crucifixus est ex infirmitate, "was crucified out of weakness"; and 2 Cor 5:16 sed nunc jam non novimus]. To abide. The Lord once abode in the house of the chief of the Pharisees, that is, he was teaching in the synagogue of the Jews.

VERS. 7.-- They murmured. It is manifest that the Jews always hated, or did not understand, the salvation of the Gentiles. Whence also it is said against Peter: Why didst thou go in to the Gentiles that have uncircumcision (Act. IX) [Vulg. Acts 11:3 ad viros praeputium habentes, "to men that have uncircumcision"]?

VERS. 8.-- But standing, etc. While others were calumniating him as a sinful man, Zacchaeus himself also standing, 326Cthat is, persisting in that truth of faith which he had begun, proves that he was not only converted from a sinner, but had even lived among the innocent, since he lived innocently even before his conversion, and he who before his conversion could give the half, after his conversion can give the whole also. If I have taken anything, etc. The first thing is to restore according to the law, then to give out to the poor what remains. This fruit of life the publican gathers from the sycomore through wise folly, in that he restores what he has seized, leaves what is near, despises things visible, and longs to follow the footsteps of him whom he does not yet see.

VERS. 9.-- He is a son. Not by flesh, but by faith, because as Abraham at God's command forsook his country for the hope of a future inheritance, so also this man, who acquires by hope a treasure in the heavens.

326DVERS. 10.-- He came, etc. Even to those murmuring he does not disdain to expound his mysteries: that he himself came upon earth chiefly for the sake of the repentance of sinners, which is to be looked upon, where also he calls himself the Son of man, that he may intimate to us the dispensation of his mercy.

VERS. 11.-- These things to them, etc. The repentance of the rich publican having been received and commended, he teaches by a parable that the repentance of sinners pleases him more than the righteousness of the proud, and that he will reign more widely among the Gentiles, who are humble concerning their ignorance of the law, than among the Jews, who are proud concerning the righteousness of the law.

Because that. Not only those who persevere as just, but also those who are converted from injustice, belong to the children of the promise.

327AVERS. 12.-- A nobleman went away. This is the nobleman to whom the blind man cried: Have mercy on me, son of David, and to whom the trumpets cried: Hosanna to the son of David: blessed is he that cometh in the name of the Lord, the king of Israel, [Vulg. Matt 21:9 ends hosanna in altissimis, and rex Israel stands only at Jo 12:13] who was appointed king by the Father, to whom the Gentiles were given for an inheritance. A far country. Leaving the blinded Jews, he passes over to enlighten the Gentiles, who cry to the Lord from afar (because from the ends of the earth), or because salvation is far from sinners (Psal. CXVIII): God, who is everywhere present, is far from those who worship idols; but they too have been made near in the blood.

VERS. 13.-- And having called ten. He calls ten servants, when he chose disciples steeped in the letter of the Decalogue. He gives ten pounds, when after the resurrection he rises and opens their understanding, that it may spiritually understand 327Bthe sayings of the law The mna weighs ten drachmas. And every word of Scripture, because it suggests the perfection of the heavenly life, shines as with the weight of the hundredfold number. Trade ye. Offer to the peoples the words of the law sifted by mystical interpretation, and receive from them the confession of faith and uprightness of manners.

VERS. 15.-- And it came to pass. Daily Christ, having received the kingdom from the Father, returns, because he always beholds the state of the Church journeying on earth. To this servant he lends money for trading; in another he examines the measure of the work accomplished. This one, labouring prudently, he endows with ampler grace; that one, following after wanton things, he deprives even of what he had given. But in the judgment many who seemed fit for teaching will, for their own negligence, 327Cbe reckoned among the unlearned. Certain men, a devotion of simpler observance, will receive the highest rewards among the apostolic. For he that receiveth a prophet in the name of a prophet shall receive the reward of a prophet (Matth. X).

VERS. 16.-- And the first came. The first servant is the order of teachers sent into the circumcision, who received one pound to trade with, because he was sent to preach one God, one faith, one baptism. But he gains ten while by teaching he joins to himself the people set under the law.

VERS. 17.-- Thou hast been faithful in a little. Little is whatever of gifts we receive in the present, in comparison with those to come. For we know in part, and we prophesy in part (I Cor. XIII). Ten 327Dcities. The ten cities are souls coming through the words of the law to the grace of the Gospel, over whom he is then set to be glorified who shall have worthily commended to God the money of the word for them; whence the Apostle: What is our hope, or joy, or crown of glory? Are not you before the Lord Jesus (I Thes. II)?

VERS. 18.-- And another, etc. A good order: being about to call the Gentiles, and about to send the Jews to be slain, who would not have Christ reign over them, he set this comparison first, lest it should be said: He had given nothing to the people of the Jews whereby they might be made better, so that what should be exacted from him who receives nothing? Out of one pound he makes five, who has moral things on account of the five senses.

VERS. 20.-- And another, etc. He who was sent 328Ato the uncircumcision, to whom the Lord entrusted one pound, and the same faith which was committed to the circumcision, to be preached. Which makes five pounds, because it converts to the grace of the evangelical faith the Gentiles that were enslaved to the five senses of the body. In a napkin, etc. He lays up the money in a napkin who, being fit for preaching, either refuses to undertake the office of preaching, or, having undertaken it, does not discharge it well, but hides with himself the gifts he has received under the idleness of the time, saying: It is enough for me that I render an account of myself; why should I preach to others, or be compelled to render an account of others? since even with God those are inexcusable to whom the law was not given, nor did they sleep when the Gospel had been heard, because through the creature they were able to know the Creator.

VERS. 21.-- Thou reapest what. For God reaps where he did not 328Bsow, that is, he holds guilty of impiety even those to whom he has not ministered the word of the law or of the Gospel. Fearing therefore the peril of the judgment, I will rest from the administration of the word.

VERS. 22.-- Thou knewest, etc. What he had thought to have said for an excuse is turned into a fault. If thou hadst known me so hard and cruel, why did that thought not strike fear into thee, that thou shouldst know I would seek my own the more diligently?

VERS. 23.-- The money. That is, the evangelical preaching, and thou didst not make it known to the prepared hearts of the faithful. Mine to the bank. Not thine. Whence the Apostle: If any man speak, let it be as the words of God (I Petr. IV). With usury. He who buys the money of the word from a teacher by believing must needs pay it back with usury by working. Or, 328Che pays usury on the loan of the word he has received who, from what he hears, studies to understand other things also.

VERS. 24.-- Take away, etc. He loses the grace conferred who has neglected to share it with others by preaching. But it is increased to him who has laboured in it. As the royal chrism which Saul lost by being proud, David merited by obeying. For the Spirit of the Lord who departed from Saul was directed from that day forward into David.

VERS. 26.-- And he shall abound. Mystically: when all Israel shall be saved, then the fulness of spiritual grace, which now we exercise sluggishly, shall be abundantly conferred upon the teachers of that time.

VERS. 28.-- And having said these things. The parable finished, he goes to Jerusalem, to show that the parable had been set forth concerning the fate of that same greatest city, which 328Dnot long after, being itself also about to kill him for hatred of his kingdom, was to perish by an enemy's slaughter.

VERS. 29.-- To Bethphage. Bethphage is a hamlet of priests on the Mount of Olives, and is interpreted the house of the mouth. Bethany is a farmstead or city on the side of the same mountain, and is called the house of obedience. These the Lord, being about to come to Jerusalem, exalted by his presence, because before his passion he filled many, by teaching, with the gifts of devout confession and of spiritual obedience. Which are well placed on the Mount of Olives, that is, in the Lord himself, who cherishes us with the unction of spiritual chrisms and with the light of conscience and of mercy. And because he does not wish a city set above itself to be hidden, he sends the disciples into the village which is over against them, that through 329Athem he may penetrate the fortifications of the whole world set over against him.

VERS. 30.-- Entering in you shall find. The preachers, entering into the world, find the people of the nations, entangled in the bonds of unbelief, free and wanton. Upon whom no man, etc., that is, no rational teacher has put the bridle of correction, who might either restrain its tongue from evil, or compel it to go into the narrow way of life. No man, by persuading what was useful, bestowed the garments of salvation whereby it might be warmed. No man sat, because none corrected its folly by pressing it down. Tied. Tied with the bonds of unbelief, in which, made over or bound fast, he was serving a lord by error; but he whom not nature but fault had made lord could not claim the mastery for himself. And therefore when the Lord is spoken of, one is acknowledged. For, 329Balthough there be many gods and lords, yet in general there is one God and one Lord.

VERS. 33.-- And as they were loosing him, etc. Despise not this little ass, because as under the clothing of sheep there are ravening wolves, so under the spoils of the body (which is common to us with the beasts) a mind full of God is quickened. They said. He had many masters, who, given over not to one superstition, but at the pleasure of unclean spirits, was hurried away to various errors. Why do you loose him. Those who had spoken against the loosing of the colt are quiet when the name is heard, because the masters of errors, who withstand the teachers coming for the salvation of the Gentiles, defend their own darkness, until, with miracles bearing witness, the power of the true owner stands forth. But after the power of the Lord's faith appears, the complaints of the adversaries giving way, 329Cthere is brought forward an assembly that may bear God in its heart.

VERS. 35.-- The garments. The garments of the apostles are the teaching of the virtues, or the discernment of the Scriptures, and grace spiritually, wherewith they cover the hearts of men, before naked and cold, that they may deserve to have Christ for their rider. They laid them on. While the people of the Gentiles carries Christ, the disciples strew their garments in the way, because, putting off the covering of their own body, they make ready a way for the simple by their own blood, that with unhindered step of mind they may press on to Jerusalem, whither Christ leads, that is, to the true vision of peace.

VERS. 37.-- At the descent. As the Lord came down from the Mount of Olives, that is, as he who was in the form of God humbled himself to the weakness of our flesh, the crowds come down with him, 329Dthat is, he is humbled who need his mercy, that they may be exalted by him. Rejoicing. Often he entered the city of Jerusalem, but was not called king, which he had always fled, save only now when he went up to suffer. Which was done for this reason, that he might the more stir up their envy against himself, because the time of the passion was now at hand. For. Chiefly for the raising of Lazarus, which had lately been done. For on that account also the crowd came to meet him, because they had heard that this sign had been done by him. They praise him also for the signs and the teaching which he wrought when he went up for the day of the feast of tabernacles, which John relates.

VERS. 38.-- Blessed. The crowd, that after a little was to crucify him, calls him king, and declares that the awaited son of David according 330Ato the flesh has come, so that a testimony against themselves is wrung from them unwilling. Peace. As at the Lord's birth the heavenly companies of men and of angels sang: Glory to God in the highest. And on earth peace to men, etc. [Vulg. Luke 2:14 Gloria in altissimis Deo; in excelsis is the reading of Luke 19:38, the verse here glossed], so to the same, soon to triumph, men sing together: Peace in heaven, and glory in the highest.

VERS. 39.-- Master. Wondrous madness of the envious! Him whom they had known to teach true things, when they hear him called master, they judge that his disciples are to be reproved, as though they themselves were the better taught, those disciples whom he had instructed who by his miracles appears to be God.

VERS. 40.-- The stones. When the Lord was crucified and the apostles held their peace for fear, the rocks that were rent, the tombs being opened, openly 330Bcried out that he was God and the Lord of the world.

Mystically: if blindness has befallen Israel, so that it falls silent from the praise of God, the people of the Gentiles, their stony heart softened, shall believe and announce their Creator.

VERS. 44.-- And they shall not leave. For when the perverse man adds perversion, he builds as it were stone upon stone. But when the soul is hurried away to vengeance, such a building of thoughts is scattered, and this for this cause, because thou hast not known the time. For God visits the perverse soul, sometimes by a precept, sometimes by a miracle. But because in its pride it despises, and does not blush for its own evils, at the last it shall be delivered to its enemies, with whom in the eternal judgment it is gathered into the fellowship of damnation. Unto peace, etc. While thou givest the flesh to pleasures, not foreseeing the evils to come, thou hast present goods 330Cwhich can be for a temporal peace. And why this, he subjoins, namely, because it does not foresee the evils that are to come upon it. For if it did foresee them, it would not have rejoiced in present prosperities.

VERS. 45.-- And going in. After the evils had been enumerated, going into the temple he cast out those that sold and bought, intimating by this that the ruin of the people was chiefly from the fault of the priests; for while he strikes those that sell and buy, he shows whence the root of perdition proceeded.

VERS. 47.-- And he was teaching. The house of God is the conscience of the faithful, which he makes a den of thieves who, having left the simplicity of holiness, plots to do that whereby he may be able to hurt his innocent neighbour. But on the contrary the Lord instructs the minds of the faithful to beware of evils. They sought to destroy him. Therefore they were not able 330Dto destroy him, because they feared the tumult of the people; or therefore they wished to destroy him, because, their own office of teaching being neglected, they saw the whole people flowing together to his doctrine.

CAPUT XX.

VERS. 2.-- In qua potestate. De Dei potestate dubitant, et subintelligi volunt diaboli esse quod facit. Unde addunt: Aut quis est, etc. In quo plane negant Dei Filium, quem non suis viribus, sed alienis signa facere credunt.

VERS. 3.-- Interrogabo vos, etc. Poterat aperta responsione tentatorum calumniam confutare, sed prudenter interrogat, ut vel suo silentio vel sua sententia condemnentur.

331AVERS. 4.-- Baptismus Joannis, etc. Quem confitemini de coelo habuisse prophetiam mihi perhibuit testimonium, et ab illo audistis in qua potestate ego faciam. Quod illi intelligentes, dubitant quid debeant respondere.

Neque ego, etc. Propter duas causas veritas quaerentibus est abscondenda. Vel, quia minus capaces sunt ad audiendum; unde dicitur: Multa habeo vobis dicere, sed non potestis portare modo (Joan. XVI). Vel quia ex odio et contemptu quaerunt; unde dicitur: Nolite sanctum dare canibus (Matth. VII).

VERS. 9.-- Coepit autem. Principibus sacerdotum et scribis de sua interrogatione confutatis, Dominus quod coeperat, exsequitur, et illis audientibus plebem, quod sua verba libentius audiat alloquitur, inferens 331Bparabolam qua et illos impietatis arguat, et regnum Dei ad gentes transferendum ostendat.

Vineam. Moraliter: Vinea Domini sabaoth, domus Israel (Isa. V): coloni, operarii, qui ad excolendum prima hora, tertia, sexta et nona sunt conducti. Et ipse peregre, etc. Qui ubique est, diligentibus se praesentior est, negligentibus abest, et longo tempore abfuit, ne perperam videretur exactio. Nam quo indulgentior liberalitas eo inexcusabilior pervicacia.

VERS. 10.-- Et in tempore. Primus servus qui mittitur, Moyses legislator. Qui per quadraginta annos fructum legis quam dederat, a cultoribus inquirebat, sed caesum dimittunt inanem.

VERS. 11.-- Et addidit, etc. Alter servus David 331Cpropheta, qui post Moysem colonos vineae psalmodiae modulamine et citharae dulcedine ad exercitium boni operis invitavit, sed et hunc contumelia affectum, dimittunt inanem, dicentes: Quae nobis pars in David, aut quae haereditas in filio Isai (Reg. XII)? Qui tamen pro hac vinea, ne penitus exstirpetur exorat: Domine Deus virtutum, converte et respice de coelo, et vide et visita vineam istam (Psal. LXXVII). Tertius servus chorus prophetarum. Qui populum corripiebant, et mala quae vineae imminerent praedicabant, sed et illos persecuti sunt et occiderunt.

VERS. 14.-- Quem cum vidissent. Aperte probat Judaeos, non per ignorantiam crucifixisse Filium Dei, sed per invidiam. Intellexerunt enim hunc esse cui dictum est: Postula a me, et dabo tibi gentes haereditatem 331Dtuam, et possessionem tuam terminos terrae (Psal. CXVII). Et ideo consulentes sibi dicunt: Ecce mundus totus post eum abiit (Joan. XII), etc. Haereditatem ergo Ecclesiae quam non moriens Pater reliquit, sed ipse sua morte emptam resurgendo possedit, hanc Judaei eripere volunt, dum fidem, quae per eum est, exstinguere conantur, justitiae Dei non subjecti, sed suam constituere volentes. Hic est haeres: Christus haeres, quia morti propriae supervixit, et testamentorum quae ipse condidit, quasi haereditaria in nostris profectibus emolumenta consequitur.

VERS. 15.-- Et ejectum illum. Marcus ordinem mutat: Occiderunt et ejecerunt extra vineam (Marc. 332AXII). In quo pertinacia eorum notatur, qui post resurrectionem praedicantibus apostolis credere noluerant, sed quasi utile cadaver projicientes, gentibus suscipiendum dederunt.

VERS. 16.-- Absit. Contradicunt sententiae quam contra perfidiam suam dictam esse cognoscunt. Quod enim in mente gerebant, in parabola contineri intelligunt. Negantibus ergo Judaeis justum fore, divinae scientiae legis (quam ipsi spernebant) ad gentes transferri, quid Salvator respondeat attende.

VERS. 17.-- Quid est, etc. Quomodo implebitur haec prophetia? Nisi quia Christus a vobis reprobatus, credituris gentibus est praedicandus, ut in se uno angulari lapide duos condat parietes. Aedificantes. Qui supra coloni, hic aedificantes dicuntur, 332Bquia qui plebem ad ferendos fructus excolere, hanc ipsi Domino inhabitatore dignam quasi domum constituere jubebantur.

VERS. 18.-- Omnis qui ceciderit. Qui credit et tamen peccat, cadit super lapidem, et non conteritur, sed confringitur, quia per patientiam ad salutem reservatur. Sed cui lapis irruit, id est, qui negat penitus, conteritur, ut nec testa remaneat. Vel cadit super eum, qui modo contemnit, et injuriis afficit, necdum penitus interit, sed quassatur, ut non ambulet rectus. Cadit super eum lapis, quem veniens in judicio opprimit.

VERS. 19.-- Et quaerebant, etc. Quasi mentientem quaerunt interficere in quo vera probantur quae dixerat. Ipse est enim haeres, illi pravi coloni qui 332Csolo humano terrore retardantur donec veniret hora ejus.

VERS. 20.-- Et observantes. Quod prae timore populi, per se non possunt, inanibus praesidiis efficere volunt, velut immunes a morte ipsius videantur.

VERS. 22.-- Licet nobis, etc. Cum nuper sub Augusto Judaea Romanis stipendiaria facta esset, Pharisaei qui de justitia sibi applaudebant, qui decimas et primitiva Deo dabant, tributa homini solvenda negabant, pro qua seditione postea regnum et tota patria destructa sunt.

VERS. 24.-- Cujus habet, etc. Alia imago Dei, alia mundi. Si ergo Christus non habuit imaginem Caesaris, cur censum dedit? Non de suo dedit, sed mundo reddidit quae erant mundi. Et tu si non vis 332Desse obnoxius Caesari, noli habere quae sunt mundi. Si habes divitias, obnoxius es Caesari. Si vis terreno regi nihil debere, omnia tua relinque et Christum sequere. Et bene prius quae Caesaris sunt reddenda decernit. Non enim potest quis esse Domini, nisi prius mundo renuntiaverit.

VERS. 25.-- Reddite ergo. Sicut Caesar exigit impressionem suae imaginis, sic et Deus animam lumine vultus sui signatam.

VERS. 27.-- Accesserunt autem, etc. Duae haereses erant in Judaeis: Pharisaeorum, qui praeferebant justitiam traditionum et observationum, unde et divisi vocabantur a populo, et Saducaeorum, qui et ipsi vindicabant sibi quod non erant, id est justi.

333AVERS. 28.-- Magister. Qui resurrectionem corporum non credunt, et animas putant interire cum corporibus, turpitudinem fabulae fingunt, quae deliramenti arguit eos qui resurrectionem asserunt. Potest autem fieri, ut in gente eorum hoc aliquando acciderit. Ut accipiat, etc. In lege litterae cogebatur nubere in vita. Sed in lege spiritus commendatur castitas.

VERS. 35.-- Illi vero, etc. Cum omnes boni et mali sint resurrecturi et absque nuptiis mansuri, Dominus tamen ut ad gloriam resurrectionis incitet, de solis electis disserit. Si autem in resurrectione neque ducent uxores, resurgent ergo corpora quae possunt nubere et ducere. Quod dicitur de his qui habent membra genitalia, sed nulla voluptate vel necessitate 333Bnubendi mancipabuntur. Et nota quod cum insidiatoribus et conculcatoribus secreta mysteria loquatur, non tamen sanctum dat canibus, sed aderant multi idonei quos propter aliorum immunditiam non oportebat negligi. Neque nubent, etc. Cum connubia propter filios, filii propter successionem, successio propter mortem, ubi ergo non est mors, neque connubia.

VERS. 37.-- Quia vero resurgent mortui. Postquam propositae quaestioni de resurrectionis conditionibus satisfecit, de ipsa resurrectione contra eorum infidelitatem agit.

VERS. 40.-- Et amplius, etc. Confutati in sermonibus non amplius interrogant, sed aperte comprehensum Romanae potestati tradunt, ex quo intelligimus 333Cvenena invidiae posse quidem superari, sed difficile conquiescere.

VERS. 41.-- Quomodo dicunt, etc. Consummaturus praecepta Dominus, in fine testamenti sui fidem concludit ut Dominum Jesum verum Deum et verum hominem ad dexteram Patris sedere credamus.

VERS. 43.-- Donec ponam. Quod a Patre subjiciuntur inimici, non infirmitatem Filii, sed unitatem naturae, qua in altero aliter operatur, significat. Nam et Filius subjicit inimicos Patri, quia Patrem clarificat super terram.

VERS. 46.-- Attendite a scribis. Duplici ratione jubemur attendere a vanae gloriae cupidis, ne vel eorum simulatione decipiamur, vel eorum exemplo ad amorem temporalium inflammemur. Et amant salutationes 333Din foro. Non salutari in foro, nec primas sedes vetat eis quibus debentur ex ordine officii, sed eis qui indebite amant. Animum enim, non gradum redarguit. Nec vero caret culpa, si is fori litibus interesse velit, qui desiderat sedere in cathedra Moysi.

Qui devorant, etc. Marcus: Sub obtentu prolixae orationis, qui ut religiosiores appareant, prolixius orant, ubi ab infirmis quibuslibet, et peccatorum suorum conscientia turbatis, quasi patroni laudes et pecunias accipiant, quorum oratio fit in peccatum, ut non solum pro aliis non possint intercedere, sed nec sibi ad salutem proficere, imo pro ipsis orationibus magis damnabuntur. Hi accipient damnationem. In hoc insinuat illos etiam, qui in angulis stantes 334Aorant, ut videantur ab hominibus, damnationem quidem mereri, sed istos majorem.

CHAPTER XX.

VERS. 2.-- By what authority. They doubt of God's power, and would have it understood that what he does is the devil's. Whence they add: Or who is he, etc. In which they plainly deny the Son of God, whom they believe to work signs not by his own powers, but by another's.

VERS. 3.-- I will ask you, etc. He could have refuted the calumny of the tempters by an open answer, but he prudently asks a question, that they may be condemned either by their own silence or by their own sentence.

331AVERS. 4.-- The baptism of John, etc. He whom you confess to have had his prophecy from heaven bore witness to me, and from him you have heard by what authority I do these things. Understanding which, they doubt what they ought to answer.

Neither do I, etc. For two causes the truth is to be hidden from those who ask it. Either because they are less able to hear it; whence it is said: I have yet many things to say to you, but you cannot bear them now (Joan. XVI). Or because they ask out of hatred and contempt; whence it is said: Give not that which is holy to dogs (Matth. VII).

VERS. 9.-- And he began. The chief priests and scribes having been refuted out of their own question, the Lord pursues what he had begun, and, in their hearing, addresses the common people, which hears his words more gladly, bringing in 331Ba parable whereby he may both convict them of impiety and show that the kingdom of God is to be transferred to the Gentiles.

A vineyard. Morally: The vineyard of the Lord of sabaoth, the house of Israel (Isa. V): [Vulg. Isa 5:7 Vinea enim Domini exercituum domus Israel est, "of the Lord of hosts"] the husbandmen are the labourers who were hired to till it at the first hour, the third, the sixth and the ninth. And he into a far country, etc. He who is everywhere is the more present to those who love him, and is absent to those who neglect him; and he was absent a long time, lest the exaction should seem wrongful. For the more indulgent the liberality, the more inexcusable the obstinacy.

VERS. 10.-- And at the season. The first servant who is sent is Moses the lawgiver. Who for forty years sought from the tillers the fruit of the law which he had given, but they beat him and send him away empty.

VERS. 11.-- And he added, etc. The second servant is David 331Cthe prophet, who after Moses invited the husbandmen of the vineyard by the measure of psalmody and the sweetness of the harp to the exercise of good work; but him also they treat with contumely and send away empty, saying: What part have we in David, or what inheritance in the son of Isai (Reg. XII)? Yet he entreats for this vineyard, that it be not utterly rooted up: O Lord God of hosts, turn and look down from heaven, and see, and visit this vineyard (Psal. LXXVII). The third servant is the choir of the prophets. Who rebuked the people, and foretold the evils that hung over the vineyard; but them also they persecuted and killed.

VERS. 14.-- Whom when they had seen. He plainly proves that the Jews crucified the Son of God not through ignorance, but through envy. For they understood that this was he to whom it was said: Ask of me, and I will give thee the Gentiles for thy inheritance, 331Dand the utmost parts of the earth for thy possession (Psal. CXVII). And therefore, taking counsel for themselves, they say: Behold, the whole world is gone after him (Joan. XII), etc. The inheritance of the Church, therefore, which the Father did not leave by dying, but which he himself, bought by his own death, possessed by rising again, this the Jews wish to snatch away, while they endeavour to extinguish the faith which is through him, not being subject to the justice of God, but wishing to establish their own. This is the heir: Christ is the heir, because he outlived his own death, and of the testaments which he himself made he obtains, as it were by inheritance, the profits in our advances.

VERS. 15.-- And having cast him out. Mark changes the order: They killed him and cast him out of the vineyard (Marc. 332AXII). In which their obstinacy is noted, who after the resurrection had refused to believe the apostles preaching, but, casting him out as it were a useful corpse, gave him to the Gentiles to be taken up.

VERS. 16.-- God forbid. They contradict the sentence which they know was spoken against their own unbelief. For what they were carrying in their mind they understand to be contained in the parable. Since therefore the Jews deny that it would be just that the divine knowledge of the law (which they themselves despised) be transferred to the Gentiles, mark what the Saviour answers.

VERS. 17.-- What is this, etc. How shall this prophecy be fulfilled? Except that Christ, rejected by you, is to be preached to the Gentiles who will believe, so that in himself, the one corner stone, he may found two walls. The builders. Those who above are husbandmen are here called builders, 332Bbecause they who were bidden to till the people that it might bear fruit were bidden to set it up as a house worthy of the Lord himself for its indweller.

VERS. 18.-- Every one that shall fall. He who believes and yet sins falls upon the stone, and is not ground to powder, but is broken, because by patience he is reserved unto salvation. But he upon whom the stone falls, that is, he who utterly denies, is ground to powder, so that not even a shard remains. Or he falls upon it who now despises and does injuries: he does not yet utterly perish, but is shaken, so that he does not walk upright. The stone falls upon him whom, coming in judgment, it crushes.

VERS. 19.-- And they sought, etc. They seek to kill him as a liar, in which the things he had said are proved true. For he himself is the heir, and they the wicked husbandmen, who 332Care held back by human fear alone until his hour should come.

VERS. 20.-- And watching him. What for fear of the people they cannot do by themselves, they wish to accomplish by empty helps, so that they may seem free from his death.

VERS. 22.-- Is it lawful for us, etc. When lately under Augustus Judaea had been made tributary to the Romans, the Pharisees, who applauded themselves for their righteousness, who gave tithes and first-fruits to God, denied that tributes were to be paid to a man; for which sedition their kingdom and their whole country were afterwards destroyed.

VERS. 24.-- Whose image hath it, etc. One is the image of God, another that of the world. If then Christ had not the image of Caesar, why did he pay the tribute? He did not pay of his own, but rendered to the world the things that were the world's. And thou, if thou dost not wish 332Dto be liable to Caesar, do not have the things that are the world's. If thou hast riches, thou art liable to Caesar. If thou wishest to owe nothing to an earthly king, leave all that is thine and follow Christ. And well does he determine that first the things that are Caesar's are to be rendered. For no one can be the Lord's, unless he has first renounced the world.

VERS. 25.-- Render therefore. As Caesar exacts the impress of his own image, so also does God the soul sealed with the light of his countenance.

VERS. 27.-- And there came to him, etc. There were two heresies among the Jews: that of the Pharisees, who set foremost the righteousness of traditions and observances, whence also they were called the separated from the people; and that of the Sadducees, who likewise claimed for themselves what they were not, that is, just men.

333AVERS. 28.-- Master. They who do not believe the resurrection of bodies, and think that souls perish with bodies, invent the baseness of a tale, which convicts of raving those who assert the resurrection. But it may be that this once happened in their own nation. That he may take, etc. In the law of the letter one was compelled to marry in this life. But in the law of the spirit chastity is commended.

VERS. 35.-- But they, etc. Although all, both good and bad, are to rise again and to remain without marriage, yet the Lord, that he may stir men up to the glory of the resurrection, discourses of the elect alone. But if in the resurrection they shall neither take wives, then those bodies shall rise which are able to marry and to take in marriage. Which is said of those who have generative members, but shall be enslaved by no pleasure or necessity 333Bof marrying. And note that although he speaks secret mysteries with liers-in-wait and tramplers, yet he does not give what is holy to dogs; but there were present many fit persons who ought not to be neglected because of the uncleanness of others. Neither shall they marry, etc. Since marriages are for the sake of children, children for the sake of succession, succession for the sake of death, where therefore there is no death, neither are there marriages.

VERS. 37.-- But that the dead shall rise again. After he had satisfied the question proposed concerning the conditions of the resurrection, he treats of the resurrection itself against their unbelief.

VERS. 40.-- And after that, etc. Refuted in argument they ask no more, but openly seize him and deliver him to the Roman power; from which we understand 333Cthat the poisons of envy can indeed be overcome, but hardly be laid to rest.

VERS. 41.-- How do they say, etc. The Lord, being about to complete his precepts, at the end of his testament concludes the faith, that we should believe the Lord Jesus, true God and true man, to sit at the right hand of the Father.

VERS. 43.-- Till I make. That his enemies are made subject by the Father signifies not the weakness of the Son, but the unity of nature, whereby in the one he works otherwise. For the Son also makes the enemies subject to the Father, because he glorifies the Father upon earth.

VERS. 46.-- Beware of the scribes. For a twofold reason we are bidden to beware of those greedy of vainglory: lest either we be deceived by their pretence, or be inflamed by their example to the love of temporal things. And they love salutations 333Din the marketplace. He does not forbid being saluted in the marketplace, nor the first seats, to those to whom they are due by the order of their office, but to those who love them unduly. For he reproves the mind, not the rank. Nor indeed is he without fault, if he wishes to take part in the lawsuits of the marketplace who desires to sit in the chair of Moses.

Who devour, etc. Mark: Under the pretence of long prayer, they who, that they may appear the more religious, pray at greater length, where from any weak persons whatsoever, and from those troubled by the conscience of their own sins, they may receive as patrons both praises and moneys; whose prayer is turned into sin, so that not only can they not intercede for others, but neither profit themselves unto salvation; nay rather, for those very prayers they shall be the more damned.
