VERS. 3.-- And Pilate. etc. Since two things had been objected 344Aagainst him, namely that he forbade the assigned dues to be rendered, and that he called himself Christ, Pilate was able to hear what he had said about the tributes: Render the things that are Caesar's to Caesar, and thence, counting this as an open falsehood of no weight, he asked only about what he did not know.

Thou sayest it. With the same word with which he answered the chief priests he answers the governor also, that by their own sentence both may be condemned.

VERS. 4.-- I find no cause. The day before he suffered, the Lord says to the disciples: The prince of this world cometh, and he hath not anything in me (Luc. XX). But since the prince, that is Pilate, absolves him, in whom he finds nothing worthy of condemnation, see what the Jews do, who search out the truth not from love of equity, but labour to condemn the just man at the goad of envy.

344BVERS. 2.-- He stirreth up the people. They accuse not him, but themselves. For to have taught the people, and by teaching to have stirred them from the former sluggishness of the body, and by such an act to have passed through from Galilee as far as Judea, that is the whole land of promise, from end to end, is a judgment not of crime but of virtue. And so Pilate, thinking that the Saviour was not even to be questioned about this, but rather having got the occasion, desires to make himself free from judging him.

VERS. 7.-- And when he knew it. Lest he should be forced to give sentence against a guiltless man, and one whom he knew to have been handed over out of envy, he sends him to Herod to be heard, that he might be either absolved or condemned by his own tetrarch; but, lest any excuse should remain to the Jews, Herod too, who was a Jew by birth and by religion, was permitted to show what he thought 344Cabout Christ, so that both regions, both Judea, in which Christ was born, and Galilee, in which he had been reared, might be shown to have conspired in his slaying.

But he answered him nothing. He was silent and did nothing, because both Herod's unbelief did not deserve to see divine things, and the Lord was avoiding ostentation. And in Herod are signified all the impious, who do not believe the law and the prophets, and cannot see the works of Christ in the Gospel. Therefore, while the chief priests accuse him, he answers few things before Pilate, none before Herod, lest, washing away the charge and being dismissed by the prince, he should put off the profit of the cross.

VERS. 14.-- And he mocked him. That he is despised and mocked in a white garment signifies that for the sins of the whole world 344Dthe lamb was offered up in chaste flesh, and suffered, and was buried: that he is mocked here clothed in a white garment, and, according to the other Evangelists, in a purple or scarlet one, marks the twofold kind of martyrdom with which the Church is adorned in her sufferings, so that she is white in action, red with blood. For he himself, blooming with the little flowers of his members, brings forth lilies in peace, roses in war. And he sent him back to Pilate. He is sent to Herod, he is sent back to Pilate; and although each of the two does not pronounce him guilty, yet each complies with the goads of another's iniquity.

VERS. 12.-- And they were made friends. The abominable compact which Herod and Pilate struck in the killing of Christ, their successors keep by hereditary right, while Jews and Gentiles, disagreeing in mind as they do in race and religion, nevertheless agree in persecuting Christians 345Aand in destroying the faith of Christ. In the type of Herod and Pilate, who were made friends through Jesus, the figure of the Jews and of the Gentiles is preserved: so that through Christ's passion a concord of both peoples was to come about, yet in such wise that the Gentile should first receive the kingdom of God, and pass on to the Jew the devotion of his own faith, that they too might clothe with the glory of his majesty the body of Christ, whom before they had despised.

VERS. 14.-- You have presented. Mark how great an eagerness Pilate has to release Jesus. First, when they accuse him, he says that he has found no cause in him; then, when they persist in what they have begun, he sends him to Herod, to explore whether he can find any wickedness in him, or whether he decides that he should be released. Then, when Herod's will is known, and no crime found in Jesus, 345Bhe seeks the sentence of the people, to whom one must be released at the Pasch. Again also a third time, wishing to release him, he asks. But the more carefully he seeks to release him, the more criminal does he find them for his death.

VERS. 15.-- No, nor Herod either. To reproach the hardness of the Jews or of the Gentiles, Pilate confesses that neither he nor Herod found anything in killing and mocking Christ, but that he complied with the shouts of another's cruelty.

VERS. 16.-- I will chastise him therefore, etc. Pilate absolves Jesus, whom he proves guiltless; but that the Scriptures might be fulfilled, he absolves indeed by judgment him whom nevertheless he crucifies in a mystery.

VERS. 18.-- Away with this man, etc. Because in place of Jesus they chose a robber, in place of the Saviour a murderer, in place of the giver of life 345Ca taker of it away, they deservedly lost salvation and life; and they gave themselves over to robberies and seditions so far that they lost both the homeland and the kingdom which they had preferred to Christ, and to this day have not received back the liberty of soul or of body which they had sold. Barabbas, etc. Barabbas, who is interpreted the son of their master or of their father, is himself Antichrist, whom they will prefer to Christ, they to whom it is said: You are of your father the devil. Pilate is interpreted the mouth of the hammerer, and signifies the devil, who is the hammer of all matter, through whom God permits the vessels to be tested, and at last crushes him, when he breaks him in the final damnation.

VERS. 21.-- Crucify him, crucify him. Great cruelty: they seek not only to kill but to crucify him, 345Dso that with hands and feet fastened to the wood he might be slain by a lingering death, lest the pain should end too quickly, and that he might long be seen upon the cross.

VERS. 22.-- I will chastise him, etc. This chastisement he offered to the desires of those abominable men by way of mockery and scourging, that they might not rage as far as crucifying. This both the words of John the evangelist and the very column testify, to which the Lord was bound, which to this day shows sure signs of the Lord's blood to those who look upon it. But although Pilate does this, the insatiable fury of the sacrilegious does not cease, but presses the harder, because they had seen the accusation which they had brought against the Lord emptied out by Pilate's careful questioning. At length the shameless men turn themselves to entreaties alone, 346Athat what they had not been able to do by accusing or as it were by reasoning, they now accomplish by demanding and by shouting and by accomplishing.

VERS. 25.-- And he released, etc. The author of sedition and of murders is released to the Jews, that is, the devil, who for his fault had been driven from the homeland of light and sent into the prison of darkness; and therefore they cannot have peace, because they did not choose the author of peace.

VERS. 26.-- They laid hold of one, etc. First the Lord carried the cross, but afterwards it was laid upon Simon, whom they met as they went out; and this fittingly: For Christ suffered for us, that you should follow his steps (I Petr. II). But he who wishes to follow him, let him take up his cross and follow. But because Simon 346Bwas not an Israelite but a Cyrenean, by him the Gentiles are designated, formerly strangers and guests to the testaments, but now in the faith citizens and of the household of God and coheirs of Christ; whence Simon is interpreted obedient, heir of Cyrene. But Simon came from the country place, that is from the village (pagus), from which come the pagans, who, strangers to the city of God, are ignorant of city conversation. But Simon, going forth from the village, carries the cross after Jesus, when the peoples of the Gentiles, having forsaken pagan rites, obediently embrace the footsteps of the Lord's passion.

VERS. 27.-- There followed him, etc. But they do not follow the cross with the same mind: the wicked follow that they may look with gladness on him dying; the women, that they might weep for him whom they desired to live, as about to die, as dying, and as dead. Nor is the lamentation of the women alone brought in on this ground, 346Csince many men also were grieving, but because the female sex, as more contemptible, could the more freely display before the chief priests who were present what it felt against them. But because the Lord knoweth who are his (II Tim. II), passing by the crowd of the raging people, he turns his eyes and his face to the women who loved and lamented him. Who bewailed him. They were sorrowing for a mortal life, which was to be ended by death, not for the blind men who were killing the physician.

VERS. 28.-- Daughters of Jerusalem. Note, when he calls them sons of Jerusalem, that not only those women who had come with him from Galilee, but also many women, citizens of that same city, had attached themselves to him. Weep not. Do not lament me as about to die, whose resurrection can quickly loose death, whose death will destroy death and the very 346Dauthor of death. Rather wash yourselves and your offspring with worthy fountains of tears, lest with the faithless you be condemned in vengeance for my cross.

VERS. 29.-- Behold, the days shall come. The days of the Roman siege and captivity. It is natural, when captivity threatens and the enemy's slaughter rages through the fields and the cities, that all who are able to escape seek out any high or hidden refuges in which they may hide themselves.

VERS. 30.-- Fall upon us. This can be understood, by way of surplus, of those who in either sex have made themselves eunuchs for the kingdom of heaven.

VERS. 31.-- For if in the green wood. The green wood is Christ and his elect, the dry the impious and sinners; whence he says: If then I, who have committed no sin, who 347Aam deservedly called the tree of life and bear twelve fruits of grace in each several month, do not go out of this world without the fire of the passion, what torments await those who are empty of fruits, and moreover do not fear to give the tree of life to the flames? And if now is the time that judgment should begin at the house of God, and all who wish to live godly in Christ suffer persecution, what is the end of those who do not believe the Gospel? (I Petr. IV; I Tim. III).

VERS. 32.-- And there were led also. That is fulfilled which was said: And he was reputed with the wicked (Isa. LIII). In death he is reputed with the wicked, that in the resurrection he might quicken the wicked, he who, made man for the sake of men, gave to men the power to become the sons of God.

VERS. 33.-- The place of Calvary. Outside the gate of Jerusalem 347Bwere places in which the heads of the condemned were struck off, and they were called places of Calvary, that is, of the beheaded. There he is crucified, that, there was the ground of the condemned, the standards of martyrdom might be raised up. And the robbers, etc. Execrable in the deed is the iniquity of the Jews, which crucifies the Redeemer of all as though a robber; yet in the mystery he is a good robber, who lay in wait for the devil, to carry off his vessels. By him who is on the right hand is signified the mortification of the flesh for heavenly glory; by him who is on the left, the mortification of the flesh for some cause of this world.

VERS. 34.-- And Jesus, etc. Because Luke set himself to write Christ's priesthood, rightly does the Lord intercede with God for his persecutors by the right of a priest, and open paradise to the robber who confesses him; nor does he pray in vain, 347Cbut obtains it for those who believed after his passion. He does not pray for those who through envy and pride understood him to be the Son of God, and deny and crucify him, but for those who, having a zeal of God, but not according to knowledge, knew not what they did.

Dividing. Of these John speaks more fully. The fourfold garment signifies the Church, which consists of four parts, spread through the whole world, distributed equally, that is, harmoniously, to all the parts. The tunic allotted signifies the unity of all the parts, which is held together by the bond of charity. Lots. In the lot the grace of God is commended. For thus it came in one to all, when the lot pleased all; because the grace of God also comes to all in unity, and when the lot is cast it is credited not to anyone's person or merits, but to the hidden 347Djudgment of God.

VERS. 35.-- He saved others. By their own sentence they condemn themselves, who even against their will confess that he saved others. He could indeed have saved himself, but because he himself is the Christ, the elect of God, he was unwilling to come down from the cross. For he who came for this, that he might be crucified for us, for this reason neglected to save himself from the cross: because by dying he took care to save, along with the rest of sinners, even those who crucified him.

VERS. 36.-- And the soldiers also mocked him, etc. Note that the Jews deride the name of Christ and of the Son of God, handed down to them by the authority of Scripture. But the soldiers, ignorant of the Scriptures, insult not Christ the elect of God, but the king of the Jews. And offering him vinegar. Vinegar 348Awere the Jews, degenerating from the wine of the patriarchs. With this vinegar the soldiers give the Lord to drink, whom at their prompting they deliver to death.

VERS. 38.-- And there was also, etc. These three tongues were pre-eminent above the rest: Hebrew, because of the Jews glorying in the law; Greek, because of the wisdom of the Gentiles; Latin, because of the Romans holding command. Therefore, will they or nill they, the Jews, every kingdom of the world, all worldly wisdom, and all the divine sacraments of the law bear witness that Jesus is the emperor of those who believe and confess him. This is the king of the Jews. The title which witnesses that Christ is king is set not below but above the cross, because although on the cross he was suffering in the weakness of a man, yet above the cross he was shining in the majesty of a king; and therefore, when he had been sacrificed, the title was set above him, that it might lie open to all who wished to read, 348Bthat is, to believe, that through the cross he did not lose his empire but strengthened it.

VERS. 40.-- He rebuked him. The rebuke itself reveals the coming scandal of the cross even among believers, of whom some are on the right hand, others on the left.

VERS. 42.-- Lord, remember me. Great grace shines out in this robber. He has no member free from the torment except his heart and his tongue; the whole of what he has free, he offers. With the heart he believes, with the mouth he confesses. He had faith, who believed that he would reign whom he saw dying beside him. He had hope, who asked for entrance. He held charity firmly, who both convicted the robber concerning his own iniquity and preached to him the life which he had come to know. Great is his virtue: he confesses him whom he sees dying in human weakness, whom the apostles were denying, 348Cwhom they had seen working miracles by divine power.

VERS. 43.-- Amen I say to thee. A most beautiful example of a conversion to be sought after, in that pardon is so quickly loosed to the robber, and the grace is more abundant than the petition. For the Lord always bestows more than he is asked. He was asking: Remember me, etc. The Lord says: This day thou shalt be with me, etc. Quickly does the Lord pardon, because that man is quickly converted.

VERS. 44.-- And there was darkness, etc. The sun drew back its rays, either that it might not see the Lord hanging, or that the impious blasphemers might not enjoy its light. And note that at the sixth hour, that is, when the sun was about to withdraw from the centre of the world, he is crucified; at daybreak, that is, when the sun rises, he rises again, because he died for our sins, and rose again for our justification (Rom. XVIII). [Vulg. Rom 4:25 qui traditus est propter delicta nostra, "who was delivered up for our offences"] When Adam 348Dhad sinned after midday, that is, when the light of faith had declined, the voice of the Lord is heard; at the same moment of time, therefore, at which he had then shut paradise against the sinning Adam, the Lord now opens it to the penitent robber.

VERS. 45, 46.-- The veil of the temple, etc. This was done as the Lord breathed forth his spirit, but Luke anticipates, wishing to join the miracle of the sun to the miracle of the veil. And the veil is rent, that the ark of the testament and all the sacraments of the law which were covered might appear and pass over to the Gentiles. Josephus says that the angelic powers, once the guardians of the temple, then cried out together: Let us flee from these seats. Father, etc. Calling upon the Father, he declares himself the Son of God. Commending his spirit, he intimates not a failure of power, but confidence in the same 349Atogether with a share of power. For he loves to give glory to the Father, wherein he forms us, that we may give glory to the Creator.

VERS. 47.-- And the centurion seeing. Seeing that he thus had the power of sending forth his spirit, which none has but the Maker of souls, the very man who had crucified him, in the very scandal of the passion, confesses him the Son of God, whom the Jews after so many miracles refused to believe. Whence by the centurion is designated the faith of the Church, which, when the veil of the heavenly mysteries had been unlocked by the Lord's death, straightway affirms him just and the Son of God, while the Synagogue is silent.

VERS. 48.-- Striking their breasts. That they strike their breasts as a sign of penitence and of mourning can be understood in two ways. For either they were grieving that one whose life they had loved 349Bhad been unjustly slain; or, remembering that they had procured his death, they were trembling that he was the more glorified in that death. But whatever cause compelled them to beat the breast, let us see the distance between nation and nation. The Gentiles, fearing God as the Lord died, glorify him with the voice of open confession; the Jews, only striking their breasts, go home in silence.

VERS. 50.-- Named Joseph. Joseph is worthy, through the nobility of his worldly power, to receive the body; through the justice of his merits, to bury it.

VERS. 52.-- This man went, etc. Not the apostles, but Joseph, just and steadfast, and Nicodemus, in whom was no guile, bury him. Such too is the burial of Christ, which has in it no fraud or iniquity; and in this the shuffling of the Jews is blocked. For if 349Cthe apostles had buried him, they would say that he was not buried whom they said was carried off.

VERS. 53.-- In a winding-sheet. From the simple burial of the Lord the ambition of the rich is condemned, who cannot do without riches even when dead.

Mystically: he wraps Jesus in a clean winding-sheet who receives him with a pure mind. Hence also the custom of the Church holds that the sacrifice of the altar be celebrated not on silk, not on dyed cloth, but on pure linen.

And laid him. Well, then, is Christ laid in the tomb of a just man, that the Son of man may have where to lay his head, and may rest in the dwelling of justice. Well in a new one, that he who receives Christ may walk in newness of life. No one buries Christ save he who believes in Christ.

In a monument that was hewn. The Lord alone is enclosed in the tomb, 349Dthat his burial and resurrection might be shown unlike the frailty of our nature. He was buried, but for as long as he willed; he rose again, but when he willed. Wherein not yet. If he were laid in a monument where another had been laid, another would be feigned to have risen. Hewn in the rock, lest, had it been built of many stones, and the foundations of the tomb dug away, he should be said to have been carried off by theft.

VERS. 54.-- And it was the day, etc. On the sixth day man was made; on the sabbath God rested. Crucified therefore on the sixth day, he fulfils the secret of the preparation. Resting on the sabbath in the sepulchre, he awaits the event of the resurrection. So must we in the sixth age of the world suffer for the Lord. In the seventh, that is, when we die, the body remains in the tomb, 350Athe soul with the Lord in rest after its labours, until in the eighth the bodies rise again.

VERS. 55.-- The women. When the other acquaintances of Jesus withdrew after the body was taken down, the women alone, who loved more closely, were looking upon the office of the burial, that at a fitting time they might offer the gift of their devotion, awaiting what Jesus had promised; and therefore they were the first to see him.

VERS. 56.-- And returning. Having looked upon the Lord's burial, they return and prepare spices and ointments: they who, having read, heard and called to mind the Lord's passion, turn themselves to the works of the virtues in which Christ delights, and on the sabbath keep silence. Being about to come to the Lord after the sabbath with their gifts, when the preparation-day of the present life is finished they await, rejoicing in blessed rest, the time 350Bof the resurrection, at which they run to meet Christ with the fragrant spices of their deeds. And on the sabbath, etc. It had been commanded that the silence of the sabbath be kept from evening until evening. Therefore the women, when the Lord was buried, so long as it was lawful to work, are busied on the preparation-day in making ready the ointments until the setting of the sun; and, the sabbath being over at the going down of the sun, that coming in the morning they might anoint him, because on the evening of the sabbath, the moment of night already coming on, they could not go to the monument.

CAPUT XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- Una autem sabbati, etc. Una sabbati idem est quod prima sabbati, id est, una dies a die sabbati vel sabbatorum, quae nunc Dominica dicitur. Quod valde mane mulieres ad monumentum venerunt, 350Cmagnus fervor charitatis quaerendi et inveniendi Dominum ostenditur.

Mystice: Exemplum datur, ut, illuminata fide, decussisque vitiorum tenebris, ad sacrum corpus Domini accedamus. Nam sepulcrum figura est altaris, in quo celebratur mysterium corporis et sanguinis Domini. Aromata quae deferunt odorem virtutum, et suavitatem orationum, cum quibus altari propinquare debemus, significat.

VERS. 2.-- Lapidem revolutum. Revolutio lapidis significat reserationem sacramentorum Christi, quae velo litterae tegebantur. Lex in lapide scripta est, cujus ablato tegmine Domini corpus mortuum non invenitur, sed vivum evangelizatur: quia etsi cognovimus Christum secundum carnem, sed jam non novimus 350D(II Cor. V). Potuit autem clauso exire sepulcro, qui clauso exivit utero.

VERS. 4.-- Mente consternatae. Mente consternantur, quia et lapidem immensae magnitudinis revolutum stupebant, et corpus tam venerabile non inventum dolebant. Sicut tentato in solitudine Domino, expleta victoria angeli ministrabant ei, ita modo post devictam mortem venerunt angeli, qui non solum verbo, sed etiam fulgenti habitu gloriam annuntiant triumphantis. Et sicut corpori in sepulcro astiterunt, sic etiam corpori consecrato in altari quotidie assistunt.

VERS. 5.-- Declinarent vultum. Nota quod nec sanctae mulieres in terram cecidisse, nec aliquis 351Asanctorum in resurrectione Domini prostratus in terram adorare legitur; unde mos coepit in Ecclesia paschali tempore non flecti genua in oratione, sed vultu in terram declinato orare.

VERS. 6.-- Recordamini. Die tertia, sicut ex hoc loco habemus, inter discipulos etiam feminis quae eum sequebantur, Dominus resurrecturum se praedixit. Parasceve hora nona exspiravit, mane prima sabbati resurrexit. Merito autem una die et duabus noctibus in sepulcro jacuit, quia lucem suae simplae mortis (quae tantum in carne erat) tenebris duplae nostrae mortis quae in corpore erat et anima, apposuit, et utramque nostram curavit. Si enim utramque suscepisset, nos a neutra liberasset.

VERS. 8.-- Et recordatae sunt. Sicut mulieres in 351Bmonumento Domini stantes, recordantur verborum quae de sua dispensatione praefatus erat, sic nos inter Dominicae passionis mysteria celebranda, non solum passionis, sed et resurrectionis debemus recordari.

VERS. 9.-- Nuntiaverunt. Sicut in principio mulier auctor culpae viro fuit, vir exsecutor fuit erroris, ita nunc quae mortem prior gustaverat, resurrectionem vidit, et ne perpetui reatus opprobrium apud viros sustineret, quae culpam viro transfuderat, transfudit et gratiam. Et quia constantiam praedicandi non habet inferior sexus ad exsequendum infirmior, mandatur viris officium evangelizandi.

VERS. 11.-- Et non crediderunt. Quod discipuli tarde credunt, non tam illorum infirmitas quam 351Cnostra futura firmitas fuit, nam illis dubitantibus resurrectio multis argumentis monstratur, quae dum legentes agnoscimus, quid aliud quam de eorum dubitatione solidamur?

VERS. 12.-- Petrus autem surgens. Joannes commemorat, quod et ipse et Petrus simul cucurrerunt, et quod Petro intus introeunte in monumentum, et vidente linteamina et sudarium, ipse Joannes inclinans se, vidit linteamina, nec introivit. Sed intelligendum est Petrum primo procumbentem vidisse quod Lucas commemorat, Joannes tacet: post vero ingressum, ut diligentius interiora dignosceret, ingressum tamen antequam Joannes intraret.

VERS. 13.-- Nomine Emmaus. Emmaus ipsa est metropolis civitas insignis Palaestinae, quae post expugnationem 351DJudaeae sub Marco Aurelio Antonio principe restaurata, cum statu mutavit et nomen.

VERS. 15, 16.-- Dum fabularentur. Loquentes de se Dominus appropinquans comitatur, ut et fidem resurrectionis eorum mentibus incendat, et quod se promiserat facturum, impleat: Ubi sunt duo vel tres congregati in nomine meo, ibi sum in medio eorum (Matth. XXVIII). Appropinquans, etc. Oculi, etc. Apparuit quidem in specie propria, sed speciem quam recognoscerent, non ostendit. Sicut et ipsi et intus amabant, et tamen dubitabant, sic ipse foris et praesens adest, et quis sit, non ostendit. De se ergo loquentibus praesentiam exhibet, sed de se dubitantibus speciem cognitionis aufert.

352AVERS. 18, 19, 20.-- Peregrinus es, etc. Quibus, etc. Peregrinum putant, quem non agnoscunt. Et vere peregrinus erat eis, a quorum fragilitate per gloriam resurrectionis jam longe stabat, a quorum fide resurrectionis nescia extraneus manebat. Vir propheta, etc. Et quomodo tradiderunt, etc. Prophetam et magnum fatentur, Filium Dei tacent, vel quia nondum perfecta credunt, vel si credunt timent tradi in manus Judaeorum, nescientes cum quo loquuntur.

VERS. 21.-- Nos autem, etc. Quodammodo redarguunt seipsos, quod in illum redemptionem speraverint, quem mortuum viderant, nec resurrecturum credebant, et maxime dolent eum sine culpa occisum, quem innocentem noverant, et ex hoc tristes 352Bincedebant.

VERS. 22.-- Terruerunt nos, etc. Merito dicuntur teruisse eos quibus de non invento corpore plus moestitiam addiderunt, quam de nuntiata resurrectione gaudium quo recrearentur, afferunt.

VERS. 24.-- Abierunt quidam, etc. Cum supra dixerit Petrum cucurrisse ad monumentum, et modo dicat Cleophas: Quidam ex nostris abierunt ad monumentum, etc. Intelligendum est, quod duo simul ierunt. Sed Petrum solum primo commemoravit, quia primitus illi nuntiavit Maria.

VERS. 25.-- O stulti et tardi corde. Hic gemina humiliandi necesitas incumbit, qui nec in Scripturis quantum oportet edocti, nec ad implenda quae novimus quantum decet, sumus intenti. Nam si 352CMoyses et prophetae de Christo locuti sunt, et eum per passionem et gloriam intraturum praedixerant, quomodo gloriatur se esse Christianum, qui neque in Scripturis qualiter ad Christum pertinent investigat, neque ad gloriam quam cum Christo habere cupit, per passiones attingere desiderat?

VERS. 28, 29.-- Et ipse, etc. Mane nobiscum, etc. Talem se exhibuit in corpore, qualis apud illos erat in mente. Probandi erant, si hunc quem nondum ut Deum diligebant, saltem ut peregrinum diligerent. Sed quia extranei a claritate esse non possunt, cum quibus veritas gradiebatur, ad hospitium vocant, imo cogunt. Quo exemplo colligitur, quia peregrini non solum ad hospitium sunt vocandi, sed etiam trahendi.

352DVERS. 30.-- Fregit, etc. Quem in expositione Scripturae non cognoverunt, in fractione panis noverunt, quia non auditores legis, sed factores justificabuntur, et veritas melius operando quam audiendo intelligitur.

VERS. 31.-- Aperti sunt, etc. Subtrahitur carnalibus oculis species infirmitatis, ut mentibus incipiat apparere gloria resurrectionis.

VERS. 32.-- Et dixerunt, etc. Mystice: Ideo in Christo illis ostensa est alia effigies, ne eum nisi in fractione panis cognoscerent, ut omnes intelligant se Christum non agnoscere, nisi fiant participes corporis ejus, id est Ecclesiae, cujus unitatem commendat Apostolus in sacramento panis, dicens: 353AUnus panis, unum corpus multi sumus (I Cor. X). Cum ergo panem benedictum porrigit, aperiuntur oculi ut eum cognoscant, et removetur impedimentum quod a Satana in oculis erat, ne agnosceretur Jesus. Et hoc impedimentum permittit inesse Dominus donec ad sacramentum panis veniatur. Sed participata unitate corporis, aufertur impedimentum inimici, ut possit Christus agnosci.

Ardens. (GREG.) Exaudito sermone, cor prius torpore incredulitatis et timoris frigidum igne sancti Spiritus est accensum, ut jam superno desiderio ardeat, et ad credendam veritatem se extendat. Quot praeceptis instruitur homo, quasi tot facibus inflammatur.

VERS. 34.-- Surrexit Dominus. Jam fama erat 353Bquod resurrexerat Jesus ab illis mulieribus facta, et a Simone Petro cui apparuerat, haec loquentes invenerant isti duo, sed et ipsi prius audierant quod resurrexerat, sed timuerunt dicere in via quando tantummodo angelos dixerunt visos mulieribus. Ignorantes enim eum cum quo loquebantur, poterant timere ne inciderent in manus Judaeorum, si quid passim dicerent de Christi resurrectione.

VERS. 36.-- Stetit Jesus, etc. Haec ostensio est illa, de qua Joannes ait (Joan. XX): Cum sero esset die, etc., et dixit eis: Pax vobis. Quod vero dicit Joannes Thomam non fuisse cum illis: cum Lucas dicat istos duos invenisse undecim congregatos, et eos qui cum eis erat, intelligendum est quod Thomas inde exisset antequam Dominus eis hoc loquentibus 353Cappareret.

VERS. 37, 38.-- Spiritum videre. Et dixit eis. Noverant discipuli fuisse verum hominem, sed postquam mortuus est, non credunt tertia die potuisse veram carnem de sepulcro resurgere. Putant ergo se videre spiritum quem emisit in passione, sed hunc errorem aufert Dominus.

Quid turbati estis. (AMBR.) Credebant apostoli, sed tamen turbantur, etc., usque ad quod clausis januis, Dominus cum corpore suo se improvisus infuderit.

Cogitationes, etc. Falsae non desuper descendentes, sed sicut mala herba in corde ascendentes, fidem quam in vobis plantavi exigo, quod ex vobis ortum est condemno, si non est veritas resurrectionis, 353Dperditus est fructus passionis.

VERS. 39.-- Palpate et videte. Multis documentis persuadet resurrectionem praebendo se, et oculis videndum, et manibus contrectandum, qui dum palpando ossa carnemque monstrat, statum suae vel nostrae resurrectionis signat, in qua corpus nostrum, et subtile erit per effectum spiritualis potentiae, et palpabile per veritatem naturae. Post resurrectionem Dominus in corpore suo duo contraria ostendit, et palpabile ejusdem naturae ut informet ad fidem, et incorruptibile alterius gloriae, ut invitet ad praemium.

VERS. 40.-- Ostendit eis manus, etc. Ut ostensa vulnerum cicatrice infidelitatis vulnus sanaret, quas 354Acicatrices et tunc servavit, et in judicio servaturus est. Primo ad fidem resurrectionis astruendam: deinde ut Patri pro nobis supplicans, quale genus mortis pro homine pertulerit semper ostendat: tertio, ut sua morte redemptis quam misericorditer sint adjuti, propositis ejusdem mortis insinuat indiciis. Sed postremo, ut in judicio quam juste damnentur impii denuntiet, ostensa quam ab illis accepit cicatrice. Non ergo ex impotentia curandi cicatrices servavit, sed ut perpetuum victoriae suae circumferat triumphum.

VERS. 41.-- Habetis hic aliquid. Ad insinuandam veritatem resurrectionis, non solum tangi, sed convesci voluit, non quod vel ipse vel nos post resurrectionem cibo debeamus indigere, sed ut corpus, 354Bnon spiritum esse manifestaret, manducavit potestate, non necessitate. Aliter absorbet aquam terra sitiens aliter solis radius calens: illa indigentia, iste potentia.

VERS. 42.-- Piscis assi. Piscis assus, ipse mediator passus, in aquis humani generis captus laqueo mortis, assatus tempore passionis. Ipse et favus mellis nobis est in resurrectione. Favus mellis in cera, id est Divinitas in humanitate. In hujus mellis comestione signatur, quia illos in corpore suo ad aeternam quietem suscipit, qui cum tribulationes pro Deo sentiunt, ab amore internae dulcedinis non recedunt. Qui hic assantur, illic vera dulcedine satiabuntur.

VERS. 43.-- Et cum manducasset. Omnes ambages 354Caufert: visus est, tactus est, manducavit, et ne in aliquo sensus humanos ludificasse videretur, mittit manus ad Scripturas, ut etsi magicis artibus videatur potuisse quod voluit, sicut dicunt pagani, saltem videant prophetare non potuisse antequam natus fuit.

VERS. 45.-- Tunc aperuit. Post visum, post contactum, post commemoratam legem aperuit sensum, ut quod vident et legunt, intelligant, deinde post sui corporis commendatam veritatem commendat Ecclesiae unitatem: Et praedicari in nomine ejus.

VERS. 46.-- Oportebat, etc. Oportebat ut ministri qui poenitentiam et remissionem erant praedicaturi 354Din omnes gentes, inciperent ab Hierosolymis. Non solum quia illis credita sunt eloquia Dei, et quia eorum est adoptio filiorum, et gloria testamenta, et legislatio; sed ideo etiam ut gentes variis erroribus et facinoribus implicitae, hoc maxime indicio divinae pietatis ad spem veniae provocentur, quod eis qui Filium Dei crucifixerunt, veniam relaxat.

VERS. 49.-- Vos autem sedete. Postquam discipulos de virtute praedicationis perfecte instruxit, cum repente si vellet posset eos roborare, distulit, dicens: Vos autem sedete. Ut exemplum sequentibus daret, ne imperfecti praedicare ante tempus praesumant. In civitate sedet, qui intra mentis claustra se constringit, ne loquendo exterius evagetur, ut cum 355Adivina virtute perfecte fuerit indutus, tunc quasi a seipso foras exeat, alios instruendo.

VERS. 50.-- Eduxit autem eos. Praetermissis omnibus quae per quadraginta dies gesta sunt, primo resurrectionis diei tacite conjungit novissimum quo ascendit in coelum. Et pulchre benedicturus discipulos, educit in Bethaniam, quae domus obedientiae dicitur, quia qui propter inobedientiam perversorum descendit, propter obedientiam conversorum ascendit.

VERS. 53.-- In templo. Ut in loco orationis inter laudum devotiones, promissum sancti Spiritus adventum, paratis per omnia cordibus exspectent. Sic 356Anos post celebrata in Hierosolymis, id est visione pacis, passionis et resurrectionis solemnia, Domino duce petamus Bethaniam, ut mente quieta corporis et sanguinis ejus sacramentis imbuamur, et domus obedientiae esse curemus.

Laudantes et benedicentes. Lucas qui sacerdotium Christi caeteris amplius exponendum suscepit, et ideo Evangelium suum a ministerio templi per sacerdotium Zachariae coepit, pulchre hoc in templi devotione complevit, cum apostolis ibi ministros, scilicet, novi sacerdotii futuros, non in victimarum sanguine, sed in laude Dei et benedictione concludit.

CHAPTER XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- And on the first day of the week, etc. The first of the sabbath is the same as the first day of the sabbath, that is, the one day from the day of the sabbath or of the sabbaths, which is now called the Lord's day. In that the women came very early to the monument, 350Ca great fervour of charity in seeking and finding the Lord is shown.

Mystically: an example is given, that with faith enlightened and the darkness of the vices shaken off, we should approach the sacred body of the Lord. For the sepulchre is a figure of the altar, on which the mystery of the Lord's body and blood is celebrated. The spices which they bring signify the odour of the virtues and the sweetness of prayers, with which we ought to draw near to the altar.

VERS. 2.-- The stone rolled back. The rolling back of the stone signifies the unlocking of the sacraments of Christ, which were covered by the veil of the letter. The law was written on stone; and when its covering is taken away, the body of the Lord is not found dead, but is proclaimed living: because although we have known Christ according to the flesh, yet now we know him no more 350D(II Cor. V). And he was able to go forth from a closed sepulchre who went forth from a closed womb.

VERS. 4.-- Cast down in mind. They are cast down in mind, because they were both amazed that a stone of immense size had been rolled back, and grieved that so venerable a body was not found. As, when the Lord was tempted in the wilderness, angels ministered to him when the victory was accomplished, so now, after death had been conquered, angels came, who announce the glory of the triumphant one not only by word but also by their shining habit. And as they stood by the body in the sepulchre, so also they daily stand by the consecrated body upon the altar.

VERS. 5.-- They bowed down their countenance. Note that neither are the holy women read to have fallen to the earth, nor any 351Aof the saints to have adored prostrate on the earth at the Lord's resurrection; whence the custom began in the Church, at paschal time, not to bend the knees in prayer, but to pray with the countenance bowed toward the earth.

VERS. 6.-- Remember. On the third day, as we have from this place, the Lord foretold among the disciples, and to the women also who were following him, that he would rise again. On the preparation-day, at the ninth hour, he expired; in the morning, on the first day of the sabbath, he rose again. And deservedly did he lie one day and two nights in the sepulchre, because he set the light of his own single death (which was in the flesh only) against the darkness of our double death, which was in the body and in the soul, and healed both of ours. For if he had taken both upon himself, he would have freed us from neither.

VERS. 8.-- And they remembered. As the women, standing at 351Bthe Lord's monument, remember the words which he had spoken beforehand concerning his dispensation, so we, amid the mysteries of the Lord's passion that are to be celebrated, ought to remember not only the passion but the resurrection also.

VERS. 9.-- They told. As in the beginning the woman was to the man the author of guilt, and the man was the executor of the error, so now she who had first tasted death saw the resurrection; and lest she should bear among men the reproach of a perpetual guilt, she who had poured guilt into the man poured grace also into him. And because the inferior sex, weaker for carrying a thing through, has not the constancy of preaching, the office of evangelizing is committed to men.

VERS. 11.-- And they did not believe. That the disciples are slow to believe was not so much their weakness as 351Cour own firmness to come; for while they doubted, the resurrection is shown by many proofs, and while we, reading, come to know them, what else are we but made solid out of their doubting?

VERS. 12.-- But Peter rising up. John relates that both he and Peter ran together, and that, while Peter went inside into the monument and saw the linen cloths and the napkin, John himself, stooping down, saw the linen cloths and did not go in. But it is to be understood that Peter first, stooping down, saw what Luke relates and John passes over in silence; but that afterwards he entered, that he might discern the inner things more carefully, yet entered before John went in.

VERS. 13.-- Named Emmaus. Emmaus is itself the metropolis, a notable city of Palestine, which, after the storming 351Dof Judea, was restored under the prince Marcus Aurelius Antonius, and changed its name along with its condition.

VERS. 15, 16.-- While they talked together. The Lord draws near and accompanies them as they speak of him, that he may both kindle in their minds the faith of the resurrection, and fulfil what he had promised he would do: Where there are two or three gathered together in my name, there am I in the midst of them (Matth. XXVIII). Drawing near, etc. Their eyes, etc. He appeared indeed in his own form, but did not show the form which they might recognize. As they both loved him within and yet doubted, so he himself is present outside and at hand, and does not show who he is. To those who speak of him, then, he grants his presence; but from those who doubt of him he takes away the appearance by which he might be known.

352AVERS. 18, 19, 20.-- Art thou a stranger, etc. To whom, etc. They think him a stranger whom they do not recognize. And truly he was a stranger to them, from whose frailty he now stood far off through the glory of the resurrection, and from whose faith, ignorant of the resurrection, he remained an alien. A man, a prophet, etc. And how they delivered him up, etc. They confess him a prophet and a great one; of the Son of God they are silent, either because they do not yet believe perfectly, or, if they believe, they fear to be delivered into the hands of the Jews, not knowing with whom they speak.

VERS. 21.-- But we hoped, etc. In a manner they refute themselves, in that they had hoped for redemption in him whom they had seen dead, nor did they believe that he would rise again; and they grieve most of all that he was slain without guilt, whom they had known to be innocent; and from this they were walking sorrowful 352B.

VERS. 22.-- They affrighted us, etc. They are rightly said teruisse those to whom they added more sadness about the body not found than they bring gladness, by the resurrection announced, wherewith they might be refreshed.

VERS. 24.-- Some of us went, etc. Since above he said that Peter ran to the monument, and now Cleophas says: Some of us went to the monument, etc., it is to be understood that two went together. But he mentioned Peter alone first, because Mary announced it to him first.

VERS. 25.-- O foolish and slow of heart. Here a twofold necessity of being humbled presses upon us, who are neither taught in the Scriptures as much as we ought, nor as intent as is fitting on fulfilling the things we know. For if 352CMoses and the prophets spoke of Christ, and had foretold that he would enter through passion into glory, how does he glory that he is a Christian who neither searches out in the Scriptures how they pertain to Christ, nor desires to reach through sufferings the glory which he wishes to have with Christ?

VERS. 28, 29.-- And he himself, etc. Stay with us, etc. He showed himself such in body as he was among them in mind. They were to be tested, whether they would love at least as a stranger him whom they did not yet love as God. But because they cannot be strangers to the brightness, they with whom the Truth was walking call him to lodging, nay compel him. From which example it is gathered that strangers are not only to be invited to lodging, but even to be drawn in.

352DVERS. 30.-- He broke it, etc. Him whom they did not know in the exposition of Scripture they knew in the breaking of bread; because not the hearers of the law but the doers shall be justified, and the truth is better understood by working than by hearing.

VERS. 31.-- Their eyes were opened, etc. The appearance of weakness is withdrawn from the eyes of the flesh, that the glory of the resurrection may begin to appear to their minds.

VERS. 32.-- And they said, etc. Mystically: for this reason another likeness was shown them in Christ, that they might not know him save in the breaking of bread; so that all may understand that they do not recognize Christ unless they become partakers of his body, that is, of the Church, whose unity the Apostle commends in the sacrament of the bread, saying: 353AWe being many are one bread, one body (I Cor. X). When therefore he reaches out the blessed bread, their eyes are opened that they may know him, and the impediment is removed which was upon their eyes from Satan, that Jesus might not be recognized. And this impediment the Lord allows to remain until the sacrament of the bread be come to. But when the unity of the body has been shared, the enemy's impediment is taken away, that Christ may be recognized.

Burning. (GREG.) When the word had been heard, the heart, before cold with the numbness of unbelief and of fear, was kindled with the fire of the Holy Spirit, so that it now burns with a heavenly desire and stretches itself out to believe the truth. By as many precepts as a man is instructed, by so many torches, as it were, is he set aflame.

VERS. 34.-- The Lord is risen. Already there was a report 353Bthat Jesus had risen, made by those women and by Simon Peter, to whom he had appeared; these two found them speaking of these things. But they themselves also had heard earlier that he had risen; yet they feared to say it on the way, when they said only that angels had been seen by the women. For, not knowing him with whom they were speaking, they could fear that they might fall into the hands of the Jews, if they should say anything anywhere about Christ's resurrection.

VERS. 36.-- Jesus stood, etc. This appearance is that of which John says (Joan. XX): When it was late that day, etc., and he said to them: Peace be to you. But as to John's saying that Thomas was not with them, while Luke says that these two found the eleven gathered together, and those who were with them, it is to be understood that Thomas had gone out from there before the Lord 353Cappeared to them as they spoke of these things.

VERS. 37, 38.-- To see a spirit. And he said to them. The disciples had known him to be true man, but after he died they do not believe that true flesh could rise from the sepulchre on the third day. They think therefore that they see the spirit which he sent forth in the passion; but the Lord takes away this error.

Why are you troubled. (AMBR.) The apostles believed, but nevertheless they are troubled, etc., as far as that the Lord, with the doors shut, poured himself in unforeseen with his body.

Thoughts, etc. False ones, not descending from above, but rising up in the heart like an evil weed. I require the faith which I planted in you; what has sprung from yourselves I condemn. If there is no truth of the resurrection, 353Dthe fruit of the passion is lost.

VERS. 39.-- Handle and see. By many proofs he persuades them of the resurrection, offering himself both to be seen by their eyes and to be handled by their hands; and while by their handling he shows bones and flesh, he marks the state of his own resurrection and of ours, in which our body will be both subtle through the effect of a spiritual power, and palpable through the truth of its nature. After the resurrection the Lord showed in his body two contrary things: both what is palpable, of the same nature, that he might form them to faith, and what is incorruptible, of another glory, that he might invite them to the reward.

VERS. 40.-- He showed them his hands, etc. That by showing the scar of his wounds he might heal the wound of unbelief; which 354Ascars he both kept then and will keep in the judgment. First, to establish faith in the resurrection; then, that, entreating the Father for us, he may always show what kind of death he endured for man; thirdly, that he may intimate to those redeemed by his death how mercifully they have been helped, by setting before them the tokens of that same death. But lastly, that in the judgment he may declare how justly the impious are condemned, by showing the scar which he received from them. Not therefore from any inability to heal them did he keep the scars, but that he might carry about the perpetual triumph of his victory.

VERS. 41.-- Have you here anything. To convey the truth of the resurrection, he willed not only to be touched but to eat with them; not that either he or we after the resurrection ought to need food, but that he might make manifest that he was a body, 354Bnot a spirit; he ate by power, not by necessity. In one way the thirsting earth soaks up water, in another the hot ray of the sun: the former by need, the latter by power.

VERS. 42.-- Of a broiled fish. The broiled fish is the Mediator himself who suffered, taken in the waters of the human race by the snare of death, broiled at the time of the passion. He is also to us a honeycomb in the resurrection. The honeycomb in the wax, that is, the Godhead in the manhood. In the eating of this honey it is signified that he receives into his body, unto eternal rest, those who, when they feel tribulations for God, do not withdraw from the love of the inward sweetness. They who are broiled here shall there be filled with the true sweetness.

VERS. 43.-- And when he had eaten. He takes away all evasions 354C: he was seen, he was touched, he ate; and lest in anything he should seem to have deluded the human senses, he puts his hands to the Scriptures, so that even if it should seem, as the pagans say, that by magic arts he was able to do what he willed, they may at least see that he could not have prophesied before he was born.

VERS. 45.-- Then he opened. After the sight, after the touch, after the law recalled to mind, he opened their understanding, that they might understand what they see and read; then, after the truth of his own body had been commended, he commends the unity of the Church: And that there should be preached in his name.

VERS. 46.-- It behoved, etc. It behoved that the ministers who were to preach penance and remission 354Dunto all nations should begin from Jerusalem. Not only because to them were entrusted the oracles of God, and because theirs is the adoption of sons, and the glory the testaments, and the law-giving; but for this reason also, that the Gentiles, entangled in various errors and crimes, might by this token above all of the divine loving-kindness be roused to the hope of pardon: that he grants pardon to those who crucified the Son of God.

VERS. 49.-- But stay you. After he had perfectly instructed the disciples about the power of preaching, although he could of a sudden, if he willed, have strengthened them, he put it off, saying: But stay you. That he might give an example to those who follow, lest the imperfect presume to preach before the time. He sits in the city who binds himself within the cloisters of the mind, lest by speaking he wander abroad outside; so that when he has 355Abeen perfectly clothed with the divine power, then he may go forth as it were out of himself, by instructing others.

VERS. 50.-- And he led them out. Passing over all that was done through the forty days, he silently joins to the first day of the resurrection the last, on which he ascended into heaven. And beautifully, being about to bless the disciples, he leads them out to Bethany, which is called the house of obedience: because he who came down on account of the disobedience of the perverse, ascended on account of the obedience of the converted.

VERS. 53.-- In the temple. That in the place of prayer, amid the devotions of praise, with hearts made ready in all things, they might await the promised coming of the Holy Spirit. So 356Alet us, after the solemnities of the passion and the resurrection have been celebrated in Jerusalem, that is, in the vision of peace, seek Bethany with the Lord for our leader, that with a quiet mind we may be steeped in the sacraments of his body and blood, and may take care to be a house of obedience.

Praising and blessing. Luke, who took upon himself to set forth Christ's priesthood more fully than the rest, and therefore began his Gospel from the ministry of the temple through the priesthood of Zachary, beautifully completed it in the devotion of the temple, when he concludes with the apostles there, ministers, namely, of the new priesthood to come, not in the blood of victims, but in the praise of God and in blessing.

Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself.
